## 5. The year's arc

*Fold pass 2, sections 5 and 7. The spine is Fable's skeleton and its 53-week table (canon § 1), amended by the canon's fixed facts (canon § 5) and its eight amendments to the week table (canon § 6). GPT's and Gemini's § 5 and § 7 are merged onto it: their compatible scenes, details and lines are kept and re-dated onto the canonical dates. Every weekday below was checked in Python against the 2025 calendar (Wed 1 Jan; Easter Day Sun 20 Apr). Entry ids: `S###` are the shared seeds; `W###` are Fable's anchors (W001–W109). Beats added in this fold that have no id yet are marked **(new)**; the Fable dated-year halves number their own entries from W110 up, so no id is invented here.*

### 5.1 Fixed calls a builder needs first

| Question | Canon answer | Tier |
|---|---|---|
| Winter house | The Cross Keys, Gracechurch Street, 1 January to Shrove Tuesday 4 March | T3 → story |
| Lent | The company stops on Shrove Tuesday, more cautious than the Rose, which plays into Lent (a new play on Ash Wednesday) and stops after Fri 14 Mar (T1). Rehearsals resume Fri 11 Apr | T5 / T1 for the Rose |
| Spring house | The Theatre, from Easter Monday 21 April | T2 |
| Summer | No tour. The company plays on through the riots and martial law to Sat 26 Jul (no play Mon 30 Jun or Thu 24 Jul) while the Rose is dark after Thu 26 Jun (T1). Dark Mon 28 Jul – Sat 23 Aug. Reopens Mon 25 Aug, the same day as the Rose (T1 for the Rose). Hired men on half wages (5s) through the dark weeks | T5 on a T3 gap |
| Next winter | Stays at the Theatre; no inn. Dark from Tue 23 Dec | T5 |
| *Love's Labour's Lost* | Finished Tue 21 Jan · licensed Fri 24 Jan · parts out Tue 28 Jan · **first played Thu 20 Feb, Cross Keys**, the day of Southwell's trial · last winter play Shrove Tue 4 Mar | T5 on T2 |
| *Romeo*, Lent | **Early notes only**: Brooke bought and first notes Thu 6 Mar (S024); names, a plan of two houses, single images through Lent. No dialogue, no line of the play, before Sat 5 Jul | T5 on T2 |
| *Richard II* | Holinshed from Wed 19 Mar · a line or two on the ride back, 8–10 Apr · **begun Thu 17 Apr**, Gaunt first · the hollow crown Sat 17 May · **deposition drafted Tue 10 Jun** (S094) · finished Sat 21 Jun · read to the fellows Mon 30 Jun · **licensed with the deposition cut Thu 10 Jul** · **first played Thu 2 Oct, the Theatre** · played whole once, privately, at Canon Row Tue 9 Dec (T3) | T5 on T2 |
| *Romeo and Juliet* | Drafting from Sat 5 Jul · Queen Mab at the Jackdaw Tue 15 Jul · orchard scene Sat 9 Aug at Stratford · Mercutio dies Sat 6 Sep · dawn parting Sat 11 Oct · the potion, and Nick's voice cracks, Fri 24 Oct · the tomb Sat 8 Nov · **finished Tue 11 Nov** (Martinmas) · licensed untouched Thu 13 Nov · first reading Wed 19 Nov · **first played Sat 6 Dec, the Theatre** | T5 on T2 |
| *A Midsummer Night's Dream* | First page Tue 28 Oct (Titania's ruined seasons) · the mechanicals from Sat 22 Nov · **unfinished on 31 Dec**, about twelve leaves | T5 on T2 |
| *Love's Labour's Won* | Only a title Heminges asks for and William has not written; a tiring-house jest | T1 title / T3 identity |
| Sonnets | Through the year, no addressee. Tied with a tape Sat 13 Dec and put at the bottom of the coffer | T3 |
| Court, Christmas 1595 | Richmond: Fri 26 Dec *LLL* · Sat 27 Dec *Romeo* · Sun 28 Dec *Errors*. Payees Heminges and Bryan; **not paid in 1595** (warrant dated 21 Dec 1596) | T1 dates and payees, T5 titles |
| Stratford journeys | **Lent ride:** out Wed 26 – Fri 28 Mar, home 28 Mar – 7 Apr, back Tue 8 – Thu 10 Apr. **Summer walk:** out Mon 28 – Thu 31 Jul, home 31 Jul – 17 Aug, back with the carrier's packhorses Mon 18 – Thu 21 Aug | T5 on T3 |
| Second Stratford fire | Tue 15 Jul, about twenty houses and malt-barns in a row north of the centre; none of the family's touched. News in London Mon 21 Jul (Gilbert's garbled call, then Anne's letter by Hobbins an hour later) | T5 date on a T1 fire |
| Richmond | Up Wed 24 Dec by tilt-boat from Old Swan with Davy Gannet; Christmas Day at the Feathers; down Mon 29 Dec | T5 |
| The coffer | 1 Jan £24 10s · 25 Mar £27 2s · 10 Apr £19 6s · 24 Jun £25 0s · 27 Jul £26 8s (£20 8s under the locks, £6 in the belt) · 22 Aug £20 15s · 29 Sep £23 10s · 24 Dec £33 0s · 31 Dec £33 10s | T5, exact on these dates |
| Illnesses (the only ones) | Cold with low fever 13–19 Jan · low-back strain from Tue 12 Aug (flat 12–15 Aug, eased by about 15 Sep) · tooth from Tue 9 Sep, abscess 10 Oct, drawn Tue 14 Oct, tender to 21 Oct. No child is ever ill | T5 |
| Carrier in at the Bell | Thomas Hobbins `[invented]`, about every third Thursday: 16 Jan, 6 Feb, 27 Feb, 20 Mar, 24 Apr, 15 May, 5 Jun, 26 Jun, 17 Jul, 11 Sep, 2 Oct, 23 Oct, 13 Nov, 4 Dec, 18 Dec; once unscheduled, Mon 21 Jul | T5 |
| The lady's days | Seven small unlabelled crosses: 5 Jan, 28 Feb, 22 Mar, 22 May, 8 Jun, 14 Sep, 29 Oct | T5 on a T3 tradition |

**The company's stock in 1595** (T5 list on T1/T2 titles). *The Comedy of Errors* (William plays Egeon), *Titus Andronicus*, *The Two Gentlemen of Verona*, *The Taming of the Shrew*, *Richard III*, "Harry the Sixth" (three parts, seldom all), the old *Hamlet* (another man's play; T3, treated as stock), *Fair Em* `[ext]`, *A Knack to Know a Knave* `[ext]`, *The Seven Deadly Sins*, *Hester and Ahasuerus*. A different play nearly every day, and never the same play on consecutive days, except *Errors* on 17 and 18 Jan, the fever and then Cowley's Egeon (S007, G006). *LLL* joins from 20 Feb, *Richard II* from 2 Oct, *Romeo* from 6 Dec. William plays about four afternoons in six: Navarre, Egeon, Gaunt, and the Chorus and the Prince. Cowley covers him on the worst day of the cold (Sat 18 Jan) and on the worst days of the tooth.

**Last Christmas, as remembered** (T5 on T1/T3). On 26 December 1594 at Greenwich the company played *The Two Gentlemen*, and Kemp's dog is a story he tells about it; no dog appears in any 1595 scene. On 28 December came *Errors* at Greenwich in the afternoon, then the scramble upriver to play it again at Gray's Inn that night: "the day of two Errors". The doubling is T3 and is told as a tavern story nobody tells the same way twice.

**Moons** (calculated; source book § 1). Full: 15 Jan, 14 Feb, 15 Mar, 14 Apr, 13 May, 11 Jun, 11 Jul, 9 Aug, 8 Sep, 8 Oct, 7 Nov, 6 Dec. New: 30 Jan, 1 Mar, 30 Mar, 29 Apr, 29 May, 27 Jun, 26 Jul, 25 Aug, 23 Sep, 22 Oct, 21 Nov, 20 Dec. The moonless Tower Hill night (29 Jun), the orchard scene (9 Aug) and *Romeo*'s first night (6 Dec) all sit on the right side of these.

**Fixed weather** (T5 unless marked). 15 Jan frost · 22 Feb late snow · 27 Mar a bright cold ridge in the Chilterns · 28 Mar rain at Long Compton · 13 Apr raw · 20 Apr soft · 29 Jun warm and dark · 12 Jul heat · 30 Jul an afternoon thunderstorm over Edge Hill · 8 Nov fog · 17 Nov clear and cold · 6 Dec clear, a full moon · 9 Dec iron frost · **22 Dec foul weather (T1)** · 24–28 Dec clearing to frost. The only other documented weather of 1595 is Cornwall's "dark and cloudy" dawn of 23 Jul (T1), which he never sees. There is never a flood and never "the wet summer": that was 1594. The harvest is thin, with no cause given.

### 5.2 Month by month

Each month gives the threads, then **Movement** (how he changes) and **What he learns** (the month's correction, the hard-won line).

#### January: the book opened · *Credit*

- **Work.** The last act of *Love's Labour's Lost*: the messenger of death walking into a comedy (S004, a memo on Wed 8 Jan) and the year-and-a-day ending. He is productive, not blocked; the difficulty is knowing when a clever passage has become an obstacle to another actor. Burbage hears Act 5 at the Jackdaw on Sat 11 Jan and says nothing for a full minute (W004). Last line Tue 21 Jan, the book to Garnish (W007); Tilney licenses it at St John's for 7s on Fri 24 Jan (W008); parts out and a first reading Tue 28 Jan (W009). Afternoons at the Cross Keys in failing light, playing Egeon and waiting.
- **Money.** S001, Wed 1 Jan: the year's book is opened with one line owed, "of the Queen, for two plays at Greenwich, xx li., not yet had". The coffer holds £24 10s. Weekly share 18–30s. Expectation is being treated too much like cash, and Edith Kettlewood's unpaid 8s for a prince's sleeves makes the difference tangible. Heminges's "Item" rules the Saturday reckonings.
- **Family.** Hobbins is in at the Bell on Thu 16 Jan. Anne's letter reaches him abed on Sun 19 Jan: the children's shoes, his father's cough (mild, gone by her next letter), a question about Lent. He first reads its practical requests as interruptions. The Candlemas packet for the twins (a groat each "to be spent foolishly", comfits and a letter) waits for February: written on Sun 2 Feb, it goes down late with the carrier on Thu 6 Feb (S012, W114).
- **Love and friendship.** Twelfth Eve, Sun 5 Jan: music from an upper window across the close, which is Morley's (W002, the year's first cross). Twelfth Day bean-cake at the Burbages' (W003). Supper at the Burbages' on Sun 26 Jan (S009). Southampton House is silent. Burbage wants a larger claim on the play; Kemp wants fewer instructions about laughter.
- **Faith.** Ordinary Sundays at St Helen's, with the Lord Mayor's pew three rows ahead. He catches himself working the week's scenes during the sermon, and Master Thwaite reaches "fourteenthly".
- **News.** The Admiral's Men at court on the 1st and 6th (T1). The Gray's Inn Prince of Purpoole rides to dine with the Mayor at Crosby Place on Sat 4 Jan (T1), past his very door (W001). Gray's Inn men retell the Night of Errors in the tiring-room (S003, Thu 9 Jan). Hilary term opens Thu 23 Jan (S008a, S008b). The Derby–Vere wedding at Greenwich on Sun 26 Jan (T1), and the first idle thought that weddings want plays. It stays court news and never becomes the *Dream*'s occasion.
- **Health and weather.** Hard frost mid-month, with a full moon on the 15th (S006); a brief, ill-advised late walk in it. The cold with a low fever runs Mon 13 – Sun 19 Jan. He plays through it on Fri 17 Jan (S007), Cowley takes Egeon on the worst day, Sat 18 Jan, and Mistress Ansell nurses him with a posset and a lecture. He resents losing a working morning more than he admits.
- **Movement.** A man finishing something, a little pleased with himself, not yet looking up.
- **What he learns.** A promise to pay a woman is not made more respectable by being spoken in excellent prose.
- **In his ear.** Mistress Ansell, at the foot of the stair: "A man may be a poet and still drink his posset while it is hot."

#### February: the comedy and the scaffold · *A voice beyond permission*

- **Work.** Rehearsals. Kemp's Costard swells, and Garnish begins his weekly tally of "Kemp's interest". Songs with Phillips. The clown war has begun, and runs to November. Full rehearsal Tue 18 Feb (W014). **First performance Thu 20 Feb at the Cross Keys** (W015): the boxes take £7 11s, and the Inns men are quoting it by Monday (W016, Mon 24 Feb). Heminges asks, half in jest, for *Love's Labour's Won*; William has not written it and does not.
- **Money.** A call from Heminges on Tue 4 Feb (W010): 50s owed to a Cheapside mercer for the Princess's gown, which becomes the "costume debt" of S027. The first good week of the year. On Sat 22 Feb the gatherers' boxes are counted (S117), and Edith's account is still under the candlestick. Small expenses keep eating apparent gains.
- **Family.** Candlemas, Sun 2 Feb: the twins are ten (S012). A letter to Anne on Septuagesima, Sun 16 Feb (W013). He keeps the twins' letter ordinary and teasing, and does not turn it into a declaration of paternal feeling.
- **Love and friendship.** A sonnet evening "for private friends" on Fri 7 Feb (S118). On Mon 10 Feb he leaves a paper for Florio at the Southampton House porter's lodge, gets no further, and hears nothing (W115). Field gives friendship without reverence on the call of Wed 26 Feb, about Harrison and the octavo *Venus* (W017). The day before, Tue 25 Feb, James Burbage takes him and Cuthbert round the Theatre's north side and jabs a knife into a rotten oak post (G013). On Thu 27 Feb the inked paper wedge comes out from under the stool's short leg and is kept as a private joke (G016). Fri 28 Feb: a face at St Helen's, and the first poem with "black" in it (W018, a cross).
- **Faith.** Southwell is tried on Thu 20 Feb and hanged at Tyburn on Fri 21 Feb (T1). William plays a king of love-games on the 20th and learns at supper what happened at Westminster (S018). On the 21st he does not go to Tyburn. He bolts the casement and speaks S019 into the dark: what is a poet for? It settles nothing.
- **News.** The Prince of Purpoole's fifteen barges come up on the tide at Tower Hill on Sat 1 Feb (S011, his only visit there). Raleigh gone for Guiana: word reaches Old Swan Stairs on Tue 11 Feb (S014a), five days after he sailed. Hilary term ends Wed 12 Feb (W012). *Long Meg* at the Rose on Fri 14 Feb (S016), with William in the yard. The Rose's other new plays: *The French Comedy* (11 Feb), *The Mack* (21 Feb). *Mahomet* on 5 Feb gets compared with *Tamburlaine* at the post by the Bull (S013).
- **Health and weather.** The cold is gone. Raw and wet, cold river mists, and a late snow on Sat 22 Feb. He sleeps better once he stops revising the ending after midnight.
- **Movement.** The first crack in his pleasure with himself.
- **What he learns.** Admiration for a sentence can live alongside fear of the conviction behind it.
- **In his ear.** Kemp, after the second day: "They laughed at *my* lines, Will. I counted."

#### March: Lent, money, the road · *The difference between a warrant and money*

- **Work.** The court's Shrovetide is heard of: the Shrove Saturday tilt, and the Gray's Inn masque at Whitehall on Mon 3 Mar (S020). Shrove Tuesday, 4 Mar, is the last play (*LLL*), and he walks wide of the apprentices' holiday (S022). On Ash Wednesday the Rose opens a *new* play, *Seleo and Olympo* (£3, T1), and Kemp is bitter about pennies left on the table (S023). On Wed 12 Mar, at a Lent morning's script-work, Kemp grumbles at a rewritten clown bit (S025). On Thu 6 Mar he buys a worn Brooke at Paul's for 6d. **Lent holds early notes only** (S024): two houses, names tried and struck, one image of light in a tomb. On Sat 8 Mar the desk is re-ordered for Lent (W019). On Wed 19 Mar Field lends him Holinshed, volume two, and the notes change colour: a king. On Fri 21 Mar he tries Verona names aloud in Moorfields (W023). The Rose shuts after Fri 14 Mar (S026), and Henslowe's Lent repairs begin: 325 elm boards, lime, sand, a painter, £8 19s (T1).
- **Money.** **Sat 15 Mar: the warrant** (T1). The three named men, Kemp, Shakespeare and Burbage, fetch the Queen's £20 that morning, and the sharers split it at the Burbage table (S027). Heminges opens it: "the warrant has no weight until someone brings the coin." Costs come off first (£5: boat and baskets 16s, rewards 34s, the Cheapside mercer 50s), and the remaining £15 goes in eight parts of **37s 6d**. Edith's 8s is paid from the stock share on Thu 20 Mar. From this month his account book keeps *expected* and *received* in separate columns. Lady Day rent, Tue 25 Mar (S028): the coffer stands at £27 2s, the 60s for Anne sewn in canvas counted in it, and the legal year turns 1595.
- **Family.** Gilbert's call on Tue 11 Mar about their father's small debts (W020); Richard turns 21 the same day. Mon 17 Mar: the gifts, and a horse bespoken at the Bull (W021): Burbage's old lath sword, ribbons, a riddle-book, gloves he did not make. Thu 20 Mar, carrier day: he sends four lines ahead to Anne by Hobbins with the day he will come, and keeps back the Lady Day 60s, sewn in canvas, to put into her hand himself (W121). **Wed 26 – Fri 28 Mar: the ride home** (S030a–c). Oxford at dusk and Woodstock by night (W025); Clopton Bridge at dusk (W026). The lath sword goes to Hamnet that night. **Sun 30 Mar, Mothering Sunday**, at Holy Trinity with Mary, and John stays home (W027).
- **Love and friendship.** Florio calls on Tue 18 Mar (W120): the paper was received, and nothing more is said. Sat 22 Mar: a music evening at Morley's, the lady at the virginals, and an unsigned quatrain in his tables before he rides north (M009, a cross). Anne at the door: "You've come, then."
- **Faith.** Ash Wednesday ashes at St Helen's. Lent fish. His father not at church, "for debt".
- **News.** The Rose dark from the 14th, with carpenters at work (T1). The court at Whitehall for Lent sermons.
- **Health and weather.** Writing-heavy days and stiff shoulders, then saddle-sore. Bright cold on the Chiltern ridge at Stokenchurch on the morning of the 27th, rain at Long Compton on the 28th.
- **Movement.** Out of the trade and into the family, with a pocket full of the Queen's money.
- **What he learns.** A warrant is not money, and a column for "expected" keeps the two apart.
- **In his ear.** Heminges, laying out the coin: "Item, the boat. Item, the baskets. Item, the gentleman who opened the door and expected to be remembered for it."

#### April: home, and a king begun · *Being at home is a verb*

- **Work.** Nothing written at Stratford beyond a few historical notes in the tables. A line or two comes on horseback on the ride back, and Gaunt's rhythm finds him in the Catherine Wheel at High Wycombe on Wed 9 Apr (W032b). Rehearsal at the Theatre on Fri 11 Apr (S033), where a cue rewritten overnight sits beside the old one in two copies. Tue 15 Apr: he buys Daniel's *Civil Wars* for 2s, after weeks of reading it standing at a stall (W033). **Thu 17 Apr: *Richard II* begun** (W034), Gaunt's voice first. Good Friday: one quiet hour (W035). Sat 19 Apr: Heminges calls about a hoarse boy and Monday's cuckoo (W125). Easter Monday, 21 Apr: the Theatre reopens with *Love's Labour's Lost*, William as Navarre, while the Rose reopens with *The French Doctor* (53s, T1). The rival bill is all he sees of it (S036). Fri 25 Apr: Burbage hears the first Richard scene (W036).
- **Money.** Fri 4 Apr: he pays £5 of John's small debts in three shops without a word (W123): the other baker 41s, the shoemaker 44s 6d, and a 14s 6d glover's reckoning. The belt's loose coin will not reach, so 42s of it comes out of the canvas meant for Anne, and on Sat 5 Apr at Shottery (W029) she has 18s of the quarter's 60s, and says so. The coffer is at £19 6s on his return on the 10th: down £7 16s since Lady Day, which is horse, fodder and inns 38s, the 60s carried home, and 58s of John's debts. Then Easter week brings the best share since February.
- **Family.** Tue 1 Apr: the fire ground on Chapel Street with the children, black stumps beside new oak, and Susanna catches him answering a question nobody asked (S032). Wed 2 Apr: his father on debts, and the arms once sought; John shows him the yellowed 1568 sketch once and puts it away (S031). There is no filing and no promise. Thu 3 Apr: market day at the High Cross with the twins and Anne, and Quiney at the glove stall (W028). Sat 5 Apr: **Shottery with Anne** (W029), when she asks "Is London where you work, or where you live?" He does not answer well. Sun 6 Apr: Holy Trinity, and John stays home (W030). In the afternoon, at the shop bench in Henley Street, he mends Hamnet's wooden wheel while Judith questions every decision and Joan finds the leather that makes it turn (G026). Mon 7 Apr: the evening before he rides (W031).
- **Love and friendship.** St George's Day, Wed 23 Apr: thirty-one by tradition (S038), a memo, with the Garter feast trumpets heard of from Whitehall. Sat 26 Apr, "my christening day", by the calendar. A birthday letter from home on Sun 27 Apr, four days late (W037). Field's call on Mon 28 Apr asks after the Holinshed (W127).
- **Faith.** Palm Sunday, raw (S034). **Easter communion** on Sun 20 Apr at St Helen's beside Gresham's tomb, with the Mayor three pews off (S035).
- **News.** The Garter feast. Henslowe's "31 of aprell" (T1) reaches Shoreditch as a joke on Wed 30 Apr (S039). His week's bills are posted at Bishopsgate (S037). They are bills, not plays watched.
- **Health and weather.** Road-weary, saddle-sore, then well. A raw Palm Sunday, a soft Easter. Riding brings fatigue, not illness.
- **Movement.** He comes back carrying her question and puts it into a play about a man who mistakes a crown for a self.
- **What he learns.** Arriving home does not, by itself, discharge absence.
- **In his ear.** Anne, on the Shottery path: "You need not answer now. You never do."

#### May: the hollow crown · *The king who can hear himself*

- **Work.** *Richard II* through Acts 2 and 3. Mon 5 May: he learns lines on a morning walk in Finsbury Fields and hears that *A Knack* is on at the Rose (S042). Wed 7 May: word of *1 Hercules* (new, £3 13s), and envy (S043). Easter term opens the same day (S043), and the Inns men are back in the yard. Thu 8 May: Phillips's jig of the slippers (W040), Kemp in huge yellow slippers; the jig is licensed this month (one secondary source). Tue 13 May is a typical writing day (§ 7.6). **Sat 17 May: the hollow crown**, written by one candle (W042). Tue 20 May: at the Rose for *Hercules* (W043, £3 9s that day), where he trades one exchange with Henslowe at the bridge foot and comes home thinking about spectacle, and what he can do with a mirror instead. His problem is no longer eloquence but what happens when eloquence stops commanding.
- **Money.** Steady at 25–40s a week. Field sells him the Plutarch at trade price, 14s unbound. Giles Allen's ground-rent demand reaches him through Cuthbert's call on Sat 10 May (W041): £24 against the £14 the lease names. It is the first audible creak in the Theatre's future.
- **Family.** Tue 13 May, a year since the first fire (S045): Gilbert's call from St Bride's, and a memo after it. **Mon 26 May, Susanna is twelve** (S049): a letter and a silver thimble. Sat 3 May, Edmund is fifteen, and gets a letter (W038).
- **Love and friendship.** Sun 4 May: dinner at the Fields' (W039). **Thu 15 May, Field's shop** as the Plutarch comes off the press (S046): sheets hung to dry, and five lines of *Theseus* that stay with him until autumn. Hobbins is in that day too. A supper with Nashe at the Split Hazel on Sun 18 May (G038) is delightful and slightly exhausting. Thu 22 May: six words at the church door (W044, a cross).
- **Faith.** **Sat 17 May: he buys Southwell's *Saint Peters Complaint*** at Paul's for 6d (S047) and reads the epistle on poets, lovers and liars standing up. He does not decide that its unnamed "cousin" is himself. Rogation, Sun 25 May, and the parish walking its bounds, Thwaite with his book and the boys with willow wands (W045). Ascension, Thu 29 May.
- **News.** May Day (S040): hawthorn on Bishopsgate's arch, and *Long Meg* at the Rose for 50s. The court to Greenwich on the 3rd. The Rose's May run (S041) includes *The Venetian Comedy* (8 May), a Venice play long before any merchant. Henslowe's 18 May is blank. Clontibret is fought 25–27 May; he will not hear of it until June. The blue-starch precept of Wed 28 May (W046) is read on Sun 1 June with Master Thwaite in full cry. Sat 31 May, at Paul's: the *True Tragedy* in octavo, his old play with another company's name on it and no author's (W047).
- **Health and weather.** Well. Long light and short nights, sunrise before four by mid-month. A cool dry month. Thu 29 May: penny loaves visibly shrunk on the assize-pan at Leadenhall, and the wheat looks thin (S050).
- **Movement.** The best writing month of the year. He knows it and distrusts it.
- **What he learns.** He becomes more willing to cut a successful line when it stops a scene from listening.
- **In his ear.** Field, at the press: "Read it after it is dry, Will. Wet ink flatters everybody."

#### June: the city breaks · *The crowd outside the yard*

- **Work.** Mon 2 Jun: the sharers map Whitsun to Midsummer (S051); Easter term ends. **Tue 10 Jun: the deposition scene** (S094). **Sat 21 Jun: *Richard II* finished** (W051), and the fair copy follows. He writes a king handing over a crown in the very weeks apprentices set up a gallows at the Mayor's door. Wed 4 Jun: Henslowe pays £7 2s for "the throne in the heavens" (S052, T1), and the rivals can now lower a god onto the stage.
- **Money.** Shares fall as the houses thin: 8–15s. Butter at 5d. The loaf a thumb short of the notch. Thu 12 Jun: Edith quotes for fresh work and asks for payment on delivery; William starts to explain the season's uncertainty, Kemp says uncertainty is precisely her point, and Heminges cuts the order to what the company can pay (G044). Midsummer rent on Tue 24 Jun (W052), and the coffer at £25 0s. On Thu 26 Jun, carrier day, the quarter's 60s goes to the Bell by Heminges's man, because William does not walk that far from home this week (S066).
- **Family.** Nothing from home, and he does not write of the riots. Under every shout is the plain relief that Anne and the children are behind Warwickshire hedges.
- **Love and friendship.** Thu 12 Jun: Verbeke hidden in the back room while the cry against strangers runs (S058). Mistress Ansell is magnificent. Heminges calls on the Saturday morning (W136): do we play, and is your Fleming safe? Field's call on Thu 5 Jun (W134) had said "come Sunday", and Whitsunday dinner at the Fields' (W135) is the last time he goes west of Cheapside until July. Kemp is unexpectedly firm about practical obligations, and William grows less comfortable with jokes that turn hungry people into material.
- **Faith.** Whitsunday, Sun 8 Jun (W048, a cross). Trinity Sunday, Sun 15 Jun (W049): the pulpits preach against disorder. Corpus Christi, Thu 19 Jun (S063), is kept only as a date, and he remembers the Coventry plays of his boyhood.
- **News, all T1, all heard or seen afterwards.**
  - *Tue 3 Jun:* the Queen's Star Chamber oration on "the present scarcity".
  - *Fri 6 Jun:* the Bedlam rescue, on his own walk to Shoreditch (S054). He sees the gate open, a dropped cap and backs running, not the rescue.
  - *Mon 9 Jun:* Whit Monday crowds; *A Knack* takes 55s at the Rose (S056).
  - *Thu 12 Jun:* fish forced cheap at Billingsgate, and the anti-alien riots.
  - *Fri 13 Jun:* **butter at Southwark**, 3d against the sellers' 5d (S059a, S059b). He hears it and keeps north of the river. A memo that night; the next morning he photographs Leadenhall, where a constable leans by the butter firkins.
  - *Sun 15 Jun:* the Counter attacked (W049).
  - *Mon 16 Jun:* a larger crowd broken up near Paul's, heard of, not walked to (S061).
  - *Wed 18 Jun:* Clontibret reaches the tiring-house, three weeks late; *2 Caesar* new at the Rose, 55s, and Rome is box office (W050).
  - *Fri 20 Jun:* Trinity term opens (S064).
  - *Mon 23 – Tue 24 Jun:* Midsummer bonfires in Bishopsgate Street under a nervous watch, and on the morning of the 24th the doorways of the close dressed with birch, fennel and lilies (S065a, S065b; he goes no further than his own door).
  - *Thu 26 Jun:* the Rose's last day (S066).
  - *Fri 27 Jun:* whippings and pillories; a crowd of 1,800 pulls the pillories down and **sets a gallows before Crosby Place**, a hundred yards from his bed (S067).
  - *Sat 28 Jun:* the sharers ask whether the City will shut them now (S068).
  - *Sun 29 Jun:* **Tower Hill, on a moonless night** (S069). He bars the door, and his heart runs from 104 to 124.
- **Health and weather.** Short sleep and heat. From 13 June to 5 July his tracks stay within 600 m of home, except the road to the Theatre, and he does not cross the river from 12 June to 19 July.
- **Movement.** The year's fear.
- **What he learns.** A crowd is something he cannot safely call either an audience or a single mind.
- **In his ear.** Mistress Ansell, bolting the hall door on the 12th: "Master Verbeke is from Norwich tonight, if anybody asks. He has always had a Norwich face."

#### July: martial law, Verona, bad news · *Waiting has a cost*

- **Work.** Mon 30 Jun: no play, and he reads *Richard* to the fellows (W053). **Sat 5 Jul: *Romeo* begins**, with a street brawl, written by a man who has just watched one from behind a shutter (W054). Thu 10 Jul: **Tilney's cut** (W056): "Leave out the deposing…", 7s, and the seal on the book. Sun 13 Jul: supper with Nashe at the Bull (M022): Marlowe remembered, and fairy-lore across the table. Tue 15 Jul: Queen Mab at the Jackdaw (W057). The Capulet feast's shared sonnet comes in these weeks, in drafts only.
- **Money.** **Mon 7 Jul: tour, or wait?** (S072). Heminges's case wins: play on while the Rose is dark and take its audience, then rest the house in August on half wages for the hired men. Shares 10–20s, from thin houses. Thu 17 Jul: Heminges's call on the summer shares (W058). The coffer is at £26 8s on Sun 27 Jul (W060): £20 8s stays under the locks and £6 goes into the belt.
- **Family.** **Mon 21 Jul: the letter.** Gilbert calls first, from the Bell (W306), with a garbled version from its yard. An hour later Hobbins arrives unscheduled with Anne's letter (S074): a second fire on the 15th, twenty houses, and all of theirs safe. Anne's four lines are about repairs and neighbours' needs, not peril. He decides to go home on foot.
- **Love and friendship.** Mon 7 Jul: Burbage is 27, and there is a supper (W055). William's call to Florio on Fri 11 Jul is about Verona (W302). Sat 19 Jul: the Swan rising across the water, seen from Davy Gannet's wherry (W059), his first crossing of the river since May. He keeps one private sonnet page with its addressee blank.
- **Faith.** Thu 24 Jul: five men die on Tower Hill (T1; S077). The calendar says only "No play". He stays in, hears the bells, and speaks a memo that says what he did not see.
- **News.** The proclamation of Fri 4 Jul, nailed at Bishopsgate, with the provost marshal's horsemen beyond (S070). Trinity term ends Wed 9 Jul (S073). The Guildhall trial on Tue 22 Jul, heard of (S075). **Cornwall burned on Wed 23 Jul** (T1). The news reaches the tiring-house on Sat 26 Jul, the last play before the break (S076, *Errors*). That morning, at a Paul's stall, he sees and does not buy the pamphlet on the executions (Allde for Blackwall, T1), while the talk around him puts Spaniards and quarterings in one breath (S078).
- **Health and weather.** Heat, river stink at low tide (S071, Sat 12 Jul), poor sleep, and a doze after dinner.
- **Movement.** Fear turns into resolve. On Sun 27 Jul he packs (W060). He will walk home.
- **What he learns.** Staying in London to finish the work is a choice with beneficiaries and costs, not a law of nature.
- **In his ear.** Heminges, on 7 July: "A wagon costs us every day it moves. An empty yard costs us only on the days we open it."

#### August: the walk, the orchard, the house · *The household does not stop for the visitor*

- **Work.** At Stratford he means to rest and is given tasks instead. Mon 4 Aug: Anne sets him a household list to write before he opens the play (G058), and he finds how many distinctions he has always collapsed into "provisions"; Mary corrects a place-name and Anne a quantity. **Sat 9 Aug, a full moon:** on the bench behind Henley Street he writes the orchard scene in one night (W067). Thu 14 Aug: the children act it at his bedside (W070). Back in London, the *Richard* parts are ready on Fri 22 Aug (W075), and the first *Richard* rehearsal on Wed 27 Aug finds no fool in it for Kemp (W076).
- **Money.** The harvest is thin, and the malt and barley talk is bitter (S080, Tue 5 Aug, with Quiney and Sturley at the Henley Street table). **Thu 7 Aug: Quiney borrows £2** (G059), no interest, and Judith Sadler makes the men say aloud whether it is a gift, a loan or a purchase. He gives 20s toward the barn of a Sadler cousin whose malt-barn went in July. Hired men are on 5s. The coffer is at £20 15s on Fri 22 Aug.
- **Family.** Four days on foot (W061–W064). Lammas, Fri 1 Aug, at home (S079): a Lammas loaf and nine trenchers. Sun 10 Aug: dinner at the Sadlers' (W068), then willow-bark boats with the children below Clopton Bridge (S081). **Tue 12 Aug: raising the barn frame, and the back** (W069). Wed 13 Aug: from the floor he watches the string-and-spool contraption the children are building; he wants to carry the family joke about it to London, Susanna says it will sound different when the people in it cannot answer, and he agrees not to use it (G061). **Sat 16 Aug: "That one, then"** (W071): Anne, at the great house by the chapel. It is a wish with an address: no inquiry, no price. Sun 17 Aug: supper before the road (W072); Edmund asks again about London and gets a truer answer than in spring: admiration is not employment.
- **Love.** The marriage is the love story of this month, and nothing else is in it.
- **Faith.** Sun 3 Aug: a sermon on fire as judgment, and his father's dry aside (W065).
- **News.** None reaches him for three weeks, which is the point. Back on Thu 21 Aug at the Bell (W074). Fri 22 Aug: home, Mistress Ansell at the door with where his letters are and when the household eats. **Bartholomew Fair**, Sun 24 Aug (S082), with Kemp and Pope: roast pig and cutpurses. Both houses reopen on Mon 25 Aug (S083). *Longshanks* at the Rose on Fri 29 Aug (S085, 40s). Drake and Hawkins sail from Plymouth on the 28th (T1), heard of on 4 Sep.
- **Health and weather.** 44–61 thousand steps a day out; heel blisters, cooled in the Avon. The back: flat 12–15 Aug, stiff on the road, a bad moment shifting the armour-basket at the first rehearsal on Wed 27 Aug (W076). Deep country sleep. Dry heat, and one thunderstorm over Edge Hill on 30 Jul.
- **Movement.** The year's turn. He comes back with a plan and a limp.
- **What he learns.** Some family jokes are not his to carry away.
- **In his ear.** Judith Sadler: "Say the word, Master Quiney. Gift, loan or purchase? I'll not have it called friendship afterwards and nobody knowing which."

#### September: the Mayor's letter · *Protection is not permission*

- **Work.** *Richard* in rehearsal, and Kemp's grievance (W076; S093, Thu 11 Sep, rehearsing with Burbage while Edith pins the king's sleeve). Thu 4 Sep: Sam asks why the book tells him to look frightened before his character has heard the news; William starts to answer from the whole play, and Phillips makes him answer from the boy's entrance (G069). *Romeo* Act 3: **Mercutio dies** on Sat 6 Sep (W078). Dress rehearsal Fri 26 Sep (W084), with the plot on its peg.
- **Money.** Michaelmas, Mon 29 Sep: rent, accounts, and the firing charge begins (S092). The Golden Pen contest between writing-masters that day (T1) he calls the only prize-fight he could win, though fine handwriting is not his trade. The coffer is at £23 10s. Shares 25–35s.
- **Family.** Hobbins is in on Thu 11 Sep. Anne's letters now come under agreed heads (money, supplies, the household), and his own letter of 31 Aug said, for once, what he was thinking.
- **Love and friendship.** **Tue 23 Sep: the call with my lord of Southampton** (S091): brief and unequal. The young earl's attention is plainly moving elsewhere, and the town already talks of Mistress Vernon (T1/T2). William comes away with more feeling than information. **Thu 18 Sep: "Sweet Shakspeare"** (W082): Field shows him the margin of Covell's *Polimanteia* at a Paul's stall. By Sat 20 Sep Kemp's new name for him has stuck: "Sweet Master Shakespeare" (W083).
- **Faith.** The Queen's birthday bells, Sun 7 Sep (W079), her sixty-second.
- **News.** Burghley's gout, by Kettleby's call on Tue 2 Sep (S087). Drake and Hawkins have sailed, heard of Thu 4 Sep (S084). The Rose's *Crack Me This Nut*, new Fri 5 Sep (S086). **Sat 13 Sep: the Mayor asks the Council to suppress plays** at the Theatre and on Bankside (T1): Cuthbert's call at noon brings it as hearsay from a scrivener's in Cheapside (W335), and the sharers weigh it after the play (S088). Tue 16 Sep: my lord's answer, by Kettleby: play on (W081). Wed 17 Sep: Field's call mentions a pamphlet on a Bedfordshire fire, Woburn, rushed out (W337; Gemini's anchor, not rechecked). No order follows. Raleigh is home "with little riches" (T1), heard of Mon 22 Sep (S090).
- **Health and weather.** Cooler mornings. The back eases by about the 15th. **The tooth begins** on Tue 9 Sep (W080): cloves, and a swollen jaw by Michaelmas.
- **Movement.** Braced for a blow that does not fall.
- **What he learns.** A powerful man's courtesy is not a promise.
- **In his ear.** Kettleby: "My lord bids me say he has read the Mayor's letter, and that the Mayor writes a very fair hand."

#### October: the king walks; a tooth; a paymaster dies · *The cost of a clean copy*

- **Work.** **Thu 2 Oct: *Richard II* first played** (W085), £11 6s in the boxes. Burbage is a wonder; the town says "the king play". He reckons the week on Sat 4 Oct (W086). Michaelmas term from Thu 9 Oct brings the Inns men to *Richard* (S096). *Romeo*: the dawn parting on Sat 11 Oct (W088), written in the grey after a night of toothache; the fair bundle tied with red thread on Sun 19 Oct, its unresolved marks kept for rehearsal (G080); the potion on Fri 24 Oct, and **Nick's voice** cracks for the first time (W091). Wed 22 Oct: Larkham shows him how one late change runs through six parts, and William agrees the copying must be paid for (G081). **Tue 28 Oct: a page about ruined seasons**, the first of the *Dream* (S100).
- **Money.** The best shares of the year, 30–45s. The quarter's 60s goes home with Hobbins on Thu 2 Oct. **Fri 17 Oct: Heneage dies** (T1); Florio calls with the news (S098). Mon 20 Oct: who signs for Christmas now? Kettleby calls about the Christmas warrant (W405), and the sharers ask the same question (W090). The Rose's *The Disguises* (new 2 Oct, S095a) and *The Wonder of a Woman* (new 15 Oct) are bills he reads at Bishopsgate.
- **Family.** Gilbert is 29 on Mon 13 Oct, and William calls him (W403): the Michaelmas money went down safe and came back as their mother's cheese, and Gilbert would have drawn the tooth for sixpence with the shop pliers. Thu 23 Oct: Anne's letter by Hobbins, whose jokes he leaves unharvested, and a warning to keep his purse in his other hose when Master Quiney comes (W406).
- **Love and friendship.** Mon 6 Oct: a birthday he does not mark (W087). Thu 23 Oct: supper near Paul's with Samuel Daniel, whose *Civil Wars* fed *Richard* (M040), a gracious mutual reckoning between two poets of the same reign. **Wed 29 Oct: at the Lord Mayor's show the lady laughs with someone else** (S101, a cross).
- **Faith.** Arundel dies in the Tower (T2, Sun 19 Oct); a memo on Wed 22 Oct, kept general (S097).
- **News.** The court to Richmond on Mon 20 Oct. **Tue 28 – Wed 29 Oct: Slany in, Spencer out** (S101). The Mayor's pew changes hands, and the light in Crosby Place's oriel goes out.
- **Health and weather.** Earlier candles, longer sleep (S099, St Luke, Sat 18 Oct). The tooth: abscess from Fri 10 Oct (W402), fierce nights with clove oil and wool 10–13 Oct, and in one of them a half-waking image of a weaver with an ass's head (T5 colour). Cowley takes his part from Mon 13 Oct (G077), and Pope his Gaunt on the 16th (W404). **Tue 14 Oct: the tooth drawn** by Yaxley for 12d (W089), then three bad days, a week of broth, and a jaw tender to the 21st.
- **Movement.** Success, pain, relief. He is writing at full stretch.
- **What he learns.** A late revision costs someone else an evening.
- **In his ear.** Yaxley, the pliers ready: "Hold the arms of the chair, Master Shakespeare, not my wrist. I have only the two."

#### November: the tomb, the tilt, the truce · *Performance under a larger sky*

- **Work.** Sat 8 Nov: the tomb, by one candle (W092). **Tue 11 Nov, Martinmas: *Romeo* finished** (W093). Thu 13 Nov: Tilney reads it and changes nothing (W094). Wed 19 Nov: the reading, with Pope's Nurse (W096). **Sat 22 Nov: three pages for Kemp** (W097): a weaver with an ass's head. Kemp says *thou* to him for the first time. Wed 26 Nov: choosing the court plays (S107). Thu 27 Nov: after the play he tries the next pages of the tradesmen; Kemp stops a joke that rests only on their stupidity, since a man can get a play wrong and still want with his whole heart to get it right, and Phillips has a joiner to prove it (G088).
- **Money.** Winter stores laid in on Martinmas (S105), William carrying with the household. Shares 25–40s. **Mon 24 Nov:** the fire brief is authorised (T1), and at supper at the Bell William gives £2 to it, takes £1 back from Quiney against the August loan, and declines to lend more (W098). £1 is still owed.
- **Family.** Quiney's news of home, slightly improved in the telling, by his call from the Bell on Sat 15 Nov (W095) and again on Sat 22 Nov (W413). Anne's packet by the carrier of the 13th reports a household decision already made; he is briefly annoyed at being informed rather than consulted, then answers it plainly on Sun 16 Nov (G089).
- **Love and friendship.** **Fri 7 Nov: my Lord Chamberlain calls** (S104): three nights at court, and caution, from a man who has seen *Richard* at the Theatre. **Mon 17 Nov: the tilt** (S106), with Southampton in armour, far off; no interpretation follows. Tue 25 Nov: Heminges is 29, and William's call to him turns to the court schedule (W414). The truce with Kemp.
- **Faith.** All Saints and All Souls, 1–2 Nov: the old prayers and the new doctrine, kept general (S102a, S102b). His mother's grace comes back to him under his breath. The Lambeth Articles are heard from the pulpit later in the month.
- **News.** Essex and the succession book (T1; S103a, S103b), shown to him at Richmond on the 3rd and heard of on Wed 5 Nov. It makes a deposition play feel suddenly warm in the hand. *Harry the Fifth* at the Rose on Fri 28 Nov (S108, £3 6s), the same day Michaelmas term ends (S109). Advent Sunday and St Andrew, Sun 30 Nov (S110). Hawkins's death (12 Nov) never reaches him.
- **Health and weather.** Well. Fog on the 8th. A clear, cold Accession Day.
- **Movement.** Completion, and a new ease with his fellows.
- **What he learns.** He can enjoy magnificence while noticing the workers holding it upright.
- **In his ear.** Kemp, reading the three pages: "An ass. You have made me an ass, Will." A long pause. "Thou hast made me a *good* one."

#### December: dusk on the tomb, a king in a parlour, the Queen at Richmond · *Three nights, no conclusion*

- **Work.**
  - *Mon 1 Dec:* Burby enters *Edward III* (T1): more old lines abroad without him (S111), heard of that day.
  - *Wed 3 Dec:* dress rehearsal, and James Burbage first speaks of a great hall in Blackfriars (W099).
  - *Fri 5 Dec, late:* Burbage's call: he cannot sleep, wants one line changed, and is refused (W415).
  - ***Sat 6 Dec: Romeo and Juliet first played*** (W100). £12 13s, the year's best house. Mistress Ansell in the gallery. Full moon that night.
  - ***Tue 9 Dec: Canon Row*** (S112a, S112b). *Richard* whole before Cecil, the one time it is played with the deposition. Iron frost; the long cold walk home.
  - *Thu 11 Dec:* a memo on Cecil's one sentence (W102).
  - *Fri 12 Dec:* Cuthbert's call about the Blackfriars hall (W417).
  - *Tue 16 Dec:* the court rehearsal before Tilney (W104).
  - *Fri 19 Dec:* at Field's, James Burbage confides the £600 plan for the Blackfriars hall (M052; T5, ahead of the T1 purchase of Feb 1596).
  - ***Fri 26 – Sun 28 Dec: Richmond*** (S115a–c).
  - The *Dream* is at twelve leaves.
- **Money.** A £5 reward from Hoby, 12s 6d a share. The court will pay next year (T1: the warrant is dated 21 December 1596). Christmas rent paid early on Mon 22 Dec, and Mistress Ansell has the latch mended and does not charge for it (W106). The coffer is £33 0s on Wed 24 Dec. **Wed 31 Dec: the book closed** (S116): £33 10s, which is the £24 10s of 1 Jan with £55 14s received and £46 14s laid out (Hunsdon's 10s at Richmond on a line of its own); £1 owed by Quiney, court money not counted as received. He sets part of it aside in his head for "a house that is ours", and names nothing.
- **Family.** Thu 18 Dec: the Christmas pack by carrier (W105): spices, oranges, cloth, holland linen for Joan, and the quarter's 60s. Anne's letter, with its joke. No surprise Christmas visit; he sends clear arrangements instead of hints.
- **Love and friendship.** Sat 13 Dec: the sonnets tied and put under everything (W103). Field's call on Mon 15 Dec (W418). Tue 30 Dec: the evening at Field's fire, *Dream* leaves read aloud, the Holinshed still not returned (M057). Field asks whether they have been paid; "No, not yet," and both men find that more useful than a flourish.
- **Faith.** St Thomas's doles, Sun 21 Dec, the shortest day (S113): 2s. Christmas Day in a strange town (S114).
- **News.** Huntingdon dead (T1, 14 Dec; the Queen hears on the 17th). **Foul weather on Mon 22 Dec** (T1) puts off the court's move. The court to Richmond on Tue 23 Dec. A gold button set with five pearls is lost from the Queen's kirtle "at a play at Richmond" on Sat 27 Dec (T1): searched for, never found.
- **Health and weather.** Well. Short days; the first sleep and the second. Court nights cut sleep without bringing illness. Clearing to frost over Richmond Green.
- **Movement.** He ends where he began, with players before the Queen at Christmas, a different man in the same cloak.
- **What he learns.** He ends the year accounting accurately, leaving a page unfinished, and not turning every absence into a sentence.
- **In his ear.** Nick, after the jig on the 28th, in a voice that has gone down a floor and will not come back up: "It's gone, sir." "It's come, Nick." (S115c)

### 5.3 Week by week: 53 weeks

**How to read the table.**

- Week 1 runs Wednesday to Sunday; weeks 2–52 run Monday to Sunday; week 53 runs Monday to Wednesday.
- **Playing** gives his company's house and bills first (T5 repertory), then the Rose from Henslowe (T1), every playing day. Receipts are shown for new plays and for the days that matter. "(new)" is Henslowe's "ne". Henslowe's own date is kept. Where it falls on a Sunday (a slip), it is moved to the adjacent free weekday and marked; where no weekday is free, it is left off the calendar. The Easter week uses the source book's realignment of his "23–31 April". 18 May is blank.
- **Main events** are the entries that must exist, with their dates: Fable's anchors, the canon's amendments, the remaining seeds, and the beats this fold adds (**new**).
- Moods merge the three bibles' words.

| Wk | Dates | Where he is | Writing | Playing: his company · the Rose | Main events | Mood |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Wed 1 Jan – Sun 5 Jan | London: lodging, Cross Keys | *LLL* Act 5; the year's account book opened | Cross Keys: *Errors*, *Two Gents*. Rose: Wed 1 *2 Tamburlaine* (£3 2s) · Thu 2 *Set at Maw* · Fri 3 *French Doctor* · Sat 4 *Antony and Valia* | **S001** Wed 1: the year's book opened, "xx li., not yet had". **S002** Thu 2: tiring-room gossip, the Admiral's at court. **W001** Sat 4: the Prince of Purpoole rides past to dine at Crosby Place (photo). **W002** Sun 5: Twelfth Eve, music from Morley's window (a cross) | Brisk, pleased, curious, thrifty |
| 2 | Mon 6 Jan – Sun 12 Jan | London | *LLL* Act 5 | Cross Keys: *Errors* (Twelfth Day), *Shrew*, *Fair Em*. Rose: Tue 7 *Knack* · Thu 9 *Faustus* · Fri 10 *Grecian Comedy* · Sat 11 *Tasso* | **W003** Mon 6: Twelfth Day bean-cake at the Burbages'. **S004** Wed 8: memo, the messenger in black. **S003** Thu 9: Gray's Inn men retell the Night of Errors. **S005** Fri 10: the Rose's what's-on, *Grecian Comedy* then *Knack*. **W004** Sat 11: Act 5 read to Burbage at the Jackdaw | Playful, overcommitted; a cold coming |
| 3 | Mon 13 Jan – Sun 19 Jan | London, mostly indoors | *LLL*, slowly | Cross Keys: *Titus*, *Knave*, *Errors* (Cowley as Egeon Sat 18). Rose: Mon 13 *Knack* · Tue 14 *Siege of London* · Thu 16 *Wise Man* (£3) · Fri 17 *Set at Maw* · Sat 18 *1 Caesar* | **W005** Mon 13: the cold begins. **S006** Wed 15: full moon, frost by Crosby Place (photo). Thu 16: carrier in. **S007** Fri 17: rehearsal from parts, played in cold light with a fever. Sat 18: the worst day; Cowley covers. **W006** Sun 19: abed; posset; Anne's letter | Thick-headed, stubborn, cared for |
| 4 | Mon 20 Jan – Sun 26 Jan | London; Clerkenwell (Fri) | ***LLL* finished** | Cross Keys: *Richard III*, *Two Gents*, Inns men in the galleries. Rose: Mon 20 *Ranger's Comedy* (Henslowe's "Sun 19", a slip) · Tue 21 *Tasso* · Wed 22 *Siege of London* · Thu 23 *Wise Man* · Fri 24 *Faustus* · Sat 25 *Grecian Comedy* | **W007** Tue 21: the last line; the book to Garnish. **S008a** / **S008b** Thu 23: Westminster Hall; Hilary term opens. **W008** Fri 24: Tilney licenses *LLL*, 7s. **S009** Sun 26: supper at the Burbages'; the Derby wedding today at Greenwich | Relief; a small vanity; recovering |
| 5 | Mon 27 Jan – Sun 2 Feb | London; Tower Hill (Sat) | Songs; the first sonnets of the year | Cross Keys: *Shrew*, *Hamlet* (old), *Errors*. Rose: Mon 27 *1 Tamburlaine* · Tue 28 *Set at Maw* · Wed 29 *2 Tamburlaine* · Thu 30 *French Doctor* · Fri 31 *Grecian Comedy* · Sat 1 *1 Caesar* | **W112** Mon 27: Heminges's call, counting the ladies. **W009** Tue 28: parts out; the first reading. **S010** Thu 30: the sharers plan the run to Shrovetide. **S011** Sat 1: the Prince's fifteen barges at Tower Hill (photo). **S012** Sun 2: Candlemas; the twins are ten | Sociable, warm, homesick |
| 6 | Mon 3 Feb – Sun 9 Feb | London | Sonnets | Cross Keys: *Titus*, *Fair Em*, *Seven Deadly Sins*. Rose: Mon 3 *Siege of London* · Tue 4 *Wise Man* · Wed 5 *Mahomet* · Thu 6 *Knack* · Fri 7 *French Doctor* · Sat 8 *Faustus* | **W010** Tue 4: Heminges's call, the Princess's gown and the 50s mercer. **S013** Wed 5: *Mahomet* weighed against *Tamburlaine* at the post by the Bull. **W114** Thu 6: carrier in; the Candlemas letter goes late. **S118** Fri 7: a sonnet evening, "for private friends". **W011** Sun 9: church; Finsbury Fields; lines on the tables | Restless, tender, focused |
| 7 | Mon 10 Feb – Sun 16 Feb | London; Holborn (Mon); Old Swan (Tue); Bankside (Fri) | Rehearsal cuts | Cross Keys: *Errors*, *Knave*, *Two Gents*. Rose: Mon 10 *Venetian Comedy* · Tue 11 *French Comedy* (new; receipt not known) · Wed 12 *Wise Man* · Thu 13 *Siege of London* · **Fri 14 *Long Meg* (new, £3 9s)** · Sat 15 *Tasso* | **W115** Mon 10: a paper left for Florio at the Southampton House lodge. **S014a** Tue 11: Old Swan Stairs, Raleigh gone for Guiana. **W012** Wed 12: rehearsal, Kemp's Costard grows; Hilary term ends. **S016** Fri 14: at the Rose for *Long Meg* (watch history). **W013** Sun 16: Septuagesima; a letter to Anne | Edgy about Kemp; envious, alert |
| 8 | Mon 17 Feb – Sun 23 Feb | London | — | Cross Keys: ***LLL* first played Thu 20**; *Shrew*, *Richard III*. Rose: Mon 17 *1 Tamburlaine* · Tue 18 *2 Tamburlaine* · Wed 19 *Wise Man* · Thu 20 *Long Meg* · Fri 21 *The Mack* (new, £3) · Sat 22 *Grecian Comedy* | **W014** Tue 18: full rehearsal. **W015** Thu 20: *LLL* first played, £7 11s (with **S018**: Southwell's trial heard of at supper). **S019** Fri 21: memo, the poet at Tyburn; he does not go. **S117** Sat 22: counting the gatherers' boxes; Edith's account under the candlestick; late snow | Triumph, then shaken |
| 9 | Mon 24 Feb – Sun 2 Mar | London; Shoreditch (Tue) | Sonnets | Cross Keys: *LLL* (24th), *Errors*, *Titus*. Rose: Mon 24 *French Doctor* · Tue 25 *Venetian Comedy* · Wed 26 *Knack* · Thu 27 *French Comedy* · Fri 28 *Wise Man* · Sat 1 *Long Meg* (Henslowe's "29 Feb") | **W016** Mon 24: second day; the Inns men quote it. **G013** Tue 25: James Burbage and the rotten post at the Theatre. **W017** Wed 26: Field's call on Harrison and the octavo *Venus*. **W117** / **G016** Thu 27: carrier in; the paper wedge out from under the stool. **W018** Fri 28: a face at St Helen's, the first "black" (a cross). **S021** Sun 2: Shrove Sunday supper with Nashe at the Bull | Admired, uneasy, convivial |
| 10 | Mon 3 Mar – Sun 9 Mar | London; Paul's (Thu) | **Early notes only**: Brooke open | Cross Keys: *Two Gents* (3rd), ***LLL* (Shrove Tue 4th)**, then dark. Rose: Mon 3 *Siege of London* · Tue 4 *Long Meg* (£3) · **Wed 5 *Seleo and Olympo* (new, £3)** · Thu 6 *1 Caesar* | **S020** Mon 3: the court's Shrovetide and the Gray's Inn masque, heard of. **S022** Tue 4: the last play; walks wide of the apprentices; my lord's birthday. **S023** Wed 5: Ash Wednesday; the Rose opens a new play; Kemp bitter. **S024** Thu 6: Brooke bought for 6d; first notes. **W019** Sat 8: the desk re-ordered for Lent | Released; idle hands; disciplined |
| 11 | Mon 10 Mar – Sun 16 Mar | London; Whitehall (Sat) | Notes; reading | Company dark. Rose: Mon 10 *Knack* · Tue 11 *1 Tamburlaine* · Wed 12 *2 Tamburlaine* · Thu 13 *Long Meg* · Fri 14 *Siege of London* (14s), then dark | **W020** Tue 11: Gilbert's call on their father's debts; Richard is 21. **S025** Wed 12: Kemp grumbles at a rewritten clown bit. **S026** Fri 14: the Rose shuts for Lent; 325 elm boards, by Raye's count. **S027** Sat 15: **the warrant fetched, and the £20 split**; full moon. | Businesslike; amused; hope restrained |
| 12 | Mon 17 Mar – Sun 23 Mar | London | Holinshed; a king stirring | Dark | **W021** Mon 17: gifts, and a horse bespoken at the Bull. **W120** Tue 18: Florio's call, the paper received. **W022** Wed 19: Field lends Holinshed. **W121** / **G020** Thu 20: carrier in; word sent ahead to Anne, the 60s kept back; Edith paid her 8s from the stock share. **W023** Fri 21: memo, Moorfields, Verona names. **M009** Sat 22: music evening at Morley's; an unsigned quatrain (a cross). **W024** Sun 23: sermon; packing | Homeward; temporarily expansive |
| 13 | Mon 24 Mar – Sun 30 Mar | London → the Oxford road → Stratford | None | Dark | **S028** Tue 25: Lady Day rent; the year turns 1595; coffer £27 2s. **S030a–c** Wed 26 – Fri 28: the ride home (High Wycombe, Woodstock). **W025** Thu 27: Oxford at dusk; Woodstock by night. **W026** Fri 28: Clopton Bridge at dusk; "You've come, then"; the lath sword. **W027** Sun 30: Mothering Sunday at Holy Trinity; John stays home; new moon | Sore, shy, glad |
| 14 | Mon 31 Mar – Sun 6 Apr | Stratford | None | Dark | **S032** Tue 1: family day; the fire ground. **S031** Wed 2: his father, debts, the arms once sought; the 1568 sketch shown once. **W028** Thu 3: market with the twins and Anne; Quiney. **W123** Fri 4: three shops, five pounds, no word. **W029** Sat 5: Shottery with Anne, "Is London where you work, or where you live?" **W030** Sun 6: Holy Trinity; John stays home. **G026** Sun 6: Hamnet's wheel mended at the shop bench | Loved, examined, awkwardly happy |
| 15 | Mon 7 Apr – Sun 13 Apr | Stratford → road → London | A line or two on horseback | Dark; rehearsals from Fri | **W031** Mon 7: the evening before he rides. **W032a–c** Tue 8 – Thu 10: the ride back (Woodstock, High Wycombe); memo on her question; Gaunt's rhythm at the Catherine Wheel Wed 9; coffer £19 6s Thu 10. **S033** Fri 11: rehearsal at the Theatre; a cue rewritten overnight. **S034** Sun 13: Palm Sunday, raw | Thoughtful, tender, tired |
| 16 | Mon 14 Apr – Sun 20 Apr | London | ***Richard II* begun** | Dark; rehearsals | **W033** Tue 15: Daniel's *Civil Wars* bought; Joan is 26. **W034** Thu 17: *Richard II*, Gaunt's first lines. **W035** Fri 18: Good Friday, one quiet hour. **W125** Sat 19: Heminges's call, a hoarse boy for Monday's cuckoo. **S035** Sun 20: Easter communion by Gresham's tomb | Grave, gathered, coiled |
| 17 | Mon 21 Apr – Sun 27 Apr | London: the Theatre | *R2* Act 1 | **Theatre reopens:** *LLL* (Easter Mon, I play), *Errors*, *Titus*, *Richard III*. **Anchor count.** Fable's anchors stand: 93 seeds (its own note says 66, which undercounts its table) and W001–W109, re-dated where the canon moves them (S047 to Sat 17 May; the riot pamphlet to Sat 26 Jul). This fold adds the 25 seeds that were not yet anchored (S005, S013, S015, S017, S029, S041, S042, S044, S048, S053, S055, S056, S057, S060, S061, S062, S063, S064, S078, S081, S086, S089, S095, S105, S109), so all 118 seeds now have a place, plus 18 beats this fold first marked **(new)**. In all, 245 anchored items. The dated year (§ 6) has since given every **(new)** beat an entry id of its own (the G, M and W110-up entries cited above). In § 6 nine seeds have no entry of their own and live inside another: S015 in W012, S017 in W013, S029 in W027 (its letter ahead is W121), S044 in S043, S048 and S062 in W050, S055 in W048, S060 in W049 and S089 in W337; S057 (June's rival bills) has no entry. Twelve ids split into entries with letter suffixes in the dated year: S008, S014, S030 (a, b, c for 26, 27 and 28 Mar), S059, S065, S095, S102, S103, S112, S115 (a, b, c for 26, 27 and 28 Dec), W032 (a–c, 8–10 Apr) and W073 (a–d, 18–21 Aug).

## 6. The dated year

### January

#### Week 1 · Wed 01-01 – Sun 01-05

**S001 · 2025-01-01 (Wed) · 07:50 · ansell_house · The year's book opened**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, calendar, photo, screen (ledger)  
At first light, with St Helen's bell ringing in the year, he rules a fresh leaf in the narrow account book and heads it "1594", because the legal year has not turned. The first line of the year: "of the Queen, for two plays at Greenwich at Christmas, xx li., not yet had" (T1 payment, T2 venue); he writes Greenwich from memory, not from a warrant he has not seen. Under it he counts the coffer, £24 10s, and notes the two plays, *Two Gentlemen* on St Stephen's night and *Errors* on Innocents' Day (titles T5), and three winter marks: finish the comedy before Shrovetide, keep clear of tavern scores, carry silver home at Lent. Below him Mistress Ansell drops a pan, and he adds a private line: "The year begins with demands from below." In the penny almanac he marks Candlemas "Twins' day" and Lady Day "rent". Photo 1: the desk, the book, quill, ink-horn, almanac, frost on the pane. *Two Gents* plays at the Cross Keys at two; he is not cast and writes Act 5 of the comedy until the light fails at four.

**S002 · 2025-01-02 (Thu) · 16:40 · cross_keys_inn · Tiring-room: the Admiral's men before the Queen**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, richard_burbage, john_heminges, thomas_pope  
sources: meeting, screen (bill)  
*Errors* is just done (he played Egeon and then waited two hours in a cold parlour). The talk is that the Admiral's Men played *2 Tamburlaine* at the Rose yesterday at two and were before the Queen at Greenwich by candle-time (T1 both). Kemp stands on a hamper and does Alleyn: "Holla, ye pampered jades of Asia!", with a stool for a chariot. Burbage says nothing and watches how Kemp gets the height. Heminges says only that they will be paid as slowly as we are. Pope counts: one company at court on New Year's Day, the other on St Stephen's; "she is keeping two dogs and feeding neither." William notices that Marlowe's line, even mocked, quiets the room. When Kemp roars it a second time Burbage throws a stuffed glove at his head. William, with his own sheets of Berowne under his arm, decides that what they cannot out-bellow they will have to out-write.

**G001 · 2025-01-03 (Fri) · 12:00 · split_hazel · Expected money eats dinner**  
people: william_shakespeare, alice_brambleby, will_kemp  
sources: meeting, location, calendar  
Dinner at the Split Hazel, Alice Brambleby's ordinary near Bishopsgate, before the play at two. Kemp arrives with a story that needs more room than the table gives, and Alice makes him move his elbows before she will allow the end of it. William enjoys the performance, then hears Kemp ask, in the same voice, whether the Queen's twenty pounds has come: the joke and the need sit in one throat. William says it is expected. Alice, setting down the trenchers: "Expected money has eaten several dinners in this house, Master Shakespeare, and never paid for one." Kemp laughs loudest, and pays for his own.

**W110 · 2025-01-03 (Fri) · 18:10 · ansell_house · Mistress Ansell's table: a plot is demanded**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, gillis_verbeke  
sources: meeting, notes  
Fish day: salt ling, a penny loaf, small beer; he pays his 3d and sits. Mistress Ansell lays the loaf against the notch her husband cut in the bread-board and finds it short by the width of a finger. Then she asks what he was walking about to on Tuesday night. He tells her: four gentlemen swear to study three years and see no woman, and four ladies arrive. Her ruling: "Then they are forsworn before supper, and I'd not give a pin for a play that begins in a broken oath." Verbeke asks, carefully, whether the ladies are punished too. Alse wants to know who does the gentlemen's washing for three years. He writes "washing" on his tables under the board. The cat is on his stool when he rises.

**W001 · 2025-01-04 (Sat) · 11:05 · crosby_place · The Prince of Purpoole rides to dine with the Mayor**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_spencer, dorcas_ansell, richard_burbage  
sources: photo, location, memo  
The Gray's Inn Christmas "Prince" and his mock-court ride up Bishopsgate Street to dine with Lord Mayor Spencer at Crosby Place (T1). William stands at the mouth of the close among citizens' backs. Photo 2: plumes, tabards, a trumpeter, the Mayor's gate open. Sir John is a furred gown on the steps receiving a law-student king with a straight face. Mistress Ansell beside him: "He will dine a play-prince at his table and shut the door on a player. Mark me, that is the City." A short memo as he walks down to the Cross Keys: these are the gentlemen who had our *Errors* on Innocents' night, and they keep a better court than Navarre's. *Two Gents* at two. Burbage, who has come to walk down with him, studies how a law-student king sits a hired horse; torch-smoke and the brass of the trumpets hang over the Mayor's porch.

**G002 · 2025-01-05 (Sun) · 09:30 · st_helens_church · The neighbour's pew**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, josias_thwaite, john_spencer  
sources: calendar, location, memo  
Morning prayer with the Ansell household. During a passage he knows by ear he finds himself counting the uses of twenty pounds that is not yet his: shoes, his father's small debts, a horse at Lent. Master Thwaite preaches on the unjust steward; the Lord Mayor sits upright in his pew. At the porch Mistress Ansell names a servant of Crosby Place under her breath, and lowers her voice without admitting she has done so. Afterwards William tries to recover the sermon and can bring back only one phrase about stewardship. His memo ends with an impatient order to himself to attend properly next Sunday. Twelfth Eve's music comes that evening (W002).

**W002 · 2025-01-05 (Sun) · 18:40 · bishopsgate · Twelfth Eve: music from a window**  
people: william_shakespeare, the_lady_unnamed  
sources: memo, photo, calendar, location  
Church in the morning, a letter begun and abandoned, a walk. Coming home in the dark by a lane off Bishopsgate Street he hears a virginals from an upper window: a pavan played very exactly, then the same strain again faster, as if the player were mocking it. He stops. Photo 3: the window lit gold on the dark lane, the shadow of a head bent at an instrument, no face. He does not know the house or who lives in it. The memo is eleven seconds: "Somebody plays as I'd like to write. Twice, and the second time laughing." In the almanac that night a small cross goes beside the 5th, with no word. The house, he will learn on 22 March (M009), is Thomas Morley's.

#### Week 2 · Mon 01-06 – Sun 01-12

**W003 · 2025-01-06 (Mon) · 18:30 · burbage_house_holywell · Twelfth Day: the bean-cake**  
people: william_shakespeare, james_burbage, richard_burbage, cuthbert_burbage, nick_sorrell, will_kemp, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, calendar  
*Errors* at the Cross Keys in the afternoon to a holiday yard, then the whole company up to Holywell for Mistress Burbage's cake. The bean falls to Nick Sorrell, who has played queens since he was eleven and is king for the first time. He takes it gravely and issues three decrees: Master Kemp to keep silence the length of a paternoster; Master Heminges to say no "Item" till morning; Sam to have the last slice. Kemp lasts about half the prayer. James Burbage cannot keep off the lease even over cake: twenty-one years, and two to run. Cuthbert moves the jug toward him. Someone says the Admiral's men are at Greenwich again tonight (T1), and for once nobody minds. Heminges breaks the decree inside the hour to give the feast-week figure anyway: the holiday boxes cleared £11 8s to the sharers' side "without a drum or a trumpet". Nick fines him a slice of cake, for Sam.

**S004 · 2025-01-08 (Wed) · 17:10 · bishopsgate · Voice memo: the messenger in black**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo  
Walking home from the Cross Keys under a half moon, low and quick. He has four couples in their colours, a pageant of Worthies falling apart, everyone laughing; the play could end there and be forgotten by Lent. "A man comes in. Black. From her father's court. He says half a line and she ends it for him." He tries it three ways, each shorter. Then: "And they don't marry. Not today. A twelvemonth. Let them earn it where we can't see." He laughs once, without sound. "Heminges will say it's no ending. …It's the only ending." The memo stops on a doorstep. At home, pacing the six steps of the chamber so that Mistress Ansell below hears only a murmur, he writes the messenger's entrance and leaves the half-line blank.

**S003 · 2025-01-09 (Thu) · 16:50 · cross_keys_inn · Gray's Inn men retell the Night of Errors**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, richard_burbage, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, memo  
After *The Shrew* two Gray's Inn students come round to the parlour door with wine and the story of 28 December (T1 as text): the hall so thronged that the Templars' ambassador left in dudgeon, the dancing ruined, and then "a company of base and common fellows" brought in to crown the confusion with a play. Kemp is delighted to be base and common and asks to have it in writing. They say the Inn held a mock trial afterwards and blamed a sorcerer. Then the old question (T3, never settled): did the company truly play Greenwich in the afternoon and Gray's Inn that night? Heminges's book says so. Kemp swears it was the night before. Burbage remembers only the cold on the river. William's memo afterwards: "My legs say one day. Let it stand. Two Errors in a day is a better tale than a clerk's slip." Kemp offers to have been the sorcerer. Burbage laughs at what any clerk hereafter will make of two houses in one night.

**S005 · 2025-01-10 (Fri) · 08:50 · bishopsgate · Bills on the post**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (bill), calendar  
On the post by the Bull two bills side by side, black-letter, title only. The Lord Admiral's servants at the Rose: *The Grecian Comedy* (T1; 28s, which he never knows). The Lord Chamberlain's servants at the Cross Keys: *Fair Em*. No author on either. He reads the Rose's first, as he always does, and the corner of his own is already torn for a pipe-light. Friday, so fish; he is not cast and goes back up to the desk. This is the pattern for every `bill` screen in the season: two houses, two titles, no names.

**G003 · 2025-01-10 (Fri) · 17:15 · cross_keys_inn · A prince on account**  
people: william_shakespeare, edith_kettlewood, john_heminges, will_kemp  
sources: meeting, notes (tables), photo  
He comes down from the desk at the play's end to fetch a roll from Garnish's basket and finds Edith Kettlewood, the company's seamstress, in the parlour with a mended doublet over her arm and an account for 8s still owing from autumn work. He begins to praise the repair, which she accepts without letting praise settle the bill. Heminges names Lady Day as the latest he can promise; Kemp says a prince who cannot pay for his sleeves should keep his arms inside his coat. Edith does not laugh until she sees the debt written down, and waits until William writes it. Photo: the sleeve on the parlour table, the new seam finer than the old. On his tables, though it is not his debt: "Edith viij s. owing. Lady Day."

**W004 · 2025-01-11 (Sat) · 18:00 · the_jackdaw · Act 5 read to Burbage**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, agnes_tipping  
sources: meeting, screen (draft)  
After the play and the reckoning he walks up to Shoreditch with Burbage. Buttered eggs and ling on the settle by the fire. He reads the last act as far as it goes: the Worthies, the messenger, the ladies' sentence of a year. Burbage says nothing for a full minute. Then: "And I get her not?" "Not for a twelvemonth and a day." "That's too long for a play." William's eyes go to the middle distance; he takes out the tables and writes it down, and it is Berowne's now. Agnes Tipping, clearing: "Dinner's sixpence, wit's extra, and that's the first time I saw a player pay for a line." He sneezes twice on the walk home.

**G004 · 2025-01-12 (Sun) · 17:00 · ansell_house · The parcel list**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell  
sources: notes (letter), screen (letter), meeting  
Anne's last letter, which came before Christmas, asks for three small things, and he cannot tell from her description what the third is: "the kind with the two ends, as before". He begins a reply explaining how busy the winter has been, crosses out the opening, and asks the actual question. Mistress Ansell, who knows exactly what the kind with two ends is, will not tell him: "Your wife asked you." She helps him wrap the other two in oiled cloth that will not come to pieces on the road. The parcel is less handsome than he meant and more likely to arrive. Gilbert will carry it to the Bell for Thursday's carrier.

#### Week 3 · Mon 01-13 – Sun 01-19

**W005 · 2025-01-13 (Mon) · 09:30 · ansell_house · The cold begins**  
people: william_shakespeare, alse_pinder, gib  
sources: body, screen (draft), notes, memo  
Body: resting heart 70, up from 61; steps 2,900; he does not go out except to dinner. The sheet for Act 5 shows fourteen lines by noon and a thing almost unknown on his papers: a blot, where he sneezed with the pen loaded. He cuts the corner off with his father's paring-knife so as not to look at it. Alse brings water and is told to keep off. Gib is asleep on the Golding he wants. *Titus* at the Cross Keys without him. In the commonplace book under *Words*: "rheum; a rheumatic morning; the King hath a cold." He is sure he could work if everyone would stop asking whether he is ill, and copies a passage only to find the same word three times in four lines. The memo, thick-voiced, complains of how small an ailment is and how much of a morning it commands.

**S006 · 2025-01-15 (Wed) · 22:10 · ansell_house · Full moon and frost by Crosby Place**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: photo, body, memo  
He cannot sleep for coughing and goes down to the door in his cloak. Photo 4: hard frost on the cobbles of Great St Helen's, each stone rimmed white under a full moon, the church porch on the left, Crosby Place's roof-line black beyond with one window lit. He walks the close twice round; a cat's prints are the only others. A memo, thick-voiced: the Mayor sits up late too; what keeps *him* from sleeping? Body: steps 3,100 for the day; resting 72; 96 on the frost walk; slept 6 h 10 the night before and 5 h 40 this night. The frost is invented; the moon is computed. A shallow puddle by the Crosby Place gate is frozen white and opaque, and hoarfrost picks out the carved stone of the oriel. He tries a line aloud to it, hears how grand it sounds through a stopped nose, and laughs at himself before he goes in.

**S007 · 2025-01-17 (Fri) · 09:00–16:45 · cross_keys_inn · Rehearsal from parts; a play in cold light, with a fever**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, abel_garnish, will_kemp, richard_cowley, augustine_phillips, nick_sorrell, humphrey_larkham  
sources: meeting, body, location, photo  
Morning in the parlour, breath smoking: *Richard III* run from the rolls for next week, each man holding only his own speeches and three cue-words, while Garnish checks every entrance against the plot pasted by the door. Cowley comes in twice on the wrong "lord" because two cues end alike, and Nick has learned his words but not the breath before his entrance; William alters three cue-words, and Larkham, silent, recopies the two rolls rather than trust anyone to remember. Burbage does the wooing scene at half voice and it is still too big for the room. Phillips hears William's throat and wants Cowley to take Egeon this afternoon; William will not. At two, *Errors*: Egeon's long tale is the worst part in England for a man with no voice, and he gets through it on breath and consonants, and Kemp says kindly that he never sounded so near death. Photo 5: the yard at half past four, stage torches just lit, breath smoking in the galleries. Body: steps 9,400; resting 74; 128 on stage; 5 h 50 the night before, 9 h 05 the night after.

**G006 · 2025-01-18 (Sat) · 11:00 · ansell_house · Cowley has the part**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_cowley, dorcas_ansell  
sources: meeting, calendar, body  
The worst day of the cold. Richard Cowley comes up the stair, as far as Mistress Ansell allows, with Egeon already learned, and asks only which of the corrections is current. William tries to explain the reason for the second; Cowley says drily that a reason will not help an entrance. Mistress Ansell sets down bread and broth between them while the two men compare the roll. William stays abed, and *Errors* plays at two without him and does not become a story about his sacrifice. Body: resting heart 76, steps 900, the fever at its height by evening.

**W006 · 2025-01-19 (Sun) · 11:00 · ansell_house · Abed: a posset, a lecture, a letter**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, gilbert_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, notes (letter), body  
He keeps his bed; Mistress Ansell goes to church for the house and brings the sermon home complete, as a penance for him. The posset is hot milk curdled with ale, ginger and a grating of nutmeg, in a pewter pot. The lecture is on men who walk about in frost at ten at night. Gilbert comes up the stair with a letter from the Bell, where the carrier came in on Thursday. Anne writes that all three children want shoes and have agreed to outgrow them in the same month; that his father has a cough and is cheerful about it; and that she would know whether he means to come at Lent or only to say so. Gilbert: "Answer the last one first, brother." Resting heart back to 66 by night; the fever is gone. Propped on two bolsters with the comedy's foul papers across his knees, he strikes twenty lines of stale conceit between Berowne and Rosaline and puts in a quicker, rougher exchange made for Arthur's height; the stillness of a fever lets him hear every false syllable. Steps about 1,400.

#### Week 4 · Mon 01-20 – Sun 01-26

**W007 · 2025-01-21 (Tue) · 16:50 · cross_keys_inn · The last line; the book to Garnish**  
people: william_shakespeare, abel_garnish, john_heminges, thomas_pope  
sources: meeting, screen (draft), notes  
He wrote the end before dinner: the cuckoo and the owl, and then one prose line to shut the door, "The wordes of Mercurie are harsh after the songes of Apollo." After *Richard III* he puts the whole comedy into Garnish's hands, forty-odd leaves, clean. Garnish reads standing, lips moving, and finds his first fault inside five minutes: a lady is called Katharine in one scene and Rosaline in the next, with the same lover. "Shall he woo them both, or will you choose?" William chooses. Heminges asks how long it plays. "Long." "How long?" "I am in pain, John." "Where?" "In the fifth act." The account book gets one line: "Navarre ended this day." Pope, who read the ending over Burbage's shoulder this morning, asks whether wanting a reward is the point of a comedy. William, who has tried the last scene both ways, pleading and plain, keeps the refusal: it is livelier and no safer at the box.

**W111 · 2025-01-22 (Wed) · 10:30 · pauls_churchyard · Reading standing; Master Harrison reports**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_harrison  
sources: meeting, location, screen (book)  
The first free morning in six weeks. He walks to Paul's and reads standing at three stalls, a page here and a gathering there, and buys nothing. At the White Greyhound his two poems lie on the board (T1). Harrison tells him how they go, as if of a third party: "Your Venus moves, Master Shakespeare. The Lucrece is… respected." He asks whether there will be a third. William says he has been busy with a comedy; Harrison looks as a man looks who has been told of an illness. In Paul's Walk the talk is all of the wedding at Greenwich on Sunday. Steps about 5,400. Harrison has scarce a dozen *Lucrece* left before the term brings the lawyers in tomorrow.

**S008a · 2025-01-23 (Thu) · 10:00 · westminster_hall · Men who sell explanations**  
people: william_shakespeare, george_bryan  
sources: calendar, location, photo, memo  
Hilary term opens (T2·calc), and Westminster Hall has filled overnight with serjeants in their coifs, country knights nursing land suits and clerks with rolls under their arms. Bryan has business near the courts, and William walks west with him to stretch lungs just clear of the cold before the afternoon. He listens to men condensing other men's troubles into forms that can be carried, and watches a client repeat his grievance at greater length after being charged for having it shortened. Photo: the forest of black caps and parchment under the hammer-beam roof. The memo, walking back by the Strand, is amused and then not: "They sell explanations by the sheet. So do I. Mine have jokes in." Back east by one for the play.

**S008b · 2025-01-23 (Thu) · 14:00 · cross_keys_inn · Hilary term opens**  
people: william_shakespeare, margery_pell  
sources: calendar, location, meeting  
The almanac says "Hilary term begins" (T2·calc), and the yard says it too: black gowns in the galleries, clerks with papers still under their arms, a different laugh. *Two Gents* plays to them. Margery Pell at the gallery stair gives the house in one line when the boxes come in: "Lawyers, lawyers' men, two aldermen's wives and a horse-courser. Thirty-three shillings in mine." William, not cast, watches the gowns from the parlour door. They laugh before the end of a line, having guessed it. A comedy about young men at their books opens to these very men in four weeks.

**W008 · 2025-01-24 (Fri) · 10:00 · st_johns_clerkenwell · The Master of the Revels reads a comedy**  
people: william_shakespeare, edmund_tilney, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, location, notes  
He and Heminges carry the book out by Moorgate to the old priory. Tilney reads with a pen in his hand for most of an hour while his men stitch at masquing stuff for Sunday's wedding. He strikes two oaths and nothing else: "The Queen's ladies may hear God's name in church, Master Shakespeare. Elsewhere, 'by Jove' serves." He asks whether there is truly a King of Navarre in it; William says only the name, which was in the air. He smiles faintly once, at Armado's melancholy, and asks whether any of the French lords glances at a living ambassador; reassured, he signs the last leaf. Heminges counts out the fee, 7s. On the way home Heminges enters it, and William says he had never been cut so little and felt it so much.

**S009 · 2025-01-26 (Sun) · 18:00 · burbage_house_holywell · Supper: a wedding at Greenwich today**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, james_burbage, cuthbert_burbage, george_bryan, will_kemp  
sources: meeting, calendar  
Mutton, Sunday. The Earl of Derby marries Lord Burghley's granddaughter at Greenwich this day with the Queen there (T1); it was put off a week, and Tilney's office has been sewing for it.
