Bryan, who knows how great houses do things, describes a wedding night: masque, banquet, the hall cleared at midnight. Burbage asks who plays before them. Derby has his own men, says Cuthbert, and that is that. William says idly that a wedding wants a play as a feast wants a fool, something light to get the company to bed. James says a wedding wants paying for. Nothing is written down, and no play is thought of beyond the sentence. Kemp proposes that a wedding is an excellent occasion for paying players who have already been paid; Bryan says this is an unusual definition of excellent, and adds that it is the very match the young Earl of Southampton was said to have paid dearly to refuse (T2 as gossip).

#### Week 5 · Mon 01-27 – Sun 02-02

**W112 · 2025-01-27 (Mon) · 19:15 · ansell_house · Call: Heminges counts the ladies**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges  
sources: call  
"Will? John Heminges. From Aldermanbury." He has been through the book with Garnish, dividing it into rolls. "You've written four ladies and a dairymaid, and I have three boys, and one of the three is the page." William offers to cut the dairymaid; Heminges says Kemp has already seen her. They settle it: two boys hired by the week out of Pope's parish at 3s each to stand as Maria and Katharine, and Sincler, in black, for the messenger, "since he needs no padding to look like bad news." Heminges will take the schoolmaster himself. One aside: his wife says Nick is growing through his sleeves. "God keep you." "And you."

**W009 · 2025-01-28 (Tue) · 09:00 · burbage_house_holywell · Parts given out; the first reading**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, will_kemp, thomas_pope, augustine_phillips, abel_garnish, nick_sorrell  
sources: meeting, screen (part)  
The long table, a fire, Garnish's rolls in a basket: seventeen parts copied in six days. William reads the whole play aloud, every voice, and reads Costard badly on purpose so that Kemp will want to mend him. Pope tries Armado's first letter at once and the table is helpless. Kemp unrolls his part against Burbage's along the floor to see whose is longer. Phillips hums at the songs and asks for them by Friday. Nick asks one question: after the news of her father, does the Princess weep? "No. She governs." He nods and marks his roll. Down to the Cross Keys for *The Shrew* at two.

**W113 · 2025-01-29 (Wed) · 16:20 · cross_keys_inn · The old *Hamlet*: a ghost at dusk**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, richard_burbage, john_sincler  
sources: meeting, screen (stage), body  
The company's old *Hamlet* (another man's play; T3, kept as stock) fills a winter yard reliably. William plays the Ghost, which tradition later hangs on him for another play (T4, used knowingly). He stands under the stage in a sheet and flour, waiting to cry "Hamlet, revenge!" in the last of the light, with horses coughing in the stable behind him. It works, as it always does: a gallery of lawyers goes quiet. In the parlour Kemp says he was more fearful a fortnight ago with the cold. Sincler asks, seriously, how one stands so as to look dead. "Think of the reckoning, John." On stage 121; steps 9,800. The Rose has *2 Tamburlaine* today; ghosts of two kinds.

**S010 · 2025-01-30 (Thu) · 17:00 · cross_keys_inn · Sharers plan the run to Shrovetide**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, richard_burbage, john_heminges, augustine_phillips, thomas_pope, george_bryan  
sources: meeting, calendar  
After *Errors*, round the parlour table, the almanac open. Lent begins on 5 March; that gives five playing weeks. Heminges wants the new comedy on by the middle of February to get four days out of it. Burbage wants three full weeks to learn Berowne, "for he never stops talking." They fix Thursday 20 February, a term-less day, and trust the Inns to come anyway. Kemp asks what jig follows it and is told a comedy needs none; the first small cloud. They agree to stop on Shrove Tuesday rather than play into Lent and tempt the Mayor. Pope: "The Rose will play till they're stopped." Heminges: "The Rose has no mayor sleeping next door to its poet." Heminges has the afternoons counted: twenty-eight before Lent, and he wants four of them for the comedy.

**S011 · 2025-02-01 (Sat) · 11:20 · tower_hill · The Prince's fifteen barges**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, will_kemp, augustine_phillips  
sources: photo, location, memo  
The Gray's Inn Prince comes up the river from Greenwich with fifteen barges, music, trumpets and guns, and lands at the Tower to ride through the City (T1; the hour is invented). William and Burbage walk down from the Cross Keys to watch from the hill. Photo 6: the barges with streamers on the tide below the Tower, gun-smoke hanging over the water. Burbage studies how a man in borrowed majesty gets out of a boat. William's memo: a student playing a prince, and the Tower's guns answer him as if it were so; "we do it for a penny and are rogues by statute." This is the only time in the year he stands on Tower Hill. Back for the play at two. Kemp and Phillips come down too. A man in front insists on describing what everyone can already see, and Kemp gives a far more useful account of the people trying to see round him; Phillips hums the barges' trumpet tucket under his breath to steal it for a stage entrance. William keeps one image besides the photo: an oar lifted almost upright to spare another boat's dignity.

**S012 · 2025-02-02 (Sun) · 15:00 · ansell_house · Candlemas: the twins are ten**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes (letter), calendar  
"Twins' day" in the almanac (T1 baptism, 2 February 1585). After church he writes home, knowing it will not leave until Thursday's carrier and will come a week late. Two groats go in, one each, "to be spent foolishly", with a twist of comfits. To Hamnet: mind Master Aspinall's Latin, do not improve the fastening of this packet before the contents are out, and say whether a boy of ten can now outrun a boy of nine. To Judith: the same groat, the same order, a riddle her sister must read her and then keep from her, and a warning not to understand "equal" so cleverly that Susanna has to judge between them. To Susanna two lines so she is not left out, and thanks for minding the shoes; to Anne, short, that he is mended of a cold, the comedy is done, and of Lent, more when he knows the day. He dates it "From London this Candlemas day", and it is fond because it knows their habits, not because it says how much he feels.

---

### February

#### Week 6 · Mon 02-03 – Sun 02-09

**G009 · 2025-02-04 (Tue) · 08:30 · fields_printing_house · A book that cannot walk home**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field  
sources: meeting, location  
On his way to the Cross Keys he stops at Field's shop door to ask whether, when Lent comes, a chronicle might be spared for some weeks. Field can, but not this instant with a forme on the press, and makes him name the day it would come home. William suggests that books are more patient than lenders. Field: "Lenders must be patient, Master Shakespeare, because books cannot walk home." They agree he will ask again in Lent. Four minutes on the step, in *you*, because the apprentices are listening; it is the start of the Holinshed (W022).

**W010 · 2025-02-04 (Tue) · 12:10 · cross_keys_inn · Call: Heminges on the Princess's gown**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges  
sources: call  
"Will? John. From the mercer's, in Cheapside, and I'd as soon be out of it." The Princess of France wants a gown fit for one: watchet taffeta, copper lace that will pass for gold at ten paces. The mercer names 50s and will take half down. "The— the stock has eleven shillings in it till Saturday." William asks whether France could be poorer. Heminges says not when the King of Navarre goes in his own old velvet. They agree to take it on credit against the Queen's £20, whenever that comes. Nick is beside him being measured, standing like a post. "He bids me say the sleeves must be long. He's grown." "God keep you both."

**S013 · 2025-02-05 (Wed) · 08:45 · bishopsgate · *Mahomet* on the post**  
people: william_shakespeare, hired_men  
sources: screen (bill), meeting  
The Rose's bill on the post by the Bull: *Mahomet* (T1, not rechecked; the 26s it takes is never known to him). Ralph Raye, who has seen it, gives it in a breath: rant in the old *Tamburlaine* vein, fireworks, and a head that speaks. William asks what the head says. "Nothing worth the powder." He goes back up to the desk thinking how fast London tires of thunder and how slowly it tires of a face. *Titus* at the Cross Keys at two.

**G010 · 2025-02-06 (Thu) · 09:00 · cross_keys_inn · Save some breath for the answer**  
people: william_shakespeare, augustine_phillips, nick_sorrell, sam_latchet  
sources: meeting, notes, body  
In the cold parlour Phillips has Nick say the Princess's first long speech while walking, not standing rigid to show he can. Sam copies the exercise with enthusiasm and reaches the end of the room before the end of the sentence. William hears that his own pointing has put the breath in the wrong place and changes two stops on the roll. Nick's relief is practical: the passage no longer feels like a test made to catch him out. Phillips: "A boy has a pint of breath, Master Shakespeare. Don't make him spend it all on the first line." Steps 7,900 by dinner.

**W114 · 2025-02-06 (Thu) · 17:20 · the_bell_carter_lane · Carrier day: the Candlemas letter goes late**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins  
sources: meeting, location, notes (letter, ledger)  
After the play he walks to Carter Lane, where the yard is all packhorses and Warwickshire, and Thomas Hobbins gives the road back in distances and prices: twelve hours to Wycombe if the way's dry, oats up a penny at Oxford, a bridge plank gone at Shipston. He takes the letter and the two groats sewn in a paper; he left Stratford on Monday week, and the twins' day will be nine days old when this arrives. "They'll take it kindly, late." "They'll take the groats kindly." William starts to add instructions by mouth, stops, and writes them on the outside of the packet; Hobbins asks whether there is anything the mistress is expected to guess, and William adds one plain line. In return Hobbins hands up a letter from Anne: a pan mended, a lost ladle found where his father swore he never put it, a quarrel over whose turn it was at the well. William asks after his father's cough and gets "mending", pays 2d and drinks one cup. In the ledger: "to the carrier ij d.; my letter, late."

**S118 · 2025-02-07 (Fri) · 20:00 · ansell_house · A sonnet evening**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft)  
No play for him today and no rehearsal tomorrow till nine. By one candle he fair-copies a sonnet on a single leaf: no title, no number, no name on it or anywhere near it. A builder may use the 1609 text of the one that begins "Weary with toyle, I hast me to my bed," which fits a tired man a long way from somewhere. He reads it over, changes nothing, and lays it in the folder with two older leaves. These are for "private friends", passed by hand or not at all. The journal does not say for whom this one was made, and he does not say it aloud. Bed by ten; a waking hour about two.

**W011 · 2025-02-09 (Sun) · 13:30 · finsbury_fields · Church, the fields, lines on the tables**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, john_spencer  
sources: location, memo, notes, calendar  
Morning prayer at St Helen's: Master Thwaite turns the glass twice; the Lord Mayor gives the litany responses from his pew, short and cold. Afterwards William walks out by Moorgate into the fields: archers at the butts, linen stiff on the hedges, windmills on the rise. He walks fast with his head down, saying Navarre's first speech over until it sits in the breath. On the tables: "brazen tombs — cormorant Time — heirs of eternity". A memo on the way back: a king who opens his mouth on Fame had better not have a hole in his hose. Steps 8,700.

#### Week 7 · Mon 02-10 – Sun 02-16

**W115 · 2025-02-10 (Mon) · 10:40 · southampton_house · A paper left at the lodge**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_florio  
sources: location, memo  
He walks to Holborn with a paper folded small and sealed. The porter knows the name, or says he does. My lord is at court; Master Florio is within but teaching. William leaves the paper "for Master Florio, to give or not as he judges" and is not asked further in than the lodge bench, where he sits four minutes. This is the only time in the year he is inside those gates. What the paper holds is not in the journal. The memo on the way back by Holborn is one line: "Done. Now forget you did it." He rubs the inked forefinger against his thumb the length of Chancery Lane.

**S014a · 2025-02-11 (Tue) · 08:20 · old_swan_stairs · Raleigh is gone for Guiana**  
people: william_shakespeare, davy_gannet  
sources: meeting, screen (pamphlet), location  
He has an errand at Paul's Wharf and takes Gannet's boat for a penny. The news comes with the oars: "Raleigh's gone for gold, the Spaniard's in Kent, and it'll rain." The first is true; Sir Walter left England on Thursday last, the 6th (T1), and the word has come up from the west in five days. Gannet has him gone with forty ships and the Queen's blessing; the truth is nearer a handful and her leave. A sailor on the stair says Guiana is where the men have their heads in their chests. William says he has met such men in the City. It does not rain. Gannet has been watching victualling barges load salt beef below the Bridge for the western venturers, which is how he knows.

**S014b · 2025-02-11 (Tue) · 18:00 · split_hazel · A voyage arrives as an argument**  
people: william_shakespeare, alice_brambleby, george_bryan, will_kemp  
sources: meeting, notes  
By supper the Raleigh news has the whole Split Hazel. A scarred sea-gunner at the next board spreads a greased chart of the Spanish Main and swears that every kitchen pot in Guiana is beaten gold. One account promises riches; Bryan says anyone who truly knew the road to riches would go quietly. Kemp asks the gunner, with great respect, why he is in Bishopsgate. William listens to how certainty grows with distance from the ship, and how men talk of gold far off when bread is dear at home. Alice allows the talk until one speaker treats a customer's doubt as disloyalty, then puts him out with his chart. On the tables: "gold: the further, the surer."

**W012 · 2025-02-12 (Wed) · 09:00 · cross_keys_inn · Rehearsal: Costard grows**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, abel_garnish, richard_burbage, thomas_pope, sam_latchet  
sources: meeting, screen (part)  
The third week of rehearsal. Kemp has the "remuneration" passage now and will not leave it: he weighs the three farthings, bites them, tries to buy the page with them. Each pass is two lines longer. Garnish, on the book, stops him. Kemp: "Set down, set down. The groundlings never paid a penny to hear what was set down." William says, very courteously, that some of it is better than what he wrote and he would be glad to know which, so that he may write it. Garnish begins a column in the margin headed with a K. Burbage runs Berowne's great speech on love to the stable wall. Hilary term ends today; the gowns will thin.

**S016 · 2025-02-14 (Fri) · 14:00 · the_rose · *Long Meg of Westminster***  
people: william_shakespeare, edward_alleyn, philip_henslowe  
sources: screen (stage), location, body, notes, photo  
St Valentine's Day, a full moon tonight, and a play at the Cross Keys he is not in. He crosses the Bridge and pays his penny for the Rose's yard. *Long Meg* is marked new in Henslowe's book (T1; "j", £3 9s, which he never knows). It is a lost play, so the record is only this: a tall boy-player striding in breeches with a staff, the yard roaring for a woman who beats men, Alleyn easy in a part smaller than himself. William stands by a post on the left and watches the yard more than the stage. Henslowe is at the door counting heads with his lips. Home by the Bridge at dusk, the moon coming up over the Tower. Steps 11,800. He studies where the laughter starts and how attention comes back after it, and what a body can carry that a speech would have to explain. His notes afterwards are ungenerous for half a page and useful for the other half. Outside, a brief word with Alleyn is cordial enough to make him ashamed of the worst sentence. Photo: the yard roaring as a tall boy in a buff jerkin cudgels three Spanish swaggerers.

**W013 · 2025-02-16 (Sun) · 15:30 · ansell_house · Septuagesima: the answer about Lent**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes (letter), calendar  
Septuagesima; the parish turns toward Lent and Master Thwaite says so at length. He writes to Anne and answers the question she put in January, first, as Gilbert told him to. He will come at Lent. The playing stops on Shrove Tuesday; there is the Queen's money to be fetched and divided, which may be mid-March; he will ride and be with them before Mid-Lent Sunday. He does not promise a day he cannot keep. The comedy opens Thursday; he tells her the plot in four lines and says the ladies have the better of it. The letter is shorter than his last and says more. It waits for the carrier of the 27th. Last Sunday's draft is beside him, with a London story in it; he cuts the part that needs too much explaining, and what is left sounds like a man talking to his wife, not a player reporting his trade.

#### Week 8 · Mon 02-17 – Sun 02-23

**W014 · 2025-02-18 (Tue) · 09:00 · cross_keys_inn · Full rehearsal**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, augustine_phillips, sam_latchet, nick_sorrell, john_sincler, abel_garnish, will_kemp  
sources: meeting, screen (plot)  
The whole comedy on the trestle stage in a cold yard, the plot hung on a nail by the parlour door, Garnish on a stool with the book. The watchet gown has come and Nick moves in it as if born to it. Phillips takes Sam through the cuckoo song: "Sing it on the breath, Sam, not on the word. The word will come along." Sam forgets where to stand and remembers every note. Then the messenger. Sincler walks on in black among the colours and says his half-line, and the ostlers in the gallery stop forking straw. Even Kemp is quiet. William says only, "Slower in. Then nothing. Let them do it." Three hours ten; Heminges writes the figure down and underlines it. On Sunday he read the ending aloud to himself and was tempted to soften the ladies' refusal, knowing exactly where it would make Burbage uncomfortable; he left the words alone and marked a pause. Today's whole instruction is that pause: do not explain what the yard can hear.

**W116 · 2025-02-19 (Wed) · 20:30 · ansell_house · The eve: Alse hears a king's oath**  
people: william_shakespeare, alse_pinder, dorcas_ansell  
sources: meeting, memo, body  
He is walking the chamber with Navarre's opening when Alse comes with the washing and stays in the door. He gives her the oath: three years' study, one meal a day, and no woman within a mile of the court. She thinks about it honestly. "Then they'll starve, sir. Who's to cook?" Mistress Ansell, from below: "Alse! He is not to be encouraged after eight." He laughs with his shoulders. The memo later is short: the girl has never seen a play and found the hole in the first minute; good. Slept 5 h 30; awake at two, running lines with his eyes shut.

**W015 · 2025-02-20 (Thu) · 14:00–16:50 · cross_keys_inn · *Love's Labour's Lost* first played**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, will_kemp, nick_sorrell, arthur_meeks, thomas_pope, john_sincler, margery_pell, abel_garnish  
sources: calendar, body, screen (stage), photo, meeting  
Photo 7, at noon: the bill on a post in Gracechurch Street, black-letter, rain-spotted, title only. He enters first, in a paper crown and old velvet, on "Let Fame, that all hunt after in their lyues"; heart 142. The Inns have come, term or no term. Burbage's Berowne talks the yard into his pocket; Pope's Armado stops the play twice; Kemp adds eleven lines and Garnish ticks every one. By the last scene the yard is blue dusk and two torches. Burbage rides Berowne's rhymes like a horse he trusts, and Nick and Arthur bat back the men's Muscovite blunders clean. Sincler comes on in black ("The King your father." "Dead for my life."), the laughter goes out like a candle, and Nick's Princess governs. The gatherers' boxes hold £7 11s. Margery Pell: "They went out quiet. I never knew a comedy send them out quiet. They'll come again to see if it does it twice." Steps 10,900; slept 6 h. In the tiring-room afterwards his heart is still at 128.

**S018 · 2025-02-20 (Thu) · 18:30 · cross_keys_inn · Supper: what was done at Westminster today**  
people: william_shakespeare, george_bryan, richard_burbage, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, screen (pamphlet)  
The company sups in the parlour on the strength of the boxes. A young man of the Inner Temple, in to praise Berowne, brings other news with his cup: the Jesuit Southwell was tried at Westminster today, before the Lord Chief Justice, for treason and condemned (T1). He says the priest told the court he was about the age of our Saviour (T2, from Catholic accounts; hearsay here). Thirty-three. A poet, the Templar adds; some of the benchers have his verses by heart. The table goes on eating. Bryan turns the talk to the gown. William has stopped hearing; he rubs the forefinger against the thumb and asks, courteously, when it is to be. Tomorrow.

**S019 · 2025-02-21 (Fri) · 21:15 · ansell_house · Voice memo: the poet at Tyburn**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo, body  
*The Shrew* plays without him and he does not leave the house. Southwell is hanged at Tyburn this morning (T1); William is not there and was never going. By supper he has it from three mouths, no two alike. The memo is the specimen in § 8: the crowd's cry as hearsay; "he was a poet, that's the thing I can't get round"; the paper crown of yesterday; his father's night prayer, not said; and then the turn back to craft, the messenger who must say almost nothing. No kinship is claimed and nothing is seen. Body: steps 2,700; resting 66 with no exertion; slept 4 h 50. The Rose has a new play today, *The Mack* (T1); he does not look at the bill. Two things the memo circles and never lands: a poet who wrote of a burning babe, taken to the tree on a hurdle, and how easily a man's inward mind becomes treason once it is spoken aloud. Under both lies the safer question of what a man can be made to say. When he stops, the pen in his other hand has dried.

**S117 · 2025-02-22 (Sat) · 17:00 · cross_keys_inn · Counting the gatherers' boxes**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, margery_pell, abel_garnish, will_kemp  
sources: notes (ledger), meeting, photo  
A late snow has thinned the yard for *Richard III*. The boxes are broken on the parlour table. Photo 8: a drift of pennies and halfpence, Heminges's hands sorting by size. Margery Pell stands to see her box counted and is right to the penny. The week, with Thursday in it, comes to 30s a share, the best since Christmas. Garnish reads his first weekly total from the margin of the book: "Kemp's interest, Thursday: eleven lines." Kemp asks what the interest is on Burbage. William takes his thirty shillings and says nothing clever. He counts the coffer at home by one candle and finds he has no pleasure in it tonight.

**G014 · 2025-02-23 (Sun) · 14:00 · ansell_house · The missing spoon**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder  
sources: meeting, photo  
After dinner Mistress Ansell counts her spoons and comes out one short. She accuses no one; she wonders aloud which of Master Shakespeare's callers had a taste for pewter. William asks, politely, which caller is thought capable of making a profit from it. Alse finds it among her mistress's own ink-things in the kitchen, stained black to the neck, where it has been used to measure out galls and copperas. Mistress Ansell says it was put there to be found. Photo: the spoon on the board beside the ink-pot. After the week of Southwell the house has an ordinary quarrel to enjoy.

#### Week 9 · Mon 02-24 – Sun 03-02

**W016 · 2025-02-24 (Mon) · 16:45 · cross_keys_inn · The second day: the Inns quote it back**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, richard_burbage, abel_garnish, margery_pell  
sources: meeting, calendar, body  
The comedy again, to a house two-thirds of Thursday's but louder. The Inns men have it by heart already. They call "Remuneration!" at Kemp before he reaches the line, and a gallery of them chants the long Latin word with Costard, syllable by syllable. Kemp rewards them with thirteen lines not set down. Garnish: "Twenty-four in two days. At this rate he will owe us a scene by Easter." At the end the hush comes again on the messenger, and William, standing in his paper crown, learns that it was not an accident. A lawyer at the parlour door asks who made the play. Burbage points with his thumb. Heart 124 at the first entrance; the body has learned it too. A passage he distrusted is heard clean; one he thought safe has to fight the inn's noise. Kemp fetches back a drifting yard without breaking the scene, which William has to own. Afterwards Burbage asks whether the ending worked, and William answers by telling him what the yard did, not what he meant.

**G013 · 2025-02-25 (Tue) · 10:00 · the_theatre · Rotten oak in Holywell**  
people: william_shakespeare, james_burbage, cuthbert_burbage  
sources: meeting, photo, location  
With Lent coming and the company due back in Shoreditch at Easter, James Burbage walks William and Cuthbert round the damp north sill-beams of the empty playhouse. He jabs his joiner's knife two inches into a spongy oak ground-post laid in 1576 and swears under his breath. The lease follows as it always does: twenty-one years, and the ground falls back to Giles Allen on 13 April 1597 (T1), with Allen slow to talk of renewing. William offers a joke about kings and roofs; Cuthbert asks which of them will pay the carpenter. Photo: the rotted mortise close to, beside wet Shoreditch grass. William agrees to give two Lent mornings to the carpenter in March and leaves the law to the family, and the lease enters the talk without becoming a prophecy of any later building. (Folds in Gemini's M006.)

**W017 · 2025-02-26 (Wed) · 11:30 · ansell_house · Call: Field on Harrison and the little *Venus***  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field  
sources: call  
"Will? Richard Field. From the Blackfriars." Harrison means to put the *Venus* out again, and small this time, in octavo: cheaper paper, more copies, a book for a pocket (T1 edition, undated). "And not a penny in it for thee, as thou knowest. The copy's his." William says he is glad to be thought portable. Field wants to know when there will be a third poem; a press stands idle on Fridays. He has heard, from an apprentice who was not supposed to be there, that the comedy is the talk of the Inns. "I don't hold with it. Lend me the book when Garnish can spare it." "God keep thee, Richard." "And thee." He will also lay by two quires of the Rochelle crown paper at 4½d before the stationers bid it up, and he asks after old Master Shakespeare in Henley Street.

**W117 · 2025-02-27 (Thu) · 17:15 · the_bell_carter_lane · Carrier day: how the groats were spent**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins  
sources: notes (letter), location, meeting  
Hobbins has a letter for him and takes the Septuagesima one. Anne's hand, or Susanna's at her dictation; the journal leaves it open. The groats came nine days late and were spent inside the hour. Judith bought a ballad she cannot read and made her sister say it over until she had it; she now sings it at the wash. Hamnet bought a whistle and lent the odd penny to a boy who has not paid, "which makes him your father's grandson." His father's cough is gone. Of Lent: "Come, then. I shall believe the horse when I hear it." He reads it standing in the yard and laughs without sound at the last line. Hobbins: "Good news, master?" "Just news. It's rarer." Lower down, in a different ink, is a sentence about his father that he reads quickly and puts away (G018).

**G016 · 2025-02-27 (Thu) · 19:00 · ansell_house · The dangerous stool**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell  
sources: meeting, notes, photo  
Back from the Bell, he finds a joiner's man in his chamber at Mistress Ansell's order, mending the joined stool that has rocked since Christmas. Under its short leg is the reason it has stood at all: a wedge of folded foul paper, three leaves of the comedy cut in January. Mistress Ansell turns it over and asks whether the discarded words are valuable. "Not where they were." "They're the only words in this room that ever held anything up." The joiner's man is paid 3d and the stool stands level on Anne's cushion. William keeps the inked wedge on the shelf as a private measure of literary worth; photo: the wedge in his palm, the ink-stained forefinger across it.

**W018 · 2025-02-28 (Fri) · 08:40 · ansell_house · A face at St Helen's; the first "black"**  
people: william_shakespeare, the_lady_unnamed  
sources: calendar, notes, screen (draft), memo  
Morning: from the desk under the west window he sees the Friday litany let out at the church porch. A woman comes down the step with an older one, a music-book under her arm. Dark hair under the hood, and when she looks up at the rooks, very dark eyes. She crosses toward the lane with the window. He does not go down. Night: by one candle a sonnet that argues with every painted beauty in London. A builder may use the 1609 text beginning "In the ould age blacke was not counted faire". It is fair-copied on one leaf, without title, number or name, and laid in the folder. The memo is four words: "The black-eyed. Hm. No." A small cross in the almanac.

**S021 · 2025-03-02 (Sun) · 18:00 · bull_inn · Shrove Sunday supper, with Nashe**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_nashe, richard_burbage, augustine_phillips, will_kemp, thomas_pope, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, body, photo  
Quinquagesima, the last Sunday of flesh before forty days of salt fish. The sharers take the Bull's back parlour over a smoking chine of roast beef with mustard and marrow-bones and double ale in pewter, 10d a head; photo: the carved beef, the pewter, Kemp's grease-shining grin across the board. Kemp treats the fast as a personal negotiation, and Pope asks whether a man may fast from another man's explanations. Nashe arrives uninvited and stays, brilliant and broke, three-quarters of the talk and all of the noise, at war with a Cambridge doctor who is not in the room and loses nothing by it. He has heard the comedy has a pedant and a braggart in it and demands to know which of them is himself; William says the page. Phillips gets him to sing, Burbage gets him to pay for one cup, and Heminges observes that they have come through their first winter as a company without one quarrel over the purse, and touches the table. Any meeting of the two is invented (T3 contact). William begs off at nine: "I am in pain." Nashe: "Where?" "In the purse. It's catching."

---

### March

#### Week 10 · Mon 03-03 – Sun 03-09

**S020 · 2025-03-03 (Mon) · 16:40 · cross_keys_inn · Court Shrovetide, heard of**  
people: william_shakespeare, george_bryan, richard_burbage, will_kemp  
sources: screen (pamphlet), meeting  
After *Two Gents*. The court is at Whitehall since Saturday (T1). There was running at tilt on Saturday and a tourney yesterday. Tonight the gentlemen of Gray's Inn carry a masque of Proteus and a rock of adamant before the Queen (T1): the same Prince who dined with the Mayor and landed at the Tower. Bryan explains what a masque is to Sam Latchet, who asks whether anyone speaks in it. Kemp: "The scholars play before the Queen for nothing, and we play before the scholars for a penny. Who's the fool?" Burbage would like to see a rock come on. William says nobody is asked to this one; it is heard of only. Bryan has been at Somerset House waiting on my lord's clerk: the warrant for December's twenty pounds is drawn and moving, and Hunsdon's word is to stop cleanly on Shrove Tuesday and hand the Mayor no stick.

**S022 · 2025-03-04 (Tue) · 14:00–18:00 · cross_keys_inn · Shrove Tuesday: the last play**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, will_kemp, abel_garnish, john_heminges  
sources: location, body, calendar, meeting  
The almanac carries two lines: "Shrove Tuesday" and "My lord's birthday" (T1; Hunsdon is 69). The comedy is played a third time, to a holiday yard thick with apprentices, pancake-fed and loud; Kemp gives them everything. Garnish's last tally before Lent: "Thirty-five lines in three days, and one whole song." The company drinks the patron's health and strikes the trestles. It is the apprentices' licensed day (custom, T2; no disorder is recorded this year, and he sees none). He goes home the long way all the same, by Cornhill, wide of a crowd about a cock-throwing in Leadenhall. Steps 9,900. The Rose takes £3 with *Long Meg* (T1) and will open again tomorrow. There is a harder, hungrier edge in the lads' voices than in a cheap-bread year, and he notes it without a word.

**S023 · 2025-03-05 (Wed) · 13:00 · the_theatre · Ash Wednesday, and the Rose opens a new play**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, john_heminges, hired_men  
sources: screen (bill), calendar, meeting  
Church first: the commination, read by Master Thwaite with relish. Then up to Shoreditch, where the sharers are looking over the cold playhouse they will open at Easter. Ralph Raye has come from over the water with a Rose bill in his hand: a new play today, *Seleo and Olympo* (T1; £3 to Henslowe). Kemp is bitter: "We stop like Christians and they take three pound for it. That's pennies left lying on a table." Heminges says the Mayor counts which house played in Lent and which did not. William says Henslowe will be shut within a fortnight and they will all be equally idle. His own account book still says "1594" at the head of the leaf, as Henslowe's does; the year turns on Lady Day. Raye says Henslowe's flag was up over the Bankside as bold as brass. Heminges, mildly: "A season may have a name without every tradesman stopping at once."

**S024 · 2025-03-06 (Thu) · 19:30 · ansell_house · Brooke bought; Verona, first notes**  
people: william_shakespeare, gib  
sources: notes, screen (book), photo  
At Paul's in the morning he bought a worn copy of Brooke's *Romeus and Juliet* for 6d, lacking the last leaf but one. Tonight a new heading in the commonplace book, "Verona". The page is the specimen in § 8, and it is **notes only**: two couplets copied out of Brooke, a list of names with "Romeo is better in the mouth", a plan of two houses and a street, a query on how short the time can be made (Brooke takes the better part of a year), a query on how word goes between the houses (Brooke has a letter that goes astray), a note on their first meeting, "hands. pilgrims?", and one image seen walking home, a torch brought into a vault. No speech, no scene, no line of any play. The last note on the page: "NOT YET. The King first. This after. Summer. It wants heat." Photo 9: the poem open under a candle, the cat's flank across the right-hand page. Brooke's jogging fourteeners make him smile, and then make him impatient.

**W019 · 2025-03-08 (Sat) · 10:00 · ansell_house · The desk re-ordered for Lent**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gib  
sources: notes, screen (tables), photo  
No rehearsal, no play, no reckoning. He clears the board: the comedy's foul sheets tied and laid on the shelf; Brooke on the left; a fresh quire, 5d; pens cut with the paring-knife; a new horn of Mistress Ansell's ink, 2d. On the tables a Lent list, to be wiped as done: "Costard: write Kemp's nine in. — letter to A. — the Q's money. — horse. — R. Field, a Chronicle?" Mistress Ansell looks in to count the candle-ends and approves of the tidiness as a sign of repentance. He counts the coffer for Saturday. The body has nothing to do; he walks to Finsbury and back twice. Steps 6,800.

**G018 · 2025-03-09 (Sun) · 16:00 · ansell_house · His father's careful omission**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes (letter), screen (letter), memo  
He rereads Anne's letter of 27 February at the board and finds what he skimmed in the Bell's yard: a sentence in a different ink saying that his father has "a little reckoning at the whittawer's, fourteen and six, which need not trouble you". He knows the construction and dislikes how readily he knows it. His first reply is more severe than the sum deserves. He puts it aside and writes instead that he will talk of the house's needs when he comes, and that Anne is to judge what must be paid before then. The memo, at the window: "Fourteen and six, and it need not trouble me. It's meant to." The severe draft goes into the candle.

#### Week 11 · Mon 03-10 – Sun 03-16

**W118 · 2025-03-10 (Mon) · 08:30 · cross_keys_inn · The baskets carted to Shoreditch**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, abel_garnish, nick_sorrell, sam_latchet, hired_men  
sources: meeting, location, body  
The company clears its winter house. Three apparel baskets, the property hamper, the trestles (the inn's) left stacked. Garnish carries the books himself and lets no one else touch them. The ostlers who have heard *Errors* forty times give Sam his cues from the hay-loft as he goes. The host shakes hands all round: "At Michaelmas, then, masters, if my lord writes again." Heminges pays him the last week's yard-money, 13s 4d. A hired cart, 8d, up Bishopsgate Street; William walks behind it with Nick, who asks whether the Theatre's stage is truly twice the breadth. Steps 8,200. Nobody looks back at the yard.

**M007 · 2025-03-11 (Tue) · 11:30 · pauls_churchyard · Daniel's *Civil Wars*, read standing**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_harrison  
sources: screen (book), notes, location  
At Simon Waterson's stall by the White Greyhound, a fresh quarto: Samuel Daniel's *First Fowre Bookes of the Civile Wars* (T1 for the year; stall and imprint `[ext]`, to verify), 2s. Too dear in Lent, so he reads the first book standing under the cloister, out of the drizzle. Daniel has Richard ride captive behind his cousin while his queen watches from a window, and has made her a grown woman where the chronicle's queen was a girl of ten (T2 source relation). His right thumb taps four beats on his thigh. Harrison, coming out of his own shop: "You may read it, Master Shakespeare, but you may not wear it out." He does not buy it, and comes back to read it standing three more times before he does (W033).

**W020 · 2025-03-11 (Tue) · 18:40 · ansell_house · Call: Gilbert on their father's debts**  
people: william_shakespeare, gilbert_shakespeare  
sources: call, calendar  
"Brother? Gilbert. From St Bride's, and the shop's shut, so I'm speaking for nothing." It is Richard's birthday at home and neither of them has sent. A Stratford man at the Bell says their father is being dunned again. "Father owes the baker. No, not that baker. The other baker." And the shoemaker, and the whittawer for skins, "which is a glover owing a glover". Three shops, small sums let run a year. Gilbert has added them on a shop-ticket: near £5. He does not ask William to pay; he reads him the list twice. William asks which baker stands at the church porch on Sundays. Gilbert does not know what he means. "Never mind. I'm for home in a fortnight." "Then take a full purse and an empty face. God keep you." "And you."

**S025 · 2025-03-12 (Wed) · 10:00 · the_theatre · Kemp grumbles at a rewritten clown bit**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, richard_burbage, abel_garnish, thomas_pope  
sources: meeting, screen (part)  
Lent work on the cold stage: mending, sorting, and Costard. William has written nine of Kemp's added lines into the book in a fair hand and cut two, and Garnish has recopied the roll. Kemp reads it as a man reads a will. "You've set down what I made up. Now I can't make it up." "You can make up something new, Will. I'll set that down at Easter." "And then?" "Then we'll have a long play." Burbage, bored, mounts the stage and gives Alleyn's Tamburlaine to the empty galleries, a king's speech at full pitch, to hear what the house does with a voice. The Rose is playing the very part across the river today (T1). The galleries give it back clean. "This house likes a king," he says. Kemp threatens three minutes of shin-scratching with a turnip after Easter if the cut stands. Burbage, from the stage, says he is tired of French rhymes and wants an English king whose crown burns his forehead, something to wash *Tamburlaine* out of London's mouth "and not your hunchback again". Pope observes that the argument has now run longer than the business in dispute, and they settle on a visible cue for Kemp's exit rather than a ruling on who has won.

**S026 · 2025-03-14 (Fri) · 15:30 · bishopsgate · The Rose shuts for Lent**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, hired_men, augustine_phillips  
sources: screen (bill), meeting  
The bill on the post says *The Siege of London* at the Rose (T1; 14s, the season's thin end). Raye, back from his banking across the water, says it is the last: the players are stopped, and Henslowe has carpenters and a painter in from Monday to board and paint the house (T1 repairs; £8 19s by his own book). Kemp, meeting them at the gate: "Ten days of Lent they got, and a new coat of paint out of it. We got our consciences." William points out that conscience needs no elm boards. No bill goes up beside the old one tomorrow. Both companies are now equally idle, and tomorrow the Queen pays. Raye counts 325 elm boards on the Bankside, at 24s, with lime-tubs beside them (Henslowe's items; not rechecked). Phillips, come up from Southwark, is briefly pleased that another house must spend money while it earns none, then admits the same is true of their own, and the talk turns to boards, copy and what may be mended in a pause.

**W119 · 2025-03-15 (Sat) · 07:30–10:30 · whitehall_palace · By water for the Queen's money**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, richard_burbage, davy_gannet  
sources: location, meeting, body, calendar  
The three men named go to fetch it (T1 warrant; that they collected it this day is T5). Gannet's boat from Old Swan on the flood, 2d a man; he has Raleigh crowned in Guiana by now. At Whitehall they go no further than the outer precinct: an hour in the passage of the office where the Chamber pays out, with a tailor and two trumpeters, and never a sight of the palace beyond. A clerk of the Treasurer of the Chamber's office reads them out as "William Kempe, William Shakespeare and Richard Burbage, servants to the Lord Chamberlain", dated the fifteenth of March 1594, and counts down £20 in angels and silver. He looks at them as a man looks at three fish. Kemp carries the bag. The tide has turned and Kemp will not pay double oars, so they walk home by the Strand and Holborn to Shoreditch. Steps for the day 9,600.

**S027 · 2025-03-15 (Sat) · 11:40 · burbage_house_holywell · Sharers' reckoning: the Queen's twenty pounds**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, will_kemp, richard_burbage, augustine_phillips, thomas_pope, james_burbage, george_bryan  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger), photo  
The full script is the meeting specimen (§ 8); it opens on Heminges: "The warrant has no weight until someone brings the coin." The coin goes on the cloth; photo 10 is the gold and silver on the long table, the ledger beside it, seven men's hands at the edges. Heminges itemises: boat-hire and baskets 16s, rewards to hired men, boys, book-keeper and tireman 34s, the mercer's 50s for the Princess's gown. Five pounds of costs leaves fifteen, in eight parts, the eighth being the stock: 37s 6d a share. Kemp argues that the Queen's reward should go "by desert" and is talked down by William ("we'll all go to dinner equally wronged") and bought off by Phillips with a new jig, with the slippers. Kemp is first on the paper and says so three times. William means to put his share on a hired horse. In the ledger: "of the Queen, my part, xxxvij s. vj d. had." Burbage lobbies for seven yards of peach-coloured velvet in grain and is told to wait for the autumn. Edith's 8s is set aside out of the stock share, to be paid on Thursday (G020).

#### Week 12 · Mon 03-17 – Sun 03-23

**W021 · 2025-03-17 (Mon) · 09:00–15:00 · bull_inn · Gifts, and a horse bespoken**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage  
sources: location, notes (ledger), body  
A day of errands with the tables in his hand. At Holywell, Burbage gives up an old lath stage-sword, the paint worn off the hilt: "It killed four Richmonds. Tell the boy." At Paul's, a riddle-book, 2d, for a girl who reads. In Bishopsgate Street, a bunch of ribbons in three colours, 5d, and a pair of perfumed gloves, 2s 4d; a glover's son buying gloves, he knows what his father will do with them. Last, the Bull: a bay gelding, sound, a little sour, bespoken from Wednesday week at 12d a day and his own charge of fodder. The ostler shows him its teeth; he pretends to judge them. Steps 10,400. The ledger takes six lines.

**G019 · 2025-03-18 (Tue) · 08:30 · the_theatre · Repairing the thing that must vanish**  
people: william_shakespeare, james_burbage, humphrey_larkham, sam_latchet  
sources: meeting, photo, body  
The first of the carpenter's Lent mornings. A property chair of state, painted to pass for oak and gilt, has a weak joint where kings sit down too hard. James Burbage explains how to mend it without making it too heavy for the boy who must carry it off. Sam offers a cure that would work if the chair never had to leave the stage. Larkham, who keeps the property list as well as copying, writes: "chair, mended; not to be sat on by Master Kemp." William enjoys a morning in which appearance and structure can be settled with wood instead of argument. Photo: the chair upside down on the trestles, a new peg pale in the old dark wood; home by eleven for Florio's call.

**W120 · 2025-03-18 (Tue) · 11:00 · ansell_house · Call: Florio, a paper received**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_florio  
sources: call  
"Master Shakespeare? Florio. Resolute John Florio, from my lord's house in Holborn." Five weeks after the lodge. My lord had the paper; he was at court for the Shrovetide running and has it by him still. "He bids me say he is glad you prosper. I say it as he said it." Nothing more is offered, and William does not ask. Florio fills the silence the way he fills his dictionary, three words for every plain one (peregrinate, perambulate, walk), and then with a proverb: "*Chi va piano va sano.* Who goes softly goes safely, and you go at a gallop, they tell me: a comedy, and the Inns of Court by the ears." He would know whether there is Italian in it. "One proverb, and I think it is wrong." Florio says it for him, *Venetia, Venetia, chi non ti vede non ti pretia*, and laughs: "It is not wrong. It is only English." "Send it to me before you send it to a printer. God keep you." "And you, sir."

**W022 · 2025-03-19 (Wed) · 10:30 · fields_printing_house · Field lends the Holinshed**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field, jacqueline_field  
sources: meeting, screen (book), location  
He asks for a chronicle and gets a lecture first, in the shop, with *you*: plays are a waste, and a waste with a crowd in it. Then upstairs, with *thou*, and the book. Field's Holinshed, the second volume, comes down from its place heavy as a hearthstone. William opens it standing and it falls at the reign of the second Richard: a king, his uncles, a cousin banished, a crown handed across. He reads four columns without sitting. Jacqueline puts a stool behind his knees. "It is lent," Field says. "Lent in Lent, and I'll see the gap on that shelf every morning till it's home." Before he can sit again Jacqueline moves a stack of quires into the place where he stands, which ends the visit without a word of discourtesy. He carries the book back through Cheapside in both arms. That night the Verona page is closed and a new heading goes into the commonplace book: *Of Kings*. By one candle he maps the play's shape on a folded sheet, the lists, a banishment, a death, a landing, a crown handed across, and copies the heralds' challenges at Coventry word for word out of the chronicle.

**W121 · 2025-03-20 (Thu) · 12:00 · the_bell_carter_lane · Carrier day: word sent ahead, money kept back**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins, gilbert_shakespeare  
sources: location, notes (ledger), meeting  
Hobbins is in with his six packhorses and a pair of thick knitted worsted hose from his mother. William sends four lines ahead by him: he leaves London on Wednesday the 26th, by Oxford, and looks to be at the bridge on Friday by dusk; Hobbins, who leaves on Saturday, will be there two days before him. "Then they'll have time to sweep," says Gilbert, come round from St Bride's to add a paper of pins for their mother. The Lady Day money William keeps back, 60s sewn in canvas, to put into Anne's hand himself; Hobbins, who has carried it other quarters, says it would be safer in his pack than on that bay of the Bull's. "Then it'll be safe twice." Hobbins gives the road: the Oxford way drying nicely in this wind, foul in the bottoms beyond Wycombe, the river up at Shipston. "Twelve hours to Wycombe at my pace." "At mine?" "On that bay? Nine, and he'll argue every mile." In the ledger: "set by for Stratford lx s., to go in my own hand."

**G020 · 2025-03-20 (Thu) · 16:00 · the_theatre · Edith is paid**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, edith_kettlewood  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger)  
In the tiring-house, with the baskets still roped from the Cross Keys, Heminges pays Edith Kettlewood her 8s out of the stock share of the Queen's money, Lady Day kept with five days to spare. He enters it plainly and says so as he writes: the stock paid a debt, which is not the same as a man drawing his share. Edith folds her account away first, and only then accepts William's compliment on the January sleeve. Heminges: "Item, one prince's sleeves, paid for at last." William strikes the line off his own tables. Edith asks what is wanted for Easter and is told: the Princess's gown let out, and Arthur's hems let down again.

**W023 · 2025-03-21 (Fri) · 16:00 · finsbury_fields · Voice memo: Verona names**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo, location  
Walking the Moorfields path with the wind behind him, trying names aloud for how they sit in the mouth. "Romeus. Ro-me-o. Three, and it opens at the end. Juliet. Montague, Capulet. Capel. …Mercutio. He has a cold hand in Brooke and nothing else. A man could be made out of a cold hand." A pause for a laundress's cart. "Tybalt is a cat's name. Good." Then he stops himself: "Not yet. I've a king at home on the board with his uncles, and he'll not wait." No line of any play is spoken, only names. Steps 7,400.

**M009 · 2025-03-22 (Sat) · 19:30 · bishopsgate · Music at Morley's; an unsigned quatrain**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_morley, the_lady_unnamed  
sources: meeting, memo, notes, calendar  
Thomas Morley the composer, who lives in the parish (T2; unchecked as a neighbour), asks him in for an hour of Lenten viols and Italian airs, and the house in the lane off Bishopsgate Street turns out to be the one with the lit window of Twelfth Eve. Morley talks of his balletts for five voices, at the printer's this year (T1 for the book; the talk is invented), and of strings at a price no player could pay. Among the company is a married woman of the parish at the virginals; she plays a strain exactly, then again faster, as if laughing at it, and he knows the hand before he sees it. They are not made known to each other, he does not ask her name, and nothing passes but the music. Crossing the close in the dark he murmurs a memo about the jacks that leap to kiss the player's hand while a man stands envying dead wood. Then he strikes a light and writes four lines on a scrap, unaddressed: a builder may use the first quatrain of the 1609 sonnet beginning "How oft when thou my musike musike playst". A small cross in the almanac.

**W024 · 2025-03-23 (Sun) · 14:00 · ansell_house · Sermon; packing**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, josias_thwaite  
sources: calendar, notes, meeting, photo  
Master Thwaite on the prodigal, an hour and a half, which Mistress Ansell considers timely. After dinner he packs a leather bag: two shirts, the gifts, the lath sword rolled in the grey cloak, the tables, a quire, and Brooke left behind on purpose. Holinshed stays on the shelf under a cloth, against Gib. Alse asks how far Warwickshire is. "A hundred miles." She has never been ten. Mistress Ansell asks whether his wife knows the plot of the forsworn gentlemen. "Not yet." "Tell her mine opinion of it." He says he will. He does not say that he has been rehearsing what to say at a door. He makes a list of what to do in Stratford and strikes out half of it, because it reads like a plan to be absent while present. The children's things are wrapped separately, so that arriving does not begin with a quarrel.

#### Week 13 · Mon 03-24 – Sun 03-30

**S028 · 2025-03-25 (Tue) · 11:30 · ansell_house · Lady Day: rent, and the year turns**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell  
sources: notes (ledger), calendar  
Twenty shillings on Mistress Ansell's table before noon, counted by her twice. The firing-and-washing sixpences end today until Michaelmas. He remarks that the year has changed and the room has not improved; she answers that constancy is one of its virtues, and writes the receipt in her late husband's law-hand. She will keep the chamber while he is away: "I'll not let it to a Lenten preacher. They burn more candle than you." Upstairs he rules a line across the account book and writes a new head, "1595"; the year that began for the almanac in January begins for the law today (T1). Under it the coffer, counted after the rent, with the 60s sewn in canvas lying in it: £27 2s. He takes out the canvas and £5 more for the road and his father's shops, ties them in a quilted leather belt, and locks £19 2s behind both locks. There is no talk of arms or gentility in the entry; only sums.

**S030a · 2025-03-26 (Wed) · 05:40–15:30 · oxford_road · The ride home, day 1: London to High Wycombe**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: location, body, memo, photo  
He takes the bay from the Bull at first light and leaves the City by Ludgate and the Strand. At Charing Cross he does not turn up for the Oxford road but keeps west by Knightsbridge and Kensington, and cuts north by a lane to the Uxbridge way beyond. It costs a mile. The memo says only: "Not by the tree. Not five weeks after." Uxbridge by ten, Beaconsfield at one, a bright cold day with the beech-buds still shut. The bay argues every mile, as promised. High Wycombe, 34 miles, by half past three: the Catherine Wheel, a shared bed, pottage and a herring for Lent. Body: steps 5,900; in the saddle 85–100; slept 6 h 10, broken. At the Catherine Wheel he checks the parcels before he takes his boots off, and the evening memo is a short list of things he should have packed where he could reach them. He has been saddle-sore since Beaconsfield and says so to nobody. Photo: chalk-and-mud cart-ruts gold in the low sun between hedges of budding thorn, before he leads the horse in.

**S030b · 2025-03-27 (Thu) · 05:30–17:45 · oxford_road · Day 2: over the ridge to Oxford**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: location, body, photo  
Out of Wycombe in the dark and up the long pull to Stokenchurch, leading the horse the last half-mile (heart 116). At the top the cold is clean and the whole Vale lies below. Photo 11: the bay's ears, the road dropping, the Vale of Oxford blue beyond. Down through Tetsworth; mud to the hocks in the bottoms; bread and a salt eel at Wheatley. Lambs everywhere and plough-teams on the heavy land. He talks to nobody for seven hours and says Gaunt's name aloud twice to hear it. Oxford's towers at a quarter to six with the sun going. Steps 6,400 by dusk.

**W025 · 2025-03-27 (Thu) · 18:00–20:40 · oxford · Oxford at dusk; Woodstock by night**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, photo, location, memo  
He baits the horse and himself at a tavern in the Cornmarket, unnamed here by rule. Photo 12: gowned scholars in the street at dusk, a tavern door spilling light, the sign out of frame. At the next table two masters of arts dispute whether a comedy may end without a marriage; he listens with his face in his cup and does not say he has just written one. Then nine more miles in the dark, because the day's reckoning needs them, with a carter's lantern ahead for company as far as Begbroke. Woodstock and the Bear at twenty to nine. The memo, from the saddle: "Sixty-and-odd behind me. I've forgot what I meant to say to her. Good." Slept 5 h 40. At the Bear a parcel-tie has worked loose, and he repacks it tighter by the rushlight and writes a few questions for the chronicle on a small sheet while the supper is slow.

**S030c · 2025-03-28 (Fri) · 05:30–17:40 · oxford_road · Day 3: the Cotswold edge in rain**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: location, body, memo  
Thirty-two miles. Enstone, then Chipping Norton for bread and cheese at ten, wool-town grey. The rain comes on at the Rollright ridge and is steady by Long Compton; the stones are off to the left somewhere in the drizzle and he does not turn aside. He knows every inn-sign now and the speech changes at Shipston: the vowels are his own. A boy at Alderminster holds the horse while he wrings out the cloak. The last six miles he rides wet through, with a lath sword and three ribbons dry inside the bag. Steps 5,100. Total for the three days, 102 miles.

**W026 · 2025-03-28 (Fri) · 18:10 · clopton_bridge · Clopton Bridge**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, john_shakespeare, richard_shakespeare  
sources: photo, location, meeting  
Photo 13: the bridge's long line of arches in the last light, the Avon high and brown. Richard, sent down to watch the bridge since four, takes the bay at the far end and walks him up Bridge Street to Henley Street, giving the town's news in an order William would not have chosen, beginning with the price of hay; by the door William has stopped rehearsing the speech he meant to arrive with. Anne is at the door with flour on her forearms. "You've come, then." "I said Friday." "You said it in a letter." His mother, behind her: "You're thin. They don't feed players, then." The twins arrive at a run from two directions. Susanna, twelve in May, stands back by the stair with her hands folded. His father, from the shop door, to the horse: "And who's this gentleman?" The sword comes out of the cloak and into Hamnet's hand, the ribbons to Judith, the riddle-book to Susanna, who says "Thank you, sir," and opens it at once. Every word of Anne's is invented (T5).

**W122 · 2025-03-29 (Sat) · 12:00 · henley_street · Nine at table**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, john_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare, joan_shakespeare, richard_shakespeare, edmund_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, body  
With him they are ten; a stool is fetched from the shop. Lent pottage, salt fish, new bread, everyone talking across everyone. Joan wants to know whether London ladies paint. Richard passes things and says nothing. Edmund asks to hold the sword and to hear exactly how Master Burbage dies; William shows him, with a spoon, and tells him to finish school. Judith does her grandfather crossing a room and is not rebuked. John turns the London gloves inside out and counts stitches: "Three to the inch too few. But they smell like a duchess." Anne puts them on anyway. The meeting should be written as overlapping voices, with no one of the children louder in it than another. Slept 8 h 20, the best night since Christmas.

**W027 · 2025-03-30 (Sun) · 09:30 · holy_trinity · Mothering Sunday**  
people: william_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare  
sources: calendar, location, meeting  
Mid-Lent Sunday. He gives his mother his arm down Old Town, the first time in two years; Anne goes ahead with the children. Mary does not speak of what he does. She asks who mends his shirts. She notices his right forefinger and says his father's were dyed with alum the same. In the church she says, under the responses, a small old grace of her own that he recognises and does not remark on. His father is not in the pew; nobody looks at the place. Walking back she says, exactly once, "It is good you came at Lent and not only wrote it." Steps 7,200.

---

### April

#### Week 14 · Mon 03-31 – Sun 04-06

**S032 · 2025-04-01 (Tue) · 10:30 · stratford_fire_ground · The fire ground with the children**  
people: william_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare  
sources: photo, location, meeting  
Hamnet has leave from school for the morning on his grandfather's word and walks the first stretch backward, reciting Latin nouns to make Judith laugh, while Susanna names every shop-sign that is new since last year. In Chapel Street, where last May's fire took the row (T1 fire, 13 May 1594; "above 100 houses and barns" in the annal), adze-scarred black studs stand beside raw yellow thatch and wet limewash. Photo 14 is a photo-prompt specimen (§ 8): black footings, a lone chimney, a new oak frame raised pale against the sky, three children half-turned toward it and busy, Judith's arm out. Each has a version: Susanna knows whose house was whose and what was saved, Judith knows what everyone shouted and does the shouting, and Hamnet knows how hot a nail must be to bend, and has one. A carpenter astride the wall-plate calls down that Master Shakespeare's son has come home to see what fire does: "It makes work." Anne, joining them from the baker's: "It makes debt." She has bought a new leather fire-bucket nobody meant to buy, the twins dispute who carries it, and when Susanna points out that he has answered a question nobody asked, he stops performing the visit and carries the bucket himself. They go home by the Guild Chapel without a glance at any house.

**S031 · 2025-04-02 (Wed) · 16:00 · henley_street · His father: old debts, and the arms once sought**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare  
sources: meeting  
In the shop, among the paring-boards, his father cuts a pair of fine gloves by eye. William comes at the debts sideways and is seen coming: "Gilbert's been counting. He was always a counting child." John names them himself, cheerfully and lower than the truth, and William is tempted to answer the large ambition with the small sum, which would be quick and cruel. Then, unasked, John reaches behind a panel of the wainscot and brings out a yellowed, mouse-nibbled scrap, the pattern of arms he had drawn when he was bailiff: "They'd have made me a gentleman once, Will. Then wool fell." With a joke to cover his fingers he asks whether a Queen's player has friends among the heralds; William says a player is a gentleman three hours every afternoon. "A player! Well. I've seen aldermen do it for nothing and worse." Mary, coming through with the lamp, remembers the colour of the ink it was drawn in, which lets both men off their positions, and the scrap goes back behind the panel. It is a wish shown once: nothing is filed, asked or promised, and the talk is invention on a later record (T5).

**W028 · 2025-04-03 (Thu) · 11:15 · high_cross_market · Market day with the twins; Master Quiney**  
people: william_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, richard_quiney, richard_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, hamnet_sadler, judith_sadler  
sources: photo, location, meeting  
School breaks at eleven and he walks the Thursday market with a twin on each hand until they tire of hands. Photo 15: the High Cross, butter-women, a glove stall, two small figures running ahead. His father's stall has Richard behind it, who nods and goes on selling; butter is 4d, and a woman says it was 3d at Michaelmas. Richard Quiney crosses the street to take his hand in both of his, "Loving countryman! London agrees with you," asks nothing today and asks it very warmly, and Judith repeats "loving countryman" in his exact voice before he is ten yards off; Hamnet is told not to laugh, and does. At the Sadlers' bakehouse in the High Street he offers to make Anne's purchase and finds he knows less than she does about both the goods and the seller's answer. The Sadlers talk of the loaf's weight without making a lesson of it for him, and Anne lets him finish the bargain once he has listened. A penny each for gingerbread; Susanna's is carried home in a paper. Steps 9,800.

**W123 · 2025-04-04 (Fri) · 09:00–11:00 · high_cross_market · Three shops, five pounds, no word**  
people: william_shakespeare, hamnet_sadler  
sources: location, notes (ledger)  
With Gilbert's shop-ticket in his sleeve he goes quietly round three shops: the other baker in the High Street, 41s; the shoemaker in Sheep Street, 44s 6d; and the whittawer by the bridge, a glover's reckoning for skins, 14s 6d. Each is paid to the penny and asked to rule the page off and say nothing at the door in Henley Street. It comes to £5 exactly, and the belt's loose coin, after the bay and the inns, will not reach: at the shoemaker's he unpicks the canvas meant for Anne and takes 42s from it. Hamnet Sadler, the first baker, who is owed nothing, watches him cross the street three times and says only, slowly, "That's a kind morning's walk, Will." "I am buying bread." "Aye. Eight loaves' worth." In the ledger: "pd. at Stratford for my father, v li., whereof xlij s. of the Lady Day money", and no names.

**W029 · 2025-04-05 (Sat) · 14:30 · hewlands_shottery · Shottery with Anne: the question**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, bartholomew_hathaway  
sources: meeting, photo, memo, location  
A mile across wet meadow and stiles to her brother's farm with a basket for him. Photo 16: the path, a woman's back in a grey gown, ten paces ahead. Bartholomew shows them the ewes and the first pink-tipped buds in the orchard and goes back to his work. On the way home she lets him talk about the comedy, the Queen's money, the Mayor at the end of the street, and he gives her the canvas, sewn up again, and tells her it holds eighteen shillings, not sixty, and where the rest has gone. She weighs it in her hand and names its uses before it is untied, malt and corn before summer prices bite, and shoes, one pair fewer: "Your father's shoemaker or ours, Will, it's the one leather." At the brook she stops. "I'll ask one thing and then leave it. Is London where you work, Will, or where you live?" He says both; he says a man may have a trade in one town and a house in another, and hears how it sounds. She waits out the whole of it, then: "That was three answers. I asked for one." She leaves it, as she said she would, and asks about the price of London butter. The memo that night, from the garden, is nine seconds of breathing and "No. Not well done." Every word of hers is invented (T5).

**W030 · 2025-04-06 (Sun) · 09:30 · holy_trinity · Holy Trinity; John stays home**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, john_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare  
sources: calendar, location, meeting  
The household goes down to church; his father stays in the shop with the door shut (T1 basis: the 1592 list, "for fear of process for debt"; the motive is left open). Before they go William asks him straight, for once. John, bright as ever: "I don't go because Master Bailiff's man stands at the porch with paper, and I've no wish to read it." "The paper's lighter than it was, Father." "Is it so? Then some fairy's been at my reckoning. I'll not thank a fairy. It spoils them." He does not come. In the pew William counts the row: his mother, Anne, three children, Joan, two brothers. One gap, his father's. The sermon is on patience.

**G026 · 2025-04-06 (Sun) · 16:00 · henley_street · A wheel and an audience of three**  
people: william_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, joan_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, photo, notes  
After church and dinner he mends Hamnet's small wooden wheel at the shop bench while Judith questions every decision. Joan supplies a scrap of leather that makes the repair possible, and declines his attempt to turn her help into a joke about his own uselessness. The wheel turns at last, unevenly but every time. Hamnet accepts the result and runs it down the yard; Judith says unevenly was her prediction from the start. Photo: the wheel on the bench, the leather shim, a boy's hands reaching in at the edge of the frame. On the tables: "an even wheel is rarer than a true one."

#### Week 15 · Mon 04-07 – Sun 04-13

**W031 · 2025-04-07 (Mon) · 19:00 · henley_street · The evening before he rides**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, edmund_shakespeare, john_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, notes  
Supper, then the hall fire. Each child may ask one thing from London. Susanna: a book with no riddles in it. Judith: a song nobody in Stratford has. Hamnet: to know whether the sword was ever in a real fight. "It has killed the same man four times a year since before you were born." He is satisfied. Edmund asks to come to London and is told fifteen is for school. His father sings half a catch and forgets the rest. Late, Anne counts what is left in his purse aloud, without comment, and gives him back a shirt she has turned at the collar. She does not ask her question again. He writes on the tables: "one answer."

**W032a · 2025-04-08 (Tue) · 05:30–17:30 · oxford_road · The ride back, day 1: to Woodstock**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: location, body  
Over the bridge at first light; the children are asleep and Anne is in the door, as she was. The bay has had eleven days' grass and is no sweeter for it. Shipston, Long Compton dry this time, the stones standing clear on the ridge. Chipping Norton at noon. He rides with the reins slack and the tables on the saddle-bow and writes nothing. Woodstock and the Bear by half past five, 32 miles. Steps 4,800; slept 6 h. At Shipston he reties a knot in the pack that Anne tied, and at the Bear he reads over his spring notes and finds them less good than they seemed before he went home; he sleeps early instead of forcing a conclusion from the day.

**W032b · 2025-04-09 (Wed) · 11:20 · oxford_road · Day 2: voice memo on her question**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo, location, body  
Past Oxford without stopping, on the rise toward Wheatley, at a walk. "Is London where you work or where you live. I said both. It's the answer of a man who'd like to be asked something else." Hoofs, a lark. "A man may take the name of a thing for the thing. He's called a husband, so he thinks he is one. He's called a king…" A long gap. "That's not hers. That's the other business. Keep them apart." Then, quieter: "She asked for one answer. Go back with one." The ridge at Stokenchurch at four, leading the horse down; High Wycombe by six, 36 miles. At the Catherine Wheel, with rain on the yard, the old man's rhythm comes to him, this and this and this, a land counted over like a man's blessings; a line or two for Gaunt goes on the tables, and no more. A strap wanted mending at Stokenchurch and the bay had its own views on the hill; on the tables, "the horse governs my day more than several gentlemen who think they do."

**W032c · 2025-04-10 (Thu) · 05:30–15:00 · bull_inn · Day 3: home, and the coffer counted**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gib  
sources: location, body, notes (ledger)  
Thirty-four miles in, again by Kensington and Knightsbridge and not by the Oxford road's end. The bay goes back to the Bull at three: sixteen days at 12d, and the ostler finds a girth-gall to argue over. All told, horse, fodder and inns, about 38s. Mistress Ansell has aired the chamber and kept a tally of everything that happened in the parish, which she delivers on the stair. Gib is on the Holinshed, cloth and all. He unlocks both locks and counts: with the 4s he brings back, £19 6s. In the ledger the journey is cast up in four lines: horse, fodder and inns, 38s; carried home, 60s; for my father, 58s more; £7 16s out of the coffer since Lady Day. He is asleep by eight. Steps 5,600. Mistress Ansell has kept Heminges's note calling Friday's rehearsal where his papers cannot bury it, and wants the hired saddle-bag out of her passage tonight. He unpacks Anne's list before his own notes.

**S033 · 2025-04-11 (Fri) · 09:00 · the_theatre · Late-Lent rehearsal; a cue rewritten overnight**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_cowley, abel_garnish, richard_burbage, john_heminges, nick_sorrell, arthur_meeks, humphrey_larkham  
sources: meeting, notes, screen (part, plot), photo  
The first rehearsal on the big stage: the comedy for Easter Monday, with a yard three times the Cross Keys' to fill. Garnish has made a new plot on its pasteboard and hung it by the stage door. Everything is too small and too quick; Burbage finds the size by noon. Cowley, as Dull, misses the same entrance three times: his cue is a Latin tag he cannot hear from behind the arras. William takes the roll home. Overnight he rewrites the cue-line so that it ends on a plain English word a man could hear through oak, and alters the speech before it to fit. Garnish pastes the slip on in the morning: "You have mended the play to fit the player. It is against nature." "It's cheaper than a new player." Two men turn out to hold different versions of the same cue, and William, blaming their learning, finds his own correction on the wrong copy; Larkham will not recopy a line until it is settled which book is the book. Arthur's Lenten growth has split the waist-hooks of Rosaline's bodice, and William pencils in two jokes overnight about a lady of uncommon stature.

**W124 · 2025-04-12 (Sat) · 12:00 · ansell_house · Mistress Ansell's table: what Stratford said**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, gillis_verbeke  
sources: meeting, notes  
Lent herrings, and an examination. Did he give his wife her opinion of the forsworn gentlemen? He did. "And?" "She said the ladies were right to make them wait, and a twelvemonth was short." Mistress Ansell is silenced for the space of a herring; it is the first time anyone has outflanked her on severity. The loaf: a finger and a half short now. Verbeke asks after the country, whether the corn is up. William says it is up and thin. Alse wants to know if his children are like him. "The elder girl corrects me. The younger mocks me. The boy outruns me." "Then they're well," says Mistress Ansell, and takes his threepence.

**S034 · 2025-04-13 (Sun) · 09:00 · st_helens_church · Palm Sunday, raw**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, john_spencer, dorcas_ansell  
sources: calendar, location  
Palm Sunday in a raw east wind (invented weather). No palms in this church these thirty years; Master Thwaite preaches the entry into Jerusalem as a lesson in the fickleness of crowds, "who cry Hosanna on the Sunday," and looks at nobody. The Mayor's pew is full. William, in the south aisle with the Ansell household, has his mind on a king riding into London behind another man, and the dust thrown on his head; Holinshed has it. He writes nothing in church. The almanac says only "Palm Sunday. cold." In the street the apprentices have sprigs of sallow in their caps for palms, which is as near as the parish comes.

#### Week 16 · Mon 04-14 – Sun 04-20

**W033 · 2025-04-15 (Tue) · 11:00 · pauls_churchyard · Daniel's *Civil Wars* bought**  
people: william_shakespeare, michael_drayton  
sources: screen (book), location, notes (ledger), meeting  
Still on Waterson's board, where he has read in it standing four times since 11 March (M007): Samuel Daniel's *First Fowre Bookes of the Civile Wars* (T1 for the year; imprint to verify). Today he reads the second book standing, the same Richard and the same cousin in grave smooth stanzas, and gives in. He buys it, 2s, the dearest book since Golding. Michael Drayton is at the next board and sees what he has: "Sam's kings. You'll not find a laugh in four books, Will." A Warwickshire voice; they talk five minutes of home and of sonnets as a trade, each a little careful. In the ledger, "Daniel ij s." In the almanac it is Joan's day, with nothing sent. That night he reads Daniel against Holinshed, a finger in each.

**W034 · 2025-04-17 (Thu) · 05:30 · ansell_house · *Richard II* begun: Gaunt's first lines**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (draft), notes  
Maundy Thursday, first light, a clean sheet. He writes the head and then the King's opening address to his uncle, "Ould Iohn of Gaunt time honoured Lancaster", and gives the old man his first answer, four words: "I haue my Liege." He will play this part; he says the four words aloud several ways. By dinner the quarrel before the King is on the page, two men throwing down gages, and the King between them, graceful and wrong. Sixty lines, no blots. On the tables: "he likes to hear himself. let him." The composition date is this story's choice and never a record (T5 on T2).

**W035 · 2025-04-18 (Fri) · 15:00 · ansell_house · Good Friday: one quiet hour**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, calendar, memo  
Before church he reads a passage of the chronicle on the King's last days and writes one question beside it, on the difference between a thing done and the account that survives it. Church in the morning, the long gospel, the parish in black. In the afternoon the house is still and he does no work on the king. For one hour he opens the commonplace book at "Verona" and only looks. He adds two notes and no more. Against "how short can it be made": "dayes. fewer than a weeke. from a Sunday?" And under the link-boy's torch: "all of it at night, or hurrying toward it." No line of the play. Then he shuts it. The memo, at the window: "Three things on one board. A king, two houses, and Easter. Sit still."

**W125 · 2025-04-19 (Sat) · 16:00 · ansell_house · Call: Heminges, and a hoarse boy**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges  
sources: call  
"Will? John Heminges. From Aldermanbury, with a house full of boys." Sam Latchet has a cold in the head and croaks like a rook, and on Monday he is to sing the cuckoo to the biggest house of the spring. Phillips has him by the fire with honey and is keeping him dumb. Arthur Meeks is learning the owl in case, and doing it too well: "he sings it as if it were a judgment." William says the owl may be a judgment and the cuckoo cannot. They agree: Sam if he can, Arthur if he must, and no word to Sam that there is an if. Heminges asks if the king goes forward. "He's talking." "God keep you. Easter joy." "And you, John."

**S035 · 2025-04-20 (Sun) · 08:30 · st_helens_church · Easter communion by Gresham's tomb**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, john_spencer, dorcas_ansell, gillis_verbeke, thomas_morley  
sources: calendar, location, photo  
Easter Day, soft weather (invented).
