Every parishioner is looked for at the table (T2), and he goes up in his turn from the south aisle, past Sir Thomas Gresham's great plain tomb (T1), and kneels three places from a Flemish weaver and three pews from the Lord Mayor of London. Photo 17: the tomb in a shaft of sun, lilies on the ledge. Nothing is confessed in the journal; he takes the bread and the cup as the law and the day require, and is quiet afterwards. Mistress Ansell's Easter table has beef at last, a boiled piece, and a loaf she does not measure because it is Easter. Body: steps 5,200; resting 58; slept 7 h 40. Rosemary is strewn on the floor, and the table gathers neighbours of very different means into one order without dissolving any of the differences; Morley, the composer, kneels in the row behind. At Mistress Ansell's board Verbeke says what all of them think: the loaf she would not measure is smaller than Christmas's.

#### Week 17 · Mon 04-21 – Sun 04-27

**S036 · 2025-04-21 (Mon) · 08:40 · the_theatre · Easter Monday: both houses open**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, sam_latchet, margery_pell, james_burbage  
sources: screen (bill), calendar, body  
On the Bishopsgate post, two fresh bills: the Rose reopens with *The French Doctor* (T1; "easter mondaye", 53s) and the Lord Chamberlain's servants open the Theatre with the comedy. The flag with the white swan goes up at noon. Holiday crowds over the fields; James Burbage stands at the yard door counting them as timber. The stage is twice the breadth of the Cross Keys and the comedy has grown to fit. Sam sings the cuckoo, clear, and never knows there was an if. The hush at the messenger holds in a house of two thousand. Margery Pell: "'Prentices, sweethearts, six lawyers out of term and a midwife. Fifty-two shillings in mine." Steps 11,600; heart 126 at his entrance. The yard is full of holiday 'prentices cracking hazelnuts, and the trumpet sounds from the turret at two sharp.

**S038 · 2025-04-23 (Wed) · 06:10 · finsbury_fields · St George's Day: thirty-one, by custom**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo, calendar  
The almanac says only "St George". The Garter knights feast at Whitehall today (T1). He is walking early, conning an old duke for the afternoon. "Thirty-one. Or on Saturday, if the font's to be believed over my mother. She says St George. She was there." (Birthday T4; baptism 26 April, T1.) A breath. "At thirty-one my father was a chamberlain of the borough and had a house. I have a chamber with a cat in it and a king half-made." Then, briskly: "Enough. He banishes them in the next scene. Six years. No, ten, and then he gives four back, like a man tipping a waiter." He laughs without sound. Nobody has remembered the day, and he has told nobody. On the way back he thinks of buying something he does not need, and buys paper. "Marlowe was twenty-nine. Southwell thirty-three. I have three children, two poems in print and half a king." The memo laughs at the paper and then admits it was not entirely a joke.

**W126 · 2025-04-24 (Thu) · 16:50 · the_theatre · *Titus*; the house reported; the carrier's in**  
people: william_shakespeare, margery_pell, abel_garnish, hired_men, gilbert_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, calendar, body  
*Titus* to a full yard that likes its pie. He plays an old Roman and is dead by the fourth act; he watches the rest from the tiring-house door, as he will learn to do. Margery Pell's line: "Butchers' men in the yard, and they clapped the cook." Garnish complains the property hand has gone missing again and must be a bladder till Saturday. The almanac says "Carrier in at the Bell", but Raye has the baskets to shift and William is wanted for the reckoning of the hired men's wages, 10s apiece. He sends a boy to St Bride's: will Gilbert fetch what's come? The boy comes back with "He says aye, and you owe him a cup." Steps 10,800.

**W036 · 2025-04-25 (Fri) · 17:30 · the_jackdaw · Burbage hears the first Richard scene**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, agnes_tipping  
sources: meeting, screen (draft)  
Fish and eggs on the settle. He has brought four sheets: the quarrel before the King and the lists at Coventry, where Richard stops the fight with a dropped staff and banishes both men. He reads; Burbage's lips move. At the ten years made six in a breath, Burbage laughs out loud: "He does it because he *can*." Then the long speech of the sentence: "Give me the long one again. No, don't read it, *give* it to me." He takes the sheet and reads it himself, standing, to the fire, and Agnes Tipping stops with a jug in her hand. He hands it back. "He's pleased with every word he says. I know that man. Write me the rest." William eats his eggs cold. The fourth sheet has Mowbray's farewell, a man sent out of the only language he owns, "Within my mouth you haue engaold my tongue"; Burbage does not ask for that one, he asks who will play it.

**S037 · 2025-04-26 (Sat) · 08:45 · bishopsgate · The week's Rose bills, as a watch-history**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (bill), calendar  
His christening day in the almanac; he passes over it. He has read the Rose's bill every morning this week and gone to none of them, and a builder should set the week out as six items on the real calendar (T1 titles; the day-numbers in Henslowe's book run one ahead all week): Mon 21 *The French Doctor* · Tue 22 *A Knack to Know an Honest Man* · Wed 23 *The Grecian Comedy* · Thu 24 *The Wise Man of West Chester* · Fri 25 *The Wise Man* again · Sat 26 *Godfrey of Bulloigne*. Receipts, never known to him: 53s, 55s, not known, 58s, £3, 29s. On the tables: "the Wise Man two days running — they have a thing that draws." *Richard III* at the Theatre at two, and the week's reckoning after: 38s a share. Raye, who has seen *The Wise Man*, says it is wizard's disguises and Welsh jokes and holds two thousand in its hand. On the tables: "no fool in the king at all. then every line must hold."

**G033 · 2025-04-26 (Sat) · 17:00 · the_theatre · Kemp remembers**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, john_heminges, richard_burbage  
sources: meeting, photo, notes  
After *Richard III* and the reckoning (38s a share), Kemp, of all men, produces a gingerbread from his hat, somewhat crushed, and declares that William's age has had enough chances now to become official. William says the font's day is the one his family can stand upon, though his mother holds by St George. Heminges asks whether celebrating twice obliges the company twice. Burbage eats a corner off it before anyone has agreed. They talk over the afternoon's faults with their mouths full and leave the day pleasantly unimportant. Photo: the broken gingerbread on a property chest, four hands at it. On the tables, only: "W. K. — gingerbread."

**W037 · 2025-04-27 (Sun) · 12:30 · ansell_house · A birthday letter, four days late**  
people: william_shakespeare, gilbert_shakespeare  
sources: notes (letter), meeting  
Gilbert comes after church with the packet he fetched on Thursday and has carried three days in his other doublet. A letter meant for St George's Day. Anne: the frame they saw in Chapel Street is roofed; the money came safe; the bailiff's man has not been at the porch these two Sundays, "and your father says it is the weather." A line from each child in three hands, or one hand three ways. Susanna's is correct. Hamnet's reports a race won. Judith's is a drawing of a man on a stage with his arms out, labelled by her sister "Father talking". Gilbert reads it over his shoulder: "Is the porch your doing?" William rubs his forefinger. "It's the weather." He reads the letter again when Gilbert has gone.

### May

#### Week 18 · Mon 04-28 – Sun 05-04

**W127 · 2025-04-28 (Mon) · 12:15 · the_theatre · Call: Field, on a gap in a shelf**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field  
sources: call  
"Will? Richard Field. From my own shop, where there is a gap on the shelf." Six weeks. William says the Holinshed is in daily use and in excellent health. "It is in a playhouse man's chamber with a cat." "The cat reads only the second volume." Field lets that go and comes to the matter: Sunday, dinner, after sermon; Jacqueline has a hen. And bring the comedy, if the book-keeper will loose it, since he cannot in conscience come to hear it. "I'll bring it. Thou'lt read it with the door shut." "With the door shut. God keep thee." "And thee, Richard." *The Shrew* at two; he is not in it and goes home to the king.

**G034 · 2025-04-29 (Tue) · 08:00 · ansell_house · The king's account of himself**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), body  
At the board from first light on the scene where the King explains himself to men who do not share his account of it. He catches himself improving every voice into the same eloquence, the uncles as fine as the nephew. A second pass restores the differences: York's breath runs short, Gaunt's runs long, the King's never runs out. The sheet is cleaner at the end than at the start, and most of the morning has gone in removing his own convenience. On the tables: "not all of them may sing." Seated 07:40–10:30, heart 64; steps by noon 1,200.

**S039 · 2025-04-30 (Wed) · 12:40 · the_jackdaw · "The 31 of April"**  
people: william_shakespeare, hired_men, will_kemp, john_heminges, thomas_pope  
sources: meeting  
Dinner at Tipping's. Ralph Raye has been across the water to pay Henslowe twelve pence on his debt and watched it written down. "He has yesterday's play set against the thirtieth. I saw the page. By Master Henslowe's book, today is the thirty-first of April." (T1: the label "ye 31 of aprell" is real; that the company saw the book is invented.) Kemp wants to be paid for the extra day. Pope proposes they play twice on it. Heminges, who has never misdated a line in his life, is quietly scandalised and asks whether the sums at least were right. "To the farthing." "Then he knows what matters," William says. "A man may lose a day. Nobody loses a shilling by mistake." It becomes the company's word for never: "on the thirty-first of April." Kemp offers to sell Henslowe a thirty-second of May for sixpence.

**S040 · 2025-05-01 (Thu) · 06:30 · bishopsgate · May Day**  
people: william_shakespeare, alse_pinder, dorcas_ansell, will_kemp  
sources: photo, screen (bill), calendar  
Alse was out before four with the parish girls and is back with wet shoes and hawthorn; Mistress Ansell allows one bough over the door and no more. Photo 18: a maypole in a suburb lane beyond the gate, ribbons, girls with green boughs. The Rose's bill is *Long Meg* again (T1; 50s on the holiday). The Theatre plays the comedy to a May Day house in its best clothes, and the hush still comes. Walking up in the morning he has the Duchess of Gloucester's scene in his head, an old woman asking an old man for revenge and getting patience; he will play the old man. On the tables: "God's is the quarrel." At the gate the girls have hung hawthorn and cowslips over the arch. Kemp, met on the way up, improves a child's attempt at a dance and is at once asked to improve it again. William's kept image is a garland carried carefully through the dirt of the road.

**G035 · 2025-05-03 (Sat) · 17:00 · the_theatre · The seam at the elbow**  
people: william_shakespeare, edith_kettlewood, richard_burbage, sam_latchet  
sources: meeting, photo, notes  
Burbage has split a seam at the elbow in a gesture he considers essential to Richard of Gloucester. Edith asks him to make it again, slowly, and shows him with her two hands how much cloth it takes. Sam watches a principal player corrected in material terms with deep satisfaction. William alters an entrance while they argue, and Edith warns him that changing a direction is cheaper than changing a sleeve only if somebody tells her before the cutting. Burbage makes the gesture a third time, smaller, and finds he likes it better. Photo: Edith's hands on the sleeve, pins in her mouth.

**W038 · 2025-05-03 (Sat) · 19:00 · ansell_house · Edmund is fifteen: a letter**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes (letter, ledger), calendar  
Edmund's day in the almanac. After the reckoning (34s a share) he writes to the youngest brother, one page. Finish school. Latin is the only property a player never has to hire. If, at seventeen, he still wants to hear how Master Burbage dies, he may come and see it for a penny like a Christian. He encloses nothing but adds that the sword's first owner sends his commendations to its second. The court went down to Greenwich today (T1), and with it my lord and Kettleby and any chance of a summons before Michaelmas. He counts the coffer. The letter will go by the carrier of the 15th.

**W039 · 2025-05-04 (Sun) · 12:00 · fields_printing_house · Dinner at the Fields'**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field, jacqueline_field  
sources: meeting, location  
After sermon, over Paul's and down to Blackfriars with the comedy under his cloak. A hen in a French pot, the presses still, the apprentices at their catechism below. Field says grace long and carves short. He has a great book coming off in sheets, he says, the Lives out of Plutarch again, and William must come and see it hang. Jacqueline has read ahead in the comedy between courses and has a complaint about the Princess's people: "*Non, non.* You make them speak French as an Englishman dreams it." She mends two words on the leaf with her own pen. Field: "Thou'rt wasted on players. I say it as thy friend." Then, without a pause: "Leave the book. I'll read it with the door shut." The home vowels are back in William's mouth by the cheese.

#### Week 19 · Mon 05-05 – Sun 05-11

**S042 · 2025-05-05 (Mon) · 05:20 · finsbury_fields · Lines on a morning walk**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: body, memo, screen (bill)  
Sunrise before half past four now; he is in the fields by five with an old duke's forty lines for Thursday, walking them into his legs. The memo catches the method: a cue-word, the speech at a mutter, a stumble, the cue again. Then the speech goes right and he says, "There," to a hedge. Skylarks; archers not yet out. On the way back the post has the Rose's bill, *A Knack to Know an Honest Man* (T1), which the Theatre answers this week with its own old *Knave*. Steps by eight o'clock 6,100; for the day 12,300. Walking heart 102. Between cues he tries how a king come home from sea might stoop and touch the earth with his bare hand, and what his lords would do with their faces.

**S043 · 2025-05-07 (Wed) · 17:00 · the_theatre · Word of *Hercules***  
people: william_shakespeare, margery_pell, hired_men, richard_burbage, james_burbage  
sources: screen (bill), meeting, calendar  
Easter term opens today and the gowns are back in the galleries. But the yard was thin, and Margery Pell knows why: "They're all over the water. There's a new play with a giant in it." The Rose's bill is *The First Part of Hercules* (T1; new, £3 13s, the season's best there). Raye saw the end of it: Alleyn in a lion's skin with a club like a ship's mast, and monsters. Burbage wants to know how tall the monsters were. James Burbage wants to know what the timber cost. William feels plain envy, and puts it in the commonplace book under *Of Kings*: "They have Hercules. I have a man who talks. Make the talking the labour." Easter term opened today and runs to 2 June (T2). Burbage scowls into his cup at the lion-skin.

**S041 · 2025-05-08 (Thu) · 08:40 · bishopsgate · May on the Rose's bills**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (bill), notes  
On the post this morning: *The Venetian Comedy* at the Rose (T1; 30s, never known to him). A builder may set the month's Rose bills out as a watch-history he reads and does not attend (T1 titles, the source book's dating): *Seleo and Olympo*, *The French Doctor*, *A Knack to Know an Honest Man*, *The Venetian Comedy* (8th), *The French Comedy*, *Long Meg*, *Tasso's Melancholy*, *The Grecian Comedy*, *Godfrey of Bulloigne*, both *Tamburlaines*, *1 Hercules* (new on the 7th) and *2 Hercules* (new on the 23rd). He reads the Rose's bill first, as always. On the tables, under the day's list: "they have Venice now, and Hercules." He goes to none of them in May except *Hercules* on the 20th.

**W040 · 2025-05-08 (Thu) · 16:40 · the_theatre · Phillips's jig of the slippers**  
people: william_shakespeare, augustine_phillips, will_kemp, sam_latchet, arthur_meeks  
sources: meeting, screen (stage), calendar, photo  
After *Fair Em* the promise of 15 March is kept: Phillips's new jig, the one of the slippers (T2; licensed this month by a secondary report). Kemp dances a cobbler, Arthur a scold in a borrowed kirtle, Sam a prentice who steals the shoes; Phillips leads on the cittern with a tabor boy. The whole stage is Kemp's for a quarter of an hour and nobody's lines trouble him. The yard will not let him off. William watches from the tiring-house door: a man who cannot keep to eleven set lines keeps time to a hair for two hundred bars. He says so to Phillips. "He'll keep anything he made himself." Kemp, passing, wet through: "First on the paper, and first in the slippers." Kemp's slippers are yellow, Turkish and enormous, and Phillips drives the cittern at a breakneck measure. Photo: the slippers propped beside the cittern's carved head at the stage edge.

**W128 · 2025-05-09 (Fri) · 04:40 · ansell_house · "This England"**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell  
sources: screen (draft), memo, notes  
Not cast; at the board from first light. Gaunt is dying at Ely House and has one speech left to spend. It comes in a single run of some twenty lines, each "this" laid on the last: "This royall throne of Kings, this sceptred Ile," down to "this Realme, this England", and then the turn, the land leased out like a tenement, "bound in with shame, / With inkie blots, and rotten parchment bonds". He walks it, saying it, because he will have to breathe it in October. Mistress Ansell at dinner: "That was no comedy. You walked like a man following a hearse, and then you stood." "He was dying." "Who?" "I am. In the second act." She considers that the most sensible thing he has yet done in a play. The memo: "Leased out. That's the Burbages' word. Keep it."

**W041 · 2025-05-10 (Sat) · 18:10 · ansell_house · Call: Cuthbert on Giles Allen and the lease**  
people: william_shakespeare, cuthbert_burbage  
sources: call  
"Master Shakespeare? Cuthbert Burbage. From Holywell." Allen rode in from Essex today, and Cuthbert has been with him about the ground: two years less a month till the lease falls in (T1; 13 April 1597). Allen was civil and named his figure, £24 a year where they pay £14, for a short extension, with a clause about what may be done with the house after five years that he would not explain. "My father says he'll pull the house down. I say, let's first ask what the timber's worth." He wants the sharers to know before James tells it his own way on Monday, and he wants the poet in particular to keep his father from cursing Allen to his face. William asks what a new play is worth to a landlord. "Nothing. To my father, a year." "Then I'll tell him I've a king coming." "Tell him it's long. God keep you." "And you."

**G036 · 2025-05-11 (Sun) · 16:00 · ansell_house · The use of a clean sheet**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes (letter), screen (letter)  
He writes home on a clean sheet and resists filling the generous space with a defence of his trade. He answers the house's questions in the order Anne asked them. At the bottom he adds the story of Burbage's sleeve, because Anne will understand the seamstress's part of it better than the player's. He leaves room at the foot for her answer on the one matter that needs one: whether the Sadlers' cousin still means to build his barn this summer. It goes by Thursday's carrier with the thimble (W130).

#### Week 20 · Mon 05-12 – Sun 05-18

**S045 · 2025-05-13 (Tue) · 19:00 · ansell_house · A year since the first fire**  
people: william_shakespeare, gilbert_shakespeare  
sources: call, memo, notes (letter), screen (draft)  
The day is the writing day tabled in § 7: Act 3 at the board from five, "sit — ground — stories" on the tables, Egeon conned at noon, *Two Gents* watched from the tiring-house door. At seven: "Brother? Gilbert. From St Bride's, and back since Saturday." He has been down to Stratford on the shop's business: the buckets and leather hooks hang filled by every well in Henley Street as the bailiff ordered, Anne's beans are a span high, Hamnet won twopence at the butts with blunt arrows, and their mother would know whether William eats. Hobbins is in on Thursday and Gilbert cannot leave the shop, so will William fetch what's come; and the girl's day is the twenty-sixth. "I'd forgot it." "So had I, till the almanac told me." "Then send the almanac. God keep you." A full moon tonight, and a year ago today the middle of Stratford burned (T1 annal); the memo at the window: "I was here. I heard it nine days after, from a carter, and it was all out and counted by then. A man can't run a hundred miles to a fire." He writes to Anne to ask whether the frame in Chapel Street is truly finished and what the plasterer asked, because "rebuilt" can hide a deal of work still owing, and then thirty more lines for the king before bed.

**S046 · 2025-05-15 (Thu) · 10:20 · fields_printing_house · The Plutarch on the line**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field  
sources: screen (proof), photo, location, meeting  
The scene is the specimen in § 8. The press-room is hung with damp sheets of North's Plutarch (T1; the month is unchecked). Photo 19: the sheets like washing, a pressman's arm on the bar. He reads a forme of the Life of Julius Caesar where it hangs: the soothsayer, the Ides. A turned letter is ringed in brown ink; Field's inky thumb taps the ring. "Fourteen shillings to you, unbound, and my Holinshed home by Whitsun." William pays the fourteen and promises nothing about Whitsun. Field, wiping his hands: "It's market day at home. I never pass a Thursday but I know it." No play is drafted from the Caesar life this year; he only reads, and carries the sheets off flat under his arm. The next sheet on the line is the Life of Theseus, with an Amazon queen in it, and he reads that standing too. He follows one correction from type to margin and asks what becomes of an error once enough copies have escaped; Field, checking register with a bodkin: "It acquires company."

**W130 · 2025-05-15 (Thu) · 11:30 · the_bell_carter_lane · Carrier day: the thimble goes ahead**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins  
sources: location, notes (letter, ledger), body  
Three minutes from Field's door. He has a silver thimble, bought on Monday for 2s 6d, sealed in a paper marked for Anne: "for Susanna, not before the Monday after Rogation Sunday." He has learned from the groats. With it go Edmund's letter and Gilbert's parcel. Hobbins hands up a short one from Anne: Hamnet has outrun the Sadler boy to the bridge and back, and describes in his own line a water-wheel he has built in the gutter; Judith, in hers, disputes what it is called and has a second ballad; and "Susanna has found a fault in your last and will tell you of it herself." Then he walks the whole City north in forty minutes to speak Egeon at two. Steps for the day 14,100.

**G037 · 2025-05-16 (Fri) · 09:00 · the_theatre · Let the other man finish**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, thomas_pope  
sources: meeting, notes, body  
A rehearsal of *Richard III* for Saturday. Burbage begins the King's answer before Pope's Buckingham has fully landed his speech. William asks him to wait, and Burbage waits so theatrically that the pause becomes another way of commanding the stage. They try again without announcing anything. For a moment the King hears something he did not expect, and the empty yard changes more than it did under the louder reading. On the tables: "a king who listens frights more than one who shouts. use it." Steps 9,000.

**S047 · 2025-05-17 (Sat) · 10:00 · pauls_churchyard · Southwell's book**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (book), notes, location, body, photo  
He walks to Paul's to check Daniel's third book against a better copy. On a stall, plain, thin and new: *Saint Peters Complaint, with other Poemes*, 1595, no author named and no one in doubt (T1). He reads the epistle standing, "The Author to his loving Cosen": poets, by making the follies and feignings of love their customary subject, have so discredited the faculty "that a Poet, a Lover, and a Liar, are by many reckoned but three wordes of one signification." Then the verses to the reader, on finest wits distilling Venus' rose; he is the author of a *Venus*. He reads until the stallman's elbow moves him aside, buys it for 6d without a word, and carries it home inside his doublet. No kinship is claimed or thought. At home he copies one short passage into the commonplace book with a rule under it, to keep the author's words apart from his own uneasy answer, and leaves the answer blank. Steps 6,800; that night, the hollow crown (W042).

**W042 · 2025-05-17 (Sat) · 21:30–01:10 · ansell_house · The hollow crown, by one candle**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (draft), photo, body, memo  
After the reckoning (40s, the spring's best) and supper, the king lands in Wales and learns he has lost everything in an afternoon. The speech that has been three words on the tables since Tuesday comes whole: "For Gods sake let vs sit vpon the ground, / And tell sad stories of the death of Kings". Then Death keeping his court within the hollow crown, and the little pin. He does not walk it. He sits. Then on, without stopping, to the castle wall at Flint and a king coming down. Photo 20: one candle, a page three-quarters written, the glover's knife, a guttered second candle beside it. Body: seated at the desk at 23:40, heart 84; bed 01:10; slept 3 h 50; steps 3,300. The memo, at one: "I'll not write better this year. …Don't say that. Sleep."

**G038 · 2025-05-18 (Sun) · 17:30 · split_hazel · Nashe spends the table**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_nashe, will_kemp, alice_brambleby  
sources: meeting, notes  
The second of the year's three Nashe suppers. He arrives at the Split Hazel with a grievance that has acquired several magnificent descriptions on the walk from Paul's. Kemp contests one image and improves another, and Nashe, delighted, steals the improvement back. William enjoys himself and realises at the end that he has scarcely spoken except to keep it going. Alice Brambleby ends the evening's most elaborate argument by setting down the actual bill, 2s 8d and the jug, which nobody can dispute and Nashe, who is broke, admires. Contact T3; the supper T5.

#### Week 21 · Mon 05-19 – Sun 05-25

**G039 · 2025-05-20 (Tue) · 08:00 · ansell_house · No one says "king" the same way**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), body  
Before *Hercules*, a morning's revision of the Welsh scenes, tracking what each man means when he uses the King's title. In one mouth it is service, in another resistance, in a third habit and in a fourth hope. He writes in the margin: "not every king a crown. some are only a cough." The morning ends with less new matter than he wanted and a scene that can now move. Seated 07:50–10:40; steps 1,500 before he walks to the Bridge.

**W043 · 2025-05-20 (Tue) · 13:00–17:30 · the_rose · *Hercules*; Master Henslowe at the bridge foot**  
people: william_shakespeare, philip_henslowe, edward_alleyn  
sources: screen (stage), photo, location, body  
Not cast in *The Shrew*; over the Bridge. Photo 21: the Rose from the river, the flag up, wherries thick at the stairs. *The First Part of Hercules* again (T1; £3 9s). It is a lost play, so only this: Alleyn in the lion's skin, taller than the door he comes through, a voice that fills the house like water; a labour, then a bigger labour; the yard counting them. He stands by a post on the right and enjoys it against his will. At the bridge foot afterwards Henslowe is at a stall counting something else and knows him. Civil, appraising: "You've a pretty wit, Master Shakespeare. Does it pay?" "By the share." "Ah. I pay by the play. Six pound, and I never argue the jig." All the way home he thinks what can be set against a giant, and comes to a looking-glass. Steps 12,600. It is the unpleasant pleasure of watching a rival do a thing well. His first private remark is that they have spent heavily; his second, walking, is that the money was not wasted.

**W044 · 2025-05-22 (Thu) · 17:20 · st_helens_church · Six words at the church door**  
people: william_shakespeare, the_lady_unnamed  
sources: calendar, memo, location  
He has played the Ghost in the old *Hamlet* and is walking home with flour still in his hair. A christening party is coming out at St Helen's porch, gossips with a cake-basket, a nurse, the child roaring. She is the last down the step, in dark green, and the latch has caught her sleeve. He frees it; her hand rests on his one moment for balance, gloved. She looks at the ink on his finger and then at him. "You are the one that writes." Six words; a low laugh in them. He says, "Mistress," and nothing more that he can afterwards remember. She goes after the others. The memo is hours later and short: "She knew me by my hand. …Not safe." A small cross in the almanac. She has no name in the journal, here or anywhere.

**W131 · 2025-05-24 (Sat) · 17:30 · the_jackdaw · A looking-glass against a giant**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, john_heminges, abel_garnish, agnes_tipping  
sources: meeting, notes  
After *Errors* and a reckoning of 36s, on the settle. The Rose had a second *Hercules* new yesterday (T1) and Heminges is gloomy about giants. William tells Burbage what he came to on the Bridge. "They've a god in a lion's skin. You shall have a looking-glass." "A what?" "He's given up the crown. He sends for a glass to see what a face looks like that's no king. He looks. Then he breaks it." Burbage is still for a moment, and his hand closes as on a handle. "Give it me. When?" "When I've written how he gets there." Garnish, from the next table, enters his week's total: "Kemp's interest, six days: nineteen lines, and a noise like a goose." Heminges: "A glass is four shillings, and he'll break one every time."

**W045 · 2025-05-25 (Sun) · 15:00 · st_helens_church · Rogation Sunday: the bounds**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, dorcas_ansell, gillis_verbeke, alse_pinder  
sources: photo, calendar, location, body  
Rogation Sunday. The churchwardens, being men of business, walk the parish on the Sunday afternoon and spare the working days (this parish's custom is invented). Master Thwaite goes before with the book; the boys carry willow wands and beat every mark: a post in Bishopsgate Street, the corner by Crosby Place, a stone in the garden wall. Photo 22: boys with wands at a wall, the curate reading. At each stop a psalm-verse and a prayer for the fruits of the earth, which sounds different this year. Verbeke walks with the household and is shown each mark by Alse as if he might be asked. It is a small parish: forty minutes, and bread and ale after. Steps 8,900. He listens to the names the marks go by and how they keep old uses. Over the bread and ale the talk is of a precept coming from Crosby Place against blue starch, and a neighbour says that with wheat where it is the Mayor might better look to the meal-tubs than the laundry-tubs. Nobody here knows that a battle is being fought in Ireland today.

#### Week 22 · Mon 05-26 – Sun 06-01

**S049 · 2025-05-26 (Mon) · 18:30 · ansell_house · Susanna is twelve**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes (letter), calendar  
"Susanna, xij" in the almanac (T1 baptism, 26 May 1583). The thimble is there already, if Anne has kept it back as bidden. Tonight he writes the letter that should have gone with it and will go on 5 June. To a girl of twelve who reads: he hears she has found a fault in his last and looks to be told it plainly; he has been corrected by the Master of the Revels and it did him good. He tells her there is a queen in the play he is making who learns bad news by listening to gardeners, and asks whether that is likely. He sends his love to her sister and brother in the same line, by name. To Anne, on the same sheet: the king is two-thirds made. He played Navarre this afternoon with the day in his mind and came in a line late. He asks her one question he truly wants answered: if the house could buy only one thing this summer, which should wait? He nearly writes his own answer underneath, and crosses it out, and promises to hear her read when he comes in August.

**W132 · 2025-05-27 (Tue) · 09:00 · the_theatre · A bad rehearsal**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_cowley, john_sincler, abel_garnish, thomas_pope, john_heminges, hired_men  
sources: meeting, screen (part)  
The old *Knave*, pulled out for Friday and not played since February. Nobody has it. Cowley dries in the same place four times and each time apologises to a different man. Kemp is not come, being at his slippers. The tireman has padded Pope to a barrel and has nothing for Sincler, who stands in a doublet that fits a rake: "He needs none," says Pope, "he is the before, and I'm the after." Garnish stops prompting and simply reads the play aloud while they walk through it. Heminges looks at the sun. William, with eight lines as a justice, gets all eight right and is thanked, sarcastically, by the company. They break at eleven no better. The Rose has *Hercules* again.

**G041 · 2025-05-28 (Wed) · 10:00 · the_theatre · A measure at the stage door**  
people: william_shakespeare, george_bryan  
sources: meeting, notes, calendar  
Bryan stops at the stage door on his way west with a question from a great household where he has friends: eleven lines of the comedy have been asked for in a fair copy, and which version is current? William begins to explain the two readings and the reasons for each. Then he sees that Bryan needs a choice, not an essay. They choose one and mark the sheet with a cross. Bryan is gone before William can add a second improvement that would undo the certainty.

**W046 · 2025-05-28 (Wed) · 18:00 · ansell_house · The Lord Mayor forbids blue starch**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder  
sources: meeting, screen (pamphlet)  
The parish clerk has been round with a precept from Crosby Place, to be read in church on Sunday next: no blue starch in ruffs (T1). Mistress Ansell has it by heart before supper and, for once, is of the Mayor's mind: "Starch is good wheat boiled to vanity, and blue is vanity dyed." Alse owns one band starched blue, bought at Whitsun last, and sits very still. William asks whether the Mayor has now found the root of the dearth, and that it is in our collars. "You may laugh. He is a hard man and he is right about ruffs." Alse is allowed to wash it white. He writes in the commonplace book, under *Prices*: "blew starch: forbid. the loaf: ij fingers."

**S050 · 2025-05-29 (Thu) · 11:00 · leadenhall · Ascension Day: cheese, and thin wheat**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, calendar, location, photo  
Ascension, a new moon, church early. At Leadenhall he buys a pound of cheese, 2d, where 1½d was the old price, and stands it against butter at 4d and rising. Walking up to the Theatre by the field path he looks at the corn-ground north of Hog Lane: the wheat is up and sparse, short for the end of May, after a cool dry month (invented weather; the thin harvest is T2). A man hoeing does not look round. In the commonplace book: "Ascension. cheese ij d. the li. Wheat like an old man's beard." Then, on the next line, without a heading: "a Queen in a garden, & the gardener binding up apricocks. he knows more than she." At the assize beam he looks hard at the penny loaf, hardly bigger than a fist; photo: the loaf in his hand beside the scale. Two weavers' prentices behind him mutter that the strangers and the forestallers eat white manchet while Englishmen chew bran.

**W047 · 2025-05-31 (Sat) · 10:30 · pauls_churchyard · The *True Tragedy* on a stall, nameless**  
people: william_shakespeare, michael_drayton  
sources: screen (book), location, memo  
On a board in the churchyard, new, small, cheap: *The True Tragedie of Richard Duke of Yorke*, as acted by the Earl of Pembroke his servants, printed for Thomas Millington, 1595 (T1; title wording to verify). His York-and-Lancaster play, with another company's name on the front and no author's. He opens it standing and it falls at the line Greene once threw at him, "Oh Tygers hart wrapt in a womans hide". He reads two pages: his, mostly, with holes. Drayton, at his elbow again: "Is that yours?" "It was." "Did they pay?" "They paid somebody." He does not buy it. The memo on the way to the Theatre: "My name on two poems and a tax. Not on a play. …Whose are they, then? The stationer's. The clown's. The boy's who says them." The Rose's bill for today is marked paid in Henslowe's book: the Revels' fee (T1).

**W133 · 2025-06-01 (Sun) · 09:00 · st_helens_church · The precept read, with feeling**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, john_spencer, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder  
sources: calendar, meeting, memo  
"Upon Sunday next" is today. Master Thwaite reads the Lord Mayor's precept against blue starch from the pulpit, with the Lord Mayor ten yards off in a ruff as white as a christening. He reads it with real feeling, and then preaches on it. The glass is turned once, and again. Vanity in Isaiah, vanity in the Epistles, the daughters of Zion and their round tires like the moon. "And *fourteenthly*…" Alse's band is white. A draper's wife in the north aisle is distinctly blue and looks at the roof. William counts heads, pews, the Mayor's unmoving neck. Walking out, Mistress Ansell: "Sound doctrine." "Fourteen parts of it." "You counted. That is the first sermon you ever heard to the end." Outside, Alse asks who is to decide where a permitted shade ends and an offence begins, and nobody can tell her. The memo on the forty paces home is a stifled laugh: "A city going hungry, and its magistrate at war with a colour. Keep it for a constable."

---

### June

#### Week 23 · Mon 06-02 – Sun 06-08

**S051 · 2025-06-02 (Mon) · 17:00 · the_theatre · Sharers map Whitsun to Midsummer**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, richard_burbage, will_kemp, augustine_phillips, george_bryan  
sources: meeting, calendar  
Easter term ends today and the gowns go; the almanac lies open on a property-chest. Whitsun is Sunday: Heminges wants the comedy for the Monday holiday and gets it. Then three weeks to Midsummer on the old stock, and the question of the autumn. "The— the people liked the *Labour's Lost*. Give me a *Labour's Won* for Michaelmas, Will, with the same four and the weddings in it." William says he has a king. "How near?" "He's about to be unkinged." Burbage: "Michaelmas, then, when the Inns are back to hear it." Bryan asks, carefully, whether a play of a king put down will pass the Master. "It's in the chronicle." Kemp asks what part he has in the king. William says the parts are not cast. He rubs his forefinger against his thumb. Heminges asks, too, how much of the summer's takings may be kept back rather than shared if playing grows uncertain; nobody likes the question and everybody agrees with it. Trinity term opens on the 20th, eighteen days off.

**S053 · 2025-06-03 (Tue) · 11:30 · leadenhall · Murmuring in the meal-market**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: location, notes  
The talk at Leadenhall is that the Council has spoken in Star Chamber of "the present scarcity" and is sending the country justices home to their shires to keep hospitality (as the market tells it; unverified). He buys nothing he did not mean to. Beadles stand about watching knots of idle silk-weavers and men in old soldiers' coats who have nowhere to be at noon. He writes three proverbs heard round the meal-sacks into the commonplace book under *Prices*: "A hungry man, an angry man." "Hunger breaks stone walls." "The loaf goes by the Mayor's weight and the belly by God's." Steps 5,800.

**G043 · 2025-06-04 (Wed) · 08:00 · ansell_house · A whole king, not yet a whole book**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), body  
He reads the king through from the first gage to the castle wall, enough to feel the whole movement instead of the speeches that went well. Several of the joins are still only marks and reminders: "here a messenger", "here York, angry". He finds a passage that has been carrying the wrong weight because it was pleasant to write, and draws a line down its margin. The morning's work is not finishing anything but knowing exactly what is unfinished: the deposing, the prison, the end. Seated 07:30–10:50, heart 62; dinner at the Jackdaw after.

**S052 · 2025-06-04 (Wed) · 12:30 · the_jackdaw · The throne in the heavens**  
people: william_shakespeare, hired_men, james_burbage, richard_burbage, thomas_pope  
sources: meeting, screen (bill)  
Raye has the news from the Rose with his dinner. Henslowe has paid his carpenters £7 2s for making a throne in the heavens (T1): a chair that comes down out of the roof with a god in it. And yesterday a new play, *The Seven Days of the Week* (T1; £3 10s), which the whole Bankside is running to. James Burbage, joiner, explains with a knife and a crust exactly how it is rigged, what it should have cost, and where it will jam. Pope proposes they lower Kemp. Burbage looks at William. "A glass," William says. "Four shillings. And it comes down by itself." James: "I could build you a heavens for five pound, if I had five pound and a lease." Kemp wants to know why they cannot winch him down on a barrel. William says a boy and two men with clean verse can build a castle out of air, and it costs nothing to strike.

**W134 · 2025-06-05 (Thu) · 12:50 · the_theatre · Call: Field, on an octavo and a Sunday**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field  
sources: call, calendar, location  
"Will? Richard Field. From the shop." He has had Millington's little book in his hand. "I hear thou sawest it. Short's work; honest presswork on a dishonest copy. Come Sunday and be angry at my table and not in Paul's." William says he was not angry; he was curious whose it was. "The man's who entered it. That's the trade. If thou'dst print thy plays thyself…" "They're not mine to print. They're the company's." "Then whose is the complaint? Sunday. And the Holinshed." "Sunday. Not the Holinshed." After the play he walks to the Bell with Susanna's letter. Hobbins is in and has nothing for him this time.

**S054 · 2025-06-06 (Fri) · 08:40 · bedlam · The Bedlam gate standing open**  
people: william_shakespeare, gillis_verbeke, margery_pell, richard_cowley, humphrey_larkham  
sources: photo, location, meeting, body  
On his ordinary walk north, just through the gate, shouting ahead and then feet. He sees the end of it only. Photo 23: the Bedlam gate standing open, a dropped cap in the road, backs running north toward Norton Folgate. Apprentices have fetched a silkweaver out of the hospital (T1/T2). A water-bearer says the man was put in by the Lord Mayor for words spoken at his door about the dearth; that part is street hearsay and unverified. William stands against a wall until the road is empty; heart 104 standing still. At the Theatre Margery Pell has it already, with numbers. At supper Verbeke has it too, from the looms: a silkweaver, like himself, and English. "So it is not yet us," he says, and cuts his bread smaller. At the Theatre Cowley has one account and Larkham another; they agree only that people were running toward the noise, and Margery Pell's figure is two hundred. When someone proposes going down to see which story is true, William will not. Rehearsal begins with an unusual attention to the sounds outside the house.

**W048 · 2025-06-08 (Sun) · 09:00 · st_helens_church · Whitsunday**  
people: william_shakespeare, the_lady_unnamed, john_spencer, josias_thwaite, dorcas_ansell  
sources: calendar, location, memo  
Whitsunday; rushes on the floor, the church full. Master Thwaite preaches the gift of tongues, keeps to one glass, and then reads, by order and after Friday, a portion of the Homily against Disobedience and Wilful Rebellion, which William hears out carefully with a king half-unmade at home. The Mayor's pew has two more servants at the door of it than at Easter. In the north aisle, among her household, a dark head in a small ruff, very white. She does not turn. During the second lesson she does, once, toward the south side, and away. He has lost the place in the psalm. Nothing is said, and they do not meet at the door; she is gone by the north porch. In the almanac that night a small cross. The memo is one breath: "Whitsun. She looked, or she looked at the window. Leave it."

**W135 · 2025-06-08 (Sun) · 12:30 · fields_printing_house · Whitsun dinner: whose is a play**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field, jacqueline_field  
sources: meeting, location  
Lamb, the first peas, the shop shut. Field has the comedy to give back, read with the door shut, and a verdict he delivers in two parts. It is wanton. It is also the best-made thing he has read that was not in Latin, and if William will give him the copy he will print it as it should be printed. William says the copy is Garnish's and the company's. "Then one day a Millington will have it from a hired man for forty pence." Jacqueline: "He is right, and you are right, and it will happen." They talk of home after that: the thin corn, whether the bailiff will stint the maltsters. Jacqueline asks if the streets are quiet by Bishopsgate. He says quiet enough, and tells them nothing of Friday.

#### Week 24 · Mon 06-09 – Sun 06-15

**S056 · 2025-06-09 (Mon) · 14:00 · the_theatre · Whit Monday**  
people: william_shakespeare, margery_pell, will_kemp, richard_burbage, abel_garnish  
sources: calendar, screen (bill), body, meeting  
Holiday. The Rose's bill is *A Knack to Know an Honest Man* (T1; 55s). The Theatre plays the comedy to the biggest yard since Easter Monday, and it is a different yard: more apprentices than sweethearts, loud before the play and during it. Kemp feels it and feeds it; Garnish stops counting at fifteen. The hush at the messenger comes late and breaks early. Afterwards Margery Pell empties her box without her usual line. Asked, she says: "'Prentices thick as flies, and no good humour in them. They laughed like men who mean to laugh at something else after." Burbage shrugs it off. William does not. Heart 131; steps 11,200. After the comedy, Kemp's jig of the slippers, and the yard will not let him go.

**S094 · 2025-06-10 (Tue) · 03:50–11:00 · ansell_house · The deposing**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder  
sources: notes, screen (draft)  
Not cast. Light before four; he is at the board with Holinshed shut, because the chronicle only says it was done and he must say how. Westminster Hall; the cousin in the chair's shadow; the King sent for to give it up with his own mouth. It takes seven hours and three sheets. The crown held between two hands like a well with two buckets. Then the unmaking, item by item, as Heminges would: "With mine owne Teares I wash away my Balme, / With mine owne Hands I giue away my Crowne". Then the glass, sent for, looked in, broken. He does not walk once. At eleven Alse comes up, sent: "The mistress says you were so still she feared you were dead." "Somebody is. Tell her I'm well." Canon places seed S094 on this day; the scene exists from today (T5 on T2/T3). The text is the 1608 quarto's or the Folio's.

**G044 · 2025-06-12 (Thu) · 09:00 · the_theatre · No more credit**  
people: william_shakespeare, edith_kettlewood, john_heminges, will_kemp  
sources: meeting, notes  
Before the news from Billingsgate comes up the road, Edith Kettlewood quotes for new work, a cloak and two sleeves for the autumn, and names her terms: payment on delivery, no more accounts. William begins to describe the uncertainty of the season; Kemp interrupts to say that the uncertainty is exactly her point. Heminges accepts the terms and cuts the order to what the stock can pay now, the sleeves and not the cloak, 6s. Edith: "Pay me in coin, Master Heminges, and you may pay the weather in promises." The costume will be less fine and the dealing more honest. By dinner William is home and keeping the house.

**S058 · 2025-06-12 (Thu) · 16:00–21:00 · ansell_house · Fish, and the cry against strangers**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gillis_verbeke, alse_pinder  
sources: meeting, memo, location  
Word comes up from the river by dinner: apprentices at Billingsgate have made the fishwives sell at their own price (T1/T2), and by afternoon it is not fish any longer. It is strangers, Dutch and French, a cry going from street to street (T1). Verbeke comes home early by the back wall with his cap in his hand. Mistress Ansell acts before anyone speaks. He is put in the lumber garret over William's chamber with a jug and a pot. She makes the household swear, William first, hand on her Bible: if any ask, the back room is let to a man from Norwich. "From Norwich. Say it." "From Norwich." Nobody comes. Late, through the ceiling, Verbeke's careful voice: "In Ghent also they burned the houses of the wrong people. Always it is bread, and then it is us." William is not cast today or tomorrow and keeps the house.

**S059a · 2025-06-13 (Fri) · 17:00 · ansell_house · Voice memo: butter at threepence**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo, body, location  
He has kept north of the river all day; the news crosses to him. At Southwark market this morning some three hundred apprentices took the butter from the sellers, who asked fivepence the pound, and sold it themselves at three, and paid over the money (T1). Five hundred pounds of butter by one telling. At the window, watching the close: "They paid. That's the thing. They didn't steal it. They held a market and set a price and paid. It's almost a law-court. It's almost orderly." A breath. "And they'll be whipped as thieves. And the women who asked fivepence, who's whipping them? Who sold *them* the butter?" Then: "I have a play in the coffer where a crowd is never seen and a king goes down. …Keep the door shut and think." Body: steps 3,900; heart 88 at the window; slept 5 h 10. "A just price," he adds, "sounds different depending on whether you buy or sell."

**W136 · 2025-06-14 (Sat) · 07:30 · ansell_house · Call: Heminges, do we play?**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges  
sources: call  
"Will? John Heminges. From Aldermanbury, and all quiet here." The question is the afternoon. He has sent to Holywell and to Margery Pell. "The— the yard will be thin, she says, and what there is will be 'prentices with nothing to do. I say we play. If we shut, the Mayor writes that we feared our own audience." William agrees: play, an old comedy, nothing with a crowd or a crown in it. *The Shrew*, then. The flag goes up as usual. One thing more: come by the fields, not by the gate, and bid Phillips and Pope come over by boat and not by the Bridge. "And your Fleming?" "What Fleming? There's a man from Norwich lodges here." A pause. "God keep the man from Norwich. And you." Heminges adds what he heard on Thursday: the aldermen were already blaming "stage-plays drawing prentices together".

**S059b · 2025-06-14 (Sat) · 08:30 · leadenhall · The morning after: firkins and a constable; the loaf**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell  
sources: photo, notes, location  
Before going north he walks the two hundred yards to Leadenhall to see for himself. Photo 24, first frame: butter firkins set out on a stall as on any Saturday, and a constable with a brown bill leaning by them, bored. The price is cried at fourpence and nobody argues with the bill. Back at the lodging, the second frame: this morning's penny loaf on the bread-board, short of the notch by a full thumb. Mistress Ansell has left it lying there on purpose for the household to look at. In the commonplace book, *Prices*: "butter iij d. by the prentices' law, iiij d. by the constable's, v d. by the seller's. the loaf: a thumb."

**W049 · 2025-06-15 (Sun) · 09:00 · st_helens_church · Trinity Sunday; the Counter**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, john_spencer, dorcas_ansell, gillis_verbeke  
sources: calendar, meeting, memo  
Trinity Sunday. Every pulpit in the City has had its order, and Master Thwaite preaches obedience with the Mayor's eye on him: the powers that be are ordained of God, masters are to keep their servants in, and whoso resisteth. The Mayor's pew now has halberds at the church door. Verbeke comes to church with the household, because not to come would be noticed. In the evening a neighbour brings it to the door: apprentices have gone against the Counter in the Poultry and taken prisoners out (T1). Mistress Ansell: "Yesterday butter, today a prison. Mark me, they have left off asking." The memo, late: "They've found out a door opens if enough push. That's not a thing a city forgets."

#### Week 25 · Mon 06-16 – Sun 06-22

**S061 · 2025-06-16 (Mon) · 19:00 · ansell_house · A crowd near Paul's, heard of**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder  
sources: memo, location, body  
He had meant to go to Paul's this week to check Daniel's third book against a better copy, and he does not. Alse comes back from the Cheapside conduit with her buckets half full and her news complete: a press of apprentices and masterless men in the churchyard at noon, then officers, then running (T2; not rechecked). The talk on her way home, by her account, was of seizing the armourers' shops and breaking open Ludgate, which is street rumour and nothing more. Mistress Ansell rules that nobody in her house goes west of the Stocks again this month, lodger included. His own day was the Theatre road there and back, a play, and home by six. The memo, at the window: "Two idle sorts of men in a hot square, and it wants only one blade drawn." Steps 8,100; heart 84 at the window.

**W137 · 2025-06-17 (Tue) · 08:10 · ansell_house · Call: Cuthbert on the house**  
people: william_shakespeare, cuthbert_burbage  
sources: call  
"Master Shakespeare? Cuthbert Burbage. From Holywell." His father was up half the night walking round the playhouse with a lantern. The matter is wood: a timber house in the fields, and a city in this temper. Two hired men are to sleep in the tiring-house from tonight at 6d each, and the yard gates to be chained after the play. Also, and he says this drily, the flag: his father would fly it higher, and he would have it down on days there is trouble, "since a white swan is a thing to throw at." William says keep it up; a house that hides looks guilty. "That's my father's opinion. I didn't think to hear it from the poet." "It was Heminges's first." "Then it's three to one. God keep you." "And you."

**G045 · 2025-06-17 (Tue) · 09:00 · ansell_house · A page he does not write**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), memo  
After Cuthbert's call he tries to set down a private account of the week's disturbances and finds that every sentence makes the people in it simpler than they were. He crosses out a phrase that would sound well in an alderman's mouth. What remains is a list of things heard, each with who said it: "Pell: two hundred. Verbeke: a silkweaver, English. Alse: Ludgate." He leaves it a list. The memo is one line: "If I write them I'll make them a crowd, and they weren't." It is no scene of writing for any play.

**W050 · 2025-06-18 (Wed) · 16:50 · the_theatre · Ireland in the tiring-house; Rome at the Rose**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_sly, hired_men, richard_burbage, george_bryan, margery_pell  
sources: meeting, screen (bill, pamphlet)  
After a thin *Errors*. Sly has a cousin's letter three weeks old out of Ireland. At a place in Monaghan the Earl of Tyrone's men fell on Sir Henry Bagenal's column marching to relieve a fort, and mauled it two days running (T1/T2: Clontibret, 25–27 May). The numbers in the letter are not believed even by Sly. There is talk Tyrone is to be proclaimed traitor (T2). Bryan, who has seen a war from the edge, says only that a relief column is the worst place God made. And Raye, from over the water: a new play at the Rose today, a second part of *Caesar* (T1; 55s). "Rome sells," says Burbage. William has Plutarch at home in unbound sheets and says nothing at all, and his eyes go to the middle distance. Bryan separates what came through a household's letters from what strangers have added since, and William writes down no number of the dead because nobody at the table is sure of one. Sly's cousin says Tyrone was trained in the English manner, and there is talk of pressing men for Ireland before harvest. When Burbage says Rome sells, William thinks only that a yard will follow politics anywhere if it is given bodies, entrances and wants.

**S063 · 2025-06-19 (Thu) · 20:30 · ansell_house · Corpus Christi, as was**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: calendar, memo  
The almanac still prints the day, for leases and reckonings; nobody keeps it. The Rose has *Long Meg* (T1). The play at the Theatre was thin and he was a duke in it. At the window in the long light, a memo: his father used to tell how on this day, when he was young, whole towns turned out to see the trades play Scripture on waggons, the glovers among them, Herod raging in the street. "All gone before I could see it. He tells it as a man tells a fair he was at." A pause. "And now players are a thing the Mayor writes letters about. Same trade. Different day." He does not name a town, and nothing here is doctrine; it is only a feast remembered at second hand.

**S064 · 2025-06-20 (Fri) · 22:00 · ansell_house · Trinity term; the gardeners**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: calendar, screen (draft), notes  
Trinity term opens (T2), short and jittery; at the Theatre the yard was thin under the watchmen's eyes, and Raye says the Rose is thinner. By one candle after a late dusk he writes the garden scene promised to himself on Ascension Day: a queen hiding among trees, and two gardeners binding up the apricocks and pulling weeds, who know more of the realm than she does. A kingdom's misrule is explained in caterpillars and pruning. It goes quickly, thirty-odd lines by eleven, with the deposing already written behind it. On the tables: "the gardener is right and is not thanked." The Queen's last line is a curse on his plants, and he leaves it in.

**W051 · 2025-06-21 (Sat) · 04:10–09:40 · ansell_house · The last line of *Richard II***  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gib  
sources: screen (draft), notes, memo  
Trinity term opened yesterday; the City has been quiet three days. From first light: Pomfret, the king alone, hammering out how a prison may be a world; then music, a groom, the murderers, and he dies fighting, which surprises the writer. The coffin is brought to the cousin, who has what he wanted and must now wear it. The last couplet goes down a little before ten: "…grace my mournings heere, / In weeping after this vntimely Beere." He rules a line under it. Sixty-odd days from Gaunt's four words. Gib is on Holinshed; he is let lie. In the account book: "The King ended this day. Not yet shewn." The memo: "No fool in it anywhere. Not one. …Well. Tell him after Midsummer." He plays at two as if nothing had happened.

#### Week 26 · Mon 06-23 – Sun 06-29

**S065a · 2025-06-23 (Mon) · 21:30 · bishopsgate · Midsummer Eve: a bonfire and the watch**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, gillis_verbeke, will_kemp, augustine_phillips  
sources: photo, location, memo  
The comedy in the afternoon; then the long dusk. The parish lights its fire in Bishopsgate Street as it always has (custom, T2), birch boughs over the doors, a table with bread and ale set out by the better houses. Photo 25: the bonfire, faces lit from below, the watch's cressets passing behind. But the watch is doubled this year and goes by in harness with the constables' eyes on every knot of boys, and the boys know it. Nobody leaps the fire. Mistress Ansell stays twenty minutes and takes Alse in. Verbeke watches from the door of the close. The memo: "A feast with the guard set on it. Fire, and nobody easy. It ought to be the merriest night of the year." Warm, still, no storm. Kemp and Phillips have walked him home from the play. Phillips hears a rhythm in the bells and taps it on the doorpost, and Kemp points out a small boy solemnly supervising grown men who do not know they are supervised.

**S065b · 2025-06-24 (Tue) · 04:50 · ansell_house · Midsummer morning: the close**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: photo, location, body  
He has not slept past three for a fortnight. He goes down the stair in his shirt and cloak and out into the close, which smells of last night's ash from the street. Every door in Great St Helen's has been dressed in the night (Stow's custom, `[ext]`): green birch over the lintels, fennel and St John's wort, white lilies in a pot on a step, and over the Ansells' door a little lamp in a bracket still burning against the daylight. Photo 26 is the prompt in § 8: the close at first sun, the dressed doorways in a row, the lamp's flame gone pale, the church porch beyond with nobody in it. He stands under his own lintel a quarter of an hour and does nothing. Nobody else is up but the sparrows in the birch. In by six. Steps before breakfast 900. He has not been south of Cornhill in a week, and today he does not go.

**W052 · 2025-06-24 (Tue) · 11:30 · ansell_house · Midsummer rent**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell  
sources: notes (ledger), calendar, photo  
Midsummer Day, quarter day. Twenty shillings on Mistress Ansell's table before noon, counted twice; she writes him a receipt in her late husband's law-hand. She remarks that the quarter's candles came to more than the quarter's bread, and that she has never before had a lodger of whom that was true. Upstairs the coffer, both locks: after the rent, £25 0s exactly. The shares have fallen to 8s and 12s these three weeks; he enters that without comment. Sixty shillings are set apart in canvas for Thursday's carrier. In the ledger a rule, and under it: "Midsomer. xxv li. the King in the coffer also." At dinner the penny loaf looks smaller than winter's. Verbeke says memory makes bread bigger; Alse, cutting it four ways, says cutting does not.

**W138 · 2025-06-25 (Wed) · 18:15 · ansell_house · Mistress Ansell rules on a king**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gillis_verbeke, alse_pinder  
sources: meeting  
She has heard no walking for a fortnight and asks what he has done up there. He tells her the whole, since it is finished: a king who thinks the name is the thing; a cousin wronged, who comes back for his own and takes everything; a crown handed across; a prison; a coffin. She hears it through, with her knife down. "And the cousin reigns?" "He reigns." "Prosperously?" "Uneasily." "That is not punishment, that is indigestion. I call it a bad example, and I'd not show it this month of all months." Verbeke asks who speaks for the king at the end. "A groom. And the man who had him killed." Alse wants to know why he broke the glass. "It told him the truth." "Then it was a good glass," she says, "and he should've kept it."

**S066 · 2025-06-26 (Thu) · 17:10 · the_theatre · The Rose goes dark**  
people: william_shakespeare, hired_men, john_heminges, will_kemp, margery_pell  
sources: screen (bill), meeting, calendar  
The Rose's bill this morning was the second *Caesar* (T1; 20s). Raye brings the rest at the day's end: it is their last. The Admiral's men are packing hampers for the country, and no bill goes up tomorrow. Nobody over there will say why (T3; no cause is known). Kemp: "They've run." Heminges, slowly: "Or been told. Either way the whole Bankside has nowhere to go of an afternoon but here." Margery Pell, flat: "I'd as soon they had somewhere." The almanac says the carrier is at the Bell. William does not walk that far this week; he sends the canvas and a line by Heminges's man. Nothing comes back from home, and his own letter says nothing of butter or prisons. Raye has a rumour too: the justices are warning every playhouse that one more tumult brings the Council's padlocks. In his tables William writes the fact, the Rose's last day, apart from the reasons offered for it, and leaves the reasons unchosen.

**S067 · 2025-06-27 (Fri) · 19:40 · crosby_place · A gallows at the Mayor's door**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, gillis_verbeke, john_spencer  
sources: screen (pamphlet), photo, memo, location  
By order of the Star Chamber the butter-rioters were whipped and set in the pillory today (T1). The answer came by afternoon: a crowd, eighteen hundred by report, pulled two pillories down, went against the Counter again, and carried timber to Bishopsgate and set a gallows up before the Lord Mayor's own gate (T1). William comes home from a play nobody listened to and finds it there, a hundred yards from his bed. Photo 27: a raw frame standing in the lane before Crosby Place at evening, empty, nobody near, every shutter closed. Mistress Ansell is at her window. She looks a long minute. "That is not how you ask for bread." She shuts the shutter and bars it. Twenty are taken, they hear later. His memo is four words long: "At his door. Ours." Two pillories were pulled down in Cheapside, it is said, before the timber came to Bishopsgate. That night the Mayor writes to the Lord Treasurer (T1).

**S068 · 2025-06-28 (Sat) · 17:00 · the_theatre · Will the City shut us?**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, james_burbage, richard_burbage, will_kemp, george_bryan, margery_pell  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger)  
The reckoning takes two minutes: 8s a share. Then the real matter, in the tiring-house with the door shut. The Mayor has written to the Lord Treasurer about yesterday (T1, the letter of 27 June); he will blame the playhouses next, as he always has. James Burbage: "Let him. I'm my Lord Chamberlain's man and I'll open my own doors." Heminges wants it written that they played old comedies and kept the peace. Bryan would send to Kettleby at Greenwich before the Mayor's letter gets there. Margery Pell is called in and asked plainly what the yard is. "Dry tinder, masters. I've stood that stair nine years." Kemp, for once, has no jest. William says the only safe house in London tomorrow is a shut one, and that it is Sunday, thank God. They agree to meet Monday and see what Monday is. Before they part the wardrobe chests are locked with a second iron, the licensed books go home to Aldermanbury with Heminges, and Garnish carries the parts himself. Heminges sets the question of the country roads for July.

**S069 · 2025-06-29 (Sun) · 21:30–23:15 · ansell_house · Tower Hill; the bar on the door**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, gillis_verbeke  
sources: body, memo, location, photo  
St Peter's Day, church, a hot still afternoon, no moon tonight (new moon the 27th). About nine a sound begins to the south-east that is not bells: a great many voices, less than half a mile off, on Tower Hill. A thousand apprentices, it will be said, and some of the Tower's own warders among them (T1). He is not there and sees none of it. Mistress Ansell bars the street door; Verbeke goes up to the garret without being told; Alse is put to bed in her mistress's chamber. He bars his own. Photo 28: the inside of the chamber door, the bar across it, one candle. He sits. Body: no steps from 21:30 to 23:15, heart 104 rising to 124; steps for the day 2,100; slept 2 h 40. The memo is whispered: "It comes and goes like a sea. …If they come up Bishopsgate they come for him, not us. They'd pass this door. They'd pass it." Then nothing but the room. He helps Mistress Ansell drop the bar, both of them making it sound more ordinary than it is. Toward eleven she asks whether anyone has remembered the candle upstairs; it is his, and the only light in the house.

#### Week 27 · Mon 06-30 (the rest of this week is in the July half)

**W053 · 2025-06-30 (Mon) · 09:00–12:30 · burbage_house_holywell · The morning after: *Richard* read to the fellows**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, john_heminges, james_burbage, augustine_phillips, george_bryan, will_kemp  
sources: meeting, calendar, screen (draft)  
The almanac says "No play". The streets up to Holywell are swept and silent, with watchmen at the gate who look at every face. The sharers sit at the long table with nothing to do, and William has brought the only thing he has: forty leaves. He reads the king to them, every voice, three hours. At the hollow crown Burbage puts his hand flat on the table and leaves it there. At the deposing nobody moves. Then Heminges, twice: "I don't— I don't say we can't. Show me where it's written that we can, this week." James: "They set a gallows at the Mayor's door on Friday, and you'd put a king down on my stage?" Bryan: send it to the Master whole, and let the cutting be his and not ours. Agreed. Kemp arrives at the fourth act, having stopped for the news at every door from the gate. He listens to the end. "And where do I come in?" "Late, Will. You've done it already." Burbage takes the leaves home under his arm without asking.
