### July

#### Week 27 · Tue 07-01 – Sun 07-06 (Mon 06-30 is in the January–June file)

**W300 · 2025-07-01 (Tue) · 16:40 · the_theatre · A thin *Errors*; Garnish recovers a king**  
people: william_shakespeare, abel_garnish, richard_burbage, margery_pell, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, calendar, body  
The flag goes up again after two dark days and draws under three hundred. Watchmen stand at the gate and look at every face going north. Margery Pell: "Old men, and country folk who'd not heard. Nine shillings in mine, and I never took it so quiet." He speaks Egeon's tale to a yard that hears every word, which is worse. Afterwards Garnish wants the forty leaves, to make the fair book for the Master. Burbage has had them since yesterday noon and brings them wrapped in a shirt. He gives them up as their king gives up the crown, with the line for it out of the fourth act. Garnish: "I am not your cousin, and these want ruling." Six days to copy; Heminges's man to carry the book to Clerkenwell on Monday; 7s set by for the fee. William walks home by the same road he came. Steps 9,700; heart 100 at the gate with nothing chasing it.

**S070 · 2025-07-04 (Fri) · 18:10 · bishopsgate · The proclamation at the gate**  
people: william_shakespeare, gillis_verbeke  
sources: location, screen (pamphlet), photo  
Coming home from a comedy he was not in, he finds a knot of people at the gate and a printed sheet nailed to the post, the royal arms at its head, the ink still smelling. It is the Queen's proclamation, given at Greenwich this fourth of July: no unlawful assemblies in or about the City, on pain of martial law, and Sir Thomas Wilford named provost marshal to see it done (T1). Photo 29: the sheet on the post, and beyond it under the arch a mounted provost's man in a steel gorget, halberds catching the low sun against the dark stone of the gate. Verbeke, home from the looms, reads it slower than William and twice over. "It says nothing of strangers." "No. Apprentices, vagrants, any company gathered." "Then for once we are forgotten. It is the best thing a law can do to us." Behind them two citizens are already arguing what "gathered" will mean, and whether a wedding counts; neither has read past the heading. William is counting something else: a playhouse yard is two thousand people gathered by a flag. He says it to nobody, copies the heading onto his tables, and resolves to come home from Shoreditch tomorrow by Bishopsgate Street and not by the fields. The rider looks at them both, and they go in under the arch one at a time.

**W054 · 2025-07-05 (Sat) · 04:00–10:30 · ansell_house · Verona begins with a brawl**  
people: william_shakespeare, gib  
sources: screen (draft), notes, memo  
Light at four. Brooke comes down off the shelf for the first time since March, and the "Verona" page in the commonplace book is opened at the line left blank in Lent: "alike in —". He looks at the gap a long moment and leaves it open; it is not this morning's work. Then, on a clean sheet, a play. The question he has carried since March is how quickly a yard can learn a feud without a lecture on where it began, and the answer is: by watching it start. It begins in the street with two serving-men of one house, swords and bucklers, talking big: "Gregorie, on my word weele not carrie Coles." Two of the other house come by; a thumb is bitten; "Do you bite your thumbe at vs sir?" Then it grows faster than anyone in it can think: kinsmen, old men calling for swords in their gowns, and the citizens' cry, "Clubs, Bils and Partisons". The Prince stops it with a sentence of death for the next offence. Ninety lines by half past ten, no blots, the room already hot. In March he wrote "it wants heat"; it has it. Gib sleeps through all of it on the Golding. The memo: "They jest first. Then somebody's out. That's the order of it." An old comedy at two, and 10s at the reckoning. The date of composition is this story's choice, never a record (T5 on T2).

**W301 · 2025-07-06 (Sun) · 09:00 · st_helens_church · A short sermon; an empty pew**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, john_spencer, dorcas_ansell  
sources: calendar, location, memo  
Master Thwaite reads the heads of the proclamation from the pulpit and then preaches for a single turn of the glass, which frightens the parish more than length would. The Mayor's pew has halberds at the door of it and a new man standing behind. In the north aisle one pew is empty: the household with the dark head in it. A neighbour of Mistress Ansell's says half the better sort have gone out of the City till the streets are quiet. He has lost nothing and feels robbed. No cross goes in the almanac: the mark is for days he sees or hears her, and this is a day he looked. The memo, walking the close after dinner: "Gone into the country. Good. Sensible. …I am writing a boy who's sick for a woman who never comes on the stage. Let that serve." Steps 4,100; he does not leave the parish.

#### Week 28 · Mon 07-07 – Sun 07-13

**S072 · 2025-07-07 (Mon) · 17:00 · the_theatre · Tour, or wait out the summer?**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, will_kemp, richard_burbage, james_burbage, george_bryan, augustine_phillips, thomas_pope  
sources: meeting, calendar, photo  
After *Love's Labour's Lost* to a half yard, the tiring-house door shut, the sharers on upturned property-chests. The Admiral's men are gone into the country and the Rose is nailed up (T1 dark; the tour is T2, and where they went is not known; Kent is only the tiring-house guess). Kemp is for the road: "In Kent they've not heard one word of it set down. I could be new every night." Bryan, who was at Elsinore with him ten years since (T1), puts the other side: a marshal's man in every county by the look of that paper, and players on a road are vagrants till they have shown my lord's warrant to each constable in turn. Heminges has it in figures, on a sheet of towns and carriage costs. "Item, oats and horse-hire, and both dearer every mile in a year like this. Item, country mayors, who dread a crowd worse than ours does. Item, hired men at five shillings in the country and ten here. Item, the whole Bankside with nowhere to spend a penny of an afternoon but in this yard. A wagon costs us every day it moves. An empty yard costs us only on the days we open it. I say play on till St James's tide, rest the house in August, and open at Bartholomew." James wants his house earning. Burbage wants three clear weeks to con a king. William votes with Heminges, and Pope makes him say the rest aloud: that a month with no play in it is a month he can write in, and that it suits him better than it suits the hired men. He says it. Carried, Kemp against: last day Saturday the 26th, open again Monday 25 August, and the question looked at again on the 17th, since nobody pretends the City's temper is settled. Photo: Heminges's sheet of towns beside a jug and a heel of bread, the tour weighed and set aside.

**W055 · 2025-07-07 (Mon) · 19:00 · the_jackdaw · Burbage is twenty-seven**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, augustine_phillips, thomas_pope, agnes_tipping  
sources: meeting, calendar  
"Burbage" in the almanac (T1 baptism, 7 July 1568). A capon between four on the settle, 14d a head. He has had the king's leaves one week and has not yet come out of them. He calls for ale in the royal plural, and when the tapster is slow he gives him the castle wall: "We are amazed, and thus long have we stood to watch the fearful bending of thy knee." The tapster bends it. Pope gives the health: "Seven-and-twenty, and deposed already." William has brought a gift in a cloth: a small looking-glass in a pearwood frame, 2s 6d. "To practise on, till John buys the one thou'lt break." Burbage looks into it a long moment, and it is not Burbage who looks. Phillips sings; Agnes Tipping forgives the slate for one night only. At nine William rises. "Where does it pain you tonight, Will?" "In the head. There's a city in it."

**G049 · 2025-07-08 (Tue) · 08:00–11:00 · ansell_house · Two swords and no machinery**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), memo  
He goes back over Saturday's brawl and finds he has to refuse a gift. The June streets offer him a store of picturesque detail, every one of them true, and he will not draw on it: the scene must have its own people and its own causes, or it becomes a report with swords. What he works instead is practical. Who carries a weapon in the street, and who only a buckler? Who can restrain whom, and who is too old to be obeyed? How fast does a stage fill when six men enter from two doors, and where does the Prince stand so that the yard sees him stop it? The margin fills with arrows and initials. The memo is more blocking than philosophy: "Gregory left, Sampson right. Two of theirs from the far door. Tybalt late. Late is better. …The old men want their swords and their wives won't give them. Keep that."

**G050 · 2025-07-09 (Wed) · 11:00 · ansell_house · Field declines a theory**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field, dorcas_ansell  
sources: meeting, location  
Field, on his way back from Leadenhall, stops at the foot of the stair, as far as Mistress Ansell allows callers, for a small Italian book he lent in June. William brings it down and, because the king goes to Clerkenwell tomorrow, begins a fine speculative remark about what language a Master of the Revels can hear and what he cannot. Field lets him get to the second clause. "Is this a thing written, a thing rumoured, or a thing thou knowest?" The remark loses all its elegance at once. William answers accurately: it is a thing he fears. Field nods as if that were an honest price and takes the book without asking him to confess anything more. At the door, as a householder notes a debt: "And my Holinshed is neither written, rumoured nor known, but lent." Mistress Ansell, who heard all of it from the hall, says afterwards that the printer is the only one of his friends who can be got rid of in under a minute.

**S073 · 2025-07-09 (Wed) · 14:00 · the_theatre · Trinity term ends**  
people: william_shakespeare, margery_pell, will_sly  
sources: calendar, meeting  
The almanac says "Trinity term ends" (T2·calc); the long vacation runs to October. Coming up Shoreditch at noon he passes pack-mules with lawyers' trunks going out to country air. It is the gowns' last afternoon, and they spend it on *Titus*. Margery Pell: "Lawyers saying farewell to their laundresses. Twenty-six shillings in mine, and I'll not see a black gown till Michaelmas." By the week's end they will be in every shire in England, telling June over supper, improved. Sly has heard the Admiral's men are in Kent by now, or else the west; nobody over the water can say which (T2 tour; the dates are not known). William is an old Roman and dead by the fourth act. He stands at the tiring-house door and thinks of his own book, which has lain at Clerkenwell since Monday with a pen going down its margins. Tomorrow at ten.

**W056 · 2025-07-10 (Thu) · 10:00 · st_johns_clerkenwell · Tilney's cut**  
people: william_shakespeare, edmund_tilney, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, location, notes, photo  
Out by Moorgate and Smithfield with Heminges, further from home than he has been since Midsummer. Tilney has the king open at the fourth act. He is courteous, a little bored, and has plainly read every line. His first question is not about the chronicle at all: what will a yard be made to *see*, and what only to hear? He taps a long fingernail on the leaf where the king holds out the crown: "Here cousin, seize the Crowne." "It is a pretty play. The fourth act is prettiest where it is shortest." In the margin against the Parliament, in his hand: "Leave out the deposing of the King in the Parliament, & only report it" (T5, in the manner of his note on another play). The bishop's protest may stand; the scene may go from the quarrel straight to "Conuey him to the Tower". William, very civil, says it is all in the chronicle. "The chronicle is read by one man, sitting. This would be heard by two thousand, standing, a fortnight after a gallows at the Lord Mayor's gate." York may bring word of it; nobody is to see a crown change hands. He signs the last leaf and seals it. Photo: the foot of the book, Tilney's hand and the Revels seal, the lines illegible, a line drawn down one margin. Heminges counts down 7s and breathes out: the cutting is the Master's and not theirs. The foul papers, the deposing and all, are William's, and they stay in his coffer at home. On the walk home he says only, "He has cut the glass." Burbage, told at the stage door, answers out of the lost scene and will not give the pearwood glass back. Steps 12,800.

**W302 · 2025-07-11 (Fri) · 11:30 · ansell_house · Call: Florio on Verona**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_florio  
sources: call, notes (tables)  
"Master Florio? William Shakespeare, from Bishopsgate. I'd ask you of a town." "Verona? I'll tell you Verona. But you'll put a sea-coast on it, I know you poets." He tells it as his father told it him and as the books have it: a river bent round the city like an arm; an old Roman playing-place of stone, bigger than any in London; and the lords of the place buried not in a church but beside one, in the open street, in tombs like stone tents. Mantua is a day's ride off. In the heat the young men walk abroad with swords, there being nothing else to do with a July afternoon. A proverb to go with it: "*Amor, tosse e fumo malamente si nascondono.* Love, a cough and smoke are ill hidden." Of my lord he says only that my lord is with the court. William writes on the tables: "tombs in the street — Mantua, a day — heat." "God keep you, sir." "And send you a map."

**G051 · 2025-07-12 (Sat) · 17:30 · split_hazel · A supper below the permitted noise**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, thomas_pope, alice_brambleby  
sources: meeting, photo, calendar  
An early supper after the reckoning (14s), and the three of them keep it quieter than usual without anyone saying they are afraid. Pope is still wet from the bolster. Kemp, who cannot sit at a table without performing at it, does a comic hesitation with nothing but a cup: he means to drink, thinks better of it, means to again, and the not-drinking gets the laugh. He finds that the smaller he plays it the better it goes, and is surprised, and does it smaller. Alice Brambleby lets the business run until a table by the door begins watching too closely, and then sets a jug down between Kemp and his audience. "The ordinary closes when the watch says, Master Kemp, and I'd as soon the watch had nothing to say." William goes home with an observation about timing and no belief that every gathering in London is now forbidden. Photo: the cup on the board, half-raised, Kemp's hand on it, the window behind going gold.

**S071 · 2025-07-12 (Sat) · 19:00–21:40 · old_swan_stairs · Heat, stink, the river**  
people: william_shakespeare, davy_gannet  
sources: photo, location, body, meeting  
The hottest day yet (fixed weather, T5). Pope played in a bolster that could be wrung out, and the tiring-house flies had the best of the reckoning. Afterwards he walks down to the water for air and finds there is none there either. Photo 30: low tide in heat, grey mud, wherries stranded on their sides, a dog asleep on the stair. The river smells of everything the City has thrown at it since May: weed, brewers' mash, offal. Davy Gannet sits in his boat on the mud with no fares and gives the news free: "The Queen's at Greenwich with five thousand men about her, the Rose'll not open till Christmas, and it'll thunder." He is offered the trip across for a penny and shakes his head; he has not been over the water since May. He stays until a moon a day past full comes up behind the Bridge, while Gannet tars an upturned keel by lantern. Photo, second frame: the same mud at night, the tarred wherry shining, the moon-track copper on slack water, the arches black to the east. It does not thunder. Steps 11,900; slept 5 h, with half an hour stolen after dinner.

**M022 · 2025-07-13 (Sun) · 19:00 · bull_inn · Supper with Thomas Nashe**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_nashe  
sources: meeting, notes (tables)  
Nashe turns up at the Bull's door, ragged and electric, with the air of a man who has eaten nothing since Friday and said a great deal, and William stands him a shilling supper: roast mutton and a cup of Rhenish. Nashe talks ninety to the dozen for two hours. He rails at Gabriel Harvey, who will not die and will not stop printing. He does a sleeping alderman dreaming of thumb-rings. And he spins out of his own *Terrors of the Night* (T1) a stream of night-sprites so small they are almost nothing: spokes of a gnat's wagon, a spinner's legs, blisters on a lady's lip that some imp has put there for spite. Over the third cup he falls silent and remembers Kit Marlowe laughing in a garden at Scadbury, two summers gone, and then talks of something else very fast. At nine William pleads a pain in the head before Nashe can call for a fourth pint. "Where?" "Behind the eyes. It's full of gnats." On the tables, walking home: "a wagon — spokes of — the dreamer's nose — small. SMALL." The supper is one of Nashe's three in the year (canon § 5.8).

#### Week 29 · Mon 07-14 – Sun 07-20

**W303 · 2025-07-14 (Mon) · 18:00 · ansell_house · Mistress Ansell's table: two houses, a loaf, a knot**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gillis_verbeke, alse_pinder  
sources: meeting, notes (commonplace)  
She has heard him walking again, quick and stopping, and wants the matter. Two great houses in one town, alike in everything, at a quarrel so old nobody knows the cause; their servants fight in the street; the Prince promises death for the next. "Then the Prince is the only man in it with sense, and I suppose he is not the chief part." "I am to play him, Mistress." "Then speak up." The loaf goes against the notch. "It wants a full thumb. Mark me, by Michaelmas we'll be eating the board." Afterwards Verbeke, who has been easier since the proclamation forgot him, shows William a weaver's knot on a length of thrum, the one that will pass the reed without catching, and makes him tie it four times. He gives him three words to go with it: *draad*, *spoel*, *knoop*. Thread, shuttle, knot. Alse ties it at the first try and is told not to look pleased. Under *Words* in the commonplace book: "thrum; a weaver's knot; he says the shuttle *sings*."

**W057 · 2025-07-15 (Tue) · 11:20 · the_jackdaw · Queen Mab at the Jackdaw**  
people: william_shakespeare, augustine_phillips, richard_burbage, thomas_pope, sam_latchet, agnes_tipping  
sources: meeting, screen (draft)  
Dinner; the mutton is smaller and the sixpence is not. He has two sheets written since Saturday and no one to try them on but the men who will speak them. The friend with the cold hand in Brooke has become a man who cannot stop talking, and here he talks about dreams: a fairies' midwife "in shape no bigger then an Agot stone, on the forefinger of an Alderman", in a hazel-nut coach, driving over lawyers' fingers and soldiers' necks. Nashe's gnats are in the wagon-spokes and nobody at the table knows it. He reads it fast, as it must go. Pope stops chewing at the parson's tithe-pig. Sam Latchet, sent in with a message for his master, forgets the message. When it ends the settle is quiet, and Phillips, who is to speak it, says one word: "Again." William reads it again, slower. Phillips's lips are moving by the tenth line. Burbage: "And what do I say, while he says all that?" "Thou say'st 'Peace.' Once." "…It's enough." Agnes Tipping: "An alderman! You'll have us shut."

**W058 · 2025-07-17 (Thu) · 12:20 · the_theatre · Call: Heminges on the summer shares**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges  
sources: call  
"Will? John Heminges. From Aldermanbury, with the book open." The Bankside has found the way to Shoreditch: yesterday's boxes were the best since Whitsun, and Saturday's share will be seventeen shillings or near it. That is not the matter. "Item, August. Four weeks dark, and five hired men who'll eat whether we play or no. If I pay them nothing, the— the Admiral's will have two of them by Bartholomew." He proposes half wages, five shillings a week each, out of the stock, to hold them: five pound for the month. William says pay it; a hired man who knows his cues is cheaper than a new one. "And the *Labour's Won*, for Michaelmas?" "On the thirty-first of April, John. You shall have a king at Michaelmas, cut to the Master's measure." One thing more, and his knees are bad in the heat, he says, as if that were a second item: his wife says Nick has had new shoes twice since Easter. "God keep you." "And you."

**W304 · 2025-07-17 (Thu) · 17:40 · the_bell_carter_lane · Carrier day: *who* and *whom***  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins  
sources: location, notes (letter), meeting, body  
The first time he has walked as far as Carter Lane since May. Hobbins is in, eight days on the road, with the way given in the usual coin: dust to the fetlock, oats dearer at every bait, no rain since Aylesbury. There is a packet. Anne: the hay is in and light; the barley stands short; the house is busy with all of it. Hamnet has found a jay's feather by the Guild Pits and will not say where. Judith has a third ballad by ear. And Susanna, in her own hand, on a separate half-sheet, plainly as she was bidden: "You wrote *who* and it should be *whom*. Master Aspinall says so." Of the queen and the gardeners: a queen would hear it only if she hid, "for servants say everything, but not to your face." He laughs with his shoulders in the yard. He has nothing to send but a line of thanks; the Midsummer money went in June. Steps 14,300.

**M020 · 2025-07-18 (Fri) · 05:30–10:00 · ansell_house · The feast: a sonnet for two voices**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), memo  
Coatless in his shirt at the board, a bluebottle at the casement, sweat on the wrist that rests on the paper. He reaches the moment at old Capulet's feast where the boy, masked, takes a girl's hand among the torches. Instead of a speech and an answer he gives them one sonnet between them, a quatrain each and the rest shared, to end on a kiss: "If I prophane with my vnworthiest hand, / This holy shrine". Then he starts a second and lets the Nurse break it off in the middle of a quatrain, which is the best joke in the scene and nobody in the yard will know it is one. The serving-men with their napkins and joint-stools go in above it, for whoever is a servant that day. A forty-second memo, standing: he tries the girl's "You kisse by th' booke" in Nick's light, dry voice, and then the boy's answer in Burbage's hush, and laughs once. "She's laughing at him. Good. Let him not hear it." Drafts only; the feast will be gone over again (T5 on T2; Q2 spelling to be checked).

**W059 · 2025-07-19 (Sat) · 18:40 · paris_garden_swan · The Swan rising**  
people: william_shakespeare, james_burbage, cuthbert_burbage, davy_gannet  
sources: photo, location, meeting  
The reckoning is 17s, and James Burbage will not rest till he has seen what the goldsmith is building. So William is on the river for the first time since May, in Gannet's boat with the two Burbages, pulled along the Bankside in the long light. The Rose goes by with its flagstaff bare. Beyond it, in Paris Garden: photo 31, a ring of new timber scaffolded against the sky, wider than the Rose, wider than the Theatre. James judges it as a joiner. "Green oak. It'll twist. …It's bigger than mine." Cuthbert, drily: the Lord Mayor wrote to my Lord Treasurer against this very house last November (T1), and there it stands, on ground the City cannot touch. That is all William knows of the builder: a goldsmith, and letters written against him. Gannet has the week's news: six Spanish galleys seen off the north coast of Cornwall. Four, by the true report (T1, 13 July). Nobody in the boat believes either figure.

**W305 · 2025-07-20 (Sun) · 20:30 · ansell_house · A shut window; a sonnet with music in it**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), memo, location  
Church; the north-aisle pew empty a third Sunday. Walking at evening he goes by the lane off Bishopsgate Street, not by design, and the upper window is shuttered, with the look of a house whose people are away. That is twice he has looked and she has been elsewhere. At the desk by the last of the light he takes out the scrap of four lines written after the music at Morley's in March and finishes it: a sonnet about a player at a keyboard, and the wood that is luckier than the watcher. A builder may use the 1609 text of the one beginning "How oft when thou my musike musike playst", whose first quatrain is already the March scrap. He changes one word in the couplet because it lets the speaker off too lightly, and then fair-copies the whole on a single leaf. No title, no number, no name on it or near it; the journal does not say whose hands are meant. It goes into the folder, which is thicker than it was at Candlemas, apart from every company paper. The memo: "Written to a shutter. …Well. It's a sonnet. It doesn't need her in." Bed at half past ten.

#### Week 30 · Mon 07-21 – Sun 07-27

**W306 · 2025-07-21 (Mon) · 16:40 · the_theatre · Call: Gilbert, "there's been fire again"**  
people: william_shakespeare, gilbert_shakespeare  
sources: call, body  
He is hardly out of the Duke's gown after *Two Gents*. "Brother? Gilbert. From the Bell, and I've run here from the shop." Hobbins came in an hour since, out of his time, light-laden, having turned round at Stratford with the town's letters. "There's been fire again. Last week. The ostler says half the High Street, a drover says Henley Street, somebody says forty houses. Then Hobbins says twenty, and the north end, but he's had three cups and four days in the saddle, and he'll tell me nothing more till he's eaten." Is there a letter? "There's one in Anne's hand, for you, and he'll give it to nobody else. I've not seen it but to look at the seal." Are they— "I tell you I don't know. Come and read it." William says he is coming. "Run, then. God keep— go on." It is the only call of the year that ends without its commendation. Heart 118 before he has taken a step.

**S074 · 2025-07-21 (Mon) · 17:40 · the_bell_carter_lane · The letter: a second fire**  
people: william_shakespeare, gilbert_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins  
sources: notes (letter), memo, location, body, calendar, photo  
Shoreditch to Carter Lane in under an hour. The almanac had no carrier marked for today; Hobbins came up unlooked-for, and he puts the letter into William's hand himself. He breaks it in the yard, standing. Anne, five lines (every word invented, T5): "Husband, there was fire here on Tuesday the fifteenth, at the north end beyond the Rother Market. Some twenty houses and barns are gone. None of ours is touched, nor any of us hurt, and the children have each a different tale of it. Sadler's cousin has lost his malt-barn, and Master Quiney is writing to everyone. Do not come for this. Come for harvest, if you come." That is the whole of it (T1 fire; the date is this story's, T5; the size follows the source book, T3). Gilbert reads it over his arm and lets his breath go. "Twenty. Not forty. And the north end." William asks Hobbins to say which part he had from Anne and which from the road. Hobbins, who reckons in distances and prices, gives it so: the twenty is the bailiff's count; the forty was an alehouse at Wendover; the town means to sue for a new brief before winter, and that he had from Quiney's own mouth. William reads the letter again for the hand, which is steady. His own is not. Photo, at home by candle: the letter on the desk, the seal broken, the carrier's dust on its wrapper. The memo, on the way home: "Six days. It was six days out, and I was reading fairies to players. …She says don't come. She says come for harvest. That's one thing said twice, if you know her." Steps 15,600; heart 126 on the way there, 74 at ten at night.

**G053 · 2025-07-22 (Tue) · 09:00 · the_theatre · Half a play, honestly named**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, will_kemp, nick_sorrell, thomas_pope  
sources: meeting, notes, screen (draft), body  
Not a reading, he tells Garnish, who has come to see whether he should be copying anything: a trial of some pages on the men who will speak them, before he goes into the country. The street, Mab, the feast. Nick reads the girl at the feast and stops at her entrance. What does she know of the boy when she comes on? Nothing. Then what does she know of the man her mother has chosen? A name and a face across a hall. "Then she's not shy, master. She's only careful." It is a gap William's fluency had covered and the boy has found it in a minute; he writes it down. Kemp, given a serving-man with a napkin and a joint-stool, finds an ordinary comic pressure in it, a servant who cannot get the room cleared before the dancing, and plays it straight without asking for a song. William is more pleased than he lets Kemp see. Burbage says the boy at the feast talks like a man who has read too many sonnets. "He has." Pope, reading old Capulet, says he likes the old man better than the young one. William goes home with fewer illusions about the draft and a clearer reason to finish it. Steps 8,200.

**S075 · 2025-07-22 (Tue) · 16:45 · the_theatre · The Guildhall trial, heard of**  
people: william_shakespeare, margery_pell, george_bryan, will_kemp, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, screen (pamphlet)  
He has told Heminges he goes into Warwickshire on Monday, and Heminges has said only, "On what?" "On these." The other news comes in with the boxes. The men taken for Tower Hill were tried at the Guildhall today, and five are condemned (T1). Margery Pell had it from a serjeant's wife in the twopenny gallery before the second act, and the slips of paper going from hand to hand in Paul's Walk by five o'clock say the same. The charge was not riot; it was high treason, levying war against the Queen (T1). Bryan explains it to the boys as it was once explained to him: gather a number to pull down what the Queen has set up, be it only a pillory, and the law may call it levying war. Kemp has no jest; he has kept none for this matter since June. Heminges asks when. Nobody knows yet. William stands at the tiring-house door with a letter in his sleeve, and finds he can hold the two things at once and can think about neither.

**W307 · 2025-07-23 (Wed) · 18:30 · ansell_house · No play tomorrow; a letter that will get there first**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gillis_verbeke, alse_pinder  
sources: notes (letter), meeting, calendar  
It is cried by noon: tomorrow, on Tower Hill. The sharers settle it in a dozen words at the stage door, and Garnish takes down Thursday's bill; the almanac gets two words, "No play". At supper Mistress Ansell asks about the fire and has the letter read to her. "Twice in two years. What do they do in that town?" "They malt, Mistress." "Then they dry it too near the thatch." Of tomorrow she gives no ruling at all, which he has not known her do before. Verbeke says a grace longer than usual, and in his own tongue. Upstairs William writes to Anne, five lines: the house here goes dark on Saturday; he sets out Monday, on foot, by Banbury; he looks to be at the bridge on Thursday the last of July. He does not say he has read "do not come". Hobbins goes down at first light and will be at Stratford four days ahead of him.

**S077 · 2025-07-24 (Thu) · 21:40 · ansell_house · Voice memo: the bells**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo, body, calendar  
The almanac says "No play". Five men die on Tower Hill this day for the rising of the twenty-ninth of June (T1). He is not there and goes nowhere: to Mistress Ansell's table and back is the day's whole journey. In the forenoon the house hears a bell begin to the south-east, from All Hallows by the Tower, faint on the wind, and then others, and none of the four of them says what for. About noon a neighbour's journeyman comes to the street door with an account of the morning, offered as if details were a gift, and William does not go down for it; Alse is sent to say he is at his work. He is not. He tries all afternoon to work and cannot settle to a line. The memo is at the window at dusk, low. "I heard the bells. That's all I know of it, and all I'll say I know. Five. I haven't a name for one of them." A breath. "They wanted butter at threepence. Then a pillory down. And a court has called it war upon the Queen. …A fortnight since, a gentleman at Clerkenwell told me nobody is to see a king put from his chair. Not in a play. I said yes, sir." A long gap. "I have a prince in the new one who says the next man that draws shall die for it. I wrote that on a Saturday and thought it only a line. …Five 'prentices nobody will ever put in a chronicle. Enough." Body: steps 1,400; resting 70 all day; slept 4 h 20. Nothing is seen and nothing is described.

**W308 · 2025-07-25 (Fri) · 07:30–11:30 · fields_printing_house · St James's Day: shoes, a book of fables, a letter of lead**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field, jacqueline_field  
sources: location, notes (ledger), meeting, body  
Errands for a hundred miles, with the tables in his hand. A cobbler in Bishopsgate Street: both shoes new-soled and a heel-piece, 9d. At Paul's: a little book of fables with woodcuts, 4d, for a girl who asked in April for a book with no riddles in it. A paper of London pins, 2d. Then Blackfriars, and upstairs, where it is *thou*. Field has had word by the same carrier that his own kin are untouched. "Thou'lt *walk*? A man with a share in a playhouse?" "The horse was dear." "The horse is always dear. Leave my Holinshed with me while thou'rt gone." "It lies under a cloth, and Mistress Ansell has her eye on it." Jacqueline gives him bread and a hard cheese for the first day. For the boy, Field finds a thing in the box of battered sorts: a worn piece of type, a capital H with a chipped foot, no use to any printer. "Tell him it has been in Plutarch." *Love's Labour's Lost* at two; the house is sober, and so is the King of Navarre. Steps 13,100.

**S078 · 2025-07-26 (Sat) · 10:00 · pauls_churchyard · Spaniards and quarterings in one breath**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field, john_harrison  
sources: screen (pamphlet), meeting, location  
He comes for a quire and stays for the noise. On a stall by the north door, black-letter, with a woodcut, a new sheet on the five: the words "Thursday the 24 of July" plain on the title (T1: printed by Allde for Blackwall; the rest of the title wording is not given here). He reads that much, standing, and does not buy it, and does not turn the leaf. Paul's Walk is humming with two things at once, and the talkers do not keep them apart: the hangings on Thursday, and a rumour come up the west road since dawn that the Spaniard is ashore somewhere in Cornwall, nobody can say where. Field is outside Harrison's shop, pale, with a clerk reading out a copy of a letter that names no town at all. A grocer tells the pillar he leans on that if they can burn Cornwall unopposed then the Armada is not dead history but only resting. Harrison, gravely, says it will sell. William buys his quire, 5d, and goes up to Shoreditch for the last play before the break, with Cornwall and Tower Hill knotted together in his ears.

**S076 · 2025-07-26 (Sat) · 16:50 · the_theatre · Cornwall burned; the house goes dark**  
people: william_shakespeare, george_bryan, will_kemp, thomas_pope, john_heminges, margery_pell, augustine_phillips, richard_burbage, alice_brambleby, abel_garnish  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger), calendar  
*Errors*, the last play before the break, to the best Saturday of the month. The news is in the gallery before the jig, and this time it has names. Margery Pell brings it with her box: a man had it from the court post. On Wednesday, at six in the morning, four galleys put men ashore in Mount's Bay, and a place called Mousehole is burned, and Newlyn, and Penzance, and the church at Paul on the hill (T1; the three days' lag is this story's). Kemp: "Four galleys, or forty?" Bryan: "If it were forty we'd not have played to the end." Galleys burn and go, he says; they cannot lie on that coast. Nobody knows more, and by the jig the yard has made it forty galleys and Plymouth taken. Pope dances the old woman's part to a house that will not settle and gets his laugh anyway. Then the house goes dark the way a house does: Heminges pays the hired men a whole week and the first of the half-wages in hand, Garnish locks the books and the parts in the chest with the second iron, and the swan comes down. In the ledger: "Dark till Bartholomew." After, a pot at the Split Hazel with Bryan, Pope, Heminges and Burbage, where the tables argue Cornish beacons and Tower Hill in one breath, and Alice Brambleby asks every teller where his account began; most began at the next table. William says he walks on Monday, and saves eighteen shillings of horse-hire by it. Burbage offers to keep the Verona sheets safe while he is gone. "They go where I go." "Then mind the rain on them." "There is no rain this summer, Dick."

**W060 · 2025-07-27 (Sun) · 15:00 · ansell_house · Packing**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gillis_verbeke, alse_pinder, gib  
sources: notes (ledger, tables), meeting, calendar, body  
Church, where Master Thwaite prays for the west country by county and for the Queen's ships by number, which he has wrong. After dinner the coffer is opened and counted: £26 8s. Six pounds go into a linen belt next the skin, and £20 8s stays under two locks. The pack is listed on the tables and argued over. Mistress Ansell has advice about what a light pack should hold, and is offended when he says half of it is heavy; the final list is shorter than either of their first ones. The quire of paper and the Verona sheets in an oilcloth; ink-horn, two quills, his father's knife; a clean shirt; the fables, the pins, the chipped letter H; Jacqueline Field's bread and cheese; the grey cloak rolled on top. Then the list is wiped. Brooke stays on the shelf: he has what he wants out of it. Field's Holinshed goes under its cloth, and Gib goes on the cloth. Mistress Ansell: "A hundred miles on your feet, and you with a share in a playhouse! What is the plot of that?" "A man goes home, Mistress." "Then see he arrives, and I'll allow it." Verbeke ties the pack-strap with the knot that does not slip and says a short thing over it in Dutch. Alse asks whether there are players in Warwickshire. Bed by nine, in daylight. Steps 3,600; asleep by half past.

#### Week 31 · Mon 07-28 – Sun 08-03

**W061 · 2025-07-28 (Mon) · 04:10–18:40 · banbury_road · Day one: to Chalfont St Giles**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: location, body, photo, calendar  
Out of the close at ten past four, in grey light, in new-soled shoes and a broad hat, the oiled canvas road-wallet on his back. He goes by Ludgate and the Strand, and at Charing Cross keeps west by Knightsbridge and Kensington as he did in March, cutting north by a lane to the Uxbridge way beyond. It costs a mile, and the journal does not say why. After that it is only road. He eats and drinks and rests as a man does on a long ordinary day, and stops more often than he meant to, to set a strap that rubs. Uxbridge by eleven: bread, Jacqueline's cheese, a quart of ale, 1d. A watchman at the bridge looks at the pack and lets it by. Then north-west into the chalk, with beeches closing overhead. Photo 32: the white flint road doubling back east round a hanger near Chalfont, wild thyme at the verge, evening light low through the trunks. Twenty-eight miles. He sleeps in a loft over an alehouse at Chalfont St Giles, on last year's hay, for 2d, after bacon and pease-pottage, and counts the parcels before he takes his shoes off. Steps 58,500; road heart 108–116; the left heel raw by Uxbridge; slept 5 h 50.

**W062 · 2025-07-29 (Tue) · 03:50–19:40 · banbury_road · Day two: to Grendon; the constable**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: location, body, memo, photo, notes (tables), meeting  
Up before the alewife. Amersham at six, Wendover at nine under the scarp, Aylesbury by noon, where a pedlar in the market-place sells him a ballad-sheet, 1d, for a girl who cannot read it and will have it at one hearing. All day along the headlands the reapers shake their heads over thin, light-eared wheat. He had expected the road to give him ideas and it gives him almost none; it takes the attention whole. The one he has is that waiting for a message is like waiting for a bend in the road to show the next place. The memo is at twenty past three, between Aylesbury and Waddesdon, walking (the specimen in § 8): short barley, why he walked, and a boy under a wall who must not answer too soon. He reaches for the scene and speaks no line of it. Grendon Underwood at dusk, twenty-nine miles: an alehouse with no sign, a bench, and the parish constable (T4, Aubrey's tradition, played knowingly). He comes in with a horn lantern, swelled with the proclamation and the Cornwall news, and examines the stranger under it. "It forbids assemblies." "I am one man." "So says every assembly, taken singly." Whose man is he? "My Lord Chamberlain's." "Of which chamber?" Is he a "deficious vagrom"? He warns the tapster to "comprehend all auspicious persons" in the Queen's name. He holds the pack suspicious, being tied with a foreign knot, and lets its owner lie at the alehouse on condition he does not gather there. William bites his lip till his eyes water. Photo 33: the bench, a staff of office propped against the wall, its owner's legs only. On the tables, six of the constable's words, and: "a constable — keeps the peace by not understanding it." Steps 60,700; resting 66; slept 5 h 30 on a settle.

**W063 · 2025-07-30 (Wed) · 04:20–17:30 · banbury_road · Day three: to Banbury; thunder toward Edge Hill**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: location, body, photo, notes (tables), memo  
Four words are still on the tables at first light: "wall — dark — alone — waits." Bicester by seven, then the long plain road to Aynho. Photo 34: barley close up, thin, short in the ear, the reapers small beyond. A man binding at the road's edge gives him the yield unasked: "Two for one where it should be four. And the maltsters'll have it before it's dry." About three the sky to the north-west goes the colour of slate, and thunder stands over the hills by Edge Hill, where he must go tomorrow (fixed weather, T5). It reaches him the last mile into Banbury and has him wet to the shirt in forty paces. Twenty-two miles. A clean inn off the market-place, its sign left out pending check: a dry sheet, a fire to steam the cloak at, supper and bed 10d, and a thin Banbury cheese that is mostly paring. The Verona sheets are dry in their oilcloth. The memo, by the fire, low: "Remember: she speaks first. Not he. She. …No. Let him think he does." Steps 46,000; road heart 110; slept 6 h 15, the best night of the four.

**W064 · 2025-07-31 (Thu) · 04:30–17:00 · clopton_bridge · Day four: Clopton Bridge at five**  
people: william_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, richard_shakespeare  
sources: location, body, photo, meeting  
Washed air after the storm. Wroxton, then the long pull to the edge, and at eight he is on Sun Rising Hill with Warwickshire under him. Photo 35: the whole Vale of the Red Horse, hedges and elms to the haze, Stratford's spire a pin in it. Edge Hill is behind him by noon. He is slow now, the heel bound in a strip of shirt. At the brook below Ettington he sits an hour with both feet in the water and shaves by feel. The bridge at five: fourteen arches, the Avon low and reed-green. Three figures have been posted on it since dinner, by their own account: the twins at the parapet end and Susanna a little apart, as one who was passing. Judith: "You walk like Grandfather." She shows him. Hamnet wants the number of miles and is content with a hundred. Susanna looks at his feet and says nothing, in her mother's manner. At the town end Richard has come down from the shop; he takes the pack off William's back and carries it up Bridge Street without making a speech about anybody's condition, and the only argument is where the awkward parcel should ride. At the Henley Street door, Anne (every word of hers is invented, T5): "I wrote don't come." "Thy letter said come for harvest." "…So it did. Why did you walk?" "The horse was dear." She looks at the feet. "Sit down before you fall down." By half past five his feet are in a tub of cold well-water in the yard and she is dressing two burst blisters with plantain leaf and mutton tallow. Twenty-one miles. Steps 44,000; resting 70; slept 8 h 20.

**S079 · 2025-08-01 (Fri) · 12:00 · henley_street · Lammas at home**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, john_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare, joan_shakespeare, richard_shakespeare, edmund_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare  
sources: calendar, notes, meeting, photo, body  
The almanac says "Lammas" (T1 feast). He wakes at six to a house already an hour into its day and comes down on feet Joan calls a disgrace to the family. It is a fish day, and there is a loaf: Hamnet Sadler has sent up a Lammas loaf of the new wheat, with word that it is the size the assize allows. Photo 36: the loaf on the board, nine wooden trenchers round it; the tenth is under his own hands. Ten at table. His father says grace and then looks at him. "My son the Queen's servant, come home by the Queen's highway like one of the Queen's beggars. Did they take your horse for debt? It runs in the blood." His mother looks at his ankles before she looks at his face, and puts the heel of the loaf on his trencher without a word. The talk is the harvest. The rye is in, and light; the wheat at Shottery stands thin; nobody at the board will put a figure on barley before the children. Richard says four words in the whole meal, and they are about the weather holding. Afterwards his feet go into a basin of salt water and stay there. Steps 3,100; resting 60.

**W330 · 2025-08-02 (Sat) · 10:00 · henley_street · Three tales of one fire; three things from London**  
people: william_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, notes, photo  
On the bench under the orchard wall he has the fire told to him three times, as her letter promised. Susanna gives it in order: the cry about two in the afternoon, the wind in the south-west, the bell, buckets from the Guild Pits, twenty houses and barns by dark, and nobody hurt (T5 detail on a T1 fire). Judith gives it in voices: the widow who would not leave without her hens, the bailiff's man shouting orders at the wind. Hamnet gives it as a thing he very nearly saw: he ran to the top of the street, was turned back by his uncle Richard, and has a bent nail from the ground since. Then London's bundle is opened. The fables with woodcuts go to Susanna, who asked for a book with no riddles in it. The ballad is Judith's; he sings it once, badly, and she has it, tune and all, before dinner. The chipped letter H goes to the collector: "It has been in Plutarch." "Who is Plutarch?" "A Greek. Master Field will tell thee he is very dear." On *whom* he yields to Master Aspinall. Susanna: "And the queen? Did she hide?" "She steps into the shadow of the trees. Thou hadst it right before I told thee." Photo: the bench, the bent nail and the letter H side by side on it, a child's hand reaching. Anne, passing with a basket: "Three weeks, then?" "Till the eighteenth." "Hm." Steps 5,200.

**W065 · 2025-08-03 (Sun) · 09:00 · holy_trinity · The sermon on fire**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare, richard_quiney, judith_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, joan_shakespeare, richard_shakespeare, edmund_shakespeare  
sources: calendar, location, meeting, memo  
Down Old Town to church, nine of them in a string. His father stays at home with the shop door shut (T1 basis: the 1592 list; the reason is left as it lies). The vicar preaches the fire. Twice in fourteen months the Lord has laid His hand on this town, and why? For a people that turn God's barley into drink, and steep and dry it on the Sabbath, and call it their living (T5 sermon; that malt was the town's living is T1). Quiney, two pews forward, has the back of a man composing a petition. In the porch he takes William's hand in both his own. "You walked from London! Then you'll walk as far as my shop. It is not for myself, Will, it is for the town." At dinner the sermon is given to John at Henley Street, with Judith doing the vicar. John: "If the Lord meant the maltsters He has a poor aim. He's unroofed a wheelwright, two weavers and a widow, and left every great malt-loft in the High Street dry as a bone." Mary says his name, once. The memo, in the garden at dusk: "Fire as judgment. Riot as war. Everybody has the reason for it by Sunday. …I think it falls on them. I wrote that in March. I've not changed it." Steps 6,400.

### August

#### Week 32 · Mon 08-04 – Sun 08-10

**G058 · 2025-08-04 (Mon) · 08:00–10:00 · henley_street · A morning spent usefully seated**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare  
sources: notes, meeting, body  
His feet still will not take a shoe, so Anne gives him a task he can do sitting without becoming an invalid in his own account: the household list for the winter, written fair, before he is allowed to open the play. He finds how many things he has always collapsed into one word, "provisions": the salt and the candles and the rush-lights are three lines, the malt is two, and the soap is not soap but a quarrel with Joan about soap. Mary corrects an old field-name he has spelt as Londoners say it. Anne corrects a quantity, and is right, and does not say so. The list is two leaves by ten. He writes for the house before he writes for the play, and nobody remarks on it, which is the point. Steps 1,900.

**S080 · 2025-08-05 (Tue) · 18:30 · henley_street · Harvest, malt and barley**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_shakespeare, richard_quiney, abraham_sturley, anne_shakespeare, richard_shakespeare, bartholomew_hathaway  
sources: location, body, meeting, photo  
The day is spent at Hewlands, where Bartholomew Hathaway is cutting wheat with every hand he can borrow. William binds behind the sickles from six till four with an hour for dinner: a plain day for a labourer and a penance for a player. The ears are short and the stooks stand far apart. Walking back by the end of the burned row he does not go up it; the children have claimed that for Thursday. Photo: a bushel of thin barley at a malt-house door, two men's hands disputing it. The evening is at the Henley Street table with Quiney, who has come to him since he did not come to the shop, and has brought Alderman Sturley for weight. The matter is barley. Stratford malts; it is the town's whole living (T1). In a thin year the maltsters buy the standing crop before it is cut, and the poor who would eat barley bread find it gone into the steep. William, who has walked through a hundred miles of short corn, says the maltsters should be made to sell. Sturley asks him which price he has actually paid, for what, and where. He has paid none. Anne supplies the town's side plainly and does not rescue him. Quiney, a mercer who malts like everyone: "Two-and-twenty shillings the quarter, and it will be thirty by Christmas. What would you have us do, sell at a loss and be burned out besides?" (T5 prices.) John, who was informed against for dealing in wool before Quiney could count (T1), enjoys him greatly. Then the plan: two county gentlemen are to view the losses and certify them, so the town may join both fires in one brief for the Queen's collection (T5; Gemini's names for the certifiers are not used), and a man with a great lord's ear might… William says he will give what he can give, and that his lord's ear is kept for plays. Anne watches him say it. Steps 10,800; blisters where a pen does not reach.

**W066 · 2025-08-07 (Thu) · 09:30 · high_cross_market · Market; the burned row**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_shakespeare, richard_shakespeare, joan_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare  
sources: location, photo, meeting, notes (ledger)  
Thursday, and the town comes to the Cross whatever has burned. Butter, a little cheese, eggs; the corn-sellers few, and attended like physicians: the new wheat opening at six-and-thirty shillings the quarter, and the poor women at the sacks saying aloud who is buying the barley and for what. Gloves on his father's stall, Richard behind it and Joan selling. The children take him north, past the Rother Market, to see what each has described. It is smaller than his fear made it in London and worse than five lines made it: some twenty plots in a row at the north end, wattle charred to lace, a chimney standing alone, the wet-ash smell coming up again under the sun, weeds already in it. Photo 37: men on ladders pulling down what will not stand, and an old man on the fourth rung of the tallest, directing them with a glover's flat hand. His father is sixty-odd and has appointed himself. "Not that one, the *other* one! Would you bring the street down?" At the end plot Sadler's cousin, a maltster, stands in the square of his own barn with a carpenter, pricing oak. The frame goes up on Tuesday if it is pegged by then, and wants a dozen men. William gives 20s toward the timber and, before he has thought, his name for the dozen. In the ledger that night: "to a neighbour's building, xx s." Steps 9,600.

**G059 · 2025-08-07 (Thu) · 11:30 · high_cross_market · A loan with an end to the sentence**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_quiney, judith_sadler, hamnet_sadler, anne_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger), calendar  
At the Sadlers' bakehouse in the High Street, with the second batch of penny maslin loaves coming out on the peel. Quiney has followed him from the burned row and, between the loaves, asks for two pounds: not for the town, this time, but to carry his own malt-house to Michaelmas. William says yes before Anne can look at him, and begins to take it out of the belt. Judith Sadler, floury to the elbow, stops his hand. "Say the word, Master Quiney. Gift, loan or purchase? I'll not have it called friendship afterwards and nobody knowing which." Quiney says loan. William says loan. She makes them say when: a pound by Martinmas or soon after, the rest by Lady Day following; no interest, because William will not take it and Quiney will not offer it. Hamnet Sadler, at the oven, says that is the first honest bargain made in his shop this year, and he includes his own. Quiney accepts the clarity with more relief than William expected, and goes out lighter. Anne says nothing until the street, and then: "That's two pounds you know where it is." In the ledger: "lent R. Q. xl s., to be had again, j li. at Martinmas, j li. at Lady Day. no use." (Canon § 5.2: £2 on Thu 7 Aug.)

**W331 · 2025-08-08 (Fri) · 16:00 · henley_street · Thirty shillings, and a word she will not take**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger)  
Anne is at the back door stringing beans, and he sits down on the step below her, which is as near as the two of them come to an appointment. He puts thirty shillings in her lap. "That's over the quarter's." "It is." She counts it, as she would count it from anyone, and ties it in her apron. Then the second question (her words are invented, T5). "And what's in the box in London, that you lock twice?" He tells her to the shilling: twenty pound and eight, and what a share may bring between Bartholomew and Christmas if the Mayor lets them alone. She strings six beans. "What is it for?" "A house. Here. I've said so." "You've said so since Susanna was in a biggin. Don't promise me a house, Will. Buy one and I'll believe in it." "Not with twenty pound." "No. But you could tell me which, and then I'd know you'd looked." He has not looked. He says so, which is new. Inside, his mother has heard all of it and sets a pan down very gently. Steps 7,300.

**W067 · 2025-08-09 (Sat) · 22:00–02:30 · henley_street · Full moon: the orchard scene**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare

He has written nothing in nine days and meant to write nothing. The moon comes up full over the orchard wall a little before ten (calc). By its light and a kitchen candle in a horn lantern he sits down on the bench with the oilcloth, the inkhorn wedged in a knot-hole of the plank, and writes what he has carried from Aylesbury. Photo 38: the wall, the apple boughs, the bench, paper weighted with a stone. A boy has come over a wall into the dark. A girl comes to a window above who thinks herself alone, and says it: "O Romeo, Romeo, wherfore art thou Romeo?" He makes the boy wait. "Whats in a name? that which we call a rose, by any other word would smell as sweete." The moon goes in as he looks at it: "Lady, by yonder blessed Moone I vow, that tips with siluer all these frute tree tops." "O sweare not by the moone." About one a casement opens over his head. Anne: "Will. Who are you talking to?" "A girl at a window." A pause, while each considers where the other is standing. "Is she like to be long?" "She's not fourteen. She's quick." "Then let her be quick, and come to bed." He comes at half past two with a hundred and ninety lines, and a nurse calling from within. The memo, at the last: "He never once asks her. She does it all. …Parting is such sweet sorrow. God. Leave it. Leave it in." The date is this story's choice, never a record (T5 on T2; Q2 spelling, to be checked). Body: steps 8,800; seated at half past midnight, heart 92; bed 02:30; slept 2 h 15.

people: william_shakespeare, hamnet_sadler, judith_sadler, anne_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare

St Lawrence, and a Sunday. Before church, the bench by day in apple-shade (photo): the inkhorn still in its knot-hole, two quills, the foul sheets of the night weighted with the same stone. He sleeps through the second lesson with his eyes open, a player's skill, and Anne lets him. Dinner is at the bakehouse by the Cross, which stands, with the twins' godparents (T2): a shoulder of mutton done in the bread oven and a pie of Judith Sadler's that the three children have been promised since Thursday. Sadler is slow about everything but a batch. He sets a penny loaf on the board, unasked. "You'll have heard in London that bakers are thieves. Weigh it. It's what the assize gives for a penny with wheat where it is. The loaf is small because the wheat is dear, and I'm not the villain of it." William says that in Southwark they blamed the butter-wives, and whipped the boys who said so. Judith Sadler: "And what do they blame you for, in London?" "The Lord Mayor blames us for the boys." She laughs before her husband has finished considering it. Tuesday is settled over the cheese: the frame at seven, twelve men, ale after at the cousin's charge. Anne looks at William's hands, then at Sadler's, and says nothing at all. Walking home the three children go ahead in a row, arguing about the pie. Steps 6,900; asleep by nine; slept 8 h 40.

**S081 · 2025-08-10 (Sun) · 14:30 · clopton_bridge · St Lawrence's afternoon: willow boats**  
people: william_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare  
sources: calendar, meeting, photo  
A warm Sunday afternoon with nothing in it, which in a working town is a feast of its own (St Lawrence, T1 feast). On the grass at the town end of the bridge he takes his father's paring-knife to a willow wand and makes three little bark skiffs with elder-twig masts, one each, and the twins race them in the shallows among the reeds, where the water is a hand deep and slow. Hamnet's will not sail straight and he argues that it was built crooked. Judith names hers after the ballad and sings it down the current. Susanna sits by his knee and reads aloud from Golding, the two lovers and the wall between their fathers' houses with a cranny in it (to be re-keyed from the 1567 text), and wants to know why they did not simply use the door. Photo: three willow-bark boats with twig masts among sunlit reeds below the arches, children's hands at the frame's edge. Nobody is told anything, and nothing is learned, and at four they go home to see whether the pie has survived.

**G060 · 2025-08-10 (Sun) · 16:30 · hewlands_shottery · Anne's road**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, photo, location, body  
With the children left in Joan's charge, he and Anne walk out toward Shottery by the field path while the evening comes on. She names the people of the land as they pass it: who held this close before her father, which hedge her brother laid, a stile where a cousin broke an arm at seven. They are uses of the land that come before his place in her life, and he listens to them as a man listens to a part he will never play. Once he begins to turn one of her stories into an anecdote, with the turn already made in his mouth, and hears himself, and stops. She notices that he stopped. They talk of the road back plainly: the carrier on the eighteenth, the horses' pace, what he will carry. Then they walk a while without talking, and neither of them needs the silence to mean a quarrel or a peace. Photo: the path through stubble toward the long thatched roof at Hewlands, her back a few paces ahead. Steps 7,800 for the day.

**W069 · 2025-08-12 (Tue) · 07:00–10:40 · stratford_fire_ground · Raising the frame; the back**

The frame lies pegged on the ground at the end plot: green oak, almost yellow, four bays of it. Twelve men, poles, two ropes and a carpenter to call the lift. The first cross-frame goes up sweetly. On the second he has the foot of a post with Richard. Richard lifts as a man does who lifts every day, from the legs, saying nothing. William lifts as a man does who is being watched, from the back, and something in the small of it lets go like a snapped lute-string. He does not fall. He stands very still with his hands on the post while the frame goes up past him and is braced, because it cannot be put down again. His father, from a ladder: "Hold her! Hold— Will, are you praying or lifting?" Then he sees the face. Anne has been at the edge of the plot with a jug since eight and has watched the whole of it. She says nothing kind. "Thirty-one, and showing off to carpenters." He goes home between Richard and Sadler, bent like a hoop, two furlongs in a quarter of an hour. Once he is flat on the bed over the hall she is another woman: a bag of hot bran, her hands, the shutter closed against the light, and one sentence. "You great fool. Lie still. I have you." Steps 9,200, and then none. Resting 68.

**W332 · 2025-08-13 (Wed) · 09:00–20:00 · henley_street · Flat: three visitors and a ceiling**  
people: william_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare, john_shakespeare, richard_quiney, edmund_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, memo, body  
He cannot turn over without a noise he would cut from any play. The bed is too soft, so he is laid on the chamber floor on a folded blanket with a bolster under his knees. His mother comes first, with a grease of her own mother's making that smells of bay, and rubs it in with a thumb like a joiner's. Under her breath, as she works, goes the small old grace. He knows it and does not remark on it. His father comes second and stands in the door. "Some fairy's been at my son's back, as one was at my reckoning in Lent. I'd know that fairy's hand anywhere." It is the nearest he will come to speaking of the five pounds, and he goes downstairs pleased with it. Quiney comes third, sits on the bed-foot, and talks for half an hour about the brief, the bailiff, and what a word from a great lord's servant might do, to a man who cannot get up and leave. Of the two pounds he says nothing at all, which is to his credit. Edmund brings ale, stays to ask how a king is put down on a stage, sits on the floor by him to watch him write three lines one-handed, and is told to finish school. The memo, to the ceiling, at dusk: "There's a crack in the plaster the shape of the Thames. Blackfriars. The Bridge. …I can't lie on a floor in London. I've a king to bury." Steps 400; resting 69; slept by snatches, 5 h 10.

**G061 · 2025-08-13 (Wed) · 11:00 · henley_street · Permission refused**

Between his mother's visit and his father's, the children bring their enterprise up to the chamber so that the invalid may see it: a string-and-spool contraption of Hamnet's design, with a bucket-handle, two of Joan's bobbins and a weight, meant to carry a message from the stairhead to the window without anyone walking. It carries nothing. There is a dispute over whose fault that is, conducted in three registers, and it is so funny that William, flat on the floor, begins at once to tell it back to them as he will tell it in London. Susanna stops him. "It'll sound different when we're not there to answer." Joan, at the door with clean linen, waits to see whether he will make the objection itself into a second joke. He does not. He says he will not use it, and means it. The children go back to the mechanism, which by dinner works, by a method Hamnet insists he intended from the first. Photo: from the floor, the contraption on the boards, three children's hands competing at it. On the tables, nothing.

Market day, and he is on the floor again with Saturday night's sheets beside him. Susanna finds she can read his hand, and the other two find there are parts. So it is played at his bedside, to an audience of one lying flat. Susanna is the girl and stands on a stool for a window, reading clear and unhurried. Hamnet is the boy below and reads at a gallop; at "With loues light wings did I orepearch these walls" he stops to ask why the boy has not brought a ladder, or a friend. Judith, who cannot read a word of it, had the page read to her once at dinner. She is the Nurse within, "Madam!", from the stairhead, in three sizes of voice. There being nobody else, she is also the moon, with a candle, and will not be talked out of it. He laughs, and it hurts, and he laughs. Afterwards Susanna folds the sheets square. "She is too quick to say yes." "She says so herself. 'Thou maiest thinke my behauior light.'" "Saying you know it doesn't mend it." He changes nothing. On the tables, later, one-handed: "S. — too quick. (She is right. It is the play.)" Under it: "a moon with a candle." Anne, from the door, has seen the end of it. "Is that what you do for money?" "That's it." "…It's better than I thought." Steps 700; resting 67.

**W071 · 2025-08-16 (Sat) · 17:30 · new_place · "That one, then"**  
people: william_shakespeare, anne_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, photo, location, memo, body  
His first walk since Tuesday: a stick Richard cut him, Anne's arm, and the pace of a funeral. She takes him along the High Street without saying where, and he has not the back to argue. In Chapel Street the new oak of last year's rebuilding has begun to silver. At the corner by the Guild Chapel she stops, as one stops to rest a lame man (all her words are invented, T5). "In April I asked you a thing at Shottery." "Whether London is where I work or where I live." "You said both, which is neither." "I work there. I don't live there. I've known it since Aylesbury." "Then where?" "Here. In a house with my name on the deed and not my father's." "*A* house." She turns him by the elbow. Across the lane stands the great house: brick and timber, three storeys, a garden gone to nettle behind a gate that hangs. Photo 39: the front in late light, one pane broken. "That one, then." "Anne. It's the second-largest house in the town." "It's falling down. It'll come the cheaper. I've passed it every market day these thirteen years, and I know what I'd do with that garden." He looks a long time. He asks no one whose it is; he makes no inquiry, now or this year. "Give me three years." "I'll give you till I believe you." They walk home slower than they came. The memo: "That one. She had it picked before I'd the wit to want it." Steps 2,900; resting 61.

**W072 · 2025-08-17 (Sun) · 18:00 · henley_street · Supper before the road**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_shakespeare, mary_shakespeare, joan_shakespeare, edmund_shakespeare, richard_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins, anne_shakespeare, susanna_shakespeare, judith_shakespeare, hamnet_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, calendar, notes (ledger), memo  
Church in the forenoon, standing through the sermon because sitting is worse. Hobbins looks in after dinner: six o'clock at the bridge, six horses and a spare, and a man with a back had best not carry his own pack, and had best ride the spare the first stage whatever he thinks of it. It is 2s for the pack's carriage and the spare's saddle. He has packed before the evening can be arranged round him, and the inkhorn, missing an hour, is found inside Hamnet's contraption before it can become anybody's grievance. Supper is ten at table and a cold pie. Commissions for the Christmas pack are taken, as at any departure. Judith wants a ballad with a murder in it. Susanna wants his answer on *too quick*, written, so that she may keep it. Hamnet wants to know whether a player's sword is sharp, and is told it is no sharper than the lath one and a deal heavier. Edmund wants to come. "Finish school." "You did not." "And look at me, walking home at thirty-one." Then, because Edmund asks it straight, a straighter answer than in spring: nothing has been arranged for him in London, and nobody's admiring a play is a living. Joan says he may take the back to London and leave the rest. His father raises the cup. "To my son's lord, whoever keeps his chamber. A player! Well. He's put twenty shillings in a neighbour's barn and his spine after it, and I never did so much when I was bailiff." It is a blessing, and the board lets it pass for a jest. Mary puts a jar of the bay grease in the pack without a word. Purse: 16s left of the six pounds. After, when the children are abed, he sits with Anne on the low chamomile bench in the dark and they talk of nothing in particular: Susanna's handwriting, the twins' school-shoes, whether Joan will marry. The memo, upstairs, very low: "Two houses. I keep one in each and live in neither. …No. I live here. I said so. Hold to it." Steps 4,100.

#### Week 34 · Mon 08-18 – Sun 08-24

**W073a · 2025-08-18 (Mon) · 05:40–18:00 · oxford_road · Back with the carrier's horses: to Chipping Norton**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins, anne_shakespeare, richard_shakespeare

At the door, in the grey, Anne: "Mend the back. And when you write, say something." At the bridge by six: six packhorses nose to tail and the spare, gloves and cheeses and a town's letters under the cloths, a bell on the leader, and Hobbins, who reckons everything aloud. Richard has carried the pack down and hands a parcel up to the pack-saddle without a word, which is how Richard says most things. Photo: dawn at the bridge, Richard's hands and the parcel, the leader's bells. "Light this time, and no calls at Oxford. Four days. Eight when I'm laden and the way's foul." Because of his back Hobbins puts him up on the spare nag from the bridge to Shipston, ten miles, sitting like a sack by order, and he is glad of it and says he is not. From Shipston, after the bait, he walks. The pace is the horses', two and a half miles in the hour, and for a strained back it is exactly right. The long rise to Long Compton, with the old stones away on the right hand this time. Hobbins gives the harvest in prices: oats at Shipston a penny the peck over last month; hay not to be had. Twenty-one miles; an inn at Chipping Norton, supper and a shared bed, 14d. The memo, in the yard among the horses: "I am too old to walk to Warwickshire." He is thirty-one. "…Three years. What's a house worth? Forty pound? A hundred? I don't know. I know what a play is worth." Steps about 30,000; road heart 102; resting 64; slept 6 h 10.

**W073b · 2025-08-19 (Tue) · 05:30–18:20 · oxford_road · Enstone, Woodstock, Oxford: wisely and slow**

On foot all day. Photo 40, about seven, on the upland past Enstone: the string of horses going away down the white road, the bell-horse leading, the carrier's broad back, dust hanging. He walks at the tail, where a man can talk to himself. The girl's *yes* goes with him, and his daughter's verdict on it. Somebody in the play must say what Susanna said, and be as little heeded: an old man, a friar, with a basket of herbs. Near Woodstock a hired rider passes them at a hand-gallop; a mile on they pass him, leading the horse lame. Hobbins does not turn his head. "I've known three that rode post, and I've outlived them. Soft and steady has the miles." On the tables, against a horse's flank: "Fryer — *Wisely and slow, they stumble that run fast.*" Under it, the second act as a list: Friar, dawn, herbs · the friends hunt him · the Nurse comes for word and is long about it · married by afternoon. Oxford by six, twenty miles, the back no worse. They lie at a tavern in the Cornmarket whose sign the journal leaves out of frame; supper among gowns, 9d. At the next board a scholar is construing Ovid for a wager, wrong. William does not correct him. By the rush-light he reads over the orchard sheets once and finds them full of voices that are not in them: a moon with a candle, a boy who wants a ladder. He does not write those down, and puts the sheets away before the light makes the reading into mere persistence. Steps 43,500; resting 63; slept 6 h 30.

**W073c · 2025-08-20 (Wed) · 04:45–19:30 · oxford_road · The long day: Oxford to Beaconsfield**

Thirty-two miles, the longest stage, and he walks every one of them. Up the hill out of Oxford, Wheatley, Tetsworth for bait, and the climb to Stokenchurch, where in March he rode the other way like a man who had been paid. At the foot of it Hobbins offers him the spare and he declines: the back is better going than sitting, he says, and finds by the top that this is true. Hobbins knows every house between Avon and Thames by what it owes and who would sell: this farm mortgaged, that inn changing hands at Michaelmas, a widow at Wheatley who will not take forty pound. It comes to William that here is the one man on the road who could tell him whose the great house by the chapel is, what is owed on it, and what it might go for. He opens his mouth. He asks the price of oats at Wycombe. The answer takes a mile. High Wycombe at five; Beaconsfield at half past seven by the last light; an inn, bread and bacon, 11d, which he checks against what is left in the belt before he lets himself sit. Hobbins, over the ale: "They say at the Bell you make kings." "I make them and my fellows wear them." "And there's a living in it?" "There's a share in it." "Ah. Then you're a carrier. You've only the one horse too many." Steps 66,500, the year's longest day on foot; road heart 106; resting 65; slept 5 h 50.

**W073d · 2025-08-21 (Thu) · 04:30–18:10 · oxford_road · Uxbridge, and London smoke**

The last stage: twenty-nine miles, all of them walked, and stiff for the first five. Uxbridge by ten. The corn in the Middlesex fields is in, and the stubble is already being gleaned by women who do not look up. From a rise in the afternoon he sees the smoke lying flat and brown along the river, Westminster to the Tower. Three weeks ago he walked out of that with his head down. Hobbins: "There she is. Dear, dirty, and wants everything by Thursday." The string keeps the carriers' road in by Holborn; the track does not pause anywhere on it, and nor does he. Holborn Bars at five, Newgate, and the string goes down toward Carter Lane with its bell ringing between the houses like a small country church let loose. The memo is at St Giles, walking, under the noise of it: "Twenty-four days. I wrote one scene. One. …It's the one the rest hangs from. …And a house. I went for a fire and came back with a house I can't pay for. That's her arithmetic. I like it better than mine." Steps 60,500; resting 64.

**W074 · 2025-08-21 (Thu) · 18:20 · the_bell_carter_lane · The Bell: three weeks of news in one breath**  
people: william_shakespeare, gilbert_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins

The yard is Warwickshire: unloading, a host calling names off letters, and Gilbert on a mounting-block, who has known the day since the last carrier. "Brother. You look like Father." He has walked none of it and has a view of all of it: the walking was folly, the barn was vanity, the twenty shillings was right. Then London, the way it comes after a silence. The Spaniard was three days in Cornwall and is gone, and every parish from here to Dover has been turned out to count its pikes. The marshal's men have been off the gates since the first week of August (invented). The Queen came to Whitehall on Monday and went into Surrey this morning (T1 moves, 18 and 21 August). Bread's no better. The Rose has bills up for Monday, and so has the Theatre; he saw them on one post in Fleet Street. Bartholomew Fair is cried for Sunday, and a certain haberdasher's journeyman will be behind a stall there whether he likes it or no. "And Father owes the baker." "Which baker?" "Sadler. He told thee nothing of it, I'll be bound, and thee eating his mutton." William pays Hobbins his 2s and gives Gilbert the gloves their mother sent. It is late, the back has had enough, and Gilbert has the Bell's ale on his side; he takes a bed in the Bell for the night, shared with a Coventry man, 2d. The road back: 9s all told.

**W075 · 2025-08-22 (Fri) · 08:00 · ansell_house · Home; the king in rolls**
