Nine hours in a Carter Lane bed, the soundest since Stratford, and then the mile and a half to Bishopsgate carrying his own pack, for the look of the thing. Mistress Ansell is at the door. Before she asks anything about the journey she hands him a basket of washing to carry in, since his hands will be free once the pack is down; then the place of his letters (on the shelf, under Gib); then when the house dines; then a dry word on the quantity of road he has brought into her hall on his shoes. He is welcomed by being given the house's work. The coffer first: two locks, £20 8s as left, and 7s out of the belt makes **£20 15s**, written and ruled off. Gib is on the cloth that is on the Holinshed, and has plainly been there since July. Mistress Ansell wants the journey as a plot and gets it: fire, walk, barn, back. "And is the man in it the wiser?" "He has seen a house he means to buy." "With what?" "That is the fourth act, Mistress." The loaf is still a thumb short. Verbeke says the looms are quiet and nobody has shouted at him since Lammas. At the Theatre in the afternoon the whole yard smells of size and new paint: James has had the posts done over like marble and the heavens blue. Garnish has not wasted August. On his desk behind the arras lie the king's parts in rolls, each tied and named, and the plot ruled on its pasteboard. Gaunt's roll goes into William's hand: his own lines come back in another man's fair secretary, cue-words ranged right after the long rule, and a dying man's England to get by heart in six weeks. The king's roll, Garnish says, would reach from here to the Curtain. Burbage had it on Monday. Steps 6,800.

**M030 · 2025-08-22 (Fri) · 19:30 · ansell_house · Call: Burbage, home and hungry**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage

"Will? Dick Burbage. From Holywell, and I heard you were in." He is exuberant, as a man is who has had a month with nothing to do but learn. The marshal's men are off the gates, the Rose's men are back from wherever they were and open Monday, and so do we, "the same afternoon, and let the town choose." That is half the matter. The other half: he wants to come round tonight and hear what William has of Verona, every leaf. "Sunday. After church. Thou shalt have the orchard." "What orchard?" "Sunday." The aside is the king's roll: he has had it five days and has worn a hole in it at the fold where the crown goes, and Garnish says he will not write him another. "Then con it by the hole." "God keep thee, Will. Walk in like a man with a back, Monday; the yard will want to see thee." "And thee, Dick."

**S082 · 2025-08-24 (Sun) · 13:30–18:00 · smithfield · Bartholomew Fair**

St Bartholomew, and the fair lies across the feast (T2; the exact days of 1595 are not established). Morning prayer first, then Smithfield with the fellows, because tomorrow there is a house to open and tonight nobody will con a line. Photo 41: a pig turning on a spit over charcoal, a puppet booth's striped canvas, a sea of hats. Everything is being sold at once: broadcloth by the yard in the cloister alleys, hobby-horses, ballads, rattles, drums, a calf with five legs for a penny. Pope bargains for a bolster and gets the feathers thrown in. Sam is given sixpence to spend on one thing and spends longer choosing it than the men spend on dinner: a whistle, in the end, after a long flirtation with a knife. He eats pig till Phillips takes the bone from him. They have sixpenny trenchers of pork with damson sauce, and William keeps one hand on his purse whenever an elbow touches it. At the puppet booth a wooden king has his crown knocked off by a wooden devil and set on again for the next house, every quarter-hour. Burbage watches it through three times, and nobody likes to ask as whom. A cut-purse is taken a yard from Arthur Meeks, who gives the whole arrest back to them afterwards in four voices. Kemp is found in a ring of two hundred, dancing for nothing, and bows when he sees them. Gilbert is behind a stall of points and hat-bands and affects not to know any players. William buys three gingerbread men before he thinks, one with gilt on it. He stands with them in his hand. They go home to Alse, and Mistress Ansell says fairings rot the teeth. Steps 14,200; the back complains from the fourth hour.

#### Week 35 · Mon 08-25 – Sun 08-31

**S083 · 2025-08-25 (Mon) · 14:00 · the_theatre · Both houses open**  
people: william_shakespeare, margery_pell, will_kemp, abel_garnish, john_heminges, hired_men, will_sly, richard_burbage

The almanac: "Play: Loues Labors Lost, the Theatre, 2 of the clock (I play)", and under it "Rose: Knack to know an honest man." Two bills on the Bishopsgate post this morning for the first time since Midsummer week, black-letter both: his lord's servants, and my Lord Admiral's. He reads the Rose's first, as always, and finds the sight of it reassures and irritates him in about equal parts. The swan goes up at one. The company has had no play in a month and Bankside none in two, and the town comes: a full yard by the second trumpet. Margery Pell: "'Prentices let off for the fair, their masters after them to see where they went, and not one lawyer. Fifty-two shillings in mine." He walks on as Navarre in the smell of fresh paint and finds the oath to study three years has a new sound in his own mouth. Kemp has stored up a month. Garnish, at the book, ticks fourteen lines of interest in one scene and breaks a pen. Heminges's half-wages have held: all five hired men answer the plot, and Raye is told he may now bank on both sides of the water. At five Sly brings word from Bankside that Henslowe opened to a house you could have counted (T1: the Rose reopened this day with *A Knack*; Henslowe's own share was 17s). Burbage: "They've been in Kent. Kent has kept the taste." Walking home he feels the company drop back into its six-day gear like a mill-wheel taking the water. Steps 11,900; heart 131 at the first entrance.

**W076 · 2025-08-27 (Wed) · 09:00 · the_theatre · First rehearsal of the king; no fool in it**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, abel_garnish, richard_burbage, thomas_pope, augustine_phillips, john_heminges, george_bryan, john_sincler, will_sly  
sources: meeting, screen (plot), body  
Garnish reads the plot from the pasteboard, scene by scene, and gives out the rolls as he comes to each name. Burbage needs none; he has been the king since June. Phillips, Bolingbroke. Heminges, York. Bryan, Northumberland. Pope gets a gardener: eighteen lines and an apricock tree. The pile goes down. Kemp stands with his hand out like a man at a dole, and the last roll is the Groom's, for Sincler. "There's none for me." "No, Will." "A king play! With no fool in it! Who's to tell them when to breathe?" William, very civil: there is no place in it where a man could laugh and the king still stand. "Then it wants one the worse. I'd have got you a laugh at the deposing." "The Master has cut the deposing." "Has he so? Then he and I are served alike." He is to have the jig after: the whole stage and nobody's lines. He takes it as a man takes a groat in settlement of a pound. Then they run the first act, and the summer shows: Burbage has held some passages so hard all month that he has lost the joins between them, and goes from the lists to the farewell to Gaunt as if three different men had learned them. William asks him for a plain reading, no more, before anybody changes a word, and the joins come back by the second time through. Then they work the lists at Coventry, and William, a duke of threescore, stoops to shift the armour-basket out of Sly's way. The back answers at once. He plays Gaunt the rest of the morning from a stool, which Burbage says is the best thing in it. Not cast this afternoon. Steps 7,200; resting 66; he goes home by way of every wall.

**G068 · 2025-08-28 (Thu) · 08:00–11:30 · ansell_house · The distance between two windows**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), photo, body  
Seated, because the back will allow nothing else after yesterday, he goes over the orchard scene for the stage it will be played on, not the garden it was written in. What can be spoken across a gap of twelve feet and a gallery rail, and what must be whispered for the yard to lean in? He sketches the plainest stage relation: a boy on the boards, a girl above, no grand architecture, the Nurse's voice from behind the arras. The urgency has to come from time and exposure, from the house waking behind her, not from anything a carpenter could build. He cuts four lines of the boy's that admire the window. When a neighbour's voice comes in at his own casement, scolding a prentice, he shuts it, and at once misses the air. Photo: the sheet with its little diagram, a square for the window, an arrow for the Nurse, his hand flat beside it.

**S085 · 2025-08-29 (Fri) · 14:00 · the_rose · *Longshanks*, from the yard**  
people: william_shakespeare, edward_alleyn, philip_henslowe  
sources: screen (stage), location, body, notes (tables)  
Not cast today, and the back wants walking: over the Bridge for the first time since May, slowly, and a penny at Henslowe's door. The bill said *Longshanks*, new (T1: marked "ne"; Henslowe's share 40s). Watch-history: the Rose, the yard, left of the stage by a post he can lean on; a fair house, not a great one. It is an English king, the first Edward, and the title is the casting: Alleyn is the tallest man on either bank and plays every inch of it, in gilded mail. What the play does with him the journal does not say beyond its name; the text is lost, and that it is Peele's is only T3. What William marks is the manner: drums, a pageant, blows given and taken from outside. This king never doubts. He stands under the heavens, with the throne that was hung there in June gilt above him (T1), and fills the house like water in a jug, and the yard loves it. On the tables, against the post: "Their king is all *is*. Mine is all *seems*. Five weeks." Henslowe is at the stair-foot with his eyes on the boxes and does not see him, or sees and prices him. Alleyn, coming out by the tiring-house stair in his own clothes, does see him. "Master Shakespeare. Come to count my inches?" "To learn them, Master Alleyn." Each is perfectly polite and each knows the other has been measuring. Home by the Bridge again, to save the penny. Steps 12,300; resting 64.

**W077 · 2025-08-31 (Sun) · 15:00 · ansell_house · A long sleep; a true letter**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell  
sources: notes (letter, ledger), body, calendar  
Saturday's reckoning was 26s, the first share since July. He went to bed on it at nine and slept till the bells: 9 h 10, the longest night of the summer. Church. Then, at the desk with the window open on the close, the letter he was told to write. He makes three beginnings in the old manner, with London news in them, and strikes all three. The fourth he keeps, and it is the shortest he has sent her (draft retained): "Anne. I am come safe and my back mends. I have counted: twenty pound fifteen shillings, and a share of six-and-twenty since. I do not promise. I tell thee what I think on, walking: that I work here and do not live here, and that the garden wants digging before the house wants glass. Three years, if the playhouse stand and the City suffer us. Say if that is too long. I was told not to come and came, and am glad of it. Tell Susanna she is right and I shall not mend it; tell Judith the moon was well played; tell Hamnet the sword is no sharper than his. Thy Will. From London this last of August." He reads it once, does not improve it, and seals it for the carrier, who is due on the eleventh. Mistress Ansell, below, has heard no walking at all and distrusts it. Steps 4,400.

### September

#### Week 36 · Mon 09-01 – Sun 09-07

**S087 · 2025-09-02 (Tue) · 11:10 · ansell_house · Call: Kettleby; my Lord Treasurer's gout**  
people: william_shakespeare, ambrose_kettleby  
sources: call, meeting  
"Master Shakespeare? Ambrose Kettleby. From Somerset House, where I am sent up for my lord's linen and his letters." My lord is with the court in Surrey. He has heard the house is open and would know two things: that it opened quietly, and when the new king is to be seen. "My lord has a mind to see it, and says he will come unlooked-for. So I am to tell you he is coming." Thursday the second of October. Kettleby writes it down, audibly. Then the court's news, offered as a man offers a comfit: the Lord Treasurer lies there very ill of the gout, "and cannot stir hand nor foot, nor feed himself" (T1: Lady Wolley's letter of Sunday the 31st; that Kettleby has it is invented). William asks what that means for players. "It means that every paper in England which wants his hand is waiting on his foot. Petitions. Warrants. Complaints out of the City." A small dry pause. "My lord bids me say (and I soften it) that the City is never so busy with its pen as when the harvest is bad. You will keep an orderly house." "The orderliest in Middlesex, sir." "That is the county I should be anxious for. God keep you." "And you, sir." That afternoon, after rehearsal, he gives it to Bryan and Heminges, and Bryan reads what nobody says: an old man who cannot hold a pen, and two younger men waiting to hold it for him, and a play about a weak king and his favourites about to walk out onto a stage between them. Heminges says, "Item: we play it as licensed," and nobody adds anything.

**W333 · 2025-09-03 (Wed) · 06:30 · ansell_house · A rose, tried on Alse**  
people: william_shakespeare, alse_pinder, dorcas_ansell, gib  
sources: meeting, screen (draft), notes (tables)  
A writing morning, the first at this desk since July. The second act is going down out of the tables: the Friar at dawn with his basket, and the line the carrier gave him. Alse brings the water and lingers, as she does when he has been walking the boards. He tries a thing on her, not from today's sheet but from the garden's. A girl at a window, a boy below whose house is at war with hers, and she says his name is no part of him: a rose would smell the same called anything. Alse considers it with the jug on her hip. "But it wouldn't *sell* the same. Not if you cried them in Cheap for stinkweed." He writes that on the tables. "And does he hear her?" "He's under the window the whole while." "And she don't know? Oh, sir. That's hard on her." A moment. "Is it true they're boys? Even her?" "Even her." "Then it's hard on *him*." At dinner Mistress Ansell has the Friar explained, and approves of him until she learns he marries them the same afternoon. "Without their fathers' leave? Then he is no better than the Nurse. Mark me, it will end in a churchyard." He looks at her. The loaf is measured. Gib is found asleep on Brooke. At eleven at night, in a silent parish, he folds two sheets of Field's crown paper into small leaves and drafts two fourteen-liners side by side that could hardly be less alike: one slow and public-sounding, on seasons passing since a first sight; one short-breathed and against himself, on desire that knows better and goes on. Neither leaf carries a name or a superscription, and the second goes into the folder face down. Steps 3,800.

**G069 · 2025-09-04 (Thu) · 09:00 · the_theatre · Sam's question**  
people: william_shakespeare, sam_latchet, augustine_phillips, nick_sorrell  
sources: meeting, notes, body  
A rehearsal of the Queen's garden. Sam, who has a waiting-woman's three lines, puts up his hand like a schoolboy. Why does the book tell him to look frightened when he comes on, when nobody has told his lady anything yet? William begins to answer from the whole play: the realm, the king's fall, what the gardeners know. Phillips stops him. "Answer from his entrance, Will. He's not read the play. He's read his roll." The correction is small and exact, and William takes it. From the entrance, then: Sam has seen his lady not sleep, and that is enough to be frightened of. Sam nods and does it, and it is right. Nick, waiting to go on as the Queen, pretends very hard not to enjoy the sight of the author made to obey his own chronology. On the tables: "from the door, not the book."

**S084 · 2025-09-04 (Thu) · 17:40 · old_swan_stairs · Drake and Hawkins have sailed**  
people: william_shakespeare, davy_gannet, george_bryan  
sources: screen (pamphlet), meeting, location  
After *Richard the Third*, in which he is dead by the third act and out of his gown by four, he goes down to the stairs with Bryan for the air. The river has the news before Paul's does. Davy Gannet, holding his boat to the step with one oar: "Drake's out! Sir Francis and old Hawkins, from Plymouth, six-and-thirty sail and four thousand men, gone to burn the King of Spain in his bed. And it'll blow." It is seven days old. They sailed on Thursday the 28th of August, and the count that comes up from the west with the merchants' letters is seven-and-twenty ships and some five-and-twenty hundred men (T1); Gannet is out by his usual third. He raises an imaginary pot to "Frankie Drake", and then, lower, says that two old sea-dogs in one command never yet made easy sailing. Where bound, nobody on the stairs can say: the Indies, the Isthmus, the plate fleet. A man on the top step says it is the answer for Penzance. Bryan: "That's what the ballad will say. Nobody's told me so." And there is the ballad-seller already, with a sheet out and a ship on it that has served for three other voyages. William does not buy it. He thinks of five-and-twenty hundred men who went aboard in a dearth year because a ship feeds you, and says only, "It'll be spring before we know." (Builders: no further word of the fleet reaches London within the year.) Steps 11,400.

**S086 · 2025-09-05 (Fri) · 08:30 · bishopsgate · *Crack Me This Nut* on the post**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (bill), calendar, notes (tables)  
On the post by the Bull, beside his own house's *Two Gentlemen*, a new title for the Rose: *Crack Me This Nut* (T1, Henslowe; not rechecked). He reads it twice for the pleasure of the title and does not cross the river for it; he has a street to write in the morning and the back is not fit for the Bridge twice in a week. By evening the lane outside is noisier than usual, apprentices back from Bankside with the jokes still in them, and he shuts the casement on it and goes to bed early, to be up at first light. On the tables, before the candle: "hot day — the street again — the talker." Henslowe's receipt (over £3) he never knows.

**W078 · 2025-09-06 (Sat) · 04:50–10:20 · ansell_house · Mercutio dies**  
people: william_shakespeare, augustine_phillips  
sources: screen (draft), memo, notes (ledger)  
The third act opens where the first did, in the street, at the hot end of the day: "for now these hot daies, is the mad blood stirring." He has known since Brooke that the friend with the cold hand must go. He has not known till this morning how. It is under the boy's own arm, the boy coming between with the best of meanings, and it is hardly a wound at all: "No tis not so deepe as a well, nor so wide as a Church doore, but tis inough, twill serue." The man cannot stop jesting even now. "Aske for me to morrow, and you shall finde me a graue man." He thinks once of Kit Marlowe, dead over a reckoning at Deptford two summers since, and puts the thought away. Then the curse on both houses, which in this first setting-down runs as the short quarto will have it: "A poxe of your houses" (check the reading against Q1 and Q2). No blot, and one passage struck: he gave the dying man eight lines more and took them back. The talker is silenced in the middle of the play, and everything after must go on without him. The memo, standing, pen still in hand: "In July I said they jest first and then somebody's out. I didn't know it would be him. …Phillips will never forgive me. Best part in it, and dead by three o'clock." *Two Gents* at two; 31s at the reckoning. He tells Phillips nothing yet. The date is the story's, never a record (T5 on T2). Steps 10,600; slept 6 h 50.

**W079 · 2025-09-07 (Sun) · 09:00 · st_helens_church · The Queen's birthday bells**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, john_spencer, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, richard_field, jacqueline_field  
sources: calendar, location, meeting, photo  
Bells from before eight: St Helen's, then every steeple inside the wall taking it up, for her Majesty is two-and-sixty this day (T1 bells and age). Photo: the bell-ropes in the tower arch, blurring upward through dusty light, the ringers in their shirtsleeves. Mistress Ansell reckons aloud what the ringing costs the parish in ale; Alse asks whether every noise must be put down as a charge, and is told that in this parish it is. Master Thwaite preaches on length of days and numbers the mercies of the reign. At "*fourteenthly*" Mistress Ansell shifts her feet and the glass is turned. The Mayor is in his pew, in the chain, and gives the responses as a man reads a bill of lading. Seven weeks of him remain, which the parish knows to the day. Dinner is at Blackfriars, the first sit-down dinner there since Whitsun, and it is *thou* on the stair. Field has a grievance on the shelf. "My Holinshed, Will. The second volume. I see the gap every morning." "It's safe. A cat keeps it." "That is not what a book is for." Jacqueline has made a pottage with a French name, and asks after his wife as one householder asks after another. Field wants Stratford street by street: who was burned out, who has rebuilt, whether his father's tan-yard smells as it did. He hears "the great house by the chapel" go by in the telling, looks up, and is too careful a man to ask. Home by Cheapside with the bells still going at four. Steps 8,900.

#### Week 37 · Mon 09-08 – Sun 09-14

**W080 · 2025-09-09 (Tue) · 11:20 · the_jackdaw · The tooth begins**  
people: william_shakespeare, agnes_tipping, thomas_pope, richard_burbage, dorcas_ansell  
sources: body, meeting, notes (ledger)  
It announces itself on a crust. Dinner at the Jackdaw, mutton and the heel of a loaf, and on the left side below something answers the bread like a struck bell. He puts the bread down. Pope, who misses nothing that happens to another man's dinner: "Where does it pain you today, Will?" "For once, where I say. Here." Burbage, as the king, is gracious about it and offers a royal progress to the barber. Agnes Tipping has buried two husbands and has a remedy from each: a clove held on it, or a hot knitting-needle. He chooses the clove. A pennyworth from a grocer in Bishopsgate Street on the way home and another for the night: "Item, cloves, ij d." He is Egeon at two, and tells the shipwreck out of the right side of his mouth. At supper Mistress Ansell examines him by candle like a horse. "The nether great tooth, on the left. It is black at the neck. You'll lose it." "It will settle." "It will settle when it's in a dish." He writes till ten on nothing but stubbornness, and wakes at one, and at three. The back, his whole study since August, is not thought of once today. Steps 10,200; resting 62; slept 5 h 40, in three pieces.

**S093 · 2025-09-11 (Thu) · 09:00 · the_theatre · Rehearsing the king with Burbage**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, abel_garnish, richard_cowley, augustine_phillips, nick_sorrell, edith_kettlewood, hired_men  
sources: meeting, screen (part), photo  
The Welsh shore: the king come home from Ireland to find his power gone. All week Burbage has played it a fraction too loud, fighting Phillips's Bolingbroke as if the scene were a quarrel he could win. He will not have the roll in his hand, nor Garnish's voice in his ear. "Give me the long one again. No, don't read it, *give* it to me." William gives him the cue from memory, and then steps up from the yard and says, low, for him only: half the voice, and watch it all as if from a gallery seat, as if it were happening to someone you were sorry for. Burbage looks at him, and goes again, and is quieter than William expected, and the loss of command shows before a word explains it. William gets back his own lines as he has never heard them: "For Gods sake let vs sit vpon the ground, and tell sad stories of the death of Kings." He does sit, on the bare boards, like a man choosing a chair. Cowley, as Aumerle, has six lines in the scene and is afraid of each; he holds his roll as if it might go off. Twice Burbage stops, and not for words. "He's a weak king. I'll not play him weak. I'll play him a strong man at being weak. Dost see it? He's the best in England at it. He does it better than my cousin there does winning." Phillips, who is the cousin, bows. By eleven the hired men have left their dice and apples at the tiring-house door to watch. Photo: Burbage's hands on the property crown while Edith pins the king's sleeve at the elbow and he holds still for her. Nick, waiting for the Queen's scene, says quietly to William: "He means it all, doesn't he? Even the parts he's putting on." William, with a clove in his cheek, says that is the part. Garnish records no interest this morning; Kemp is not called. Steps to noon 4,900.

**W334 · 2025-09-11 (Thu) · 17:40 · the_bell_carter_lane · Carrier day: her letter**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins  
sources: location, notes (letter), memo, calendar  
"Carrier in at the Bell." Hobbins, laden this time and nine days on the way, takes the letter of the last of August and has one for him, written the Sunday after he left, and a small parcel from Henley Street tied with a knot of great confidence. Anne (every word invented, T5): "Will. You went off bent and I would know you are come straight. The barn has its roof on. Your father told the Cross you raised it single-handed, and Sadler let him. The children are brown as nuts and have quarrelled since Thursday over your girl at the window. Susanna says she said yes too quick. Judith says she did right, a man under a window will not wait for ever. Hamnet says the boy should have brought a ladder, and rope. I walked by that house on market day. Another pane is gone; I did not mend it. Send the Michaelmas money when it falls and not before. I had rather you ate. Your wife, Anne." He reads it standing in the yard, and again by the taproom window. It answers a letter she has not yet had. The memo, in Carter Lane: "'When it falls and not before.' She keeps my books for me at a hundred miles. …Rope. A ladder of rope. Hm." The parcel he does not open; it is not a parcel to open alone. Steps 13,300.

**G074 · 2025-09-12 (Fri) · 18:30 · split_hazel · A knot from home**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, alice_brambleby  
sources: meeting, photo, notes  
He brings the parcel to supper, and that is a mistake. It is secured with a knot of such unnecessary confidence that Kemp takes the opening of it for an exhibition: he tries it with his fingers, with his teeth, with a fork, and finally with a speech, and the table joins in. Which of the children tied it? William will not say, partly because a month ago he promised not to carry every household joke out of Henley Street, and he can feel the story assembling itself in his mouth. Alice Brambleby produces a knife without being asked and ends the contest in one stroke. Inside: two pairs of knitted hose for the winter, a cake of his mother's, and a slip in Susanna's hand with nothing on it but "whom". The contents are ordinary, useful, and more welcome than the performance. Photo: the cut knot on the board beside the hose, Kemp's fork still in it.

**W335 · 2025-09-13 (Sat) · 12:15 · the_theatre · Call: Cuthbert, from Cheapside**  
people: william_shakespeare, cuthbert_burbage  
sources: call  
"Will? Cuthbert Burbage. From a scrivener's in Cheapside, and I'll be short." He has a friend who copies for the Guildhall, and the friend has had a letter under his eye this morning, from my Lord Mayor to the lords of the Council (T1: 13 September). "It's against plays. He had it by ear from the clerk who read it over, so take what I say for hearsay. It names the Bankside. It speaks of the late stir and mutinous attempt, and lays that at the playhouse door. And it prays their lordships to write to the justices of Surrey to put the plays down." A breath. "And of Middlesex." "Middlesex is not the Bankside." "No. Middlesex is my father's house and two miles of fields. Whether the Theatre is named in so many words my man could not swear." (The sources differ on the letter's wording; this hearsay keeps both readings open.) Does James know? "Not yet, and I'd as lief he heard it after he's eaten. Say nothing till the play's done. I'll be there by five." "God keep you, Cuthbert." "And all of us. Play well. You may be doing it for the justices."

**S088 · 2025-09-13 (Sat) · 17:00 · the_theatre · The Mayor's letter; the sharers weigh it**  
people: william_shakespeare, cuthbert_burbage, james_burbage, john_heminges, will_kemp, richard_burbage, george_bryan  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger)  
*Love's Labour's Lost* to a good Saturday; then the tiring-house door shut and the boys sent home. Cuthbert tells it again, and Bryan makes him say which words are the letter's and which are the clerk's. James does what was expected, and goes purple, and would march on Crosby Place. "Eighty-four! Fleetwood sent for me in 'eighty-four, and I told his man I was my Lord of Hunsdon's servant and would not come, and I did not come, and the house stood (T1). And two years left on the ground, and he'd have me shut by the justices of Middlesex!" Heminges waits for him to finish. "Item. A letter asking is not an order granted. Item, the lords must first meet and then agree, and they are at Nonsuch with the Queen, and my Lord Treasurer cannot lift a pen. Item. The— the reckoning today is thirty-three shillings a share, and I should like more Saturdays like it before any man here says tour." Kemp says tour. Bryan gives the court's view of a mayor: a City clothworker with six weeks left in the chain, writing to be remembered, and my Lord Chamberlain is not the man to let him lock out her Majesty's Christmas servants. Heminges, who distrusts comfort, asks what they will do if that is wrong by Monday. Nobody knows; they agree they will play because no order has come, and for no better reason. Burbage cares for one thing, which is Thursday fortnight. "If they shut us on the first of October I'll play him in the street." William has said little. Asked, he says what he has turned over since noon. The Mayor writes of June, and they are about to answer him with a play in which a king is put down. Cut or no, he would have my lord hear of this from them before he hears it at the board. Agreed: Heminges to send to Kettleby tonight; play Monday as billed; no talk of it at the Jackdaw. Kemp talks of it at the Jackdaw.

**W336 · 2025-09-14 (Sun) · 09:00 · st_helens_church · The pew filled; music at evening**  
people: william_shakespeare, the_lady_unnamed, john_spencer, josias_thwaite  
sources: calendar, location, memo  
The Mayor is in his pew the morning after his letter, furred and exact, three pews from a man it was written against. He gives the litany its answers short and cold. Neither looks at the other, one of them by choice. The north aisle has filled again with the households that went out of the City in July. Among them is a dark head under a plain hood, which does not turn. He learns nothing from the sermon. At evening, walking for the sake of his back and by no design, he comes through the lane off Bishopsgate Street. The shutter is open. There is music behind it: a pavan, taken slowly, with a hard place in the left hand that is stopped, and gone over, and stopped, and at last played through as if it had never been hard. Then the lid, shut. He stands no longer than a man might to get a stone out of his shoe. In the almanac, against the fourteenth, a small cross and no word: the first since June. Nothing is written tonight. The jaw has a pulse in it, and he lets that be the reason. The memo, in the close, very low: "Home, then. …She goes over the hard place till it isn't one. I know no one else who does that. I know one." Steps 5,600; slept 6 h 10, woken twice.

#### Week 38 · Mon 09-15 – Sun 09-21

**W081 · 2025-09-16 (Tue) · 16:45 · the_theatre · Call: Kettleby, "play on"**  
people: william_shakespeare, ambrose_kettleby, john_heminges  
sources: call, notes (ledger)  
In the tiring-house, Heminges at his shoulder and the paint of *Titus* still red on three of the hired men. "Master Shakespeare? Kettleby. From Somerset House. I have been to Nonsuch and back since Sunday on your company's affairs, and my horse wishes you all at the devil." My lord has had the Mayor's letter told him. "I am to give you his answer in his own words, and I decline. I will give you the sense." The sense is: play on. No letter has gone out to any justice. The lords have other matter before them, with a fleet at sea and the Lord Treasurer in his bed. "My lord says (and here I soften it) that the Lord Mayor has a ropemaker's conscience in a furred gown, and six weeks left to wear it. He says he has read the Mayor's letter, and that the Mayor writes a very fair hand. He says he answers to one lady, and she likes a play. He says if any justice of Middlesex comes to your door you are to send the man to him." Heminges, at William's ear: "Ask if that is in writing." William asks. "Nothing my lord says is in writing, sir. That is why he is still Lord Chamberlain. Keep your house quiet, open your king on the day, and wear the badge. God keep you." "And you, sir, and your horse." Heminges writes "play on" in the company book and looks at it.

**W337 · 2025-09-17 (Wed) · 08:40 · ansell_house · Call: Field, "a Cambridge man has thee in his margin"**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field  
sources: call, screen (bill)  
"Will? Richard Field. From my own shop, with a book open that I did not print." It came yesterday in a parcel from Cambridge: a learned thing in quarto, with a letter in it from England to her three daughters, the two universities and the Inns of Court, praising their poets by the yard (`[ext]`, check against the 1595 title). "I was reading it for the workmanship, which is middling. And on one leaf, in the margin, small, there's a name I know. I'll not tell thee what he calls thee. Come and see it with thine own eyes. Harrison has copies at the Greyhound by this. Tomorrow, ten, at Paul's." "Does he abuse me?" "He does not. That's why I'd have thee read it standing by an honest man." William says his jaw is the size of an egg and he is not fit to be praised. "Then it will do thee good." The trade has a fire in it too this week: Woburn in Bedfordshire burned on Saturday, and a stationer is already rushing a godly tract on it through the press (T1 per Gemini; not rechecked). "Tell thy Quiney to send up his town's certificate before Woburn has drunk every charitable purse in England." "I'll write tonight." "And, Will: my Holinshed." "God keep thee, Richard." "And mend thy face." On the Bishopsgate post an hour later, two bills: his own house's old *Hamlet*, in which he is to cry revenge this afternoon through a clove (T4 role, used knowingly), and at the Rose a new play, *The New World's Tragedy* (T1: new this day; Henslowe's share £3 5s). He reads that title twice. A new world, a fortnight after Drake sailed for it. Henslowe has an almanac too.

**W082 · 2025-09-18 (Thu) · 10:00 · pauls_churchyard · "Sweet Shakspeare"**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field, john_harrison, richard_burbage, will_kemp  
sources: screen (book), meeting, location, memo, photo  
At the sign of the White Greyhound, standing, as he reads everything. Field puts the book in his hands, and Harrison, beaming, finds the leaf for him with a stubby thumb: Master Covell's *Polimanteia*, printed at Cambridge this year (T1). The page is close type, praise of England's writers running down it like a muster-roll. Out in the margin, small, among the side-notes, are two words: "Sweet Shakspeare" (T1; what stands round them, Gemini's "All praise worthy. Lucrecia", is to be keyed from the book, `[ext]`). He reads it. He reads the names above and below. He reads it again. No man has put his name in a printed book before to say he was good. Three years ago a dying man put him in print as a crow in borrowed feathers; this one calls him sweet, and he finds he cannot decide which is harder to stand still under. Harrison, at his elbow, reports it as of a third party. "There is a Cambridge gentleman calls you sweet, Master Shakespeare. I have told two customers. The *Lucrece* is… respected." Field, low, for his ear only: "For the *poems*, mark. Not for a king in Shoreditch, nor a boy under a wall. Thou'rt wasted on players." William shuts the book very gently and does not buy it; he has it by heart. The memo, in Cheapside: "Sweet. …Like a pippin. Like a child's physic. …A man in Cambridge whom I never saw. Thirty-one years, and it comes in a margin." He laughs with his shoulders as far as the Stocks. At the Jackdaw after the play he cannot keep from telling Burbage, low. Kemp is on the next bench, eating. Kemp stops eating.

**G072 · 2025-09-19 (Fri) · 17:00 · ansell_house · The name left off**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), memo  
After the play, with the clove doing less than it did, he takes one of the private leaves out of the folder and considers, for the first time in plain words, whether giving it to anyone would make it clearer or make it false. He changes a possessive, *my* for *thy*, and sits a long while, and changes it back. The leaf carries no name and he does not write one; the capture keeps the revision and does not read it as evidence of any person. He puts it back face down. Then he turns to the letter he promised Field he would write to Quiney about the certificate, plain business, and finds its obligations every bit as hard to meet in the right words. The memo, one line: "A sonnet can lie by leaving a man out. A letter can't. …Enough."

**W083 · 2025-09-20 (Sat) · 16:50 · the_theatre · Kemp's new name for him sticks**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, abel_garnish, margery_pell, john_heminges, thomas_pope, sam_latchet, humphrey_larkham, richard_cowley  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger), body  
It began on Thursday night and has had two days to ripen. "Sweet Master Shakespeare, will your sweetness walk on at the third trumpet?" "A stool for Sweet Master Shakespeare, he has the toothache. It is all that sugar." The hired men had it by Friday. This afternoon Sam Latchet says it to his face in simple good faith, taking it for a title, like Cavaliero. Garnish, who dislikes Kemp's margins, will not write it; then he forgets himself on the plot and has to scrape it off the pasteboard. Margery Pell brings it back in with her box. "There's a lawyer's clerk in the twopenny rooms asks which of you is the sweet one. I said the one that looks sour." William is all courtesy, which the company knows how to read. "You have me by heart in two days, Will. It took the gentleman of Cambridge a whole book." "I was first on the Queen's paper, sweet Master Shakespeare. First. Read it again. A margin's a small place to be praised in." "It is. I mean to be comfortable there." Pope: "Equally wronged." The boxes are a shilling out at the count, and Larkham will not rest till it is found: a hurried change between Pell's box and the stair, not a thief, and he makes Heminges enter the correction on the line where the error was and not at the foot, where it would make the afternoon look better than it was. Cowley holds the candle. The reckoning is 29s a share, and Kemp tells out William's with a flourish and the name. It will be daily now. Under it, as everyone but Kemp can hear, is a play going up in twelve days with no fool in it. Steps 11,100; resting 62.

#### Week 39 · Mon 09-22 – Sun 09-28

**S090 · 2025-09-22 (Mon) · 08:30 · old_swan_stairs · Raleigh home, with little riches**  
people: william_shakespeare, davy_gannet, michael_drayton  
sources: meeting, screen (pamphlet), location  
An errand to Paul's for paper before rehearsal, by way of the river. Gannet has it first and biggest. "Raleigh's home! Landed at Plymouth with a ship so deep in gold she'd hardly steer. I told you in February. And it'll hold fair." In February he said Raleigh was gone for gold, and has kept the second half ready ever since. In Paul's Walk by nine the tale is a third smaller and still shrinking. He is landed, that much is sure; word came to the court yesterday (T1: Lady Ralegh to Cecil, "this Sunday"). But the phrase going from pillar to pillar is "with little riches" (T1 wording; that it is abroad in Paul's is invented). Some stones that may be ore. A river as wide as a sea, and a golden city always one nation further up it, and men whose heads grow beneath their shoulders, as reported by men who had it from other men. William asks the pillar whether anyone has actually spoken with a man off the ship. Nobody has. Drayton is at a stall, Warwickshire in a London cloak. He asks whether Will has seen himself in Cambridge print, and has plainly looked for his own name on the same leaf first. Of Raleigh he says: "Eight months, and he comes home with a story." William: "It's a living." He buys his quire, 5d, and is at the Theatre by ten with the left side of his face in a scarf. Steps 12,400.

**S091 · 2025-09-23 (Tue) · 10:40 · ansell_house · Call: my lord of Southampton**  
people: william_shakespeare, henry_wriothesley_southampton, john_florio  
sources: call, memo, meeting  
A writing morning; the tables say "banished." The voice is there without warning, light and quick, laughter under it. "Master Shakespeare? Southampton. From Holborn, where I am for two nights, with Florio at my elbow frowning at me." "My lord." He has stood up. "They tell me you've left off poems for players. Is it so? I call that a desertion." "The players pay by the week, my lord. A poem is paid once, in honour, which I have had of your lordship and do not forget." "Prettily said. You see, Florio, he *can* still." He talks as a man does who is happy and cannot keep to one matter: the court at Nonsuch, a horse, the tilt on the Queen's day. "I'm to run. Come and see me unhorsed." The court, he says, is very certain he is in love; the court is always certain; and he laughs at it before William can decide what face to make. Then the matter. "I want something of you. For a lady. Fourteen lines, such as you make, that a man might send with a pair of gloves and not be laughed at. Or be laughed at, but kindly." He does not say what lady. "Is she fair, my lord?" "She is— yes. And she mocks me." "Then she is wise, and it must be good." William finds he has one more thing to say, and says it gently, as from the play: "My lord, I have a friar who bids his lovers love moderately. He is not much heeded." A laugh, not meant for William. "Then he is a friar." Florio's voice, off, about a horse that will not wait, ends it. "Make it good. God keep you, Master Shakespeare." "And your good lordship." It is gone. (Contact is T3, the story's choice.) By evening the gossip has come the other way, as gossip does, by Bryan from a Carey groom: my lord of Southampton courts one of the Queen's maids, Mistress Vernon, with too much familiarity, and the court says so (T1 for Rowland Whyte's letter of this day; that its talk reaches the tiring-house is invented). The memo: "He was happy. I never heard him so. …For a lady. I have a drawer of that ware, and not a leaf of it would fit. …Well. I am his to command. I said so in print." He writes nothing for it tonight.

**W338 · 2025-09-25 (Thu) · 12:10 · the_theatre · Call: Heminges; a gown let down, a crown new gilt**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges  
sources: call, body  
"Will? John Heminges. From Aldermanbury, with the tailor standing by." Tomorrow is the full rehearsal in apparel, and there are items. "Item, the crown. The old one from *Harry the Sixth* is gone green at the points. I have had it new gilt, four shillings. Item, a looking-glass to be broke every time we play him, which the Master has saved us, and I'll not pretend to grieve. Item." He starts it twice. "The— the Queen's gown. Nick has grown two fingers since it was cut at Candlemas. My wife says he is out of it at wrist and ankle. The tailor will let it down for eighteenpence, and says there is no more cloth to let." William asks how the boy does. "Well. Eats like two. He sings in the yard of a morning, low, to try it. I do not listen, and I hear it." A pause that neither fills. "Your voice is thick." "It's a tooth." "You sound like Pope in his bolster. Have it out before Thursday. Gaunt must be heard at the back." "After Thursday." "And the *Labour's Won*?" "When this king is buried, John." "God keep you." "And you." The jaw is hot to the hand tonight. Resting 66, and for the first time it does not come down by evening; slept 5 h 20.

**W084 · 2025-09-26 (Fri) · 08:30–12:30 · the_theatre · The king in apparel**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, nick_sorrell, augustine_phillips, james_burbage, abel_garnish, thomas_pope, will_kemp  
sources: meeting, screen (plot), calendar, photo  
The full rehearsal, in the clothes, to an empty house and James Burbage in the lords' room with his arms folded. Photo: the plot on its peg by the tiring-house door, the entrances in Garnish's hand. The throne stands on its dais under the blue heavens. Before they begin, Burbage lays the pearwood glass on the seat of it, face up, and takes it off only when he must sit. Nobody asks. Gaunt is carried on in a chair, in a furred gown with his beard chalked white, and dies well enough for a man speaking round a clove: "this England" goes out to the empty galleries and comes back. Then William, washed of the chalk, stands at the tiring-house door and watches the rest. The Master's cut shows like a drawn tooth. The king goes off at Flint a king and comes on in a London street a prisoner, and between the two York walks down and reports the whole unmaking: "plume-pluckt Richard". Burbage, coming off: "I lose a crown in the tiring-house, Will. Every day. Behind a curtain." "It's the law." "It's a hole." But the parting in the street holds. Nick's Queen is exact and still in the lengthened gown; on her highest line there is a grain in the voice that was not there at Easter. Phillips, tuning for the jig, looks up, and down again. In the garden the Queen steps into the shadow of the trees to overhear, as a girl in Warwickshire held she must. Pope's gardener gets the only laugh, from the hired men. Kemp watches from the yard, alone, eating an apple, and at the end says, "It wants a dog." Over the water it is *Faustus* (T1).

**G073 · 2025-09-27 (Sat) · 06:30–09:30 · ansell_house · Juliet will not explain herself twice**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), body  
Back in Verona for a morning, with the king off his hands till Thursday. The girl has heard that her cousin is dead and by whose hand, and he finds he has written her a passage that explains to the yard a feeling the yard has already watched her have. He has been protecting the audience from an uncertainty it can perfectly well bear. He cuts it, eleven lines, and gives her instead one decisive turn and fewer words defending it. The margin note is practical and short: "trust the boy." Then, because it is Saturday and the reckoning is at five, he puts the sheets under the cloth and goes to play. Steps 9,400; resting 67.

**W339 · 2025-09-28 (Sun) · 13:00 · ansell_house · A jaw like a quince**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, gillis_verbeke, josias_thwaite  
sources: body, meeting, notes (letter)  
He sits through Master Thwaite on St Michael and the angels with his hand to his face, and the parish takes it for devotion. Saturday's share was 34s, the best since Whitsun, and he would give half of it to chew. At table Mistress Ansell has done with remedies and come to judgment. "Look at him. A jaw like a quince. There is Master Yaxley in Bishopsgate Street will have it out for a shilling, as I am a Christian woman. A man who can walk a hundred miles for a barn can walk forty yards for a tooth." "After Thursday, Mistress." "What is Thursday?" "The king." "The one put down by his cousin." "That one." "Then you will speak his uncle's dying with a face like a pudding, and it will serve you both right." Verbeke says, gently, that in Ghent they tie a thread to the tooth and the other end to a door, and a friend who does not love you too much shuts the door. Alse has to leave the room. Sops in broth. Upstairs he writes six lines to Anne for Thursday's carrier. He tells her of the margin. Of the Mayor's letter he says nothing, and of the tooth only that he has one. Steps 2,300; resting 68; slept 4 h 50.

#### Week 40 · Mon 09-29 – Tue 09-30 (the rest of this week is in the October–December file)

**S092 · 2025-09-29 (Mon) · 11:30 · ansell_house · Michaelmas: rent, a quarter's reckoning, the Golden Pen**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, michael_drayton  
sources: notes (ledger), calendar, meeting, screen (ledger), photo  
The almanac: "Michaelmas. Rent, Mistress Ansell, xx s., by noon." At Paul's first, where the forenoon's talk is a wager of penmanship: two writing-masters trying this very day, before judges, for a pen of gold (T1: the Golden Pen, 29 September). A stall has a writing-master's specimen sheet pinned up for the occasion. Photo: flourished secretary, chancery and italic hands on one sheet, beside which his own playhouse scrawl would look like hen-tracks. Drayton: "Would you enter?" "It's the only prize-fight I could win, and then only if they judged by speed." Fine handwriting is a neighbouring trade, he thinks, and no more a proof of fine thinking than a good saddle is of a good horse. The rent is paid at half past eleven on the hall table, in silver, told over twice by her. From today firing and washing are 6d the week, and the first fire of the season is laid in his chamber, not yet lit. The loaf goes to the board. In July she said that by Michaelmas they would be eating the board. It wants a thumb and the breadth of a nail, and she lets the notch speak for itself. Then the quarter, in the narrow book, receipts left and payments right, each on its own line: nine weeks' playing either side of a dark month; shares since Bartholomew of 26, 31, 33, 29 and 34 shillings; a walk; a barn; thirty shillings to his wife; forty lent to Quiney, in the expected column; cloves. Coffer, counted and ruled: **£23 10s**, with the sixty shillings for Stratford already sewn in canvas apart, for Thursday's carrier. By old custom the City chooses its next Mayor at Guildhall today (`[ext]`, verify), and the close knows by dinner that it will not be Spencer. *The Shrew* at two; the Rose plays *The Wise Man* (T1). Resting 69. Three days.

**W340 · 2025-09-30 (Tue) · 08:15 · bishopsgate · Two kings on one post**  
people: william_shakespeare, abel_garnish, richard_burbage, nick_sorrell, will_kemp  
sources: screen (bill, part), calendar, body, memo  
On the post inside the gate, pasted fresh over yesterday's: the Rose's bill, *Longshanks* again today (T1), and beside it, early, as is the way with a new play, the Theatre's for Thursday. Black-letter, the title only: the tragedy of King Richard the Second, by the Lord Chamberlain his Servants. No maker's name; there never is. Two English kings side by side in the wind, the tall one who never doubts, and the one he has spent since April teaching to do nothing else. A carter stops, spells out the nearer, and says to his boy, "A king play. Thursday." So the town has named it before seeing it. At the Theatre Garnish hangs the plot on its peg and walks the hired men through the lists till they can find their own feet. Burbage runs the prison alone on the bare stage, twice, for nobody: "I wasted time, and now doth time waste me." Nick asks whether the gown shows. It does not. Kemp, passing: "Sweet Master Shakespeare. How does the sweet tooth?" William goes home at noon and cons Gaunt aloud at the window, the roll in one hand and the other holding his face together. The memo, at dusk, thick: "Two days. …Let it hold till Thursday. Then let Yaxley have it, and welcome." Steps 6,300; resting 70; slept 4 h 40.
