### October

#### Week 40 · Wed 10-01 – Sun 10-05 (Mon 09-29 and Tue 09-30 are in the July–September file)

**G075 · 2025-10-01 (Wed) · 09:00 · fields_printing_house · A correction no reader will thank him for**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field, jacqueline_field  
sources: meeting, screen (proof), photo  
He walks to Blackfriars for a quire of the Rochelle crown paper at trade price, 4½d, and stays an hour, chewing on the right side. Field has a forme on the stone with one turned letter and a dropped line, and shows him what it costs to put right a fault no reader will ever thank them for: the forme unlocked, a line of type lifted out, the whole page re-justified, a sheet pulled again. William recognises his own trade in it. A cue altered in one part means two other rolls altered, and he has been changing the king's rolls since July as if paper were weather. Field says so before he can: "Thou changest a word and some poor devil copies three pages. Here it's my journeyman's morning." Jacqueline, passing with an armful of quires, points out that neither man's recognition has yet moved the pile that blocks the passage to the press. They move it, and the conversation becomes less grand and more useful. Photo: the corrector's ring on a damp proof, a brown stroke out to the margin, Field's inky thumb beside it.

**W400 · 2025-10-01 (Wed) · 19:30 · ansell_house · The eve of the king: canvas for the carrier, and half a mouth**  
people: william_shakespeare, gilbert_shakespeare, alse_pinder, dorcas_ansell  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger), screen (part), body  
Gilbert comes after the shop shuts to carry the Michaelmas money to the Bell, 60s sewn in canvas. The carrier is in tomorrow, and tomorrow at about a quarter to three William will be dying at Ely House. With it go four lines for Anne: the king is played Thursday; more when he knows whether anybody came. The lower left tooth has ruled him three weeks and the jaw is thick with it; he cons Gaunt's roll with half a mouth and a clove. Alse, bringing the washing, hears "this England" from the door and asks the honest question: "Is he sorry to leave it, sir, or sorry for it?" "Both." "Then say it sorrier at the end." Mistress Ansell, from below: "Alse! Not after eight." Gilbert, going: "If it fails, brother, there's always ribbon." In the ledger: "sent to Stratford lx s., by G." Body: resting 67; slept 4 h 45.

**S095a · 2025-10-02 (Thu) · 08:40 · bishopsgate · Two bills on the post: *The Disguises* against the king**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (bill), calendar  
On the post by the Bull, two bills side by side, black-letter, title only. The Lord Admiral's servants at the Rose: *The Disguises*, a new one (T1; 43s to Henslowe, which he never knows). The Lord Chamberlain's servants at the Theatre: *The Tragedie of King Richard the second*. No author on either. He reads the Rose's first, as he always does, and thinks what a title must do in the time it takes a carter to pass: promise something quickly, whatever the play makes of the promise afterwards. Theirs promises a disguise; his promises a king, and a tragedy, which tells the town how it ends before it has paid. He goes up Bishopsgate Street toward Shoreditch with half a mouth and a clove, not looking back at either. The rival title goes in his tables among the Rose's bills, not among things he has seen.

**W085 · 2025-10-02 (Thu) · 13:00–16:50 · the_theatre · *Richard II* first played**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, augustine_phillips, nick_sorrell, thomas_pope, will_kemp, abel_garnish, margery_pell  
sources: calendar, body, screen (stage), photo, meeting  
Photo 42, an hour before the doors: the throne on its dais and a looking-glass laid on the seat. The glass has no business in today's play; the Master cut its scene in July and the deposing is only reported. Burbage put it there and will not say why, and Garnish locks it in the property chest at half past one. The Rose has set a new play against them, *The Disguises* (T1; 43s, which he never knows), and the town comes north anyway. He speaks Gaunt with heart 138, and "This royall throne of Kings" lands in a hush. Dead by the middle of the second act, he stands at the tiring-house door for the rest. Burbage sits upon the ground to tell sad stories and two thousand people lean toward him. In the fourth act the crown changes heads between two speeches, and the Abbot says "A wofull Pageant haue we here beheld" of a pageant the house has not been let behold; William's heart is 112, standing still. Kemp has the jig after and dances it like a man owed money. The boxes hold £11 6s. Margery Pell: "Citizens, their wives, clerks come before their masters, and half the Bankside. I never carried my box down so heavy, nor a yard so quiet." Steps 12,900; slept 8 h 20 after.

**W401 · 2025-10-03 (Fri) · 12:00 · the_jackdaw · Burbage will not come out of the king**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, agnes_tipping, will_kemp, hired_men  
sources: meeting, body  
Fish day at Tipping's: ling for the company, buttered eggs for the man who cannot chew. Burbage has not come out of the part since yesterday. The tapster is slow with the ale and gets it in full: "We are amazed, and thus long have we stood / To watch the fearful bending of thy knee." The tapster bends it. Agnes Tipping: "Kings pay the same as carters, Master Richard, and sooner." Raye, back from the Rose side, says the new play there had a fair yard and a restless one, with half of them asking the way to Shoreditch. Kemp salutes the poet across the table with a spoon: "Sweet Master Shakespeare eats like a nun and fills a house like a bear-baiting." William says, courteously, that he would rather it were like a wedding. Resting heart 66; the jaw is no better for fame.

**W086 · 2025-10-04 (Sat) · 17:00 · the_theatre · The week's reckoning**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, will_kemp, richard_burbage, james_burbage, cuthbert_burbage, abel_garnish, augustine_phillips, margery_pell  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger), photo  
After *Errors*, the boxes on the tiring-house table. Cuthbert Burbage and the gatherers unlock the slotted boxes one by one onto Heminges's green baize: pennies, half-groats, threepences, a few sixpences, and one clipped shilling Margery Pell swears she never took. The galleries alone this week come to more than the whole house took in a June week. Photo: the spilled silver heap beside the opened boxes, Cuthbert's hand still on a lid. Heminges has his Items ready: a crown new gilt, 3s; rosemary and a black pall for the coffin, 14d; "and a looking-glass at four shillings, bought in May and not yet looked in." It comes to 45s a share, the best week of the year. James Burbage says a house that takes eleven pound of a Thursday is a house worth a landlord's envy, and is steered off the lease by his son. Garnish reads his tally: "Kemp's interest in the king: nothing, there being nowhere to put it. In the jig I do not tally. It is all interest and no principal." Kemp: "Eleven pound six, and not a line of it mine. I'd have Sweet Master Shakespeare learn to empty a house. It's the rarer art." They fix the king for once a week, on a changing day: Tuesday next, then Thursday the sixteenth. William takes his forty-five shillings and enters them without a jest.

#### Week 41 · Mon 10-06 – Sun 10-12

**W087 · 2025-10-06 (Mon) · 05:40 · ansell_house · A birthday not marked**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo, calendar, screen (draft)  
The almanac has nothing against the sixth, and he knows what day it is. Before light he sits to the third act and for a while writes nothing. The memo at the window is low and short: "Two-and-twenty today, at Holborn or wherever the court lies. The year before last I sent a book with a name on the front of it. Last year another. This year nothing goes, and it will not be missed, and that's the whole of the matter." A breath. "No. The whole of the matter is that I've a girl waiting for night in the third act and must be at Shoreditch by nine." Nothing is written for the day and nothing goes to Holborn. The sheet on the board is Verona's, and by seven it has eleven new lines on it. He plays an old Roman at two and is dead by the fourth act.

**G076 · 2025-10-07 (Tue) · 09:00–10:30 · the_theatre · The boy and the author's certainty**  
people: william_shakespeare, nick_sorrell, augustine_phillips, richard_burbage  
sources: meeting, notes, body  
The king is played this afternoon for the second time, and the morning goes on the Queen's farewell at the Tower gate. William has written into Nick's roll, in the margin, *weep here*, and Nick will not. He is polite about it and immovable: he can weep, he says, but not before the scene has given him cause, and on Thursday the cause came three lines later. William explains, fluently, why the tears must come first, and hears the explanation fail halfway through, the way a line fails when it is only clever. Phillips, from the stage edge: "Give it him without the margin, and let's hear." Nick gives it plain. It is less to look at and more to believe, and Burbage, waiting on his cue, forgets to fidget. William strikes the direction out of the roll with the paring-knife's point and changes the instruction, not the boy. On his tables: "a direction is a guess. N. S. knew better." Heart 70 at the rehearsal; the jaw makes every consonant a negotiation.

**S096 · 2025-10-09 (Thu) · 14:00 · the_theatre · Michaelmas term: the Inns come back for the king**  
people: william_shakespeare, margery_pell, richard_burbage, will_kemp, abel_garnish  
sources: calendar, meeting, screen (bill), body, notes  
"Michaelmas term begins" in the almanac (T2·calc; it runs to 28 November), and by one o'clock the galleries are black with gowns: Gray's Inn, Lincoln's Inn, the Temples. The bill is the comedy, their old favourite, and they shout "Remuneration!" at Kemp as at a returning friend. But they have come for something else. Some were in town early and saw the king on Tuesday, and the rest have heard. Margery Pell: "Every third man asked at my stair when the king is played. I said Thursday next and they wrote it down like a hearing." One of Gray's Inn gives Gaunt's England back to William at the tiring-house door, most of it right, and asks if it is to be printed. "It's the company's, sir. Ask the book-keeper." Garnish looks at the young man as at a burglar. A Middle Temple man behind him has kept a different line, one William had not thought a lawyer's, York's warning to the king, "If you do wrongfully seize Hereford's rights" (normalised; re-key from Q1), and wants to know whether the king's council were truly so careless of a dead man's will; he means to argue it at a moot. The Rose's bill is *The Grecian Comedy* (T1). Heart 122 as Navarre; the jaw aches through every vowel. At night, with the jaw too loud for Verona, he takes a foul sheet some weeks old out of the folder and copies the sonnet on it fair on a single leaf, with no title, number or name. A builder may use the 1609 text of the one that begins "Farewell thou art too deare for my possessing". It goes back into the folder with the others. No addressee is stated in the journal, and none is to be supplied.

**W402 · 2025-10-10 (Fri) · 08:15 · bishopsgate · Master Yaxley looks in his mouth**  
people: william_shakespeare, barnaby_yaxley  
sources: meeting, body, location  
The cloves have stopped answering. He goes down Bishopsgate Street to the barber-surgeon's pole before rehearsal, and Yaxley sits him by the window and looks, humming. "There's your villain. Lower left, and he's dug himself a cellar. The gum's proud. That's matter gathering." He will not draw it with the swelling so hot. A hot bran poultice two nights, salt water, and Tuesday at ten. "Twelve pence, and you'll thank me after. Nobody thanks me during." William asks whether it could wait till Lent. "It could. You couldn't." He is not cast on Tuesday and takes the hour. Body: resting 68; slept 5 h 10; the left side of his face is a stranger's.

**W088 · 2025-10-11 (Sat) · 05:10–08:00 and 20:30 · ansell_house · The lark and the nightingale; three leaves kept**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gib  
sources: screen (draft), notes, memo, body, photo  
He has not slept since two, so he writes the lovers' one dawn at a real one. The girl speaks first, and lies: "Wilt thou be gone? It is not yet neare day: / It was the Nightingale, and not the Larke". The boy answers with the truth, "Nights candles are burnt out, and iocand day / Stands tipto on the mystie Mountaine tops", and then lies with her because she asks it. No lark sings in Great St Helen's in October, only rooks, and his own candle is at the socket by the time the pane goes grey. Eighty lines, down to "More light and light, more darke and darke our woes", and the Nurse at the door. At dinner Mistress Ansell has counted: "You burned three candles to the socket Tuesday. Three! I heard you walking. Was it the lovers again?" "Tuesday was the tooth. The lovers were this morning. They burned one." "And did they part?" "At sunrise." "Then they've more sense than you." The reckoning after *Titus* is 38s. Resting 70. That night, with cold rain on the casement and a woollen clout soaked in aquavitae held to his jaw, he does a thing nobody has asked of him. From his foul papers he copies fair, on three clean leaves, the scene the Master struck from the king in July: the crown handed across, the glass called for, "Give me the glass, and therein will I read" (normalised; the deposition text was first printed in Q4, 1608, so re-key from it). Garnish's book has its own three leaves locked in the chest; these are his. He folds them once and lays them in the coffer under the account book, where they are nobody's business. He does not say why, even to the memo.

**G078 · 2025-10-12 (Sun) · 16:00 · ansell_house · Soft food, hard company**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, gillis_verbeke  
sources: body, meeting, photo  
After evening prayer Mistress Ansell sets before him a supper chosen entirely to spare the tooth: a pottage of peas boiled to nothing, a sop of bread in warm ale, a baked apple. He complains about the texture of every one. She tells him the food has already yielded more than he has. Alse begins a riddle about what a barber-surgeon charges for a tooth and what he charges for the rest of the head, and is stopped by her mistress before the answer. Verbeke finishes it under his breath in Flemish, and nobody asks him to translate. William laughs, holds his jaw, and discovers that laughing is not what hurts, which improves his temper more than the apple does. Photo: the board by candle, the pottage bowl, the apple split and steaming, Gib's tail across the corner of the frame. Body: resting 69; abed by eight and awake at midnight with the jaw.

#### Week 42 · Mon 10-13 – Sun 10-19

**G077 · 2025-10-13 (Mon) · 07:00–10:00 · ansell_house · The tooth takes the morning; Cowley takes the part**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_cowley, dorcas_ansell  
sources: body, meeting, notes, calendar  
The fourth of the fierce nights: oil of cloves, wool in the cheek, two hours' sleep at most. He tries the Friar's cell at first light and finds that every character on the sheet has become unnecessarily irritable, the Friar most of all; he strikes eleven lines and keeps none. At nine Richard Cowley comes up as far as the foot of the stair, which is as far as Mistress Ansell allows, with today's part already conned, and asks only which of the marked cuts is current. William begins to explain the reason for the second; Cowley says, as he said in January, that a reason will not help an entrance. They settle it on the stair, roll against roll. William stays in, eats what Mistress Ansell calls broth and he calls water, and resents how sensible the arrangement is. In the almanac, against tomorrow: "Yaxley. x of the clock." Body: resting 72; steps 600 by noon.

**W403 · 2025-10-13 (Mon) · 18:30 · ansell_house · Call: Gilbert is twenty-nine**  
people: william_shakespeare, gilbert_shakespeare  
sources: call, calendar  
"Gilbert? Will. From Great St Helen's, with a face like a pudding. God give you joy of the day." Gilbert's day in the almanac. Gilbert: the money went down safe by the carrier of the second, and a Stratford wool-man in at the Bell on Saturday has brought word of it back, with a cheese, their mother's make, a whole one, and a note that half is William's. "You can't chew. I'll eat your half and call it my birthday." News with the cheese: the frame they raised in August is thatched and stands, "and Father says it's the first thing a player ever held up." William says the tooth comes out tomorrow at ten. "Good. You've others. Twelve pence?" "Twelve." "I'd have done it for six with the shop pliers. God keep you, brother." "And you. Keep me the rind."

**M038 · 2025-10-14 (Tue) · 02:15 · ansell_house · Clove oil at two in the morning: a weaver in the dark**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: body, memo, notes  
The worst of the nights, the one before the drawing. He paces the six steps of the chamber in his cloak with a drop of oil of cloves burning the gum numb, and between pain and the oil his head goes where it likes. It goes to the constable at Grendon in July, all big words; to the weavers' and shearmen's arguments over the old guild plays he heard as a boy; to Golding's Pyramus and Thisbe, talking through a crack in a wall. Then, half dreaming, a figure that is all three at once: a weaver who will play the lover and the lady and the lion, with an ass's head on him, very solemn about it. He laughs silently into his shawl until the jaw stops him. The memo is muffled and short: "A man with a head like— no. A man who'd play the wall if you let him. …Clove's working. Mark it, and sleep." It is an image, not a page: nothing is written but one word on the tables, "weaver", and he does not know yet what it is for. Body: resting 73; slept 3 h 30 in all.

**W089 · 2025-10-14 (Tue) · 10:05 · bishopsgate · The tooth drawn**  
people: william_shakespeare, barnaby_yaxley, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder  
sources: body, meeting, notes (ledger), memo  
A joined stool, a basin, a boy to hold his shoulders, and the pelican laid out on a cloth where he can see it. Yaxley is cheerful: "Hold the stool, sir, and think on your sins. It distracts." William gets as far as the second sin. Body: peak 148 in the chair at 10:05. Yaxley shows him the tooth in the basin, three roots like a small black claw, and asks if he will have it for a keepsake; he will not. Twelve pence. He walks the two hundred yards home with a clout to his jaw. Mistress Ansell has broth ready and has forbidden the stair to everyone, and Alse is sent up once with salt water and a look of awe. The memo at dusk is thick and slow: "Gone. A month's tyrant, and no bigger than a pea. …I gave a king a better speech at his going." Steps 1,900; slept 3 h 30 the night before; resting 73. In the ledger: "to the barber xij d."

**W404 · 2025-10-15 (Wed) · 11:30 · ansell_house · Heminges at the foot of the stair**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, dorcas_ansell  
sources: meeting, body  
Callers stop at the foot of the stair, and today the landlady stands on the second step to make sure of it. Heminges has a custard from his wife in a cloth and a question. The king is billed for tomorrow, the Inns have written it down, and Gaunt has a face like a loaf. William comes down in a nightcap to hear it settled. Pope will do it: he has nothing till the garden in the third act. "He'll die at Ely House and rise a gardener." "He'll get a laugh on 'this England'." "He will not. He had the roll last night, and Mistress Pope says he was near weeping at it." Garnish has cut the long cue for him. One thing more: the Rose has a new play today, something of a woman and a wonder (T1). "Let them. We've a king and a custard." Mistress Ansell: "He is to have the custard and no more talk." Steps 1,400; resting 72.

**S098 · 2025-10-17 (Fri) · 19:00 · ansell_house · Call: Sir Thomas Heneage is dead**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_florio  
sources: call, meeting  
"Master Shakespeare? Florio. From my lord's house in Holborn, and the house is in black." Sir Thomas Heneage died this day at the Savoy (T1): Vice-Chamberlain, Treasurer of the Chamber, and husband these eighteen months to my lord's mother. My lord is with her. Florio has a proverb for it in two tongues, that there is a remedy for everything but death. William says what is proper, speaking carefully round the gap in his jaw, and sends his duty to my lord and asks nothing. Then the thought neither says first: this is the office that paid the players in March. A name on an account has become an absence, and the absence changes everyone's practical work before it changes anyone's heart; nobody on either end of the call pretends to have lost a friend. "Who signs now?" "A widow must first cast up a dead man's accounts. After that, who knows? *Chi vive, vedrà.*" He has not forgotten the Chaucer and will send it when the house is quieter. "God keep you." "And you, sir." At the table below, Mistress Ansell hears that a great man is dead and asks only whether he made a good end. William goes up and writes one line for Heminges: "Sir Tho: Heneage dead. Who pays?"

**S099 · 2025-10-18 (Sat) · 17:10 · ansell_house · St Luke: earlier candles**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gib  
sources: body, calendar, notes (ledger)  
"St Luke" in the almanac. He walks up to Shoreditch for the reckoning only, since *Two Gents* wants no dukes of his, and hears how Thursday went. Pope's Gaunt drew no laugh, the Inns filled three galleries, and Heminges will not say the boxes were better without him. The share is 36s. His line of last night has reached Aldermanbury: Heminges and Bryan will wait on my lord's people at Somerset House within the week, so that the Christmas money is not stranded with a dead man's clerks. The sun is down before five now and the plays end in dusk; there is no inn-yard this winter, the Mayor having seen to that, and the Theatre will be played cold. Mistress Ansell lights the hall candle at five and enters the firing sixpence. He eats broth, reads nothing, and is in bed before the Bow bell with the cat on his feet. Body: steps 6,400; resting 66; slept 8 h 05, the first whole night in five weeks.

**G080 · 2025-10-19 (Sun) · 17:00–19:00 · ansell_house · The fair copy is not the last copy**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes, screen (draft), photo  
After church, with the jaw quiet for the first Sunday since August, he fairs what he has of Verona: the brawl, the feast, the orchard, the friar's cell, the fight and the banishment, down to the lovers' dawn of last Saturday. It is a coherent copy and it is not finished, and he leaves the rehearsal marks in the margins where they are, because he knows by now how much other people must do before *finished* is a useful word. He does not write it on the first leaf. He ties the leaves with a red thread from Mistress Ansell's work-basket and lays the bundle apart from the folder of private verse, on the other side of the board, so that no leaf can wander from one to the other. Photo: the red-tied bundle squared on the board, the folder at the far edge, his own hand flat on the bundle. Then he writes to Anne before he begins another correction, three lines against Thursday's carrier, so that the letter is not the thing that waits. Gib is on the Holinshed.

#### Week 43 · Mon 10-20 – Sun 10-26

**W405 · 2025-10-20 (Mon) · 11:00 · the_theatre · Call: Kettleby on the Christmas warrant**  
people: william_shakespeare, ambrose_kettleby  
sources: call  
"Master Shakespeare? Ambrose Kettleby. From Somerset House, among the hampers." The court removes to Richmond today (T1) and my lord with it. Heminges's question has reached him by three hands. Sir Thomas's place is not filled; his lady must render his accounts before any man can sign a new one; the Chamber will pay nobody anything till then. "You will be sent for. Whether you will be *paid* for is a question for another office." William asks whether my lord knows the players eat daily. "My lord says the Queen's debt is the best in England, and he says it with oaths I shall not carry." And the king play? "My lord has heard of it from four persons and disliked none of the reports. That is all I am to say." "God keep you, sir." "And you. Mend your hose."

**W090 · 2025-10-20 (Mon) · 17:00 · the_theatre · Sharers: who pays at Christmas?**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges, will_kemp, richard_burbage, george_bryan, thomas_pope, augustine_phillips  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger)  
After *The Shrew*, the tiring-house door shut. William gives them Kettleby word for word. Heminges, twice: "The— the matter stands thus. If we're sent for, we go. A company that asks the Queen for money down is a company that's not sent for twice." What it will cost is the boat up, lodging and the boys' meat for five days, out of a stock that holds £6. Then the question of the paper. Last year three men were named and fetched it in a morning. This year whoever is named will wear out a pair of shoes between Whitehall and wherever the Chamber sits. Kemp begins, "I was first on the—", stops, and thinks about the shoes. "Let the grocer be first. He likes a passage to wait in." Bryan will stand with Heminges, knowing which door to ask at. So it is settled, by nobody wanting it. William says he was second on the Queen's paper once and it was the only time he was ever read aloud. Pope: "And you were paid before Easter. Ah, the old days."

**G079 · 2025-10-21 (Tue) · 09:00–10:45 · the_theatre · The household inside the tragedy**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_pope, will_kemp, nick_sorrell  
sources: meeting, notes, body  
The jaw is tender for the last day, and he works on the small things that must carry the tragedy between its great ones: the Capulet house getting ready for a wedding, servants sent for cooks, a father who cannot stop giving orders, the curfew bell rung and the baked meats called for. He has the leaves on a trestle and three men to try them. Pope reads old Capulet's fussing plain and flat, without decoration, and it becomes more dangerous than any shouting: a man who will have his way down to the spices. Kemp, reading a serving-man, finds a beat nobody wrote, a pause before he answers that shows the whole drudgery of service without stopping the scene, and gets a laugh from Nick that he did not ask for. William writes the pause in. He sees that the house's pressure must be believed before the lovers' haste can outrun it, and that a kitchen in a hurry is part of the clock. Kemp, gathering his cloak: "You see? Give a man a pause and he'll fill it with a whole life." "I see." He does not promise more. Resting 64; the tooth's hole has closed over.

**G081 · 2025-10-22 (Wed) · 09:00–10:30 · the_theatre · Someone must copy the improvement**  
people: william_shakespeare, humphrey_larkham, john_heminges, nick_sorrell  
sources: meeting, notes, screen (part), photo  
The king is billed again for Thursday week, and since Pope's Gaunt there are two versions of the second act in the company's mouths: Garnish's shortened cue and William's own. Humphrey Larkham lays three rolls side by side on the trestle and shows him how one late change runs through them like damp through plaster: Gaunt's roll, York's, and the Queen's, since Nick's entrance hangs on the cut word. Nick would rather learn the new cue by ear this morning than wait for clean paper. Heminges, who has seen two versions of a speech survive a season and fight each other on the stage, says no: "Paper first, then mouths." William backs him, and backs paying Larkham the copying out of the stock, 8d, which is not generosity but the plain cost of being the author. Larkham marks each corrected roll with the small sign he and Garnish agreed in winter, so that a stale copy can be known on sight. Photo: three part-rolls unrolled under a weight, the same cue altered three times in three hands' worth of care. That night he writes the girl's night alone with the vial.

**S097 · 2025-10-22 (Wed) · 21:40 · ansell_house · Voice memo: an earl dead in the Tower**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo, body  
New moon; the close is black. Word has gone round since Monday that Philip Howard, who was Earl of Arundel, died in the Tower on Sunday (T2 for the day). He had been ten years a prisoner by the common count, and was never brought out to die. William has it from the Jackdaw and from a parish clerk, in lowered voices. The memo: "They did not hang this one. They waited. Ten years, and then a Sunday." A pause. "They say he asked to see his wife and the son born after they took him, and was told he might, if he'd go once to their church. And would not. I don't know that it's true. It's the kind of thing that's told." A pause. "Southwell in February, by the rope. This one by waiting. The same winter, near enough." Then: "My father will hear it at the shop door and say something merry about the price of lime." Then: "I've a king at Pomfret who peoples his prison with his own thoughts, and the yard pities him for a penny. …No. Too neat. Leave the man his own death." Nothing here is doctrine and no one is prayed for aloud. Resting 60; the jaw is healed.

**W406 · 2025-10-23 (Thu) · 17:30 · the_bell_carter_lane · Carrier day: a warning about Master Quiney**  
people: william_shakespeare, thomas_hobbins  
sources: notes (letter), location, meeting  
"Carrier in at the Bell." After the comedy he walks to Carter Lane by Cheapside in the last light, and finds the six packhorses steaming in the yard. Hobbins has the road in prices: oats dear at Oxford, malt dearer at home, the harvest in "and you could carry it in your hat". Anne's letter is one side. The money came. Susanna is reading the Gospel to her grandmother of an evening and corrects the curate under her breath. Judith has a new song off a pedlar and will not say what she gave for it. Hamnet and the Sadler boy have dammed the brook at Shottery and been made to undam it. Then: "Master Quiney is for London at Martinmas on the town's business and means to find you. He is a good man. Keep your purse in your other hose." William sends four lines back, the three written on Sunday and one more: the tooth is out, the king draws, he is well. Hobbins writes the letter into his list and reads the line back to him, which he did not do in the spring. Steps 13,300.

**M040 · 2025-10-23 (Thu) · 18:30 · pauls_churchyard · Supper near Paul's with Samuel Daniel**  
people: william_shakespeare, samuel_daniel  
sources: meeting, location  
From the Bell it is five minutes to Paternoster Row, where Simon Waterson the stationer (as in M007) has asked him to an ordinary to meet his poet. Samuel Daniel is quiet, scholarly and exact, and owns at once that he came to Shoreditch last week, in a plain cloak, to see the king played. He still mistrusts a penny yard. But hearing the boy speak the Queen's sorrow in the garden aloud, he says, made his own printed stanzas on the same queen seem cold marble beside it; he has been reading them over since and does not like them so well. William, who has read Daniel's *Civil Wars* standing at a stall until he bought it in April, says the stanzas taught him where to look. Over baked wardens and a cup of claret they talk of the second Richard as two craftsmen talk of a joint: where Daniel's chronicle bends, where the play cheats, which is the better lie. There is no edge in it. They part at Paul's Chain as equals, and William walks home by Cheapside more pleased than a supper warrants. The jaw manages the wardens and declines the crust.

**W091 · 2025-10-24 (Fri) · 09:00 · the_theatre · The potion, and Nick's voice**  
people: william_shakespeare, nick_sorrell, augustine_phillips, arthur_meeks, abel_garnish  
sources: meeting, screen (draft), memo  
He wrote the girl's night alone with the vial on Wednesday and has brought the sheet for Nick to try, on an empty stage with frost still on the yard rail. Nick reads it once to himself, exactly, then gives it. "I haue a faint cold feare thrills through my veines, / That almost freezes vp the heate of life". It is very good. Then she thinks to call them back, "Nurse!", and the voice goes out from under him: a crack, and a note that belongs to a man. Nobody laughs. Arthur Meeks, on a hamper, studies his nails. Nick stands quite still, then finishes the speech a third lower, without the call. Afterwards, to William alone: "Will she be long, Master Shakespeare? The lady. Will there be much more of her?" "Two acts. She sleeps through some of it." Phillips, quietly, at the door: "Send him to me mornings. It can be nursed. It can't be mended." The memo walking home: "Six weeks to the day it's wanted. I'll not cut her. I'll not write her a line lower. …Hold, boy."

**G083 · 2025-10-26 (Sun) · 17:00 · ansell_house · A letter without an anecdote to sell**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes (letter), screen (letter), memo  
The fuller answer to Thursday's letter, to wait in the coffer lid for the carrier of the thirteenth. He starts with a London story fit for a supper table, Daniel and the cold marble, and strikes it, because it is a story for strangers. Instead a joke only the house in Henley Street can take: that he hears the brook at Shottery has been undammed, and he will thank Hamnet not to begin on the Avon. Then the plain part. Christmas work will *likely* keep him at court and not at home. After *likely* he writes what he does know, that my lord's gentleman says they will be sent for, and when he will know more, which is when my lord says so. He does not soften it by promising a surprise or a Twelfth Night ride he cannot make. The memo, after: "Likely. A small word to carry a hundred miles. Better small and true."

#### Week 44 · Mon 10-27 – Sun 11-02

**W407 · 2025-10-27 (Mon) · 08:30 · ansell_house · The Chaucer comes from Holborn**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, gib  
sources: screen (book), notes, meeting  
A boy in my lord of Southampton's livery leaves a great black-letter folio at the street door with a slip in Florio's hand: "Lent, not given. *Chi presta, perde*: who lends, loses. Prove the proverb false." Mistress Ansell carries it up herself, to see what a lord's book weighs. He opens it standing, at the Knight's Tale: Theseus, duke of Athens, riding home with the queen of the Amazons to be wed, and two young men who see one lady through a window. He reads until the bell for nine. Beside it on the board lie Golding at the fourth book and the Plutarch sheets at the life of the same Theseus: three Athenses, and Verona not yet out of the house. On the tables: "a Duke to be married — four days — the old moon." Gib is on the Chaucer by noon, having tired of Holinshed.

**M041 · 2025-10-28 (Tue) · 08:45 · bishopsgate · An Italian bill at the Rose**  
people: william_shakespeare, augustine_phillips  
sources: screen (bill), calendar  
SS Simon and Jude in the almanac, and the new Lord Mayor sworn at Guildhall today (T2, custom). On the post by the Bull the Rose has a new title, black-letter: *Barnardo and Fiammetta* (T1, Henslowe's "ne"; 42s, which he never knows). Phillips, passing on his way up to Shoreditch with the lute in its bag, stops to read it with him. He has it from Raye that it is two lovers and a jealous old husband, out of Boccaccio or somebody's Boccaccio, and that the Admiral's tireman has been buying Italian sleeves. "Henslowe has smelt Italy, Will." William laughs aloud in the street: he has two Italian lovers of his own in a red-tied bundle at home and the vault of their deaths still to write. "Let him go first. The road's wide." The title goes on his tables among the Rose's bills. He is not cast today and goes back up to the desk.

**S100 · 2025-10-28 (Tue) · 21:00 · ansell_house · A page about ruined seasons**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell  
sources: notes, screen (draft), photo  
He is not cast, the friar's letter has miscarried in Mantua, and the tomb is not ready to be written. All day the Chaucer has been at his elbow: a duke to be wed, and the Golding, and two young men and one lady. At dinner Mistress Ansell lays the loaf to the notch, two fingers and more, and asks whose fault he thinks it is. "A married couple's, Mistress, who can't agree." "That is the first sound doctrine I ever had from you." So by candle, on a fresh quire of Field's Rochelle paper, he writes a page that belongs to no play he owns: a queen, not of any country, telling her lord that their quarrel has put the year out of joint. "The Oxe hath therefore stretcht his yoake in vaine, / The Ploughman lost his sweat, and the greene corne / Hath rotted, ere his youth attainde a bearde", and on, to a fold standing empty in the drowned field, crows fatted on the murrain flock, and the nine men's morris filled up with mud (Q1 1600; re-key every line). It comes easily, forty lines. He has last year's drowned summer to draw on, which everybody remembers, and this July's barley seen from the road, knee-high where it should be belt-high; in May he wrote the wheat down as an old man's beard. Warwickshire mud and a fairy's anger go onto one sheet and do not quarrel. Then her charge to the two of them: "this same progeny of euils, / Comes from our debate, from our dissention". He rules no head on the sheet. Around it, in the margin, the practical questions crowd in at once: who are these two, and whose wedding is it, and what lovers, and what workmen, and how many boys. No answer is written and no occasion is named; the page is a speech before there is a scene to put it in. The notes page is the specimen in § 8. The link of this speech to any real weather is T3; the day of writing is invented.

**S101 · 2025-10-29 (Wed) · 10:30 · old_swan_stairs · The Lord Mayor's show: Spencer out**  
people: william_shakespeare, davy_gannet, the_lady_unnamed, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, will_kemp, john_spencer  
sources: photo, location, memo, calendar  
Sir Stephen Slany goes by water to Westminster to take his oath (T2), and half the parish is down at the stairs to see a mayor who does not live among them. Photo 43: the Mayor's barge with the companies' streamers, drums on the water; and at the edge of the crowd on the stair-head a woman's dark head, turned away, laughing. She is laughing at something said by the person beside her, whom he does not look at long enough to know. It is nothing, and it is enough. Gannet, resting on his oars: "New Mayor! He'll pull down the playhouses by Christmas, the old one's to be an earl, and the Spaniard's in the Downs." A passenger tries to hire Gannet's boat to follow the barge and is told, without the oars stopping, that he may follow it in his own boat or swim; it is the least ceremonious line of the morning and the only one William writes down. Kemp, who has come down for the drums, takes off his hat toward Bishopsgate with enormous solemnity: "Farewell, thy sour worship. Go sweeten." Mistress Ansell watches the sword go before another man: "He went out as he came in, liking nobody. Well. He kept his own door." Ansell enjoys the order made visible; William enjoys the labour it takes to keep the visible order moving, the oarsmen, the marshals with their staves. Spencer's going is not a victory for anyone at the stairs. The City's fears will outlast him, and the next Mayor will have the same letters to write. That night the oriel of Crosby Place is dark for the first time since January, and there are no halberds at the gate. The memo: "A hundred yards, and it weighed like the whole City. A lamp put out. …And the other thing. Put that out too." A small cross in the almanac.

**S102a · 2025-11-01 (Sat) · 09:00 and 18:30 · st_helens_church · All Saints**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, dorcas_ansell, gillis_verbeke  
sources: calendar, meeting  
All Saints: morning prayer and a sermon before the play. Master Thwaite gives the saints as a cloud of witnesses, to be followed and not sued to, and is done in one glass, because the churchwardens have spoken to him. *Errors* at two; the reckoning is 35s. At supper the talk is the old one. Mistress Ansell remembers from her childhood the bells rung through this night for the souls, and how it was stopped. She does not say she misses it. She says her husband has been dead six years at Hallowtide and that she boils his ink every Monday by his receipt, "which is remembering enough for a Christian woman." Verbeke says that in Ghent they remembered both ways in one street, and it went ill. William says nothing and is asked nothing.

**S102b · 2025-11-02 (Sun) · 20:15 · ansell_house · Voice memo: All Souls, as was**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: memo, calendar  
The almanac prints no feast today; the old one has no place in the book. At the window after evening prayer, low: "The dead I talk to are in books. Old Ovid, daily. Kit Marlowe every time the Rose hangs out the Scythian, and they will again before the month's out. Tom Kyd, whose Spaniard still fills a yard. Greene, who called me a crow, whenever I pass the sign of the Jackdaw. I owe every one of them a line and have paid none." A breath. "If a man may not pray for them he may still steal from them. It comes to a kind of remembrance." Then: "My mother's mother baked soul-cakes at Hallowtide, spiced. My mother never baked them for us. She knows the words, though." Then he says half of something his mother says under her breath, stops, and does not finish it. He does not know whether the words come back from belief, or memory, or only because they are warm, and he does not announce an answer to himself either way. "…Enough. The Friar's letter. Why it never came." The memo names no one of his own, and has no cause to.

### November

#### Week 45 · Mon 11-03 – Sun 11-09

**S095b · 2025-11-03 (Mon) · 16:50 · the_theatre · Kemp's fan, and a turn with the fiddlers**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, richard_cowley  
sources: meeting, screen (draft)  
After the play Kemp corners him in the tiring-house, waving a scrap of paper on which somebody (Garnish, he suspects) has written what is coming to him in the lovers' play: the Nurse's man, a fan to carry, and a part like a dropped glove. Phillips's Mercutio has the bawdy and the fireworks and dies to a roar; Pope's Nurse has the whole second act; and the clown carries a fan. He demands a scene. William has written the house finding the girl dead in her wedding bed, and the musicians come for the wedding left standing in the hall with nothing to play. He sees at once what Kemp in that hall will do: three minutes of a servant quarrelling with fiddlers over a song, right beside the bed, and the tears afterward will sting twice as sharp for it. He sits on a drum with the board on his knee and writes the turn there and then, Peter and the musicians, with the fiddlers' names out of their own catgut and a tune Kemp may call for and not be let finish. Cowley, who will be a fiddler, reads over his shoulder and asks if he is to be paid by the insult. Kemp reads it twice. "It's short." "It's what the scene will bear." "It's a crust, Master Shakespeare." "Then it's a crust you'll make them laugh at." Kemp takes it, and the clown war is not over.

**W408 · 2025-11-04 (Tue) · 09:00 · the_theatre · Phillips retunes a queen**  
people: william_shakespeare, augustine_phillips, nick_sorrell, arthur_meeks, sam_latchet  
sources: meeting, screen (part)  
A fortnight of mornings. Phillips sits on the stage edge with the lute and takes Nick through Juliet's roll a full third below where he has pitched every queen since he was eleven. "Sing it on the breath, not on the height. She's fourteen, not a whistle." Nick has found the trick of it himself and reports it like an account: "I can hold it if I come in under the note, sir. If I come in on it, it goes." It makes her graver and older than her mother, and William, listening from the yard, finds he prefers her so and changes no word. Arthur Meeks has Juliet's lines by heart "in case" and speaks them along silently at the back, too well. Nobody tells Nick there is an in-case. Nick tells Sam, loudly, that a prince's page should learn to stand still. Phillips counts two-and-thirty days of mornings to the sixth of December.

**S103a · 2025-11-05 (Wed) · 10:30 · pauls_churchyard · Essex and the book of the succession, heard of**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: meeting, screen (tables), location, memo  
Not cast; a book morning, and Paul's Walk is louder than the stalls. On Monday at Richmond the Queen showed my lord of Essex a book printed over sea, of who should have the crown after her, with his own name set at the front of it for patron (T1). He came from her wan and pale, and has kept his chamber since, sick (T1, from a court letter; here it is three men's talk at once). Nobody in the nave has seen the book. Everybody knows what is in it. One says it reckons up how kings have been put by before, with examples. William stands with Daniel's third book open in his hand and does not turn the leaf. He has such an example in Garnish's chest: forty leaves, and three the Master cut. On the tables: "the K. — Shoreditch only. say nothing of the 3 leaves." The memo going home by Cheapside: "In June it was a chronicle. In November it's a pamphlet. I wrote it in between and never moved."

**S103b · 2025-11-05 (Wed) · 18:00 · split_hazel · The same news at supper, with a careful man**  
people: william_shakespeare, george_bryan, richard_burbage, alice_brambleby  
sources: meeting, screen (pamphlet), notes  
By supper the Split Hazel has the same story in better order, because George Bryan has had it from a Carey groom come down from Richmond, and Bryan keeps court news as he keeps manners, by the rule. The book is called *A Conference about the Next Succession*, put out over sea under a name that is said to hide a Jesuit's (T1 for the book; the name's owner is talk). It is dedicated to my lord of Essex, as the man likeliest to have a voice in the matter, and it runs through the old quarrel of Lancaster and the second Richard for its precedents. Bryan is careful to keep apart what happened, that the Queen showed my lord the book and he came out pale, from what is now being told of my lord's private feelings, which grows more confident every time it is told. Alice Brambleby, setting down the trenchers: "There's a man endangered by praise he never asked for. Say where you heard it, Master Bryan, and I'll know how much to believe." Across the board Burbage and William exchange one long look and say nothing. Dedications are acts with consequences, William thinks; he has signed two himself. He does not think his poems and a book about the crown are the same thing, and does not say so either.

**W409 · 2025-11-06 (Thu) · 16:50 · the_theatre · An old lord at the back**  
people: william_shakespeare, margery_pell, john_heminges, richard_burbage, abel_garnish  
sources: meeting, body, screen (stage)  
The king, for the sixth time, to a full house on a raw day. He is Gaunt again with a whole jaw, heart 134, and at the tiring-house door after. When the boxes come in Margery Pell has more than her line. "Gowns, citizens, two bishops' men. And at the back of the twopenny room an old great gentleman in a frieze cloak, with two fellows who'd liefer have been anywhere. Paid for three. Swore under his breath from the castle on, good round oaths, and at the prison he blew his nose like a sackbut." Heminges guesses a country justice up for the term. Burbage wants to know if he clapped. "He struck the rail with his fist. I took it for clapping." Nobody thinks further. William goes home to the vault; the candle burns to midnight. Steps 11,400.

**S104 · 2025-11-07 (Fri) · 16:50 · the_theatre · Call: my Lord Chamberlain**  
people: william_shakespeare, henry_carey_hunsdon  
sources: call  
The full script is the specimen in § 8. He is still in a duke's gown from *Richard III* when the voice comes, from Somerset House, over a roaring fire, and two good soldier's oaths in the course of it. The company is sent for to Richmond at Christmas, three nights: St Stephen's, St John's, Innocents'. My lord will have the Navarre comedy the Queen has not seen, the tragedy of the two lovers ("she likes a death if it's young and it's Italian"), and something old to laugh at with a cup in the hand. Not the king: there is a book about the crown with my lord of Essex's name on it, and no play goes to court this winter where a king is talked out of his chair. Then the surprise: "I saw it, did they tell you? I stood at the back like a carter. Your Burbage made me weep, the villain." On Heneage's office: not filled, "you'll be paid slow". Last: clean hose, all of them. William tells Heminges at once who yesterday's country justice was. A full moon tonight.

**W092 · 2025-11-08 (Sat) · 20:00–00:10 · ansell_house · The tomb, by one candle**  
people: william_shakespeare, gib  
sources: screen (draft), notes, body, memo  
Fog since noon (invented weather); the torches for the last act of *Titus* stood in haloes, and the reckoning, 34s, was counted by candle at four. Now the vault. In March he saw a link-boy pass a doorway and wrote it down for keeping: a light brought into a tomb, and the tomb made a feasting chamber by it. Tonight it is the boy's own word for what her face does to the dark: "For here lies Iuliet, and her bewtie makes / This Vault a feasting presence full of light." He does not walk it; nobody below hears a step. The drug, the kiss, five words to die on. Then her waking, the Friar's fear, the watch coming, and the dagger given its sheath. He stops there, with the families still to be fetched. Seated at the board at 23:30, heart 86; bed 00:30; slept 5 h 20; steps 9,900. The memo, at midnight: "He gets it wrong by a minute. One minute. That's all the villainy there is in it." The quarto text is used in an invented setting; no date of composition is a record.

#### Week 46 · Mon 11-10 – Sun 11-16

**W410 · 2025-11-10 (Mon) · 22:00 · ansell_house · The Chorus, written last; a blank filled**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (draft), notes  
After Egeon and supper, the first fourteen lines of the play, written last, for his own mouth. A sonnet, since the town has learned to listen to those: two houses, a pair of lovers, "the two houres trafficque of our Stage". Then he takes down the commonplace book and turns back to the page headed "Verona" on the sixth of March, where a line was left open: "alike in — worth? state? name? alike in [ ]". In November ink, browner than March's, he writes one word in the gap: "dignitie." Both houses, the same; neither the wicked one. He looks at the page a while. The Rose plays *The Disguises* today (T1), which he has never gone to see. Bed at midnight.

**W093 · 2025-11-11 (Tue) · 06:30–10:15 · ansell_house · Martinmas: the last line; winter stores**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder, gillis_verbeke  
sources: screen (draft), notes (ledger), calendar, memo  
Martinmas. Below, the house is laying in for winter: a quarter of beef to be salted in the tub, a sack of sea-coal carried through by two men, Alse threading rushlights. Above, the two fathers are brought into the vault to see what the quarrel bought. He gives the Friar forty lines to tell what everybody watching already knows, and lets him; someone must say it plainly to the living. The Prince, whom he will play, shuts the book: "For neuer was a Storie of more wo, / Then this of Iuliet and her Romeo." A rule under it at a quarter past ten. In the account book: "Verona ended this day." Then, the same pen: "a li. of candles iiij d." (his firing is in the sixpence a week; the coal is the house's). Mistress Ansell, when he comes down: "You stopped walking a week since and I feared you'd gone into a decline. Is it done?" "It's done." "Who's punished?" "All of them." She nods, as at a satisfactory sermon. The memo is two words: "Emptied. Good."

**S105 · 2025-11-11 (Tue) · 10:30–11:45 · leadenhall · Martinmas quantities**  
people: william_shakespeare, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder  
sources: calendar, notes, photo, location  
Coming down with the play finished, he makes the mistake of offering an opinion on what the house's winter is costing, and Mistress Ansell takes him to Leadenhall to learn. A side of bacon, suet, a firkin of salt, onions on a string, a cheese for the cold weeks, all weighed against what will keep, what can be stored on a narrow stair, and what Alse can carry. He is useful carrying and no use at all at guessing prices, and is told so at each stall. Back in the close the colliers are still unloading their black sacks of sea-coal beside the brine-tubs where the Martinmas beef lies salted, and he takes a turn with a sack because it is there. Photo: the frost-rimmed brine-casks by the house wall in pale November sun, a coal-heaver's basket set down beside them. The household book is Mistress Ansell's; his own tables get only "Martinmas. bacon is a mystery." He pays nothing toward the stores, the firing being in his sixpence, and gives his back instead.

**W411 · 2025-11-12 (Wed) · 09:00 · the_theatre · The book to Garnish**  
people: william_shakespeare, abel_garnish, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, screen (draft, plot)  
Fifty leaves, clean, into the book-keeper's hands before the king is played. Garnish reads standing and finds his faults inside ten minutes. "You have a Wife, a Mother, and a Lady in the margin, and I take them for one woman. Shall she have three rolls?" One: the Lady. "And here the fellow that carries the Nurse's fan comes on as *Kemp*. You have set down the player for the part. Shall I head his roll with his own name?" "He'd like it better." Then the first leaf. "'The two hours' traffic of our stage.' Master Shakespeare, I make it three hours, and it is a lie in the first fourteen lines." "It's a sonnet, Abel. They're allowed one." Heminges asks only how many parts and how many boys: twenty-odd, and three. The Rose has the first *Tamburlaine* today (T1), and the Scythian is in William's ear all through Gaunt.

**W094 · 2025-11-13 (Thu) · 10:00 · st_johns_clerkenwell · Tilney reads *Romeo* and finds nothing**  
people: william_shakespeare, edmund_tilney, john_heminges  
sources: meeting, location, notes (ledger, letter)  
Out by Moorgate with the book. The Revels Office is all tilt-staves and painted paper for Monday; the court comes to Whitehall tomorrow (T1). Tilney reads an hour and a quarter with the pen in his hand, and the pen never comes down. "A prince who is obeyed. A friar who means well. Two children who do not mind their elders, and die of it. I find nothing here that touches the state or any person living." "Nothing, sir?" "Would you have me find something, Master Shakespeare? I can, if you are lonely for it." He signs the last leaf. Then, capping the ink: "Your other play keeps within its bounds in Shoreditch, I hear. Let it." Heminges counts out the 7s. Walking home, William says he has been cut by that man twice and has never felt so slighted as today. Heminges enters the fee and says a clean licence is the only kind he can afford. It is carrier day; Gilbert has been to the Bell, and when William gets in there is a letter of Anne's on the stair and the letter of the 26th gone down in its place.

**M043 · 2025-11-14 (Fri) · 08:40 · bishopsgate · *A Toy to Please Chaste Ladies***  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: screen (bill), notes (tables)  
The court comes up to Whitehall today for Monday's triumph (T1), and the Rose has a new bill on the post by the Bull: *A Toy to Please Chaste Ladies* (T1, Henslowe's "ne"). He reads it twice for the pleasure of the title and does not cross the river for it; he is Egeon at two. What the title does is set him thinking of the citizens' wives in the galleries who will go to see it, and of what makes them laugh: not lords, but women of their own size quarrelling. On the tables, under the Duke's wedding of three weeks since: "two maids in the wood. one little, one tall. N. S. and A. M. — let them fall out about it." He has no scene for it. He has a pair of boys of the right heights, which is more than most poets have.

**M036 · 2025-11-14 (Fri) · 09:30 · the_theatre · Bars for the Capulet feast**  
people: william_shakespeare, augustine_phillips, richard_burbage, nick_sorrell  
sources: meeting, notes  
The book is licensed and the parts are not yet out, and Phillips wants the feast before anyone learns a line of it. On the empty stage, with the lute across his knee, he plays two measures of a stately Italian pavan in a minor key and stops. Together they count: sixteen bars for old Capulet's welcome and for Tybalt knowing a Montague's voice under the mask, and then the bass viol dropped to a whisper so that the fourteen lines the two lovers share at the pillar float clear over muted strings. Burbage and Nick stand where the pillar will be and speak it at half voice to the counted bars, palm to palm, and stop when the music stops. Phillips: "There. Now nobody in the yard will hear the hautboys, and everybody will hear the pilgrims." William writes the bar-counts in the margin of the feast leaf, where Garnish will find them. Nick's voice stays under the note the whole way.

**W095 · 2025-11-15 (Sat) · 08:10 · ansell_house · Call: Quiney, from the Bell**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_quiney  
sources: call  
"Loving countryman? Richard Quiney. From the Bell in Carter Lane, these two days, and my boots not yet dry." He is up on the town's business: a brief for a collection through the shires, for the relief of Stratford after its fires, with the town's certified schedules of loss in his saddlebag for the Lord Keeper's men. He has waited on three gentlemen's secretaries and is promised a fourth, and he looks for the seal within ten days. "Two hundred houses, Will, taken one fire with another." William was on a ladder at the burned row in August and counted about twenty; he says so, mildly. "This year, twenty. I speak of the town's whole affliction." Quiney would have a word carried to my Lord Chamberlain. William says my lord's gentleman laughs at players and would not weep for malt-barns. "Then a supper, at the least. I've news of your house, and I'll make it better in the telling. A taste now: your father's barley is dry in the loft, ten quarters, and your boy sent the master a sheet of Latin with never a blot, which the master holds to be a miracle of the Church." "Send when your gentlemen have done with you. God keep you, Richard." "And you, good countryman."

**G089 · 2025-11-16 (Sun) · 14:30 · ansell_house · The letter he can answer**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes (letter), screen (letter), photo  
Thursday's letter, read again after church. Anne has decided a household matter without waiting for him: a woman from Bridge Street is to do the great wash through the winter at 4d a week, and it is paid already out of the Michaelmas money. He is annoyed for the length of a paragraph at being told rather than asked, and then sees that he had no useful answer to give from a hundred miles off, and would have given a worse one. His reply does what she asked. It sets out how the Christmas money will be laid, so much for the house, so much for the shop's debts, so much she may spend without writing first, and asks after an ordinary thing: whether the children's string-and-spool engine from August still works, or has been forbidden. The page makes no attempt to win back authority with style. Photo: her letter open on the board beside his, her hand square and black, his crowded. It waits for the carrier of the fourth.

**G087 · 2025-11-16 (Sun) · 17:00 · ansell_house · What is already promised**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: notes (ledger), screen (ledger), memo  
Before candles he counts the coffer, both locks, as if it were Saturday. The sum has grown since Michaelmas, and he finds he likes it better before he counts it. Then he lists, in a column of their own, what is already spoken for: the Christmas quarter's rent and the 60s home; the hose my lord demanded; the journey up the river; a brief for Stratford that Quiney will certainly ask him to give to; and a loan of August not yet come back. What is left is a usable sum, smaller than the total and more honest. A house of his own stays where it has stood since August, an ambition with an address and no price. The memo: "Money feels freer before it's counted. …Counted, it's only money. Good. Better that way."

#### Week 47 · Mon 11-17 – Sun 11-23

**S106 · 2025-11-17 (Mon) · 09:30–17:30 and 20:00 · whitehall_tiltyard · Accession Day: the tilt, the bonfires**  
people: william_shakespeare, elizabeth_i, robert_devereux, henry_wriothesley_southampton, george_peele, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder  
sources: photo, location, body, memo, screen (tables)  
The Queen's day: no play, bells from first light, clear and cold (invented weather). Tower guns at dawn. He walks west past a throng at Paul's Cross, where the Bishop of London, Fletcher, preaches (T1), and on by Fleet Street and the Strand. Twelve pence at the tiltyard gate, and a place among citizens far down the barrier, where the yeomen and the marshal's men spend the whole day at the unsung labour of keeping a crowd where it belongs, with staves held crosswise and a great deal of patience. Ten pairs run (T1). My lord of Essex against my lord of Cumberland; and, cried by the herald so that he need not strain for the name, my lord of Southampton against Scudamore (T1). Photo 44: two riders meeting at the barrier, splinters in the air, the stands a blur of colour. He is a hundred yards off. The earl keeps his seat, as far as William can tell. My lord of Essex's device is spoken toward the Queen's window (T1), and nobody near William hears a word; by the talk going home it had a hermit in it, a soldier and a secretary, all pleading with my lord's squire, and the gallery liked it (T1 that it was much commended; the figures are table-talk). The Queen is a stillness behind glass at the far end, nothing more. George Peele goes by with a roll of paper under his arm and they nod like old soldiers. Home on foot by dark; the Tower guns; photo 45, a bonfire at the corner of Bishopsgate Street with every steeple going. Mistress Ansell allows Alse out to it: "That is how you light a fire. For something." The memo: "I was bid come and see him unhorsed. He wasn't. I came. He'll not know." Then he goes up and works. On the tables: "hard-handed men — a play for the Duke's wedding — they are afraid of the lion." Steps 15,800.

**W096 · 2025-11-19 (Wed) · 09:00–12:15 · burbage_house_holywell · *Romeo* read to the company; Pope's Nurse**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_burbage, thomas_pope, will_kemp, augustine_phillips, john_heminges, james_burbage, nick_sorrell, abel_garnish  
sources: meeting, screen (part)  
The long table, a fire, Garnish's basket: twenty-three rolls in six days. William reads the play, every voice, and reads Peter flat as a board on purpose. He gets as far as the Nurse's first speech before Pope takes it from him bodily. "I've dandled worse. Give me the child and a cushion for my bosom." Pope does the weaning, the earthquake and the fall backward with a napkin on his head, and the reading stops for the space of a paternoster; Heminges weeps into the reckoning-book and James Burbage has to be struck on the back. Phillips hears Mab again and smiles at the fire. Burbage is silent to the end, and then: "He's all haste. I'm seven-and-twenty." "Then play him at a run." Nick marks his roll and asks nothing. Kemp has been counting aloud since the second act. "Nine-and-twenty lines, Sweet Master Shakespeare, a fan to carry, and half a song I'm not let finish." William, courteously: "Keep Saturday supper free, Will. I've somewhat for you that's not in this play."

**M045 · 2025-11-20 (Thu) · 11:30 · fields_printing_house · Nine clauses, and a quire of paper**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_field  
sources: meeting, screen (book), location  
He goes to Blackfriars for another quire of the Rochelle paper, 4½d at trade, and finds Field with news from across the river: at Lambeth this week the Archbishop and the Cambridge divines have agreed nine articles on predestination, that some are chosen before the world was made and some are not, and that a man cannot by any doing of his own move from the one list to the other (T2 for the articles and the day; Field's having it so soon is this story's). Field is a sober Protestant and approves of the clarity. William, who was raised among people who kept their counsel, agrees courteously with a proverb about measuring twice and cutting once, and asks after the price of crown paper. Field is not deceived and lets it go. Walking home by Ludgate with the quire under his arm, he thinks of God locked into nine clauses like a tenant into a lease, and of a weaver who could not keep the parts of a sentence in their places if his life hung on it, and would be the happier man. He writes nothing of it down.

**W412 · 2025-11-20 (Thu) · 18:15 · ansell_house · Mistress Ansell's table: a bottom of thread**  
people: william_shakespeare, gillis_verbeke, dorcas_ansell, alse_pinder  
sources: meeting, notes, screen (tables)  
Mutton broth, the loaf two fingers short. He asks Verbeke what a weaver is proudest of, and for once the careful man talks. The shuttle thrown true. The beam, the reed, the thrum, which is the waste end left on the loom. And the bottom: the core you wind the thread on, so that a clew of yarn is "a bottom of thread", and a man who winds well is half a weaver. He ties the weaver's knot twice on a bit of packthread and makes William tie it. Three words of his own tongue, for bread, for thread, for good evening. William writes on the tables under the board: "bottom." Mistress Ansell demands the plot of whatever this is. A duke's wedding; tradesmen who make a play for it; and in the wood, fairies. "Tradesmen playing! Then it is a satire upon players, and I approve it. The fairies are popery." Alse asks whether the weaver gets to be in the play. "He wants to be all of it."

**W413 · 2025-11-22 (Sat) · 08:00 · ansell_house · Call: Quiney names a supper**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_quiney  
sources: call  
"Good countryman? Quiney. From the Bell." A week of ante-rooms, and he is hopeful; the town's paper is before those who can grant it, and he looks for an answer Monday. So Monday, supper, at six, at the Bell, "and I'll stand the wine if the answer's yea, and you shall if it's nay." He would have William bring any of his fellows that have a charitable purse. "Players' purses are charitable to tapsters, Richard, and to nobody else in Christendom." "Then come alone and bring your own." William hears Anne's letter in his head and laughs without sound. "Monday at six. God keep you." "And you. It is not a long supper I ask."

**W097 · 2025-11-22 (Sat) · 17:30 · the_jackdaw · Three pages for Kemp: the truce**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, agnes_tipping, richard_burbage, abel_garnish  
sources: meeting, screen (draft)  
After *The Shrew* and a reckoning of 28s, Monday having been the Queen's. He lays three sheets by Kemp's trencher and goes on with his eggs. Kemp reads standing, as a man reads a writ. Six tradesmen meet to cast a play: "Is all our company heere?" The weaver would be the lover, and the lady, and then "let mee play the Lyon to": he would be every part in it. At "This is Ercles vaine, a tyrants vaine" Kemp stops and does it once, in Alleyn's own roar, lion's skin and all, and Agnes Tipping drops a jug. Then he sits down. He reads the three pages again, sitting. "He'd play the whole of it himself and be loved for it. …That's me. Thou'st put me in, thou villain." It is the first *thou* in all the time they have worked together. "I put in the best of thee, Will." "And after?" "In the third act he's given an ass's head, and a queen falls in love with him." "With the head on?" "She'd not have thee else." Kemp laughs till he spills, and is charged double for it. Garnish, at the next table, closes his book on the tally.

**M046 · 2025-11-23 (Sun) · 15:00 · ansell_house · Yellow fog, and a bank of wild thyme**  
people: william_shakespeare, gib  
sources: notes, screen (draft), body  
A sea-coal fog comes up off the river after church and by three it is as thick as wool, yellow at the window, the church porch gone from the close (invented weather). He lights two candles in the middle of the afternoon, which Mistress Ansell will remark on, and out of the November murk he writes a Warwickshire June. It is the fairy king's, the speech that answers the queen's ruined seasons with a place where nothing is ruined: "I know a banke where the wilde time blowes, / Where Oxlips and the nodding Violet growes" (Q1 1600; re-key). He writes it for the sound first and the sense after, and it comes almost too sweetly, so he leaves it unstruck and does not trust it. The thyme is off the bank above the Shottery brook; the oxlips are from the lane to Wilmcote; the violets are anybody's. Gib sleeps on the Chaucer through all of it. Body: steps 2,600; resting 60.

#### Week 48 · Mon 11-24 – Sun 11-30

**W098 · 2025-11-24 (Mon) · 18:00 · the_bell_carter_lane · Supper with Quiney: two pounds to the brief**  
people: william_shakespeare, richard_quiney  
sources: meeting, notes (ledger), location  
A capon and spiced ale, and Quiney stands the wine: the brief for a collection is granted this day (T1 for the date; his having word of it by supper is invented). He is careful to say granted, not gathered: the paper may go through the shires now, and what comes back is another matter. Before the capon is carved he lays a pound on the board, in part of the two he had at Stratford in August, "for I'll not sup on a debt"; William counts it, and the account stands at one pound still owed. He gives the news of Henley Street as promised, improved. William's father was up a ladder at the new barns at past sixty, giving orders to men who obeyed them. The elder girl reads to the Sadlers of a Sunday. The younger sang at the Hallowmas supper and was asked for it twice. The boy ran a message to Shottery and back while Quiney was still finding his hat. Then the purse. William counts two pounds onto the board for the town's collection and asks to see it written. Quiney writes it, and goes on: "I may be bold with you as with a friend. It is not for myself, Will, it is for the town." His own London charges have run long, and ten pound till Lady Day would mend them. "I'll give two to the town, Richard, and lend nothing to the man, and we'll still be friends at Lady Day." Quiney takes it like a man used to the answer, and drinks his health. In the ledger: "had again of R. Q. j li., in part of ij li. he oweth j li." and "to the Stratford brief, ij li." Before he leaves the Bell he writes Anne an Advent letter of six lines, to wait for the carrier of the fourth: the brief is granted, Master Quiney was a good man, and his purse was in his other hose.

**W414 · 2025-11-25 (Tue) · 07:30 · ansell_house · Call: Heminges is twenty-nine**  
people: william_shakespeare, john_heminges  
sources: call, calendar  
"John? Will. From Great St Helen's. God give you joy of your day." Heminges's day in the almanac. He takes joy as he takes money, by the item: a wife, a full cradle, a house that does not smoke, a company eleven pound richer of a Thursday. Then business, since he is awake. The court: seven sharers' hose and the hired men's to be looked to before the old lord looks. The boys' court suits to be let out: Mistress Pope has let Arthur's hems down twice since Michaelmas and there is no more hem, and Nick's doublet wants easing at the chest, which Nick will not discuss. "And the voice?" "Phillips says it holds if he's not startled. So nobody is to startle him. I've told the household." Tomorrow they choose the three plays in form; he wants no surprises. "You'll have none from me." "You said that in June, with a deposed king under your arm. God keep you." "And you, John."

**S107 · 2025-11-26 (Wed) · 17:00 · the_theatre · Choosing the plays for court**

The apparel baskets are open and airing round the brazier, crimson and murrey court doublets brushed over a charcoal pan to drive the damp out; the tiring-house smells of old velvet and wormwood. Photo: the velvet hung on poles round the brazier, the light coming up through it. My lord's order is three nights, and it wants only fitting. St Stephen's: the Navarre comedy, which the Queen has not seen. St John's: the lovers, ten days old by then; Burbage wants the second night, for a house that has supped. Innocents': something old. Kemp speaks for *Two Gentlemen* and his long speech of the dog, the dog that is never brought on. Bryan: "She heard the dog at Greenwich. One does not tell her Majesty the same dog twice." So *Errors*, a year to the day from the afternoon they played it twice in two houses, or once, according to who tells it. Nobody proposes the wood-play: it is three pages and a speech, and it is not made ready by being wanted. Garnish is to cut the lovers to two hours and a half, the thumb-biting in the first scene going for her Majesty's ears; William undertakes to make no changes after the copying of the fourth unless a real playing fault demands one; the Master's rehearsal is fixed for the sixteenth, and Heminges and Bryan will be named for the money. Then the hose. Nobody owns to the heel my lord saw. Kemp accuses Pope; Pope accuses Cowley, who is not there. Heminges enters: "new hose, the whole company, of the stock."

**G088 · 2025-11-27 (Thu) · 18:00 · the_theatre · A workman who wants to do it well**  
people: william_shakespeare, will_kemp, augustine_phillips, humphrey_larkham  
sources: meeting, notes, photo  
After the play, with the baskets roped and the brazier sinking, he tries the next pages of the tradesmen on the three men left. One joke leans only on their being stupid, and Kemp stops it flat: "No. Thou'st a tailor there who can't read, and I'll grant thee he can't, but don't make him a fool for it. A man may get a play wrong and want with his whole heart to get it right. That's funnier, and it's kinder, and it's true." Phillips adds, tuning, that he has known a joiner who sang bass in the waits for twenty years and never once came in on the beat, and wept at every carol. William crosses the joke out. His notes turn from easy targets to earnest labour, vanity and a sort of courage: men who will stand up in front of a duke with a wall made of a man. Larkham, who will have to copy whatever survives, asks only how many of them there are. "Six. And a lion." Photo: the brazier's last light on three faces bent over one sheet. Kemp, going: "Thou'lt let them be men first. Then make them asses."

**S109 · 2025-11-28 (Fri) · 07:30 · ansell_house · Michaelmas term ends**  
people: william_shakespeare  
sources: calendar  
"Term ends" in the almanac (T2·calc). The court removed to Richmond yesterday (T1), and today the lawyers go home or go to Christmas, and with them the gowns that have filled the galleries since the ninth of October. Margery Pell's box will know it by Monday. He marks the turn the way he marks a quarter day, with a short line under the week: from here to Richmond the houses will be citizens and their wives, and the lovers must be ready for them first and for the Queen after.

**S108 · 2025-11-28 (Fri) · 14:00 · the_rose · *Harry the Fifth*; the term ends**  
people: william_shakespeare, edward_alleyn, philip_henslowe

The Theatre plays *Fair Em* without him. The bill on the post says a new play at the Rose, *Harry the Fifth* (T1; £3 6s to Henslowe, which he never knows; not his, nor any man's whose name is on it). He crosses the Bridge for the first time since August and pays his penny. It is a lost play, so only what the title gives: an English king of that name, a yard that knows before the first trumpet how it comes out, and cheering for a man who wins. Alleyn fills the house like water. Henslowe is at the door with his lips moving. William stands by the left-hand post and watches the yard. They are the same faces that go quiet for Burbage on the ground. They pity the king who loses and they shout for the king who wins, and they pay a penny either way. Home by the Bridge at dusk. On the tables: "the cousin's son. they love him." Steps 11,900.

**S110 · 2025-11-30 (Sun) · 09:00 · st_helens_church · Advent Sunday: four parts and a list**  
people: william_shakespeare, josias_thwaite, thomas_morley, john_spencer, dorcas_ansell  
sources: calendar, memo, location  
Advent Sunday, and St Andrew's Day with it, frost in the close. Thomas Morley has some of his boys sing a verse anthem in four parts before the sermon, and the cold stone lifts for the length of it. Master Thwaite begins on the four last things and reaches the second. Sir John Spencer is in his pew without sword-bearer or halberds, a rich man at his prayers, looking at nobody. Going out by the south porch in the press, William is still carrying the anthem's last chord and trying a line against it, and Mistress Ansell has to say his name twice in the churchyard before he hears her: is he unwell, or only a poet? The memo, in the afternoon, is the four parts again, hummed badly, and then: "Four voices, and not one of them telling the others what it means. …That's a scene. It's not a scene yet." Then, because December is coming at him like a cart, he makes a list of what must be done before Richmond and finds it so long that the month looks like nothing but business. He divides it in two, what must be done and what he would merely like to better, and strikes the second half. The list is shorter by evening and no less serious. In the almanac, against the twenty-sixth: "Richmond. xxvj dayes."
