|---|---|
| Hired man | 10s a week; halved to 5s through the dark weeks of August |
| Two boys hired by the week (Maria, Katharine) | 3s a week (`fab-2a`) |
| Tilney's licence | 7s a play (`[ext]` E06) |
| Wherry | 1d across; 2d to Whitehall |
| Carrier | 2d a letter; 6d a parcel |
| Horse from the Bull | 12d a day plus fodder |
| Dinner at the Jackdaw or the Bull | 6d |
| Ansell's board | 3d a meal |
| Paper | 5d a quire; 4½d at trade for Field's Rochelle crown paper |
| Ink | 2d the horn, from Mistress Ansell |
| Books | Brooke 6d (worn) · Daniel 2s · Plutarch 14s unbound · Southwell 6d |
| Tooth | 12d to Yaxley; 2d for cloves before |
| Tiltyard entry | 12d (`[ext]` E07) |
| St Thomas's dole | 2s |
| Cross Keys yard-money; the cart to Shoreditch | 13s 4d a week; 8d (`fab-2a`) |
| Family gifts | the silver thimble 2s 6d · gloves 2s 4d · ribbons 5d · riddle-book 2d (`fab-2a`) |
| Cheese | 2d the pound (29 May, `fab-2a`) |
| Tallow candles | about 4d a pound (S100; `[ext]` E33) |

#### 9.2.4 Money: company, coffer, debts and loans

| Item | Canon |
|---|---|
| **The £20** (S027, Sat 15 Mar) | Fetched that morning by the three named men from the Chamber's paying office in the Whitehall outer precinct (never inside the palace), who walk back by the Strand and Holborn. Costs £5: boat and baskets 16s, rewards 34s, the Cheapside mercer 50s. The remaining £15 in eight parts is **37s 6d**, for seven sharers (Shakespeare, Burbage, Kemp, Heminges, Phillips, Pope, Bryan) plus a stock share held by Heminges |
| **Weekly share** | Jan–Feb 18–30s · Lent 0 · Apr–May 25–40s · Jun 8–15s · Jul 10–20s · Aug 0 · Sep 25–35s · Oct 30–45s · Nov 25–40s · Dec 20–40s, court unpaid. Named weeks (`fab-2a`): 30s (22 Feb), 38s (26 Apr), 34s (3 May), 40s (17 May), 36s (24 May), 8s (28 Jun); 38s (week to Sat 25 Oct, S100) |
| **First-day boxes** (company gross) | *LLL* £7 11s (20 Feb) · *Richard II* £11 6s (2 Oct) · *Romeo* £12 13s (6 Dec) |
| **Canon Row** | £5 reward to the company from Hoby; 12s 6d a share |
| **Court, Christmas 1595** | Not paid in 1595. The T1 warrant is dated 21 Dec 1596, to Heminges and Bryan |
| **The coffer, exact on these dates** | 1 Jan £24 10s · 25 Mar £27 2s · 10 Apr £19 6s · 24 Jun £25 0s · 27 Jul £26 8s · **22 Aug £20 15s** · **29 Sep £23 10s** · **24 Dec £33 0s** · **31 Dec £33 10s**. Also dated in § 6: on 25 Mar £19 2s stays under the locks and £8 goes in the belt (the 60s in canvas and £5); on 10 Apr the 4s brought back makes £19 6s; on 27 Jul £20 8s stays under the locks and £6 goes in the belt; on 24 Dec 9s is kept out for the journey. Between these dates, plausible only. ⛔ `fab` § 9.2's older figures (£22 15s, £25 10s, £34 0s, £34 10s), `gem`'s £84 6s 8d and `gpt`'s £28 are all superseded |
| **Year-end account** (S116 page) | Coffer £24 10s on 1 Jan + received £55 14s (shares £51 14s, Hunsdon's 10s at Richmond, warrant 37s 6d, Hoby 12s 6d, Quiney £1) − laid out £46 14s = coffer £33 10s on 31 Dec |
| **Sent home** | 60s a quarter: carried by hand in Lent, when 42s of it goes to John's shops and Anne has 18s (W123, W029); by carrier on 26 Jun, 2 Oct, and with the Christmas pack on 18 Dec. Over and above, 30s into Anne's lap at Stratford on 8 Aug (W331) |
| **John's debts** | £5 of small debts paid at Stratford on Fri 4 Apr (W123) in three shops, without a word: the other baker 41s, the shoemaker 44s 6d, and the whittawer's glover's reckoning **14s 6d**. 58s of it comes from the coffer by way of the belt and 42s from the Lent quarter's 60s |
| **Lent, coffer to coffer** | £27 2s (25 Mar) − £19 6s (10 Apr) = **£7 16s**: horse, fodder and inns 38s + the 60s carried home + 58s of John's debts |
| **Edith's 8s** | Owed for autumn work, a prince's sleeves (G003, 10 Jan); paid from the stock share on Thu 20 Mar |
| **The Quiney loan** | £2 lent on Thu 7 Aug at Stratford, no interest; Judith Sadler makes the men say aloud whether it is a gift, a loan or a purchase. £1 repaid at the Bell supper, Mon 24 Nov. £1 still owed at year's end |
| **Gifts** | 20s toward the Sadler cousin's barn (August) · £2 to the fire brief (24 Nov), when he declines to lend Quiney more |
| **Henslowe's loans** | Blackwage, Raye and Hoope owe Henslowe (T1); William never knows the sums |
| **Kemp's interest** | Garnish's tally of Kemp's added lines: 11 on 20 Feb, 24 after two days, 35 and "one whole song" after three. William writes nine into the book on 12 Mar (`fab-2a`) |
| **Journeys** | Lent: horse 12d a day plus fodder, about 38s in all with the inns. Summer walk: about 11s out and 9s back |
| **Out** | ⛔ Any price for New Place. ⛔ `gem`'s 40s loan to Sadler, its 10s to the brief and its 4s 6d Martinmas coal. ⛔ `gpt`'s 26s quarter and £2 advances. ⛔ Any Southampton gift |

#### 9.2.5 Dates fixed by the canon

- **Works.** *LLL*: finished 21 Jan, licensed 24 Jan, parts out 28 Jan, first played Thu 20 Feb (Cross Keys), last winter play Shrove Tue 4 Mar. *Romeo* Lent notes from 6 Mar. *Richard II*: Holinshed from 19 Mar; begun Thu 17 Apr; hollow crown 17 May; deposition drafted 10 Jun; finished 21 Jun; read to the fellows 30 Jun; licensed with the deposition cut Thu 10 Jul; first played Thu 2 Oct; played whole at Canon Row Tue 9 Dec. *Romeo*: drafting from Sat 5 Jul; Mab 15 Jul; feast sonnet 18 Jul (M020); orchard scene 9 Aug; Mercutio dies 6 Sep; dawn parting 11 Oct; the potion and Nick's crack 24 Oct; tomb 8 Nov; finished Tue 11 Nov; licensed untouched 13 Nov; read 19 Nov; first played Sat 6 Dec. *Dream*: first page Tue 28 Oct (S100); the mechanicals from 22 Nov; unfinished on 31 Dec at about twelve leaves. Sonnets tied in a tape on 13 Dec. Court: 26 Dec *LLL*, 27 Dec *Romeo*, 28 Dec *Errors*.
- **The company's year.** It plays on to Sat 26 Jul (no play 30 Jun or 24 Jul), is dark 28 Jul–23 Aug, and reopens Mon 25 Aug. It plays at the Theatre through the next winter, with no inn. It does not tour.
- **Trips.** Lent ride out 26–28 Mar and back 8–10 Apr. Summer walk out 28–31 Jul, at home to 18 Aug. Return 18–21 Aug, home Fri 22 Aug. Richmond 24–29 Dec. Details in canon § 5.4.
- **Illnesses** (the only ones). The cold 13–19 Jan (he plays on the 17th; Cowley covers on the worst day). The back on 12 Aug (flat to the 15th; a bad day on 27 Aug; eased by about 15 Sep). The tooth: ache from 9 Sep, abscess 10 Oct, drawn Tue 14 Oct, jaw tender to the 21st. Travel soreness is not illness. ⛔ No child is ever ill.
- **The carrier** is in at the Bell on 16 Jan · 6 Feb · 27 Feb · 20 Mar · 24 Apr · 15 May · 5 Jun · 26 Jun · 17 Jul · 11 Sep · 2 Oct · 23 Oct · 13 Nov · 4 Dec · 18 Dec, plus an unscheduled Mon 21 Jul with Anne's fire letter. ⛔ `gem`'s Monday-arrival schedule is superseded.
- **Second Stratford fire.** Tue 15 Jul (a T5 date on a T1 fire): about twenty houses and malt-barns in a row north of the centre; the family untouched. Gilbert calls an hour before the letter with a garbled version.
- **News heard.** Raleigh's sailing 11 Feb · Southwell's trial 20 Feb at supper · Clontibret about 18 Jun · the Stratford fire 21 Jul · Cornwall 26 Jul · Drake's sailing 4 Sep · Raleigh home 22 Sep · Essex and the succession book 5 Nov · *Edward III* entry 1 Dec. Hawkins's death never.
- **Others.** Nashe suppers on 2 Mar (the Bull), in mid-May, and in mid-July before Mab. Daniel supper in October. Covell shown by Field Thu 18 Sep. The *True Tragedy* octavo on a stall Sat 31 May. The riot-execution pamphlet on a stall Sat 26 Jul. Florio's call with Heneage's death Fri 17 Oct; the Arundel memo Wed 22 Oct. Cuthbert's call with Allen's £24 demand against £14, Sat 10 May. James Burbage speaks of the Blackfriars hall on 3 Dec and confides the £600 plan in mid-December. New Place is named by Anne on Sat 16 Aug ("That one, then"): no inquiry, no price. The arms are talked of in Lent, with no filing and no promise.
- **Calls.** 34 in the year, never more than two a week, none while he is away; the dated list is § 7.3, re-synced to § 6 in the final pass. January–June has 12 (Heminges 4, Field 3, Gilbert 2, Cuthbert 2, Florio 1), July–September 10 and October–December 12, including S091 and S104. By caller: Heminges 8, Field 5, Cuthbert 4, Gilbert 4, Kettleby 4, Florio 3, Quiney 2, Burbage 2, Hunsdon 1, Southampton 1.
- **The lady's crosses: seven in the year.** 5 Jan (W002) · 28 Feb (W018) · 22 Mar (M009) · 22 May (W044) · 8 Jun (W048) · 14 Sep (W336) · 29 Oct (S101). Advent Sunday, 30 Nov (S110), is not one. Her only speech in the year is six words on 22 May. "I had thought you taller" is unused and free.
- **Family-day gifts.** Candlemas: a groat each "to be spent foolishly", with comfits and a letter. 28 Mar: Burbage's old lath sword for Hamnet, ribbons, a riddle-book, gloves. 26 May: the silver thimble (sent by the 15 May carrier so that it arrives on the day; the letter written on the 26th goes on 5 Jun). Christmas: spices, oranges and cloth, with holland linen for Joan, sent 18 Dec with the quarter's 60s.

William plays on about four afternoons in six.

#### 9.2.7 Recurring objects

The coffer (oak, two locks). His father's glover's paring-knife, used as a penknife. The penny almanac. The writing-tables. The grey cloak. Field's Holinshed, never returned. The bread-board with the notch. The cushion Anne made. Burbage's lath sword, Hamnet's from 28 March. The silver thimble. The sonnet papers and their tape (13 Dec, at the bottom of the coffer). The brass inkhorn and pounce-box. Field's Rochelle crown paper. John's yellowed 1568 heraldic sketch, a wish shown once and never a filing. The oiled canvas road-wallet, his pack in July, with its left strap that wants shortening (W073a). The latch, mended 22 Dec. The red thread round the fair *Romeo* bundle from 19 Oct. The paper wedge from under the stool, 27 Feb, kept as a private joke. The part-copy marks agreed with Larkham and Garnish. Hamnet's wooden wheel, repaired in April. The string-and-spool contraption the children build in August. Kemp's dog is a story only (it travelled in the Christmas boat): ⛔ no dog ever appears on any stage.

#### 9.2.8 Running jokes

1. "I am in pain." "Where?"
2. Kemp's interest: Garnish's weekly tally.
3. "First on the paper."
4. Heminges's "Item".
5. The 31st of April, which is the company's word for never.
6. The loaf and the notch.
7. Gib on the needed book.
8. Burbage still in the part.
9. Field's Holinshed.
10. The Jackdaw and the upstart crow.
11. "Sweet Master Shakespeare", from 18 Sep.
12. Mistress Ansell's verdicts. "Bad example" is used on 25 Jun (W138); vary the wording after that.
13. Davy Gannet's news, wrong by a third.
14. Hunsdon and the hole in the hose.
15. Sincler needs no padding.
16. Thwaite's "fourteenthly".
17. Arthur's ankles outgrowing his hems.
18. The Grendon constable's big words.
19. Expected money eating dinner.
20. A prince who still owes Edith for his sleeves.
21. The landlady and the weather on the stair ("I let you the room. I did not undertake that the weather should remain outside the stair").
22. His long explanation, less use than the missing date.

⛔ Do not repeat any of them mechanically.

#### 9.2.9 Habits, tells, body and speech

- **Habits.** He writes in two sittings, first light to dinner and by candle after supper. He says lines aloud first, and Ansell can tell comedy from tragedy by his feet. He carries the writing-tables everywhere. He reads standing at stalls and buys less than he reads. He eats little before a play. He counts money on Saturday nights and writes home after the carrier comes in. He trims a quill when a conversation turns uncomfortable, smooths paper that is already smooth, and checks the tie of his purse after bad money news.
- **Tells.** He rubs the inked forefinger against his thumb when lying or sparing someone. He goes very courteous when angry. He laughs silently, with his shoulders only. He says "Go on, I hear you", and does not hear. He repeats a pleasing word through the day until it turns up in a speech. His right thumb taps four iambs on his thigh, or a board's edge, when a phrase strikes him. Cornered on politics or religion, he agrees courteously with a proverb and changes the subject.
- **Body.** About 5 ft 7 in (170 cm) and 70 kg. Brown hair drawing back from a high forehead, thin at the temples. A short beard he trims himself. Hazel eyes. Walker's calves. Ink on the right forefinger and the heel of the hand. No earring. A plain murrey wool doublet on workdays; a black velvet jerkin for patrons and court. Resting heart rate 58–62.
- **Thou and you** (`fab` § 3.1). Burbage: *thou* both ways. Kemp: *you* until Kemp first says *thou*, on 22 Nov. Heminges: *you*, "John". Phillips, Pope, Bryan: *you*, first names. The boys: *thou*, "Nick", "lad". Field: *thou* in private, *you* in the shop. Anne and the children: *thou*. His father: *you*, "sir" when it counts. Hunsdon and Southampton: "my lord", never *thou*. Mistress Ansell: *you*, "Mistress". The lady: *you*, and almost nothing aloud.

| 22 Feb | Late snow |

| 28 Mar | Rain at Long Compton |

| 20 Apr | Soft |

| 24–28 Dec | Clearing to frost |

There is never a flood, and never "the wet summer" of 1595. The harvest is thin, with no cause given. ⛔ Never generate weather from actual 2025 conditions.

### 9.3 Continuity rules

*Merged from `fab` § 9.3 (16 rules), `gpt` § 9.3 (18 checks), `gem` § 9.3 (7 locks) and the canon. A builder checks every artifact against all of them.*

**Time**

1. **Dates.** Every entry is `2025-MM-DD` with the correct 2025 weekday; never add ten days. Headings and documents say "1594" until 24 March and "1595" from Lady Day, and may write "1594/5". ⛔ No 1995 mapping survives anywhere.
2. **Sundays.** No public playing, at the Theatre, the Cross Keys or the Rose, and he goes to church. The only Sunday performance is the court night of 28 December. Henslowe's Sunday dates are slips, moved to their corrected weekdays.
3. **Times** are floating local time, with no summer time, rounded to 5 or 15 minutes. Daylight and moons follow the 1595 calculations.
4. **News lag.** An event enters the journal on the day he hears of it, at the canon's hearing dates (§ 9.2.5). He knows nothing of 1596.

**Grief care, illness and harm**

5. **Hamnet** is ten from 2 February (nine before it) and ordinary in every appearance. ⛔ No illness, premonition, "last", parting frame, lingering shot, health adjective, grave or churchyard near the children, or child-mortality talk at All Souls. The three children get equal weight. Nothing echoes the novel or the film *Hamnet*. Nobody says "Agnes". Hamnet Sadler is a different man.
6. **No plague.** The word appears only as something past ("since the sickness").
7. **Illness** is only the canon's three episodes. ⛔ `gem`'s April saddle-strained back is downgraded to soreness, and its 11–15 October tooth dates yield to the canon's.
8. **Executions.** He is never at Tyburn or Tower Hill on the day. 21 February and 24 July are a memo and silence. The empty gallows-frame before Crosby Place (photo 27) is the limit of what is shown. ⛔ Never gore, a scaffold view, torture or a victim's invented last thoughts.
9. **Riots.** He walks wide or stays in, and is never in a crowd that is doing harm. He sees the open Bedlam gate, not the rescue; Leadenhall the morning after, not the butter riot; the barred door, not Tower Hill.
10. **Radius of fear.** From 13 June to 5 July his tracks stay within 600 m of home, except the Theatre road. He does not cross the river between 12 June and 19 July. (Midsummer morning, S065b, is kept inside it: the doorways of the close.)

**The intimate threads**

11. **T3 stays T3.** The lady is never named, never "kin to court musicians", and never an affair. The lord is patron and dedicatee and nothing stated beyond. Southampton is always "my lord". ⛔ No verse is addressed or delivered to a named real person, and no hidden alias ties the sonnets' *you* to anyone. No sex scenes. No Catholic practice shown as fact, only inherited texture, stopped prayers and a bought book. No secret Mass, no priest-hiding, no kinship to Southwell. No photo supplies a recognisable Dark Lady.

**Works**

12. **No composition date is ever presented as a record.** Before 5 July the *Romeo* stream holds only Brooke extracts, name lists, a plan and single images, with no dialogue and no line of the play.
13. The deposition exists from 10 June, is cut for the public stage on 10 July (T5), and is played whole once, privately, on 9 December (T3 choice). *Love's Labour's Won* is only a title Heminges asks for. ⛔ No Hand D or *More* scene, no 1595 *King John*, and no five-act or finished *Dream*. ⛔ The Carey–Berkeley wedding is never tied to the *Dream*.
14. **Out entirely.** *Galfrido and Bernardo* (the Rose's 18 May is blank) · Simon Forman · the Davenants and the Crown by name · the Mermaid "club" · Ben Jonson onstage · any arms filing or 1596 arms promise · any purchase, price or inquiry for New Place · any tour · Hawkins's death · the "wet summer of 1595" · a penny loaf at 2d · a compulsory Wednesday fish day · everything in source book 06 § 5 marked to avoid.

**Money and objects**

15. **Money never contradicts § 9.2.4.** The coffer counts are exact on their dates and plausible between them. Expected, owed and received are kept distinct. The £20 is company money, not personal income. Court money is still unpaid on 31 December.
16. **Objects follow their chronology:** Brooke from 6 Mar, the Holinshed from 19 Mar and never back, the sword with Hamnet from 28 Mar, the red thread from 19 Oct, the paper wedge from 27 Feb, the pack in July, the latch mended 22 Dec, the tape on 13 Dec.

**The capture**

17. **Nobody remarks on a device** or speaks for the journal's benefit. "Remember:" or "Mark this", never "note to self". No modern idiom appears in captured content. Labels use plain modern English; everything inside is period.
18. **Meetings** need William present among two or more people: 25–60 lines, up to seven speaking voices, at least one concrete number, crowds never named speakers. Performances are not meetings.
19. **Calls** have two voices and stay within running-boy range: the City, Shoreditch, Bankside, Holborn, the Strand, Westminster. ⛔ None to or from Stratford, the road, Richmond or Greenwich, and none during 26 Mar–10 Apr, 28 Jul–21 Aug or 24–29 Dec. Never more than two a week. None missed and returned. The caller names himself and his place.
20. **Memos** are William alone, 30–120 s, never addressed to anyone, ending abruptly. They know no future and report no execution as seen.
21. **Screens** carry one `surface` tag. `proof` appears only at Field's. Bills are black-letter, title only, and never name an author. Pamphlets are real 1595 imprints only.
22. **Watching** requires him to be physically present. He cannot act and watch at once, never watches on a Sunday, and never between 12 June and 19 July. The Rose attendances are canon (14 Feb, 20 May, 29 Aug, 28 Nov) plus up to four more on days he is not cast. A report or a bill is not watch history.
23. **Photos** are possible human views from his eye: no face, mirror, reflection or shadow of his; no aerial or inaccessible vantage; no news event reconstructed; no lens character; no added captions.
24. **Body.** Recorded daily and never remarked. Horse and boat miles are not steps. A raised heart rate is never a diagnosis. All values are T5.
25. **Location.** News places are never points, and nor is `tyburn`. `tower_hill` has a point only on 1 February. One house is one point. Missing data beats teleporting. No later bridges.

**People and text**

26. **Speech.** Thou and you as in § 9.2.9. Oaths are period. Kemp and William use *you* until 22 November.
27. **The Mayor.** Spencer never speaks to William. After 29 October the Mayor is Slany, who is not a neighbour and not a character.
28. **Nick's voice.** Sound to 23 October; first crack on 24 October; managed through 27 December; it breaks on 28 December, in the jig after *Errors*. After that he does not sing.
29. **Henslowe's dates** are kept with corrected weekdays. Receipts come only from SB04 § 5. Illegible ones are "not known". William never knows a Rose receipt.
30. **Invented people** keep the `[invented]` flag in all metadata. Nobody living is depicted, named, alluded to or voice-cloned; voices are stock. A scene's T5 invention never inherits a T1 label from its anchor.
31. **Quotation.** Only Shakespeare and other pre-1700 texts, in old spelling, flagged "public-domain text in an invented setting", and re-keyed from the early edition. The bible's invented dialogue is never presented as historical quotation. Store the diplomatic image and the normalised transcript separately.
32. **Release.** Any external use of this material is reviewed before it is used.

**End-of-year state (31 Dec).** Coffer £33 10s; court money unpaid; Quiney owes £1; the Holinshed not returned; the *Dream* unfinished at about twelve leaves; the sonnets tied in their tape at the bottom of the coffer; Nick no longer singing; Kemp on *thou* terms.

### 9.4 To verify before anything ships externally

#### A. Every `[ext]` item

All of E01–E34 in § 9.1a, each against a primary source or an authoritative compilation. On failure, use the listed fallback. The priority checks, because they carry the most weight in scenes:

- E04, the Southwell crowd's cry (S019).
- E05, the Antwerp imprint (S104).
- E06, Tilney's 7s, which is in the money canon.
- E12, Kemp as Peter.
- E13, the name "Mothering Sunday".
- E24, the Tyburn-avoiding lanes.
- E29, the court line (S091).

#### B. Source-book items held as unverified

| Item | Status in the source book | Where it bites |
|---|---|---|
| Hoby's letter of 7 Dec 1595, its wording | Not verified (the BHO Cecil calendar would not load) | S112a, W101 |
| The names of the five executed on 24 Jul | From one secondary source, unchecked | Cast list of the offstage named |
| The *Edward III* entry of 1 Dec 1595 | Not verified | S111 |
| Henslowe "ne" dates other than 7 and 23 May; illegible receipts | Check against Foakes | Every `bill` screen |
| *Crack Me This Nut*: 5 Sep "ne", then 12 and 24 Sep | Settled from Greg only | Rose bills in September |
| Clontibret from a primary source | Not checked | News, about 18 Jun |
| The Admiral's summer tour (towns) | Nicol only | News |

#### C. Quotations to re-key from the early text

#### D. Invented names

- **Done.** The 17 invented people in § 9.2.1 were web-checked on 2026-11-15 and all are clear. Roger Kelsett (mapped away) is clear. William Greenaway is removed, not checked: ⛔ he must not appear.
- **Still to check:**
  - The invented signs. The Jackdaw, the Split Hazel, the Feathers (Richmond) and the Catherine Wheel (High Wycombe) need a check for real 1590s inns of those names at those places.
  - **The Bear at Woodstock** `[ext]`. Kept (final pass, decision 13). A long-established inn of that name stands in Woodstock, but its existence in 1595 is unverified, so it is no longer labelled an invented sign. Check a 1590s record; on failure, leave the inn unnamed ("an inn at Woodstock").
  - The Reindeer at Banbury (E15).
  - Every new walk-on name added by later builders.
- **Hold.** The Cheapside mercer stays unnamed; `gem`'s "Master Campion" collides with the poet Thomas Campion.

## 10. Open choices

These are the decisions the world makes that the editor may want to revisit. They are revision candidates, not licence for a downstream builder to choose independently. **A lighter build may omit captures, but it may not change the life:** whatever is chosen, he travels on the fixed dates, owes the same money, keeps the same rooms and reaches December with the same unfinished work. Items marked **[design]** are the three conflicts the canon pass left for the editor.

### 10.1 The capture

**1. [design] What a call is.**
- *Choice made:* an unexplained voice at a distance, limited to messenger range (London and Westminster): about 34 calls in the year, distributed as in § 7. It gives two-voice audio for text-to-speech, and lets a patron speak in his own voice, while keeping the letter-and-carrier lag that drives the Stratford story. It is the one modernity choice a viewer will feel.
- *Alternatives:* (a) **Threshold calls only** (GPT's model): about ten brief in-person exchanges at a doorway, shop or stage entrance, with no remote audio at all, so that nothing in the world is impossible. The cost is that calls become hard to tell from doorstep meetings. (b) **A messenger at the door speaking his master's words:** more period, but weaker audio and no patron's own voice. (c) **Wider range:** a few calls with Anne, which would be the most moving surface possible, or two urgent calls from Warwickshire (Gemini's Gilbert in May and Quiney on the fire). Either breaks the fire-letter plot and the year's sense of distance.
- *Effect of changing:* (a) turns the roughly 34 calls into ten doorstep scenes and meetings, and moves S091 and S104 to physical entrances (Southampton's household door, Somerset House). (c) requires re-plotting 21 July.

**2. Segmented winter sleep.**
- *Choice made:* "first and second sleep" on about a third of winter nights (T3, used knowingly). It gives a distinctive body signature and the dark-hour memos.
- *Alternative:* plain consolidated sleep all year, which removes about twelve winter memos.

**3. The gallows at the Mayor's gate as a photo (S067).**
- *Choice made:* the empty gallows-frame before Crosby Place on 27 June is photo 27 in § 6 (Fable's base numbering; shot 47 in § 7.7's list), with nobody near and the shutters closed. It is a T1 event a hundred yards from his door, and not an execution.
- *Alternative:* describe it in a memo only, with no image.

**4. How dense the build is.**
- *Choice made:* the full year, with every dated entry from § 6 plus generated ordinary routine, and continuous body and location data.
- *Alternative:* a lighter first journal with only the 33 light-cut seeds and the continuity scenes they need.

**5. The company's full bill on the calendar.**
- *Choice made:* the week table gives the company an invented repertory for every playing day, because builders need a bill for each one.
- *Alternative:* mark only "Play", with titles for his own works alone.

### 10.2 The plays

**6. [design] *Romeo*'s first performance on Sat 6 December.**
- *Choice made:* first played at the Theatre on 6 December, the full-moon night, for £12 13s, the year's best house, with the tomb in real dusk. It gives December its climax.
- *Alternative:* hold *Romeo* in rehearsal at the year's end, with the first performance in January 1596. This is safer on dating.
- *Effect of changing:* the Richmond title for 27 December (item 8) must change too, and the 6 December body and photo entries fall away.

**7. The deposition: drafted in June, cut by Tilney, played whole once at Canon Row.**
- *Choice made:* the deposition scene is drafted on 10 June (S094, moved from October). Tilney licenses the play with it cut on 10 July ("Leave out the deposing…"). Hoby's "K. Richard" on 9 December is *Richard II* played whole, once, before Cecil (T3). This makes one arc from three facts: Q1 lacks the scene (T1), Hoby's invitation (T1) and the November succession scare.
- *Alternatives:* (a) Canon Row plays Tilney's licensed version, with the deposition kept privately in William's papers (Gemini's choice). (b) Keep S094 in October as a revision made for Canon Row. (c) Hoby's "K. Richard" is not *Richard II* at all, but *Richard III*, another entertainment, a reading or a picture, which keeps the company out of the politics.
- *Effect of changing:* S112 and December's artistic tension change substantially. Whatever is chosen, the invitation stays T1 and its interpretation stays T3.

**8. The court titles at Richmond.**
- *Choice made:* Fri 26 December *LLL*, Sat 27 *Romeo*, Sun 28 *Errors* (T1 dates, T5 titles). This follows Hunsdon's "something they can laugh at", and loops back to the Night of Errors.
- *Alternatives:* *LLL*, *Errors* and *Two Gentlemen* (GPT), which keeps the court nights apart from the new tragedy; another established play, including a history (but not *Richard II*: Hunsdon refuses it); or all three left untitled ("a comedy").

**9. Kemp's quarrel resolved by Bottom.**
- *Choice made:* on 22 November William brings Kemp three pages of the mechanicals, and Kemp says *thou*. It gives the *Dream* a human origin and the year a comic payoff.
- *Alternative:* leave the friction unresolved, which is truer to Kemp's departure in 1599.

**10. William's roles.**
- *Choice made:* Egeon, Navarre, Gaunt, and the Chorus and the Prince in *Romeo*, on about four afternoons in six. These are "kingly parts" (T3/T4), and they leave him afternoons free to watch his own plays.
- *Alternative:* no named roles, only "(I play)".

**11. Hunsdon has seen *Richard II* at the Theatre (S104).**
- *Choice made:* he stood at the back "like a carter" and wept at Burbage (T5), so his caution on 7 November is informed and affectionate.
- *Alternative:* he knows the play only by report.

### 10.3 The company and the year's shape

**12. The Cross Keys as the winter house.**
- *Choice made:* public winter playing at the Cross Keys in Gracechurch Street from 1 January to Shrove Tuesday, 4 March, after Hunsdon's request of October 1594 (T1 request, T3 outcome). There is no inn in winter 1595/6; the company plays on at the Theatre, which makes the Mayor's hostility visible as a loss.
- *Alternative:* the Theatre all year.
- *Effect of changing:* January to March locations, walking distances, cold exposure, inn sounds and several rehearsal scenes all have to move together. It is not a cosmetic swap of venue.

**13. No tour, and a dark August.**
- *Choice made:* the company plays on to Sat 26 July while the Rose is dark, takes its audience, rests the house from 28 July to 23 August, and reopens on 25 August (Chambers's reading). There is no play on 30 June or 24 July. This frees him for the walk home.
- *Alternatives:* (a) a short Kent or Wiltshire tour, with its own location and inn audio but no Stratford summer; (b) the company goes dark with the Rose at the end of June (GPT and Gemini), so that "waiting costs money" becomes the July thread.

**14. Seven sharers and a stock share.**
- *Choice made:* eight parts of 37s 6d. Seven named sharers (Shakespeare, Burbage, Kemp, Heminges, Phillips, Pope, Bryan) and a stock share held by Heminges. The source book's eighth sharer is unnamed, and Sly and Cowley are ruled out.
- *Alternatives:* (a) name an eighth sharer at T3, perhaps John Duke `[ext]`, unverified; (b) seven partners and no eighth part, with no claim to fix the historical structure (GPT), which avoids inventing a load-bearing unknown.
- *Effect of changing:* the £20 split, Edith's 8s and every share figure must be recomputed.

### 10.4 Home

**15. Two Stratford journeys: Lent on horseback, summer on foot.**
- *Choice made:* a ride out on 26–28 March and back on 8–10 April, and a four-day walk out on 28–31 July, coming back with the carrier's horses on 18–21 August. This uses both open windows in the year and gives the body data two different road signatures. The walk is both penance and plot.
- *Alternatives:* (a) one journey only, summer, leaving Lent as a London writing retreat and sharpening ten months of homesickness; (b) ride both ways, if 40,000–60,000-step road days skew the year's step charts too far in the product.
- *Effect of changing:* the family arcs, the body extremes, the travel budget, the overnight itinerary and at least twenty entries have to be revised together. Do not remove the spring visit without replacing the marriage's first movement.

**16. The second fire's date.**
- *Choice made:* Tue 15 July (T5 date on a T1 fire; the source book thinks July more likely), heard of on Mon 21 July. It fits the news lag and gives him a reason to walk home, with no danger to the family.
- *Alternatives:* (a) leave it undated in Anne's letter ("this fortnight past"); (b) another July date, such as GPT's 12 July, heard of on 18 July. The discredited 21 September stays out.

**17. Gilbert in London.**
- *Choice made:* Gilbert is a journeyman haberdasher lodging in St Bride's (T3). He gives the year a family voice within call range.
- *Alternative:* keep him in Stratford, and give his four calls to Quiney and Field.

**18. New Place as a wish with an address.**
- *Choice made:* Anne names the great house by the Guild Chapel on Sat 16 August ("That one, then"). There is no inquiry, no price and no deposit. It gives the money thread a destination without implying any 1595 record.
- *Alternative:* keep it "a house", with no location.

**19. The lodging household.**
- *Choice made:* Mistress Ansell, a scrivener's widow, with her maid, her cat, her ink and a Flemish weaver as fellow lodger. This makes the June anti-alien riots personal while keeping everyone safe, and gives William a daily critic.
- *Alternative:* a male landlord, such as a leatherseller or GPT's Nettlefold couple, with no stranger in the house. The June riot thread would then have to be carried in the street.

### 10.5 The intimate threads (T3)

**20. [design] The unnamed lady.**
- *Choice made:* kept. She is a married woman of the parish who plays the virginals, with about a dozen lines and a few sightings, marked only by crosses in the almanac. Nothing happens. She gives the sonnets a weather without an identity.
- *Alternatives:* (a) remove her entirely and let the private verse be pure craft, with its *you* unaddressed (GPT has no lady). Nothing else breaks. (b) Keep fewer sonnet scenes and concentrate the year's intimacy almost entirely in the marriage.
- *Not an allowed alternative without a new project decision:* revealing a Fair Youth, a Dark Lady or a Catholic identity as fact.

**21. Southampton: one call and one sighting.**
- *Choice made:* silence over winter and spring, with Florio as go-between. One call on 23 September, in which the earl is happy and distracted and asks for "something for a lady". A birthday on 6 October that William does not mark. A sighting in armour at the tilt on 17 November. This keeps the T3 thread thin.
- *Alternatives:* (a) no direct contact at all, through Florio only; (b) the 23 September meeting as a physical exchange at his household entrance (GPT).

**22. [design] Nick Sorrell's voice breaking on 28 December.**
- *Choice made:* his voice breaks in the jig after *Errors* at Richmond, on the last court night. The ticking clock needs its bell, and the moment is gentle and funny, not a disaster. It is T5 and, admittedly, a little neat.
- *Alternative:* his voice holds through the year and breaks offstage in 1596.
